- Shiu et al. 2024, *A Drosophila computational brain model reveals sensorimotor processing*, Nature. LIF model and edge list (github.com/philshiu/Drosophila_brain_model).
- Berg et al. 2026, *Sexual dimorphism in the complete Drosophila male central nervous system connectome*, Cell 189, 5504-5526 (doi:10.1016/j.cell.2026.08.015; preprint bioRxiv 2025). Janelia male CNS v1.0 (CC-BY), soma positions for the brain panel.
- Bennett, Philippides & Nowotny 2021, *Learning with reinforcement prediction errors in a model of the Drosophila mushroom body*, Nature Communications.
- Caron, Ruta, Abbott & Axel 2013, *Random convergence of olfactory inputs in the Drosophila mushroom body*, Nature.


### Disconnected-trained control message-dependence diagnostic

- **Question / hypothesis.** Did the disconnected-trained student's 24/24 fixed-evaluation score still require informative teacher messages? The prospective gate in the prior entry required both shuffled and disconnected evaluation scores to be at least 4/24 below its correct-message score.
- **Experiment / controls.** Reconstructed the archived Fly B seed-702 state trained for eight 24-round episodes with no message, using the unchanged reward rule and teachers 501/502 training plan. The runner checked every archived training score and the archived continuous correct-message score, then verified the prior fixed-stream, round-reset correct-message score. Frozen weights were evaluated on the same 24 balanced teacher-506 held-out rounds with correct trained, mapping-balanced shuffled, or disconnected messages. Per-source-trial network and action-tie streams were fixed; evaluation had no feedback and no weight changes. No new training seed was introduced.
- **Measurements.** Correct messages: **24/24** (12/12 each mapping). Shuffled: **14/24** (7/12 each mapping), a 10/24 drop. Disconnected: **11/24** (4/12 mapping A, 7/12 B), a 13/24 drop. Both drops exceeded the preset 4/24 gate.
- **Conclusion / limits.** The message-dependence hypothesis passed. The control tie in the prior learning diagnostic reflects a student trained without teacher input that can nevertheless use informative stimulation immediately at evaluation; its 24/24 is not evidence that teacher activity caused its reward learning. This single frozen state and reused held-out teacher do not establish a general mechanism. The original 4/24 replication gain gate and the fixed-evaluation advantage-over-all-controls gate remain failed; stop the one-cue teaching line without retuning.
- **Next independent direction.** The notebook's earlier four-MBON population measurements suggest that graded source activity may carry information lost by binary thresholding. Before another teaching run, test a fixed graded receiving interface on one known-signal decoding task with frozen source recordings. Prospectively require at least 18/24 balanced correct choices and a margin of at least 4/24 over both mapping-balanced shuffled and disconnected inputs, with at least 8/12 correct per mapping; otherwise reject this interface. This would be a channel diagnostic, not evidence of teacher-driven learning.
- **Reproduce / raw data.** node tools/two_cue_transfer.mjs single_cue_disconnected_control 202 02 lanes02 150; [frozen control results](../data/two_cue_single_cue_disconnected_control_02_202_lanes02.json); [preceding learning-control result](../data/two_cue_single_cue_learning_reset_02_202_lanes02.json).

### Fixed graded four-MBON receiving channel

- **Question / hypothesis.** Can raw graded activity from four selected teacher MBONs support a cue-free mapping choice in Fly B? The prospective channel gate was at least 18/24 correct, at least 8/12 per mapping, and at least 4/24 above both shuffled and disconnected messages.
- **Experiment / controls.** Fixed source neurons 2146, 2134, 2124, 2122 from the archived population analysis. Each recorded trace independently drove one existing two-KC target pair for 0-40 ms at 300 Hz times its value divided by that neuron's calibration-teacher 95th percentile, capped at 300 Hz. This is a raw four-channel interface with no mapping decoder in Fly B. Calibration teachers 501/502 alone set the four scales; teacher recordings 501/502 supplied eight ordinary 24-round reward-training episodes to one student, seed 703. Teachers 503/504 supplied 24 balanced frozen evaluation rounds, six per teacher and mapping, with no cue. Evaluation reset state each round and fixed trial random streams. Controls used the same frozen student weights with mapping-balanced shuffled source vectors (six vectors of each source mapping assigned to each evaluated mapping) or no input. No core model change or weight sharing occurred.
- **Measurements.** Before training, correct messages scored 12/24 (mapping A/B: 7/12, 5/12). After training, correct messages scored **15/24** (11/12, 4/12), shuffled 12/24 (8/12, 4/12), and disconnected 7/24 (2/12, 5/12). Correct exceeded shuffled by only 3/24; 15/24 and 4/12 on mapping B also missed the fixed gates. Training episode scores were 12, 12, 12, 13, 13, 16, 15, 12 out of 24. A calibration-only external nearest-centroid analysis of these exact 24 held-out source vectors classified 17/24 (6/12, 11/12); that analytical readout was not connected to either fly.
- **Conclusion / limitations.** The fixed graded interface **failed its readiness gate**. The task-matched source sample also scored below 18/24 with the archived analytical decoder, so these data do not cleanly isolate channel loss from source information limits. One student seed and one fixed 24-trial sample do not justify changing scales or KC targets on this held-out set. No teaching claim follows; stop this receiving-interface line.
- **Next independent direction.** Test source-information validity without Fly B: apply the already fixed four-neuron, calibration-teacher nearest-centroid readout to balanced cue-1 recordings from fresh trained teachers 505 and 506. Prospectively require at least 18/24 and 8/12 per mapping on **each** teacher, with mapping-balanced shuffled labels at most 14/24; otherwise reject this four-MBON source representation as a basis for another receiving-channel test. This is a source diagnostic, not interface tuning or a teaching run.
- **Reproduce / raw data.** `node tools/two_cue_transfer.mjs graded_population_decode 203 02 lanes02 150`; [trial-level graded-channel results](../data/two_cue_graded_population_decode_02_203_lanes02.json); [archived population selection](../data/two_cue_population_check.json).

### Fresh-teacher four-MBON source-information check

- **Question / hypothesis.** Does the previously fixed four-MBON analytical readout retain hidden-mapping information on fresh trained teachers 505 and 506? The prospective gate was at least 18/24 correct and 8/12 on each mapping for **each** teacher, with one mapping-balanced shuffled-label control at most 14/24 per teacher.
- **Experiment / controls.** Used saved 120 ms cue-1 traces from neurons 2146, 2134, 2124, and 2122. Reconstructed the exact cue-1 standardized nearest-centroid readout from calibration teachers 501/502, including scale fitted over both cues; it reproduced the archived teacher-503/504 cue-1 counts before evaluation. Evaluated the 24 balanced test-2 recordings from each fresh trained teacher, their matched untrained recordings, one fixed mapping-balanced label shuffle per teacher, and an absent-signal constant-class baseline. No Fly B run, learning, channel change, or weight transfer; simulation seed and training budget: none new (saved teacher seeds 505/506).
- **Measurements.** Trained teacher 505: **20/24** (10/12 mapping A, 10/12 B); teacher 506: **21/24** (9/12 A, 12/12 B). Matched untrained activity scored 12/24 for each; shuffled labels scored 14/24 and 13/24; the disconnected constant-class baseline scored 12/24 by construction. The fixed gate passed on both teachers.
- **Conclusion / limitations.** These four source traces contain mapping information that generalizes to two fresh trained teachers under an external analytical readout. This does not rescue the failed graded Fly B receiving interface or show teacher-driven student learning. The teacher recordings were saved from earlier work, each evaluation has only 24 trials, the shuffle is one fixed draw, and an external decoder is an information measurement rather than a biological receiver.
- **Next independent direction.** Diagnose where the fixed graded channel loses information: with frozen student weights, compare mapping separability of the four target-KC spike counts after the existing 0-40 ms stimulation against the source-vector separability on the same teacher trials. Fit any analytical KC readout on calibration teachers 501/502 only and evaluate teachers 505/506; require at least 18/24 and 8/12 per mapping on each, with shuffled at most 14/24, to say the input layer preserves decodable information. Otherwise reject input fidelity. Include untrained and disconnected inputs; do not train Fly B or alter the failed interface gate.
- **Reproduce / raw data.** `node tools/check_two_cue_population_fresh.mjs`; [trial-level predictions and controls](../data/two_cue_population_fresh.json); [archived decoder selection](../data/two_cue_population_check.json).

### Frozen graded-channel target-KC fidelity check

- **Question / hypothesis.** Does the fixed four-channel interface preserve hidden-mapping information in its four target-KC pair spike counts during the 0–40 ms message window? The prospective gate required at least 18/24 correct and 8/12 on each mapping for each fresh teacher, with mapping-balanced shuffled labels at most 14/24.
- **Experiment / controls.** Reused the archived four MBON sources, four KC pairs, calibration-only 95th-percentile rate scales, and source recordings. In an untrained, frozen student network with wiring seed 703, each recorded trace drove its fixed KC pair for 40 ms. Neural state was reset each trial; network randomness depended on teacher, test, and source-round identity, never the mapping label. A standardized nearest-centroid readout of KC pair spike counts was fitted only on trained teacher-501/502 cue-1 recordings, then frozen for teacher-505/506 test-2 recordings. This analytical readout remained outside both flies. Matched untrained source activity, disconnected input, and the archived mapping-balanced shuffled labels were controls. Training budget: zero new episodes; one student network seed and two saved evaluation teacher seeds. Weights were checked unchanged.
- **Measurements.** Teacher 505 trained KC readout: **20/24** (mapping A/B 9/12, 11/12); teacher 506: **20/24** (8/12, 12/12). Untrained-source inputs scored 12/24 on each; disconnected inputs 12/24 on each. Shuffled labels scored **16/24** for 505 and 14/24 for 506. The earlier source-vector readout on these teachers scored 20/24 and 21/24, respectively.
- **Result / limitations.** The fixed input-fidelity gate **failed** because teacher 505's shuffled control exceeded 14/24. The high trained scores suggest mapping information can reach the target KCs, but do not establish the specified controlled fidelity claim or rescue the failed Fly B decision interface. The shuffle was one fixed draw, the sample was small, and only one student wiring seed was measured. An initial output used a random seed containing the mapping label; it is preserved as invalid and excluded from these conclusions. No learning or teaching experiment was run.
- **Next distinct question.** Treat the fixed KC fidelity line as stopped. To assess the control issue without changing the failed gate, compute an exact mapping-balanced label-permutation distribution for the *saved, frozen* KC predictions on each fresh teacher. Prospectively, the distinct evidence-strength check passes only if each teacher's one-sided probability of at least its observed 20/24 under balanced labels is at most 0.05; otherwise the apparent KC separability remains inconclusive. Do not fit or tune a new readout.
- **Reproduce / raw data.** `node tools/check_two_cue_population_kc.mjs`; [valid fixed-stream KC measurements](../data/two_cue_population_kc_fixed_rng.json); [invalid label-seeded pilot, excluded](../data/two_cue_population_kc.json).

### Exact balanced-label check of archived KC predictions

- **Question / hypothesis.** Is each fresh teacher's archived 20/24 target-KC mapping score unusual under *all* balanced label assignments? The prospective gate was a one-sided probability at most 0.05 for both teachers. This tests the prior shuffled-control issue without changing its failed fidelity gate.
- **Experiment / controls.** Used only saved, frozen predictions from the fixed-stream KC diagnostic for teachers 505/506. Enumerated the exact score distribution combinatorially over all 2,704,156 assignments of 12 mapping-A and 12 mapping-B labels per teacher, keeping predictions and class balance fixed. The original shuffled-label scores (16/24 and 14/24), untrained-source scores (12/24 each), and disconnected scores (12/24 each) remain the archived controls. No new seeds, simulation, training episodes, refitting, or channel changes.
- **Measurements.** Teacher 505 predicted mapping B on 14 trials; 3,731/2,704,156 balanced assignments score at least its observed 20/24, giving one-sided p = **0.001380**. Teacher 506 predicted B on 16 trials; 1,820/2,704,156 assignments score at least 20/24, p = **0.000673**. Both pass the distinct evidence-strength gate. An independent `math.comb` calculation reproduced both exact counts.
- **Conclusion / limitations.** The archived KC predictions are unlikely under balanced labels, despite the single 16/24 shuffled draw. This supports mapping information at the target KCs under this analytical readout. The original KC fidelity gate still **failed** by its fixed shuffled threshold, and the trained Fly B graded interface still failed its choice gate (15/24, mapping B 4/12); neither failure is reversed by this analysis. These are reused 24-trial samples from two teachers and one student wiring seed, and label permutations assume exchangeable trials within each teacher. No teacher-driven student learning follows.
- **Next distinct direction.** Test a downstream information-loss hypothesis with the existing fixed graded interface: on frozen trained student-703 weights, record the previously specified target-KC and action-MBON population spike counts on the same saved teacher-505/506 trials, with matched untrained and disconnected source controls. Fit external readouts only on teacher-501/502 calibration trials. Prospectively, if KC decoding remains at least 18/24 on each teacher while MBON decoding is at most 14/24 on each, support a KC-to-MBON bottleneck; if MBON decoding reaches at least 18/24 on both, reject that bottleneck and localize the failure closer to choice. Other outcomes are inconclusive. This would be a new measurement diagnostic, not a teaching run or an attempt to rescue the failed interface gate.
- **Reproduce / raw data.** `node tools/check_two_cue_population_kc_permutation.mjs`; [exact distributions and probabilities](../data/two_cue_population_kc_permutation.json); [archived KC predictions](../data/two_cue_population_kc_fixed_rng.json).

### Frozen trained-student KC-to-MBON pathway diagnostic

- **Question / hypothesis.** Did the fixed graded interface lose mapping information between its target KCs and the student's action MBON population? The prospective gate was KC decoding at least 18/24 on both fresh teachers with MBON decoding at most 14/24 on both to support a bottleneck; MBON decoding at least 18/24 on both would reject it.
- **Experiment / controls.** Reconstructed the archived seed-703 student after eight 24-round reward-training episodes with the unchanged four-trace, 0-40 ms KC interface. Archived training and frozen evaluation results matched exactly. At frozen trained weights, recorded 0-40 ms target-KC pair spikes and 0-240 ms per-neuron action-0/2 MBON spikes on balanced cue-free teacher-505/506 test-2 trials. External standardized nearest-centroid readouts were fitted only on teacher-501/502 test-2 trials and remained outside both flies. Each trial reset neural state and used source-identity-fixed random streams. Matched untrained-source, disconnected, and balanced shuffled-label controls were evaluated. No new teacher or student seed, weight transfer, model change, or evaluation learning.
- **Measurements.** Teacher 505: trained KC **20/24** (10/12 each mapping), MBON **20/24** (9/12 A, 11/12 B); teacher 506: KC **21/24** (9/12 A, 12/12 B), MBON **20/24** (8/12 A, 12/12 B). Both untrained-source and disconnected controls scored 12/24 for both readouts on each teacher. Balanced shuffled labels scored KC/MBON 14/24 and 16/24 for 505, and 13/24 and 12/24 for 506. The original graded Fly B choice test remained 15/24 on teachers 503/504, with mapping B 4/12.
- **Result / limitations.** MBON decoding reached the predeclared 18/24 rejection gate for both fresh teachers, so this specified KC-to-MBON bottleneck hypothesis **failed**. An external readout can recover mapping from action MBON spikes at these frozen weights; Fly B's own decision still has not passed its choice gate. The analytical readout uses 28 MBON counts and 48 calibration trials, the shuffled-label score for teacher 505 was 16/24, and this new protocol and teacher cohort differ from the original choice test. This is a pathway measurement, not a teaching result. The failed receiving interface and one-cue teaching gates remain unchanged.
- **Next distinct question.** At the same frozen trained student-703 weights on teachers 505/506, do Fly B's actual choices fail despite decodable MBON activity? Measure paired correct choices and external MBON predictions on identical trials, with shuffled and disconnected messages; prospectively, a decision-readout mismatch requires MBON decoding at least 18/24 on each teacher while Fly B choices are at most 14/24 on each. If choices reach at least 18/24 on both, the earlier interface failure may instead be cohort-specific; other outcomes are inconclusive. Do not retune the interface or claim teaching.
- **Reproduce / raw data.** `node tools/two_cue_transfer.mjs graded_population_pathway 203 02 lanes02 150`; [pathway results](../data/two_cue_graded_population_pathway_02_203_lanes02.json); [archived graded choice test](../data/two_cue_graded_population_decode_02_203_lanes02.json).

### Frozen graded-channel choice versus MBON readout

- **Question / hypothesis.** Do Fly B's actual choices fail on the same fresh teacher trials where its action-MBON activity remains externally decodable? The prospective gate required MBON decoding at least 18/24 and Fly B choice at most 14/24 on both teachers to support a decision mismatch; choice at least 18/24 on both would suggest cohort-specific earlier failure.
- **Experiment / controls.** Reconstructed archived student-703 weights after eight 24-round training episodes with teachers 501/502 and the unchanged graded four-MBON-to-KC channel; verified archived training and original frozen outcomes. Replayed balanced, cue-free teacher-505/506 test-2 trials with neural state reset per trial and source-identity-fixed network and action-tie streams. Recorded Fly B choices alongside the existing calibration-only external MBON readout. Replayed the same trial schedule with mapping-balanced shuffled source vectors and disconnected input. Evaluation had no feedback or weight changes; no new training seed or core-model change.
- **Measurements.** Teacher 505: MBON mapping readout **20/24** (A/B 9/12, 11/12); Fly B correct messages **12/24** (11/12, 1/12), shuffled **9/24**, disconnected **2/24**. Teacher 506: MBON **20/24** (8/12, 12/12); Fly B correct **16/24** (10/12, 6/12), shuffled **11/24**, disconnected **8/24**. On mapping B, Fly B chose the unrewarded action 1 on 8/12 teacher-505 rounds and 2/12 teacher-506 rounds; the external MBON readout used only action-0/2 neurons. Ten teacher-505 and six teacher-506 mapping-B trials were externally classified correctly but chosen incorrectly by Fly B.
- **Result / limitations.** The prespecified two-teacher mismatch gate was **inconclusive**: MBON decoding reached 20/24 on both, but Fly B choices were 12/24 and 16/24, straddling the 14/24 cutoff. Neither teacher reached the 18/24 choice branch. Teacher-specific action-1 intrusions suggest a separate choice competition question; they do not rescue the failed original graded interface gate (15/24 on teachers 503/504) or establish teacher-driven learning. These are reused teacher recordings and one trained student, with an external readout that is not Fly B's decision rule.
- **Next distinct question.** Did informative stimulation itself raise action-1 versus action-2 decision drive on the eight teacher-505 mapping-B trials where Fly B chose action 1? In a frozen paired replay of those same trials, compare decision-time action-1 minus action-2 MBON rates with correct versus disconnected input, with teacher 506 and shuffled messages as context. A prospective interference gate is a larger margin under correct input on at least 6/8 selected teacher-505 trials and a positive mean paired difference; otherwise reject this specific explanation. This is a mechanism diagnostic, not interface tuning or a renewed teaching claim.
- **Reproduce / raw data.** `node tools/two_cue_transfer.mjs graded_population_choice 203 02 lanes02 150`; [paired choices and controls](../data/two_cue_graded_population_choice_02_203_lanes02.json); [archived pathway readout](../data/two_cue_graded_population_pathway_02_203_lanes02.json).

### Frozen graded-channel action-competition drive diagnostic

- **Question / hypothesis.** Did correct teacher stimulation increase action-1 relative to action-2 decision drive on the eight archived teacher-505 mapping-B trials where Fly B chose action 1? The prospective gate required a larger action-1-minus-action-2 MBON rate margin with correct than disconnected messages on at least 6/8 trials, with positive mean paired difference.
- **Experiment / controls.** Reconstructed the archived eight-episode student-703 weights and replayed the same frozen teacher-505/506 cue-free trials. Only decision-time per-action MBON rates were added to the existing correct, mapping-balanced shuffled, and disconnected message comparisons. Neural state reset per trial, source-identity-fixed network and tie-break streams, source traces, and the graded channel remained unchanged. All choices matched the archived choice replay exactly; there was no evaluation feedback or weight change. No new seeds were used.
- **Measurements.** On all **8/8** selected teacher-505 mapping-B action-1 trials, the correct-message rates were **[0, 0, 0]**, and disconnected rates were also **[0, 0, 0]**. Correct-minus-disconnected action-1/action-2 margin was positive on **0/8**, with mean **0**. Shuffled-message margins averaged -0.0714 spikes per action-2 MBON. The two corresponding teacher-506 action-1 trials likewise had [0, 0, 0] correct and disconnected rates and zero paired margin. The archived correct-message choice totals remained 12/24 for teacher 505 and 16/24 for teacher 506.
- **Result / limits.** The fixed interference gate failed. These selected action-1 choices were three-way zero-rate ties resolved by the existing action tie-break random stream, not evidence of teacher-driven action-1 excitation. This does not explain every mapping-B error or identify why these trials lacked decision-time MBON spikes. The original graded interface choice gate and one-cue teaching replication remain failed; no new teaching claim follows.
- **Next distinct direction.** Test whether all-zero decision ties systematically account for mapping-B errors across both fresh teachers, rather than only the selected action-1 subset. On the same frozen correct, shuffled, and disconnected trials, record the three decision rates for every round. Prospectively, support this tie mechanism only if all 10 archived mapping-B action-1 intrusions are three-way zero-rate ties and the fraction of zero-rate trials is higher among mapping-B errors than correct choices on **each** teacher; otherwise reject it. Do not change the graded channel or reopen its failed choice gate.
- **Reproduce / raw data.** `node tools/two_cue_transfer.mjs graded_population_drive 203 02 lanes02 150`; [paired rates and choices](../data/two_cue_graded_population_drive_02_203_lanes02.json); [archived choices](../data/two_cue_graded_population_choice_02_203_lanes02.json).

### Frozen graded-channel zero-rate tie prevalence

- **Question / hypothesis.** Do all-zero decision-rate ties systematically explain mapping-B choice errors with the fixed graded channel? The prospective gate required all 10 archived mapping-B action-1 intrusions to be zero-rate ties and, on each teacher, a higher zero-tie fraction among mapping-B errors than correct choices.
- **Experiment / controls.** Reconstructed the archived eight-episode student-703 weights and replayed the same frozen, cue-free teacher-505/506 trials with correct, mapping-balanced shuffled, or disconnected messages. Added per-trial decision rates to the existing choice replay; choices matched the archive in every condition. The four-trace channel, round resets, fixed trial random streams, and dopamine learning rule were unchanged. Evaluation had no feedback or weight updates; no new teacher or student seeds were used.
- **Measurements.** With correct messages, all 8 teacher-505 and 2 teacher-506 mapping-B action-1 intrusions were three-way zero-rate ties. Teacher 505: zero ties on 8/11 mapping-B errors versus 1/1 correct choice. Teacher 506: 3/6 errors versus 5/6 correct choices. Total mapping-B zero ties were 9/12 and 8/12. Shuffled-message error ties were 5/12 and 3/10; disconnected-message error ties were 11/11 and 6/6 for teachers 505/506. Correct-message choice totals remained 12/24 and 16/24, matching the archive.
- **Result / limitations.** The first condition of the gate passed, but the error-enrichment condition failed on both teachers. Silent ties explain the selected action-1 intrusions, not the wider mapping-B error pattern; three teacher-505 and three teacher-506 mapping-B errors had nonzero decision rates. The sample contains only one trained student and saved teacher trials. The original graded interface choice gate and one-cue teaching replication remain failed; no learning or general teaching claim follows.
- **Next independent question.** Does the external MBON readout's 20/24 mapping score exploit information in trials where Fly B's own action rates tie at zero? Join the archived calibration-only MBON predictions to these frozen tie flags, without refitting or simulation. Prospectively support an informative-silence explanation only if the external prediction is at least 75% accurate on zero-rate trials and at least three choices better than Fly B on those trials for each teacher; otherwise reject it. This diagnostic would not reopen the failed receiving interface.
- **Reproduce / raw data.** `node tools/two_cue_transfer.mjs graded_population_ties 203 02 lanes02 150`; [per-trial rates and controls](../data/two_cue_graded_population_ties_02_203_lanes02.json); [archived external predictions](../data/two_cue_graded_population_choice_02_203_lanes02.json).

### Graded silent-trial archive check

- **Question / hypothesis.** Does the external action-MBON readout recover mapping information on trials where Fly B's own decision rates are all zero? The previous prospective numerical gate required at least 75% external accuracy and an advantage of at least three choices over Fly B on zero-rate correct-message trials for each teacher.
- **Experiment / controls.** Joined archived calibration-only MBON predictions, frozen Fly B choices, and decision-rate flags by teacher, mapping, source round, and action. Verified every prediction and choice against the prior choice archive. Analyzed correct, mapping-balanced shuffled, and disconnected messages separately; no simulation, new seeds, training episodes, model change, or refitting. Saved teachers 505/506 and trained student 703 were reused (original student training: eight 24-round episodes).
- **Measurements.** Correct-message zero-rate trials: teacher 505 had external 9/10 versus Fly B 1/10; teacher 506 had external 8/10 versus Fly B 5/10. Both pass the specified numerical gate. However, those subsets contained mapping A/B counts of 1/9 and 2/8, and the external readout predicted B on all 10/10 trials for each teacher. A constant-B control therefore also scores 9/10 and 8/10; external advantage over this control is zero. On shuffled-message zero-rate trials the readout likewise predicted B on 9/9 for each teacher, scoring 5/9. With disconnected messages all 24 trials per teacher were zero-rate, and the readout always predicted B, scoring 12/24. Correct-message nonzero-rate trials gave external 11/14 and 12/14 versus Fly B 11/14 on both teachers.
- **Result / limitations.** The predeclared accuracy-and-choice gate passed, but its apparent success is fully explained by mapping imbalance in the selected silent subsets. It does not establish mapping information in silent trials. Selection on zero rates is mapping-dependent, and one student plus two saved teacher cohorts limit generality. The graded receiving-channel choice gate and one-cue teacher-specific learning gates remain failed; no teaching claim follows.
- **Next independent direction.** Test whether informative source activity produces mapping-B-specific decision silence on the separate saved teacher-503/504 cohort at the same frozen student-703 weights. Record zero-rate counts by mapping under correct and mapping-balanced shuffled messages, with disconnected input as a silence control. Prospectively support the mapping-dependent silence mechanism only if correct-message B has at least four more zero ties than A out of 12 on each teacher while shuffled B-minus-A is at most two on each; otherwise reject it. This diagnoses a failure mechanism and does not reopen the graded interface gate.
- **Reproduce / raw data.** `node tools/check_two_cue_graded_silent.mjs`; [archive join and controls](../data/two_cue_graded_population_silent_archive.json); [tie trials](../data/two_cue_graded_population_ties_02_203_lanes02.json); [archived predictions](../data/two_cue_graded_population_choice_02_203_lanes02.json).
### Frozen graded-channel mapping-dependent silence on teachers 503/504

- **Question / hypothesis.** Does the fixed graded source channel cause mapping-B-specific zero-rate decision ties on the independent teacher-503/504 cohort? The predeclared gate required at least four more zero ties on B than A (12 trials each) with correct messages on each teacher, and a B-minus-A gap of at most two with mapping-balanced shuffled messages.
- **Experiment / controls.** Reconstructed the archived eight-episode student-703 state; its training scores and original frozen choice results matched the saved run. Replayed 24 balanced test-2 cue-1 source recordings per teacher, with frozen weights, round resets, fixed source-identity trial streams, no cue, and the unchanged four-MBON to KC channel. Compared correct trained traces, a balanced shuffled trace assignment, and disconnected input. No new seeds, training, learning-rule change, or parameter tuning. Original teacher seeds: 503/504; student seed: 703; new training budget: zero.
- **Measurements.** Teacher 503 zero-rate ties A/B: correct **1/12, 8/12** (B-A = 7); shuffled **4/12, 5/12** (+1); disconnected **12/12, 12/12** (0). Teacher 504: correct **1/12, 7/12** (+6); shuffled **5/12, 3/12** (-2); disconnected **12/12, 12/12** (0). The prospective gate passed on both teachers.
- **Conclusion / limitations.** Correct trained traces were associated with mapping-dependent decision silence in this frozen student, whereas the selected shuffled control was not. This supports a specific channel/choice failure mechanism, not decodable information within silent trials, teacher-caused reward learning, or general teaching. The balanced shuffle is one fixed assignment, teachers and student are reused, and the fixed graded receiving interface still fails its original choice gate. The one-cue replication and learning-control gates also remain failed.
- **Next justified experiment.** Test whether this silence pattern was present before student training. On the same teacher-503/504 recordings and fixed trial streams, replay initial student-703 weights with correct, balanced shuffled, and disconnected messages. Predeclare a structural channel effect if correct-message B-minus-A zero ties are at least four on both teachers before training; a training-dependent effect if both are at most two; otherwise call the mechanism mixed or inconclusive. No new teaching test follows from this diagnostic alone.
- **Reproduce / raw data.** `node tools/two_cue_transfer.mjs graded_population_silence503 203 02 lanes02 150`; [trial-level rates and controls](../data/two_cue_graded_population_silence503_02_203_lanes02.json).


### Initial-weight graded-channel silence diagnostic

- **Question / hypothesis.** Was the teacher-message mapping-B silence present before student reward training? The prospective gate called a structural channel effect if correct-message B-minus-A zero ties were at least 4/12 on both teacher-503/504 cohorts; at most 2/12 on both would favor a training-dependent effect.
- **Experiment / controls.** Reconstructed student seed 703 and verified its archived before-training choices, eight training scores, and trained frozen outcomes. Replayed its initial weights, frozen, on the same 24 balanced test-2 cue-1 recordings per teacher and source-identity-fixed trial streams as the trained-state silence diagnostic. Compared correct trained, mapping-balanced shuffled, and disconnected messages. The four-MBON channel, 0-40 ms timing, cue-free task, and decision rule were unchanged. Initial-state training budget: zero; no new seeds or weight updates.
- **Measurements.** Initial correct-message zero ties on mapping A/B were **6/12, 10/12** for teacher 503 (B-A = 4) and **4/12, 10/12** for teacher 504 (+6). Shuffled counts were 9/12, 8/12 and 8/12, 7/12 (both B-A = -1). Disconnected counts were 12/12 on both mappings for both teachers. At archived trained weights, correct-message counts were 1/12, 8/12 and 1/12, 7/12, respectively.
- **Result / limitations.** The fixed structural-effect gate passed: mapping-dependent silence was already present at initial weights, so student reward training is not required to create it. Training altered absolute silence, especially on mapping A, but this paired observation does not isolate a synaptic mechanism. The same two teachers, one student seed, and one fixed shuffle were reused. The graded interface's original choice gate and one-cue teaching controls remain failed; no teacher-specific learning claim follows.
- **Next independent direction.** The structural effect leaves open whether lower *aggregate commanded KC stimulation* for mapping B explains its silence. On the saved teacher-503/504 traces, apply the already fixed calibration ceilings and compute mean summed commanded rate by mapping before Fly B. Prospectively, support an amplitude association only if mean A exceeds mean B by at least 25% on **each** teacher; otherwise reject that specific amplitude explanation. This archive-only audit is not a renewed receiving-channel gate or teaching experiment.
- **Reproduce / raw data.** `node tools/two_cue_transfer.mjs graded_population_initial_silence 203 02 lanes02 150`; [initial-weight trials](../data/two_cue_graded_population_initial_silence_02_203_lanes02.json); [trained-weight comparison](../data/two_cue_graded_population_silence503_02_203_lanes02.json).

### Locked one-bit learning redesign (planned )

- **Question.** Does reward training with actual trained Fly A activity improve Fly B beyond the immediate action bias caused by receiving that same activity at evaluation? The earlier six paired students improved by 31 decisions in total, 29 on mapping B/cue 1, but later one-cue work found trial-order and state carryover, a 21/24 initial score, and a disconnected-trained state that tied the correct-trained state. The graded interface's decision silence is another reason to measure actual choices and zero-rate ties, not infer learning from external decoding.
- **Locked task/channel.** Use the previously validated one-bit neuron-2132 message (`trace < 1.915`), 300 Hz for 0-40 ms to KCs 506/545 (bit 0) or 113/1421 (bit 1), cue 1 at 70-240 ms, retained MBON counts, decision at 240 ms, and the original reward, connectome, dynamics, and dopamine update. Each 24-round episode interleaves 12 of each hidden mapping; teacher activity comes only from saved 120 ms trained Fly A traces. No mapping or correct-action value enters the message.
- **Matched training.** Eight 24-round episodes use archived independent teachers 501/502, with identical mapping order, initial Fly B weights, per-trial network and action-tie random seeds, and round-start neural resets in four arms: correct trained bits, mapping-balanced shuffled bits, untrained-teacher bits, and disconnected. These resets and fixed streams are a new *protocol*, applied equally to every arm; old failed gates remain failed. No training trial uses the evaluation teacher.
- **Frozen evaluation.** Pilot: archived teacher 506 and student 701, used only to test validity. If valid, one locked replication uses fresh independent teachers 507/508 and new students 704/705, one paired student per teacher. Both mappings remain balanced. Each frozen state receives the same held-out correct bits; also test shuffled and disconnected messages on the correct-trained state. Reset neural state each trial, fix network and tie streams by held-out source identity, and replay two fixed trial orders. Record trial actions, action rates and zero-rate ties, scores by mapping, and unchanged weights.
- **Validity gate, checked before interpreting training.** Each evaluation teacher must have at least 75% frozen accuracy on both mappings and at least 18/24 correct 2132 bits on the balanced cue-1 held-out set. The shuffle must differ on at least 6/24 bits and have mapping-conditional bit-1 rates within 1/12. The initial correct-message score must be at most 18/24, leaving at least six decisions of headroom. For every frozen state/message condition, actions keyed by source identity must match exactly across the two trial orders; evaluation must not change weights. If the pilot fails any check, stop without replication or parameter changes.
- **Prospective learning criterion.** On each fresh teacher/student pair, correct-trained frozen accuracy with correct bits must improve by at least 4/24 over its own pretraining score and exceed *each* shuffled-, untrained-, and disconnected-trained state by at least 4/24 when all receive identical correct bits. Require at least 8/12 on each mapping and a drop of at least 4/24 when the correct-trained state receives shuffled or no message. Report all pairs and silent-tie counts, including failures. Both fresh pairs must meet these criteria for preliminary evidence of learned, message-dependent use; otherwise do not claim reliable learning or teaching. A high initial score or teacher-source failure is reported, never replaced with another seed.
- **Resource limit.** One pilot, then at most the two locked fresh pairs; no channel search, alternative task, extra direction, or broader replication. Save raw trial results and reproduction commands.

### Locked one-bit pilot validity result

- **Run.** `node tools/two_cue_transfer.mjs single_cue_learning_locked 701 02 lanes02 150 506`; raw result: `data/two_cue_single_cue_learning_locked_02_701_lanes02.json`. No student training or fresh-teacher replication ran.
- **Measured checks.** Teacher 506 frozen accuracy was 94.4% for each mapping. Its recorded 2132 bit matched the mapping-associated bit on 24/24 held-out cue-1 rounds. The shuffled control changed 12/24 bits. Before training, Fly B scored 13/24 with correct messages. Frozen trial actions and MBON rates matched exactly across the two fixed orders under correct, shuffled, and disconnected messages; frozen weights were unchanged.
- **Evaluation defect and stop.** The prewritten shuffle validity criterion required mapping-conditional bit-1 frequencies to stay within 1/12 of the correct messages. That criterion is incompatible with destroying mapping information: correct bits had 12/12 ones for A and 0/12 for B, while the shuffled bits had 6/12 for each. The shuffled control preserved the overall cue-1 bit frequency (12/24) and removed the mapping association, so the criterion rejected a control that behaved as intended. The pilot returned `evaluation validity gate failed` before training. No learning claim is possible from this run. The pilot output and original gate are preserved; any corrected gate would require a newly declared pilot, not reinterpretation of this one.

### Corrected locked one-bit shuffle gate (declared before rerun, )

- **Only correction.** Keep the existing deterministic seed-based shuffle and require at least six changed bits, equal overall bit-1 counts between correct and shuffled streams, and shuffled bit-1 counts differing by at most one between the two balanced mappings. This preserves overall bit frequencies while breaking their mapping association. All other gates, channel settings, training and evaluation procedures, and seeds remain fixed.
- **Pilot and replication.** Rerun pilot student 701 with existing teacher 506 under mode `single_cue_learning_locked_v2`, preserving the original failed-gate output. If every validity check passes, use fresh student seeds 704/705 with the existing, preselected teacher recordings 504/505 respectively. This replaces the earlier plan to generate teacher 507/508 because the latest instruction explicitly requires existing recordings. Do not substitute teachers if a source gate fails. Apply the previously declared prospective learning criterion unchanged.

### Corrected one-bit pilot and stopped replication

- **Pilot, student 701 / teacher 506.** `node tools/two_cue_transfer.mjs single_cue_learning_locked_v2 701 02 lanes02 150 506`. Source gate: 94.4% frozen teacher accuracy on each mapping; 24/24 mapping-associated held-out bits. Correct and shuffled streams each contained 12 bit-1 messages; shuffled counts were 6/12 per mapping and 12/24 positions changed. Initial correct-message accuracy was 13/24 (shuffled 4/24, disconnected 2/24). All initial and post-training frozen action/rate replays matched across the two fixed orders and kept weights unchanged. Eight-episode correct-message training led to 23/24 frozen correct-message accuracy (A 12/12, B 11/12); shuffled-, untrained-, and disconnected-message training led to 8/24, 15/24, and 19/24 when all were evaluated with identical correct messages. The correct-trained state fell to 12/24 with shuffled or disconnected evaluation messages. Its own gain was +10/24; margins over shuffled, untrained, and disconnected training were +15, +8, and +4/24. This pilot meets the predeclared numerical learning criterion but is one reused teacher/student pair, not replication. Raw: `data/two_cue_single_cue_learning_locked_v2_02_701_lanes02.json`.
- **First locked replication, student 704 / teacher 504.** `node tools/two_cue_transfer.mjs single_cue_learning_locked_v2 704 02 lanes02 150 504`. Teacher frozen accuracy: 94.4% A, 98.6% B; mapping-associated bits 24/24. The deterministic shuffle changed 12/24 positions, preserved 12 bit-1 messages overall, and split them 6/12 per mapping; frozen actions and rates were order-invariant. Initial correct-message accuracy was **19/24** (A 9/12, B 10/12), above the predeclared **18/24** headroom limit. Initial shuffled and disconnected scores were 10/24 and 1/24. The validity gate failed before training; student 705/teacher 505 was not run. Raw: `data/two_cue_single_cue_learning_locked_v2_02_704_lanes02.json`.
- **Conclusion / next step.** The original pilot failure was a mistaken shuffle check; the corrected pilot passed. The independent replication stopped for high initial accuracy, a distinct and prespecified validity failure. Thus this run cannot establish reproducible learning-dependent transfer. Do not relax the headroom gate based on seed 704. A future prospective design should measure training gain without selecting students by their initial message response, while retaining matched frozen controls.

### Prospective inclusive one-bit replication (declared before runs, )

- **Current obstacle.** The valid pilot has no independent replication. The first fresh student, 704, made 19/24 correct choices before training when given correct messages, versus 1/24 disconnected and 10/24 shuffled. Its source bit was correct on 24/24 rounds. This demonstrates a large immediate message effect at initial weights; the saved pair cannot isolate the teacher-seed and student-seed contributions. The old 18/24 headroom gate appropriately stopped that locked replication, but using it to select future students would condition the cohort on their initial response.
- **Hypothesis / why it matters.** Across preselected independent student and teacher seeds, reward training with actual trained-teacher messages causes a larger change in frozen correct-message accuracy than matched shuffled-, untrained-teacher-, and disconnected-message training. If so, the pilot's learning-dependent effect is reproducible; otherwise the pilot is seed-specific or explained by stimulation and ordinary training. No neuron, threshold, channel, cue, schedule, dynamics, connectome, or learning rule changes.
- **Design / expected measurements.** New protocol mode, distinct from the failed replication: use student seeds **705, 706, 707** with existing independently trained teacher recordings **503, 504, 505**, respectively. These three pairs are fixed now; use all eligible pairs with no replacement or baseline-based exclusion. Retain eight training episodes on source teachers 501/502 and the four arms (correct, mapping-balanced deterministic shuffled, untrained-teacher, disconnected) from identical initial student weights, source-identity-fixed network and agent random streams, and state reset each round. Frozen evaluation uses the same 24 balanced held-out cue-1 rounds and correct teacher bits in all arms, plus shuffled and disconnected evaluation on the correct-trained state, with reversed-order replays. Report each initial score, training curve, score by mapping, zero-rate ties, and all paired post-training differences. The initial score is descriptive, not an eligibility gate. The paired difference under identical held-out correct messages equals the difference in improvement from the common initial state and removes the immediate evaluation stimulus as an explanation.
- **Validity criteria.** Keep the existing source gate (each teacher at least 75% frozen accuracy on both mappings and at least 18/24 mapping-associated bits), corrected shuffle gate (at least six changed positions, equal overall bit count, bit counts balanced across mappings), identical source-keyed frozen actions and rates across the two orders, and unchanged frozen weights. Any validity failure stops the sequence; report it without replacing a seed. The old student-704 headroom failure remains failed and is not reinterpreted.
- **Predeclared outcome criteria.** Preliminary independent replication requires all three eligible pairs to have correct-trained accuracy strictly above **each** control-trained state when evaluated with identical correct messages; the mean paired advantage must be at least **4/24** over each control. The correct-trained state must score at least **8/12 on each mapping** and lose at least **4/24** with shuffled and disconnected evaluation messages in every pair. Report own before-to-after gain and ceiling distance but do not require a fixed own-gain cutoff. Any nonpositive paired advantage is a failed consistency gate. Uniform positive but smaller effects, ceiling-limited pairs, or a failed mapping/dependence criterion are inconclusive for strong replication. Three pairs alone would warrant a larger confirmation, not a definitive teaching claim.
- **Next decision.** Pass: recommend a larger confirmation with fresh teacher/student seeds, without tuning. Fail: report no consistent learning-specific advantage under this locked channel. Inconclusive: keep the causal question open and design a new task or outcome measure prospectively; do not pick seeds or revise this gate after seeing scores. Commands: `node tools/two_cue_transfer.mjs single_cue_learning_prospective 705 02 lanes02 150 503`, then `706 ... 504`, then `707 ... 505` only while prior validity checks pass.

### Prospective inclusive one-bit replication result

- **Runs / validity.** Ran the three commands declared above, with no replacement or tuning. All source gates passed: teacher A/B frozen accuracies were 91.7%/97.2% (503), 94.4%/98.6% (504), and 93.1%/90.3% (505); mapping-associated bits were 24/24, 24/24, and 22/24. All shuffles preserved 12 bit-1 messages, split 6/12 by mapping, and changed 12, 12, and 14 positions. All before/after frozen conditions preserved weights and matched actions and MBON rates exactly across reversed orders. No student was excluded for initial score.

| Fly B / Fly A | Initial correct message | After correct training | After shuffled training | After untrained-message training | After disconnected training | Correct-trained with message shuffled / off |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 705 / 503 | 16/24 | 24/24 (A 12, B 12) | 8/24 | 13/24 | 21/24 | 12/24 / 12/24 |
| 706 / 504 | 16/24 | 24/24 (A 12, B 12) | 9/24 | 14/24 | 20/24 | 12/24 / 12/24 |
| 707 / 505 | 14/24 | 22/24 (A 11, B 11) | 8/24 | 12/24 | 18/24 | 12/24 / 12/24 |

- **Primary contrast and interpretation.** With identical held-out correct messages, correct-minus-disconnected training was **+3, +4, +4/24** (mean **+3.67/24**, range 3–4); correct-minus-shuffled was +16, +15, +14/24; correct-minus-untrained-message was +11, +10, +10/24. Own before-to-after gain was +8/24 in each pair. Correct-minus-disconnected mapping A gains were +2, +3, +2/12; mapping B gains were +1, +1, +2/12. Correct-trained students had zero three-way decision ties in all three evaluations, while disconnected-trained students had 3, 2, and 7. The repeated effect spans both mappings for cue 1, though the disconnected-trained controls also performed well when supplied correct evaluation messages. Removal/shuffling of those messages returned correct-trained accuracy to 12/24, showing message dependence rather than a teacher-independent lasting mapping policy.
- **Predeclared decision.** All validity, sign-consistency, mapping, and message-dependence conditions passed. The mean advantage over disconnected training **missed** the required 4/24 threshold (3.67/24); therefore the strong preliminary replication criterion **did not pass**. Three pairs are too few for a precise effect estimate (one-sided sign test for three positive differences: p=0.125). This is consistent, small learning-dependent evidence under matched conditions, not a definitive teaching claim. No further seed runs or gate changes were made. A larger prospectively powered confirmation with independently recorded teachers would be needed to resolve whether the ~4-round effect is stable; it should include all preselected students and keep the same matched contrasts.
- **Raw results / reproduce.** `data/two_cue_single_cue_learning_prospective_02_705_lanes02.json`, `data/two_cue_single_cue_learning_prospective_02_706_lanes02.json`, `data/two_cue_single_cue_learning_prospective_02_707_lanes02.json`. Commands: `node tools/two_cue_transfer.mjs single_cue_learning_prospective 705 02 lanes02 150 503`; `node tools/two_cue_transfer.mjs single_cue_learning_prospective 706 02 lanes02 150 504`; `node tools/two_cue_transfer.mjs single_cue_learning_prospective 707 02 lanes02 150 505` (the runner preserves existing output files, so reproduction requires separate copies or a clean output location).

### Fresh-teacher one-bit confirmation plan (fixed before results, )

- **Current obstacle / hypothesis / decision value.** The previous three-pair gain over disconnected training was +3, +4, +4/24, below its locked mean-4 gate, and all students used older teacher recordings. Test whether correct-message reward training produces a practical paired improvement over disconnected training across *fresh, independent teacher-student pairs*. A positive result would justify a larger cross-task study; a null or smaller effect would temper the teaching claim. No channel or neural-model parameter changes.
- **Sample and resource budget.** Exactly **8 preselected pairs**: student seeds 801-808. For pair index i=0..7, generate three new source recordings with teacher seeds `601+3i`, `602+3i`, `603+3i`; the first two provide eight alternating training episodes from source tests 0/1, and the third supplies held-out source test 2. Thus evaluation teachers are 603, 606, 609, 612, 615, 618, 621, 624. Maximum 24 teacher-source and 8 student-study runs; no replacement seeds, repeated trials, channel tuning, or extra simulation directions. At most three pairs per execution batch. All three teachers per pair are independently trained on mapping A and mapping B with the existing `source` mode. The independent pair, not its 24 rounds, is the uncertainty unit. No outcome-based interim stopping.
- **Locked procedure / measurements.** Retain cue 1, neuron 2132 at 120 ms, bit `trace < 1.915`, KC targets 506/545 and 113/1421, 300 Hz at 0-40 ms, cue 70-240 ms, retained MBON readout at 240 ms, original reward and dopamine rule, 24 balanced interleaved mapping rounds, eight episodes, per-trial network/agent random streams, and neural-state reset every round. Start correct, shuffled, untrained-teacher, and disconnected training from the same Fly B weights with identical schedules and random streams. Primary frozen evaluation gives *identical held-out correct messages* to all four trained states; also give the correct-trained state shuffled and disconnected evaluation messages. Replay all frozen conditions in original and reverse order; record teacher frozen accuracy and bit quality, pretraining accuracy and maximum possible gain (`24 - pretraining correct`), training curves, frozen scores by mapping/action, zero-rate ties, and weight invariance. Do not exclude a teacher for low learned accuracy or weak 2132 bits; report both and include the pair. This changes only source-cohort selection from the prior three-pair protocol.
- **Validity and stopping.** Require complete source recordings for all three teachers, a deterministic shuffle that changes at least 6/24 bits while preserving overall bit frequency and balancing it across mappings, unchanged frozen weights, and identical source-keyed frozen actions and MBON rates under both orders. No headroom cutoff. Stop the full sequence only for a technical/reproducibility validity failure, a missing source, or the fixed 24-source/8-student budget; retain all completed outputs and do not substitute seeds. Weak teacher learning is a measured outcome, not an exclusion. The old 3.67/24 result remains a failed gate.
- **Primary effect and uncertainty.** For pair i let `d_i = frozen_correct-trained_accuracy_with_correct_messages - frozen_disconnected-trained_accuracy_with_the_same_correct_messages`, measured in correct decisions out of 24. Report all eight `d_i`, their mean, sample SD, and a two-sided **95% Student-t interval across the eight independent pairs**: mean ± `2.364624 * SD / sqrt(8)`. Do not use round-level standard errors. Report mean paired contrasts against shuffled- and untrained-message training and the correct-trained shuffled/disconnected evaluation ablations, each with the same pair-level interval. No data-driven exclusions or threshold revision.
- **Prospective decisions.** Practical confirmation requires primary mean **at least +4/24** and its 95% interval lower bound **above zero**; additionally the lower bounds for correct-minus-shuffled and correct-minus-untrained training must exceed zero, and each evaluation-message ablation must reduce the correct-trained mean by at least 4/24 with an interval lower bound above zero. If the primary interval upper bound is at or below zero, conclude no benefit under this protocol. If the mean is below +4/24, any required interval crosses zero, or controls/ablations fail, report the gate as failed or inconclusive with the observed uncertainty; do not add seeds. Even success is limited to this cue-1 mapping-information task, not permanent map learning or biological fly communication.
- **Commands.** For every preselected teacher seed T, `node tools/two_cue_transfer.mjs source T 02 lanes02 150`. For every student/evaluation-teacher pair (801/603 through 808/624, step 1/3), `node tools/two_cue_transfer.mjs single_cue_learning_fresh STUDENT 02 lanes02 150 EVAL_TEACHER`. Preserve raw JSON and the prior failed-gate files.

The primary mean **2.75/24 is below the required 4/24 practical threshold**, and even its interval upper bound is below 4/24. Therefore the **confirmation success gate fails**. The result supports a small, reproducible learning-dependent and teacher-message-dependent effect under this fixed protocol, with uncertainty conditional on the t-interval assumptions; it does **not** support the predeclared practical magnitude, permanent mapping transfer, whole two-cue mastery, or biological fly-to-fly teaching. The prior three-pair 3.67/24 gate also remains failed. No additional seeds or channel adjustments were run.
