### Two-cue one-bit information feasibility plan (fixed before held-out scoring, )

- **Hypothesis.** The existing neuron-2132 bit (`trace < 1.915` at 120 ms) distinguishes hidden mappings A and B separately for cue 0 and cue 1. This is a new exploratory study; the preceding eight-pair confirmation remains +2.75/24 and below its locked +4/24 practical threshold.
- **Data and decoder.** Use the already saved trained and matched untrained teacher recordings for held-out teachers 603, 606, 609, 612, 615, 618, 621, and 624, test 2 only. Each teacher has 12 trials per mapping and cue. The fixed classifier predicts mapping A from bit 1 and mapping B from bit 0, as in the one-cue interface; do not refit the threshold, source neuron, or bit polarity on held-out data. Report bit-0/bit-1 counts for each mapping and cue, balanced classification accuracy (mean of class recalls), and individual teacher scores. A constant-message disconnected classifier scores 50% on balanced classes. For a deterministic shuffled-label control, swap A/B labels on every other source round within each cue and teacher, balancing the shuffled labels; report its accuracy. Matched untrained source activity is another control.
- **Minimum information gate.** For **each cue separately**, require trained-bit mean balanced accuracy at least 75% (18/24), at least 6 of 8 held-out teachers at or above 16/24, and mean trained accuracy at least 4/24 above **both** the shuffled-label and matched-untrained controls. All eight teachers remain included. If cue 0 fails any condition, stop before Fly B training. A passing cue 1 cannot compensate for cue 0. These thresholds are feasibility criteria for this fixed binary interface, not revised confirmation criteria.
- **Reproduction.** `node tools/check_two_cue_one_bit_feasibility.mjs`; write only a compact summary to `data/two_cue_one_bit_feasibility.json`. No simulations, new seeds, or neural-model changes.

### Two-cue one-bit information feasibility result

- **Hypothesis / experiment.** Applied the locked neuron-2132 threshold and fixed bit-1=>A classifier to saved test-2 recordings from the eight evaluation teachers, separately for each cue. No new teachers, student training, stimulation, or neural changes. Each teacher contributed 12 A and 12 B trials per cue. The source recordings predate this exploratory analysis and were used in the prior one-cue confirmation, so this is a new question on reused data, not a fresh independent confirmation.
- **Results and controls.** Cue 0 trained messages: **9.5/24 mean balanced accuracy** (39.6%; individual teachers 9, 10, 11, 8, 7, 11, 11, 9 out of 24). Its pooled bit-0/bit-1 counts were A **20/76**, B **0/96**. Shuffled-label control: 11.75/24; matched untrained: 12/24; disconnected balanced-class baseline: 12/24. The opposite bit polarity, checked only as a diagnostic, would score 14.5/24 (60.4%), still below the preset 18/24 threshold. Cue 1 trained messages: **22.875/24** (95.3%; individual 24, 22, 24, 23, 24, 24, 18, 24). Its bit-0/bit-1 counts were A **9/87**, B **96/0**; shuffled: 12.125/24; untrained: 12/24; disconnected: 12/24. The shuffled control swaps labels on alternating source rounds within teacher and cue while keeping mapping counts balanced.
- **Conclusion.** Cue 0 failed **all four** prespecified information conditions: mean at least 18/24, at least six teachers at 16/24, and margins of 4/24 over shuffled and untrained activity. Cue 1 passed. The fixed one-bit message is not sufficiently informative for the full two-cue teaching task under this gate. In particular, cue-0 mapping B emitted bit 1 on every held-out trial, so the fixed classifier cannot distinguish it from most mapping-A trials. This is a feasibility failure of the specified source/message, not a measurement of two-cue Fly B learning.
- **Next experiment / stop.** **No Stage 2 pilot or expansion.** Do not train Fly B on the unsupported two-cue signal or retune this interface using these held-out results. The prior one-cue confirmation remains +2.75/24 versus its unmet +4/24 practical threshold. Reproduce with `node tools/check_two_cue_one_bit_feasibility.mjs` in a checkout without the output file; measurements are in `data/two_cue_one_bit_feasibility.json`.

### Locked 2146/2134 two-neuron source check (plan fixed before scoring, )

- **Question.** Does the previously calibrated pair of teacher MBON traces contain enough hidden-mapping information separately on cue 0 and cue 1 to justify a new receiving-side diagnostic? This is an analytical source test, not Fly B learning or a revision of the failed one-bit confirmation.
- **Fixed representation.** Reuse `data/two_cue_encoding_check.json`: neurons 2146/2134, its exact calibration-median thresholds, bit=`trace > threshold`, and the cue-aware, Laplace-smoothed Bernoulli readout fitted only on teachers 501/502. First verify that its archived teacher-503/504 predictions reproduce. Evaluate trained and matched untrained source recordings on test-2 rounds only from independent teachers 603, 606, 609, 612, 615, 618, 621, 624. Keep all teachers, even weak ones. The analytical decoder is outside both flies and is not a proposed receiving circuit.
- **Controls and minimum gate.** Each teacher contributes 12 rounds per mapping per cue. Report balanced accuracy, mapping-specific bit-pair counts, and teacher-level results. Compare with a 12/24 constant-class baseline, matched untrained traces, and a deterministic mapping-balanced shuffled-label control that swaps labels on alternating source rounds within each teacher and cue. For **each cue separately**, require mean trained accuracy at least 18/24, at least 6/8 teachers at or above 16/24, and mean margins of at least 4/24 over both shuffled and untrained controls. If either cue fails, stop without a receiving simulation or new student training; do not select another threshold or decoder from these held-out records.
- **Reproduction.** Run one read-only source check with the locked encoding. Save its compact measurements in `data/two_cue_2146_2134_source_check.json`; no new simulation seeds or neural-model changes.

### Locked 2146/2134 source result and receiving decision

- **Hypothesis / experiment.** The fixed two-MBON representation contains mapping information on both cues. The saved encoding's thresholds (2146: 0.000368234652025819; 2134: 0.2325956107189357), bit direction, and teacher-501/502 Bernoulli readout exactly reproduced its archived 503/504 scores before scoring test-2 recordings from teachers 603, 606, 609, 612, 615, 618, 621, 624. No thresholds, decoder coefficients, neuron identities, or teacher records were selected using these results. No simulation or student training occurred.
- **Results and controls.** Cue 0 trained-message balanced accuracy averaged **20.5/24** (85.4%; teachers 22, 17, 20, 24, 18, 21, 22, 20); shuffled labels **12/24**, matched untrained **12/24**, disconnected **12/24**. Pooled bit-pair counts (`trace > threshold`, order 2146/2134) were A: `00` 10, `01` 5, `10` 29, `11` 52; B: 59, 24, 7, 6. Cue 1 trained accuracy averaged **21.625/24** (90.1%; teachers 19, 22, 20, 24, 21, 21, 23, 23); shuffled **11.375/24**, untrained and disconnected **12/24**. Cue-1 A counts were `00` 4, `01` 2, `10` 17, `11` 73; B counts 64, 19, 6, 7. Each mapping has 96 trials per cue across eight teachers. All four predeclared information conditions passed separately on both cues; all eight teachers exceeded 16/24 on each cue. These recordings are independent of the 501/502 calibration teachers, though their test-2 trials were already used for the one-bit study. The readout is an external information assay, not Fly B's decision rule.
- **Receiving failure review.** Earlier synthetic two-bit training scored 17, 21, and 14/24 in three Fly B seeds, with only 1/9 correct on cue-1 message `10`. A frozen `10`→`00` replay increased correct action-0 choices from 1/6 to 4/6 and altered competing MBON drive. Existing cue gating removed this interference but also removed the active 2146-zero action-0 drive on `00`; its proposed two-direction choice rescue did not replicate in the next student. The later graded four-MBON channel showed many all-zero decision ties; a constant-class explanation matched its apparent silent-trial decoding. These results show that source information alone does not establish receiving compatibility. Moreover, the earlier two-bit training labels were defined from message bits, whereas this source check predicts the **teacher's actual hidden mapping**. A pattern-specific synthetic action score cannot be treated as an actual-mapping score.
- **One distinct receiving-side diagnostic, proposed and not run.** Compare the **existing simultaneous two-bit receiver** with its **already implemented cue-gated variant** in a frozen, paired replay; change only cue gating, retaining the fixed 300 Hz/0–40 ms KC stimulation, 70–240 ms cue, target neurons, neural model, and action readout. Reconstruct and verify the archived original-interface trained student weights for seeds 520/521, then freeze them; use the first two independent saved teachers by seed (603/606), test 2, with their *actual* cue and mapping labels. Reset neural state and fix network/tie streams by source-trial identity, replay in both orders, and verify unchanged weights. Evaluate original and gated correct messages, cue/mapping-balanced shuffled messages, and disconnection on identical trials. Include a no-message mapping-A cue-only schedule at these weights to verify cue recognition. Record correct choices by cue and mapping, cue-1 `10`/`00` action-0/2 decision margins, cue-responsive activity, and three-way zero-rate ties. This is a receiving diagnostic; any weight reconstruction must exactly reproduce archived scores and is not a new training experiment.
- **Prospective compatibility gates.** Before any frozen neural replay, apply the gated projection to calibration-teacher codes, fit the same fixed-form analytical decoder there, and require at least 18/24 mean balanced actual-mapping accuracy on **each cue** across held-out teachers 603 and 606, with each teacher at least 16/24 on each cue; otherwise stop because gating discarded too much source information. Treat the neural replay as valid only if archived scores reproduce, frozen weights and source-keyed choices are order invariant, and each student scores at least 8/12 on **each cue** in the cue-only mapping-A control. For a receiver to pass, require at least 18/24 actual-mapping correct choices **on each cue** averaged over the two teachers for **each** student, at least 16/24 on each mapping within each cue after pooling the two teachers, a correct-minus-shuffled and correct-minus-disconnected margin of at least 4/24 on **each cue** for each student, and no more zero-rate decision ties than the original interface on either cue. The gated arm must additionally reduce wrong-action-2 drive on actual mapping-B cue-1 `10` trials without decreasing correct action-2 choices on actual mapping-A cue-1 `00` trials. A validity or outcome failure stops this route; do not retune targets, intensities, bit thresholds, or gates on the replay. These are exploratory compatibility gates, not a teaching claim. **No new Fly B training** until a receiver passes a prospectively run compatibility check; the present study stops at this proposal.
- **Reproduction / limitations.** Read-only source command: `node tools/check_two_cue_2146_2134_source.mjs`; compact measurements: `data/two_cue_2146_2134_source_check.json`. The proposed frozen receiving replay has no command or result yet. Two archived student states and two already inspected source teachers would make it a small exploratory compatibility test, not independent evidence of learning-dependent transfer. The earlier eight-pair one-bit confirmation remains +2.75/24 versus its unmet +4/24 practical gate.

### Frozen two-bit receiver diagnostic completed

- **Hypothesis / experiment.** Tested whether the existing simultaneous or cue-gated receiver could preserve cue recognition and act on the teacher's *actual* hidden mapping. The locked preliminary source-preservation check for cue gating passed on teachers 603/606: cue-0 scores 22/24 and 17/24, cue-1 scores 18/24 and 21/24, means 19.5/24 on both cues. Reconstructed original-interface student weights 520/521 with the existing eight-episode training utility and verified every archived training action and held-out correct/disconnected action before freezing. This exactly replayed past training; no new student learning condition was run. Each frozen state received test-2 source recordings from teachers 603/606 with actual mapping labels, per-trial resets, fixed source-keyed network and tie streams, and matched original/gated correct, mapping-balanced shuffled, and disconnected evaluations. Every evaluation reproduced its action, rates, cue response, and message-window MBON spikes in reverse trial order; weights stayed unchanged.
- **Results and controls.** Scores below are out of **48 per cue** (two teachers, 12 trials per mapping per teacher); each A/B cell is out of 24. Cue-only mapping-A validity scores are out of 12 per cue.

| Student | Cue-only cue 0 / 1 | Receiver | Cue 0 correct (A/B) | Cue 0 shuffled / off | Cue 1 correct (A/B) | Cue 1 shuffled / off | Correct-message zero-rate ties cue 0 / 1 |
|---|---:|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 520 | 8 / 0 | Simultaneous | 37 (14/23) | 21 / 25 | 10 (2/8) | 26 / 21 | 0 / 0 |
| 520 | 8 / 0 | Cue-gated | 39 (18/21) | 24 / 25 | 9 (2/7) | 21 / 21 | 0 / 0 |
| 521 | 6 / 1 | Simultaneous | 37 (15/22) | 25 / 21 | 8 (2/6) | 24 / 22 | 0 / 0 |
| 521 | 6 / 1 | Cue-gated | 36 (18/18) | 21 / 21 | 10 (5/5) | 21 / 22 | 0 / 5 |

- **Locked-gate decision.** Both students failed the **cue-only validity gate** of at least 8/12 on each cue: cue 1 scored 0/12 and 1/12. Neither receiver met the required 36/48 correct on each cue: cue 1 remained 8-10/48 under correct messages. Cue-1 A was 2/24 in three arms and 5/24 in the fourth, below its required 16/24; correct-message cue-1 scores were **below** shuffled and disconnected scores, failing both 8/48 margin gates. Cue gating did not increase zero ties for student 520, but added five cue-1 zero-rate ties for student 521 and failed that gate. Its predefined `10` drive and `00` choice comparisons passed, yet those pattern endpoints cannot override the cue-recognition, accuracy, control-margin, and tie failures. The simultaneous receiver had zero-rate ties in neither student, so silence is not its main failure here.
- **Single strongest observed limitation / interpretation.** At these archived, synthetically trained weights, **cue-1 action recognition is already absent without messages**; actual teacher messages do not restore the correct cue-1/mapping-A action. On student 520 and 521, simultaneous correct-message cue-1 mapping-A actions were 22 action 0 and only 2 correct action 2 out of 24, with mean action-0/action-2 decision rates about 0.699/0.179 and 0.652/0.119 spikes per MBON. This is an action-bias/recognition limitation at the receiver, not missing teacher source information or predominantly all-zero ties. Because the cue-only validity gate failed, the message comparisons are descriptive rather than a valid estimate of achievable two-cue teaching. The archived weights were learned on message-derived synthetic labels, so the test does not prove that every possible receiver-trained state would fail.
- **Next experiment / stop.** **Neither existing receiver passed all locked gates. Stop this route; no two-cue teaching pilot, parameter retuning, or follow-on micro-diagnostic.** The two-neuron teacher source is informative, but receiving compatibility and learning-dependent transfer across both cues remain unestablished. Reproduce in a checkout without the output files: `node tools/check_two_cue_receiver_gate.mjs`; `node tools/two_cue_transfer.mjs receiver_frozen 20 02 lanes02 150`; `node tools/two_cue_transfer.mjs receiver_frozen 21 02 lanes02 150`. Raw per-trial action, MBON rates, message-window MBON spikes, cue spikes, and zero-tie flags: `data/two_cue_receiver_frozen_02_20_lanes02.json` and `_21_lanes02.json`; preliminary gate: `data/two_cue_receiver_gate.json`. The one-bit confirmation stays at +2.75/24 below its locked +4/24 practical threshold.

### No-message actual two-cue baseline plan (fixed before new training, )

- **Question / existing evidence.** Can a fresh Fly B learn both cues under the actual hidden A/B mapping task without teacher stimulation? Existing `baseline` files for seeds 11-13 train mapping A and then B in blocks, without the current per-trial reset; they show strong later mapping-B accuracy with severe loss on A. The disconnected one-bit training files use cue 1 alone, and other disconnected conditions lack this exact fresh, balanced two-cue frozen evaluation. This exact baseline is not established. With mappings balanced and interleaved **within each cue** and no mapping input, expected accuracy is 50%; success would require a repeatable effect beyond that information limit.
- **Fixed sample, budget, and procedure.** Preselect student seeds **901, 902, 903**, without baseline exclusions or replacement. Give each eight ordinary 24-round reward-learning episodes and **no message**. Each episode has 6 trials in every cue×mapping cell, interleaved by one fixed schedule common to all students; true action is `actions[cue XOR mapping]` with actions `[0,2]`. Use the existing cue VPNs at 150 Hz from 70-240 ms, existing dopamine feedback, retained MBON readout, and a neural-state reset at every trial. Fix network and action-tie random streams by student seed, episode, and original trial index. Freeze before and after training on two separate held-out 24-round schedules (each 6 trials per cell, total 48), with identical streams before/after and a reverse-order replay. Training, held-out schedules, and seeds are fixed independently of outcomes. No teacher weights, activity, or stimulation enter Fly B.
- **Validity.** Require exactly 6 trials per cue×mapping cell per training and held-out schedule, no message drive, frozen weights unchanged, and identical frozen actions and MBON rates under reverse order. Report all three seeds regardless of initial score or weight change; a failure of implementation validity stops the baseline without replacing a seed. Record per-episode training scores, initial/final held-out scores by cue and mapping, action rates and zero ties, and weight-change magnitude.
- **Locked learning decision.** Reliable unassisted learning requires **all three** preselected students to score at least **36/48** held-out after training, at least **8/12 in every cue×mapping cell**, and improve at least **8/48** over their own frozen initial score on identical held-out schedules. A student missing any condition means the reliability gate fails; do not add seeds or retune. If the gate passes, a separate prospectively designed teacher-assisted learning-speed comparison could be justified. If it fails, report whether deficits concentrate on cue 1 or affect both cues, using the cell scores. This baseline cannot by itself establish teacher transfer. Reproduce with `node tools/two_cue_transfer.mjs two_cue_alone_baseline SEED 02 lanes02 150` for seeds 901-903; retain each raw result.

### No-message actual two-cue baseline result

- **Hypothesis / experiment.** Ran the locked eight-episode, 24-round-per-episode no-message training on fresh students 901-903, with actual hidden mapping labels balanced within each cue and per-trial neural resets. Two held-out 24-round schedules were replayed before and after training with identical trial-keyed random streams; each schedule had six trials per cue×mapping cell. There was no teacher source, activity, weight transfer, or message drive. Every student and schedule passed balance, reverse-order action/MBON-rate invariance, and frozen-weight checks. Dopamine learning changed 83, 91, and 73 weights respectively; no student was excluded by its initial score.
- **Results / controls.** The frozen initial state is the matched within-student control. Scores are correct decisions; each cell is out of 12 and each cue out of 24.

| Student | Initial / 48 | Final / 48 | Cue 0 A / B | Cue 1 A / B | Final cue 0 / 1 | Gain / 48 | Final zero-rate ties cue 0 / 1 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 901 | 13 | 21 | 6 / 5 | 6 / 4 | 11 / 10 | +8 | 11 / 18 |
| 902 | 13 | 17 | 4 / 6 | 6 / 1 | 10 / 7 | +4 | 14 / 15 |
| 903 | 15 | 18 | 5 / 6 | 5 / 2 | 11 / 7 | +3 | 16 / 16 |

- **Locked-gate conclusion.** No student reached 36/48 or 8/12 in every cue×mapping cell; only 901 met the separate +8/48 within-student gain condition. The reliability gate **failed** without a technical validity failure. Deficits affect **both** cues (cue 0: 10-11/24; cue 1: 7-10/24), with cue 1 and particularly mapping B worse in two students. Training episode scores fluctuated rather than rising consistently: 901 ended at 5/24, 902 at 11/24, and 903 at 9/24. Many final trials were zero-rate MBON ties, but this baseline does not isolate their cause. No mapping input is available in the no-message condition, and the mapping is balanced independently within each cue; a mapping-blind student has 50% expected accuracy regardless of its ability to adapt synapses. Thus failure on this interleaved hidden-mapping task does **not** establish that the dopamine rule cannot learn an observable cue rule; prior blockwise mapping runs addressed a different task. It does establish that this exact unassisted baseline does not support a controlled teacher-assisted **learning-speed** comparison to a reliably learning-alone student.
- **Next experiment / stop.** Stop after this baseline as requested. No teacher-assisted pilot, receiver adjustment, extra seed, or micro-diagnostic. Full two-cue learning-dependent transfer remains unresolved. Reproduce in a checkout without the output files with `node tools/two_cue_transfer.mjs two_cue_alone_baseline 901 02 lanes02 150` and the same command for 902/903. Raw schedules, training curves, before/after trial scores and MBON rates: `data/two_cue_two_cue_alone_baseline_02_{901,902,903}_lanes02.json`.

### Prospective blocked-mapping learning-speed pilot (locked before execution, )

- **Hypothesis and prior evidence.** Mapping is observable through repeated cue/reward trials within a block; trained Fly A's recorded two-MBON activity may shorten Fly B's acquisition and reversal time. Prior `baseline` runs on students 511-513 used ten 24-trial mapping-A episodes followed by ten mapping-B episodes, cue lanes 0/2 at 150 Hz, and reached final mapping-B frozen scores of 21-24/24. Their episode scores increased on A and recovered after the B switch; they did not use the current per-trial neural reset. The earlier RPS switch experiment did not show a teacher benefit and had weak source information, so it is not evidence of two-cue transfer. The interleaved no-message baseline had a 50% information ceiling and says nothing about dopamine learning on an observable block. The fixed 2146/2134 source has already passed an independent held-out gate: mean mapping accuracy 20.5/24 on cue 0 and 21.625/24 on cue 1 across teachers 603-624, versus 12/24 untrained and about 12/24 shuffled. No source selection or threshold fitting is repeated.
- **Fixed sample and interface.** One pilot student seed **904**, training teacher **603**, independent held-out teacher **606**; no seed replacement or initial-score selection. Reuse the existing simultaneous two-bit channel: MBONs 2146/2134 sampled at 120 ms, thresholds 0.000368/0.232596 with `trace < threshold` bits, message KC groups [506,545]/[113,1421] and the existing zero-bit KC target [1319,2016], 300 Hz during 0-40 ms, cue on VPN lanes 0/2 at 150 Hz during 70-240 ms, retained MBON counts and per-trial neural reset. Preserve the connectome, dynamics, dopamine rule and action readout. Training teacher uses archived tests 0/1 and held-out teacher uses archived test 2; recordings may recur across episodes and this limits independence.
- **Schedule and controls.** Ten balanced 24-trial episodes on actual mapping A, then a fixed switch after **240 trials** to ten on B; 12 of each cue per episode. Train copies from identical fresh weights and trial-keyed network/tie streams under correct teacher codes, cue-matched mapping-balanced shuffled codes, untrained-teacher codes, or disconnected messages (Fly B alone). The cue sequence, true reward contingency, initial weights, and trial-keyed random streams match across arms; realized dopamine feedback depends on each arm's action. Shuffling swaps codes across A/B on alternating matched cue positions and must leave approximately chance mapping information. Evaluate frozen after each episode using identical correct held-out teacher messages in every arm, plus disconnected and shuffled evaluations at baseline, switch, and final checkpoints. Evaluate the no-message arm without messages at baseline, after A and after B to verify task learnability. Evaluation has no feedback and must leave weights unchanged. Report separate cue scores and training trajectories.
- **Pre-pilot validity stop.** Run the disconnected arm first. It must reach at least 18/24 and 8/12 on each cue on frozen **disconnected** evaluation after A and after B, with at least +4/24 improvement from the corresponding block-start score. If it fails, stop before teacher-arm training; this pilot is invalid for a learning-speed claim. Also require 12 trials/cue/episode, correct actual labels, matching cue/reward/stream schedules, intact frozen weights, and source/shuffle checks; a failure stops without replacement.
- **Primary outcome and success gate.** For each block, compute early learning speed as mean frozen **correct-message** score over its first five episodes (out of 24), separately by cue (out of 12). The pilot succeeds only if correct-message training exceeds **each** shuffled, untrained, and disconnected training control by at least 3/24 in **both** blocks and by at least 1/12 on **each cue in both blocks**; final correct-message score in each block must not be more than 2/24 below any control. This paired identical-message evaluation isolates training history from an immediate message effect. The disconnected/shuffled evaluation ablations show dependence on messages but are secondary. One pilot only; a failed validity or success gate stops the study without interface retuning or more seeds. A passed pilot would justify a separately locked independent replication, not a teaching claim by itself.

### Blocked-mapping pilot result: no-message task valid, teacher comparison invalid

- **Experiment and source checks.** Ran the one preselected seed 904 with teacher 603 recordings for training and independent teacher 606 test-2 recordings for frozen evaluation. The archived fixed source-information check remained 20.5/24 on cue 0 and 21.625/24 on cue 1 across eight teachers; the selected teacher 603 scored 22/24 and 19/24, and evaluation teacher 606 scored 17/24 and 21/24. A read-only reconstruction of this pilot's mapping-balanced shuffle gave fixed-decoder scores of 121/240 on each cue, versus 205/240 and 215/240 for the correct training codes; 192/480 training codes changed. This reused fixed thresholds and calibration, with no source retuning.
- **Valid no-message feasibility result.** With actual rewards, 12 trials per cue per episode, and a fixed switch after 240 trials, Fly B alone rose from **9/24 to 22/24** frozen disconnected accuracy on mapping A (cue 0: 5/12 to 10/12; cue 1: 4/12 to 12/12). Immediately after switching, its mapping-B baseline was **0/24**; after ten B episodes it reached **21/24** (cue 0: 9/12; cue 1: 12/12). Both locked learnability gates passed. Training scores rose from 16/24 to 21/24 on A and from 4/24 to 20/24 on B. This establishes learnability for this student under the specified blocked, reset schedule; it does not establish reliability across students.
- **Invalid teacher-arm comparison.** During the post-run protocol audit, we found that the pilot runner had omitted the **existing zero-bit KC target [1319,2016]** from the simultaneous two-bit receiver. That target was part of the archived receiver and the locked plan. Thus correct, shuffled, and untrained teacher arms, and their message-conditioned frozen scores, used a different receiving interface. The raw output contains their trajectories and controls, but they are **excluded from the locked learning-speed inference**. For transparency, the recorded early correct-message means were 23.2/24 (A) and 13.6/24 (B) for correct training, versus disconnected 18.8/14.2, shuffled 22.2/13.6, and untrained 21.8/15.6. These numbers do not rescue the invalid protocol and would not meet the locked both-block speed gate even under the omitted-target interface.
- **Conclusion / stop.** The task-information objection to the earlier interleaved baseline is resolved for this one student: Fly B can learn both blocked mappings without messages, so that earlier failure does **not** implicate the dopamine rule. The teacher-assisted learning-speed question remains **unanswered** because the receiver setting was wrong. Stop after this invalid pilot as predeclared; do not rerun, add seeds, or tune the channel in this session. The runner source has been corrected to include the original zero-bit target for any future authorized run, but it was **not rerun**. The executed command was `node tools/two_cue_transfer.mjs two_cue_block_speed_pilot 904 02 lanes02 150`; its archived pre-correction settings and raw results are in `data/two_cue_two_cue_block_speed_pilot_02_904_lanes02.json`. Because the source was corrected after execution, that command in a clean checkout would run the corrected protocol and would not reproduce the invalid teacher-arm values. The earlier RPS switch failures and two-bit frozen receiver failures remain limits on interpretation; no evidence of learning-speed transfer is established here.

### Corrected replacement of the invalid blocked-mapping pilot

- **Hypothesis / preflight.** Re-ran the **same locked pilot**, seed 904, teacher 603 for training, teacher 606 test 2 for frozen evaluation, ten 24-trial A episodes and ten B episodes, with the fixed switch after trial 240. This is a replacement for the omitted-target implementation, not a new parameter choice. Before simulation, compared the corrected setting against archived `receiver_frozen` data: thresholds 0.000368/0.232596; one-bit KC groups [506,545] and [113,1421]; zero-bit target [1319,2016]; 300 Hz from 0-40 ms; cue lanes 0/2 at 150 Hz from 70-240 ms; retained MBON counts. All six targets have the KC role. The simultaneous (non-gated) two-bit drive path was unchanged. Added a run-time equality check against the archived interface and an output suffix `_corrected` so the invalid raw file cannot be overwritten. Fixed only the runner's top-level cue-timing metadata to report the timing already used by `episode`; no neural or stimulation parameter changed.

| Training arm | Early A total (cue 0 / cue 1) | Final A total (cue 0 / cue 1) | Early B total (cue 0 / cue 1) | Final B total (cue 0 / cue 1) |

The latter contains training episode scores, frozen trial-level actions and MBON rates, cue scores, controls, settings, and gates.

### Fixed-budget four-MBON pattern feasibility plan (locked before code, )

- **Distinct hypothesis.** The old four-MBON channel scaled each recorded trace independently, and trained messages produced mapping-B-specific zero-rate decision ties even at initial Fly B weights (teacher 503: A/B 6/12 versus 10/12; teacher 504: 4/12 versus 10/12). A fixed **total** KC drive carrying the *relative* four-MBON activity could remove that amplitude/silence asymmetry while retaining the population pattern and normal cue response. This differs from the old absolute graded channel and from thresholded one/two-bit codes. It may fail if mapping information resides mainly in total amplitude.
- **Fixed interface and source gate.** Reuse only the previously selected teacher MBONs **2146, 2134, 2124, 2122**, sampled at 120 ms, and their existing four two-KC target pairs **[506,545], [113,1421], [1319,2016], [818,1462]**. Use the archived calibration-teacher 501/502 95th-percentile ceilings. Let `q_i = min(1, max(0, trace_i / ceiling_i))`; command `300*q_i/sum(q)` Hz to each KC pair for 0-40 ms when the sum is positive, otherwise zero. Thus summed commanded pair rates are 300 Hz per nonzero message; the old independently scaled graded drive is the matched interface control. Cue 0/1 remains on VPN lanes 0/2 at 150 Hz during 70-240 ms. No action-neuron drive, decoder in Fly B, label/action/weight transfer, learning-rule change, or readout change. Fit an **external analytical** cue-aware nearest-centroid mapping readout to normalized source vectors from calibration teachers 501/502 only. On each saved teacher **503 and 504**, test 2, require for **each cue** at least **18/24** balanced mapping correct, at least **8/12** for each mapping, and at least **4/24** above both matched untrained source and mapping-balanced shuffled-label accuracy. If any source cell fails, stop before receiver simulation. The external readout is a measurement only and never enters Fly B.
- **Frozen receiver experiment, only if source passes.** Use one fresh, predetermined untrained Fly B wiring seed **905**, with weights frozen throughout, and teachers 503/504 test 2 (48 actual cue/mapping trials per teacher, 12 per cell); reserve teachers 609/612 and students 906/907 for any later independent evaluation. Compare fixed-budget correct trained messages, old absolute-graded correct messages, cue-and-mapping-balanced shuffled trained messages, matched untrained-teacher messages, and disconnection. Use the same cue/mapping order, per-source-round network and tie streams, and per-trial neural resets in every arm; no feedback or student training. Collect 0-40 ms target-KC spikes, 70-240 ms cue-evoked KC population spikes, decision-time three-action MBON rates, actions, and zero-rate ties. Fit external analytical nearest-centroid readouts **only on teacher 501/502 calibration trials at this same frozen student wiring**: mapping from four target-KC counts and cue identity from cue-phase KC counts under disconnection. Freeze both readouts for held-out teachers; neither is installed in Fly B.
- **Receiving validity gates.** On each held-out teacher and cue, normalized correct messages must yield at least **18/24** mapping classification from target-KC counts, at least **8/12** per mapping, and margins of at least **4/24** over shuffled, untrained, and disconnected message controls. The cue-identity readout must classify at least **20/24** trials of each cue under normalized messages and be no worse than **2/24** below disconnected cue-only replay. For each cue, normalized messages may add at most **2/24** three-way zero-rate decision ties relative to disconnection. Report old graded control, actual choices, and cue-specific activity descriptively; these gates test interface feasibility, **not** learning or teaching. Any failed gate stops this approach without KC-target, scaling, or stimulation retuning. Budget: one archive source check, then at most one frozen student seed and two saved held-out teachers; **zero** student training episodes and no broader sweep.

### Fixed-budget four-MBON source gate failed

- **Experiment and controls.** Applied the locked normalization to actual 120 ms trained Fly A traces from saved teachers 503/504, test 2. The four MBONs and their ceilings came from the earlier 501/502 calibration; the cue-aware nearest-centroid readout was fitted on those calibration teachers only and was never installed in Fly B. Each held-out teacher had 12 trials per mapping and cue. Matched untrained-teacher recordings and alternating-round, mapping-balanced shuffled labels were scored by the same frozen readout; disconnected chance is 12/24 for each balanced cue. No new teacher simulation, student simulation, or student training occurred.
- **Results (correct / untrained / shuffled, out of 24).** Teacher 503 cue 0: **14 / 12 / 10**, with correct mapping A/B **10/12 / 4/12**; cue 1: **18 / 12 / 12**, with **10/12 / 8/12**. Teacher 504 cue 0: **24 / 12 / 12**, with **12/12 / 12/12**; cue 1: **15 / 12 / 11**, with **10/12 / 5/12**. The all-zero normalized source pattern occurred on 0, 1, 0, and 1 trials in that order. Thus two of four held-out teacher-cue cells missed the predeclared 18/24 and 8/12-per-mapping gates and their +4/24 untrained margins; the shuffled margins passed in all four cells.
- **Conclusion / stop.** The fixed-total relative population pattern is informative in some saved teacher-cue cells but does **not** meet the required across-cue, across-teacher source-information gate. The normalization may have removed useful absolute activity, but this diagnostic does not isolate that cause. Per the locked stop rule, the frozen Fly B receiver experiment was **not run**: cue recognition, KC responses, decision ties, and learning under this new interface are unmeasured. Do not retune the normalization or test additional interfaces under this hypothesis. The corrected blocked learning-speed pilot remains a separate **valid negative result**; this source check does not revise it. Reproduce with `node tools/check_two_cue_fixed_budget_source.mjs`; raw per-trial patterns, predictions, centroids, controls, and gates are in `data/two_cue_fixed_budget_source.json`.
- **HackPrinceton demonstration.** Lead with the original Fly Hero rhythm game and its documented within-fly learning curve (40% to 96% of notes hit in 30 plays, 96% on a new song; dopamine-blocked 41%), then show the two-fly work as a carefully controlled open research question. The eight-pair cue-1 result was a small +2.75/24 learning-dependent advantage below its locked +4/24 practical threshold, and the corrected two-cue speed pilot did not show faster learning. Do not demonstrate those as established full two-cue teaching.

### Raw-amplitude source decomposition plan (; next session only)

- **Question and hypothesis.** The fixed-total four-MBON source gate failed on teacher 503/cue 0 (14/24) and 504/cue 1 (15/24). Test whether discarding total calibrated activity, rather than the selected neurons themselves, accounts for those source-information losses. This is an external measurement-validity diagnostic, not another receiving interface or a revision of the failed gate.
- **Smallest comparison.** On the same saved 501/502 calibration and 503/504 held-out test-2 recordings, compare the existing normalized four-vector with its unnormalized calibrated vector `q_i = clamp(trace_i / ceiling_i, 0, 1)`. Fit the same cue-aware nearest-centroid analytical readout separately on calibration teachers only. Keep the same neuron identities, ceilings, trials, labels, matched untrained recordings, and mapping-balanced shuffled-label control; report the disconnected 12/24 class baseline. No simulation, Fly B training, or channel changes.
- **Prospective interpretation gate.** Support the total-amplitude-loss hypothesis only if the unnormalized readout improves by at least 4/24 in **both** failed teacher-cue cells and, in each, reaches at least 18/24, at least 8/12 per mapping, and at least 4/24 above both untrained and shuffled controls. Otherwise reject this specific mechanism. All four cells must be reported. Even a pass would establish source information only; it would not reopen the failed fixed-total receiver plan or imply teaching.
- **Session decision.** The prior fixed-total line remains stopped. This distinct analytical diagnostic is proposed for the next session and was **not executed** here. The student-702 high-baseline issue was already examined in earlier notebook sections and is not being repeated.

### Raw-amplitude source decomposition result

- **Question / hypothesis.** Did removing total calibrated four-MBON activity cause the two held-out source-information failures of the fixed-budget representation? The prospective gate required the raw calibrated vector to gain at least 4/24 in both failed teacher-cue cells and meet the original accuracy, per-mapping, and control margins.
- **Experiment / controls.** Reused teachers 501/502 for calibration and test-2 recordings from teachers 503/504 for evaluation. The only changed variable was the external readout input: raw calibrated q_i = clamp(trace_i / ceiling_i, 0, 1) rather than the fixed-total normalized vector. The same cue-aware nearest-centroid readout form was fitted separately on calibration data only. Neurons, ceilings, trials, true labels, matched untrained recordings, balanced shuffled labels, and the disconnected 12/24 chance baseline were unchanged. No neural simulation, student seed, training episode, or interface change.
- **Measurements.** Correct raw scores (normalized score; raw A/B; untrained; shuffled), each out of 24: teacher 503 cue 0 **13 (14; 6/7; 12; 15)**, cue 1 **18 (18; 8/10; 12; 16)**; teacher 504 cue 0 **18 (24; 7/11; 12; 16)**, cue 1 **16 (15; 6/10; 12; 14)**. In the two previously failed cells, raw-minus-normalized changes were **-1/24** and **+1/24**, versus the locked +4/24 requirement. Raw cue 0 for teacher 503 was also below its shuffled-label control.
- **Conclusion / limitations.** The prespecified total-amplitude-loss explanation **failed**: retaining total activity did not restore either failed cell to 18/24 or 8/12 per mapping. This does not show that total activity carries no information under every analytical readout; it rejects this fixed representation and calibrated nearest-centroid explanation. The fixed-budget source gate remains failed, and no Fly B receiving or learning test follows. The recordings and teacher cohort were previously inspected, so this is a mechanism diagnostic, not independent confirmation. The original Fly Hero within-fly result and the earlier small cue-1 two-fly effect are unaffected.
- **Next experiment.** Stop this line. The fixed-budget source and its proposed amplitude explanation both failed their prospective gates; further scaling or readout variants on the same held-out records would be post-hoc tuning. No distinct, prejustified diagnostic is identified here.
- **Reproduce / raw data.** Run node tools/check_two_cue_raw_amplitude_source.mjs; [per-trial patterns, predictions, calibration, and controls](../data/two_cue_raw_amplitude_source.json). The locked normalized comparison is [here](../data/two_cue_fixed_budget_source.json).

### RPS return: observable three-action baseline plan (locked before execution, )

- **Purpose and prior evidence.** Reassess RPS as the main game before any new Fly A→Fly B communication. The current `js/rps.js` still presents only the previous opponent move on the original visual lanes, selects the largest of three action-MBON group rates with the original tie stream, and uses unchanged dopamine-gated KC→MBON updates. Its current three-value teacher channel still caps intermediate-neuron counts at two. The archived 10%-noise previous-opponent task reached 59.7% frozen wins after 40 episodes versus 93.5% for an observation-only lookup; paper was chosen only 18/19/16 times during each seed's 960 training rounds, and earlier trained messages did not reliably help. Those failures are preserved, not reinterpreted.
- **Hypothesis and one fixed task.** Under a fully observable, deterministic `previous_opponent` rule with offset 1 and **noise 0**, the opponent's next move is `(previous opponent + 1) mod 3` after the first round. All three opponent states and all three winning actions occur. Removing opponent randomness tests a favorable learnability case while retaining the existing input encoding, readout, neural dynamics, connectome, and dopamine rule; it does not alter the opponent rule based on Fly B's actions. One initial round per episode lacks a previous observation and is included in overall scores but excluded from state-conditional accuracy.
- **Locked design and budget.** Use only fresh student seeds **651, 652, 653**, real wiring, no teacher or channel object, **12 training episodes × 24 rounds** each. Every student sees the same opponent training seeds `41000+episode` and frozen held-out seeds `51000, 51001, 51002` (72 rounds), disjoint from training. Evaluate each student *before and after* on those identical held-out sequences with fresh, matched evaluation random streams and no learning; verify weights do not change. First fit a simple three-entry previous-move→next-move majority lookup using **only the first 24-round training sequence** and evaluate on the three held-out sequences. Compare it with random-action and best constant-action controls on those same held-out opponent moves. Do not run neural training if the lookup fails its validity gate. No seed replacement or extra episodes.
- **Predetermined measurements and gates.** Predictor validity requires all three previous-move states in training, at least **90%** held-out next-move prediction accuracy overall and **100%** on rounds with an observed previous move. For Fly B report frozen win rate, *implied next-move prediction accuracy* (the opponent move that its chosen counter-action would beat), per-state accuracy, action counts for rock/paper/scissors, training win curve, positive-reward trials and actual KC→MBON multiplier changes on positive-reward steps by selected action, and comparison with lookup/random/constant controls. Because the existing action readout outputs only an RPS move and every move is the winning counter to exactly one opponent move, Fly B's implied prediction accuracy is mathematically equal to its win rate; the two will be reported separately but are not independent measures. Reliable three-action learning requires **all three** preselected students to gain at least **20 percentage points** over their own frozen initial 72-round score, reach at least **80%** held-out wins, reach at least **70%** correct on each observed previous-move state, and choose **each action at least 12 times** across the 69 noninitial held-out rounds. Any failed condition stops this task after the baseline, with at most one focused diagnostic on the strongest measured bottleneck and no teaching run.
- **Interpretation limits / next experiment.** The initial lookup gate tests whether the *observation* is sufficient. Fly B success would establish independent learning of this one deterministic three-action rule, not teacher benefit; only then would separate teacher source-information and receiver-compatibility checks be justified. Failure with a passing lookup and rare rewarded paper updates would localize the main limitation to action selection and reinforcement opportunity, subject to checking state-specific responses. No input-encoding correction is implemented in this plan.

### RPS observable baseline result and stop

- **Non-neural validity gate.** The one-episode lookup learned all three transitions from the previous opponent move. On the disjoint 72-round held-out set it predicted **71/72 = 98.6%** next moves and won 71/72; on the 69 rounds with an observed previous move it was **69/69**. Random and training-fitted constant-action controls won **23/72** and **24/72**. This passed the locked 90%/100% lookup gate before any Fly B training, establishing that the information available to Fly B is sufficient for this deterministic task. The three first rounds with no previous move stayed in overall scores.
- **No-message Fly B results.** Student seeds 651/652/653 improved on identical frozen 72-round held-out sequences from **8→47/72**, **11→47/72**, and **15→48/72** after the fixed twelve 24-round training episodes. Their win rates and implied next-move prediction accuracies were respectively **65.3%, 65.3%, 66.7%** after training; these are numerically identical by the mandatory RPS counter-action definition, not two independent measures. Each improved by more than 20 percentage points, but all missed the locked 80% final gate. On the 69 observed-state held-out rounds, rock/paper/scissors choice counts were **24/0/45**, **25/0/44**, and **24/0/45**. Previous-move-0 wins were 23/23, 22/23, 23/23; previous-move-1 wins were 24/24 in all seeds; previous-move-2 wins were **0/22 in all seeds**. Thus all failed the per-state and every-action gates, with no student excluded.
- **Rewarded action-pathway measurements.** Across 288 training rounds per student, rock/paper/scissors choices were **47/12/229**, **54/12/222**, and **62/12/214**. Positive-reward trials by selected pathway were **36/2/77**, **44/2/77**, and **53/0/78**. Rewarded steps that actually changed at least one KC→MBON multiplier were **33/0/77**, **41/0/76**, and **50/0/78**. Thus the paper pathway received **zero measured positive-reward weight-changing updates** in every seed, versus many for rock and scissors. Frozen weights were verified unchanged during before/after evaluations; no teacher object or messages were used.
- **Focused bottleneck analysis from saved rates; no extra simulation.** On previous-move-2 held-out trials, where the winning action is paper, mean paper/scissors action-MBON rates were **0.578/1.321**, **0.490/1.282**, and **0.636/1.315** spikes per MBON for seeds 651–653. There were **no three-way zero-rate ties** in that state. The immediate limitation is the current action-MBON competition: paper never wins on observed trials, so it gets almost no rewarded practice and no positive-reward synaptic changes. The abstract observation contains the answer (lookup 69/69), while the network does distinguish at least some observations (it chooses the appropriate different actions for previous moves 0 and 1); these results do not fully separate KC encoding of previous move 2 from downstream action competition. The prior 10%-noise 40-episode plateau and weak/saturated teacher messages remain unresolved in the current implementation; removing opponent noise did not restore three-action learning.
- **Decision / next experiment.** The locked three-action baseline **fails in all three seeds**. Do not begin teacher-student RPS training or claim Fly A can teach this deterministic rule; no Fly A source information or receiving compatibility for this rule was evaluated. An input-encoding correction is **not yet justified to implement** because no single alternate existing VPN pattern has been shown to recruit a paper-winning, distinguishable response. The smallest separate next test, if authorized later, is one frozen, reward-free probe comparing previous-move-2 KC recruitment and paper-versus-scissors MBON drive with the other two observed states at these saved conditions; preselect one candidate input pattern on calibration-only data before any training comparison. Stop this session without retuning, more seeds, or a communication experiment. Reproduce the lookup and three student runs with `node tools/check_rps_observable_baseline.mjs predictor` and `node tools/check_rps_observable_baseline.mjs student SEED` for 651–653 in a location without the output files. Raw trial actions, rates, rewards, training curves, controls, gates, and update counts: `data/rps_observable_lookup.json` and `data/rps_observable_student_{651,652,653}.json`.

### RPS frozen-input diagnostic plan (locked before execution, )

- **Hypothesis and scope.** Previous-move-2 VPN input recruits a distinct KC pattern, but its paper MBON group loses the unchanged three-way competition to scissors, denying the paper pathway rewarded updates. The baseline files save trial action-MBON rates but **not trained synaptic weights or KC counts**. Replaying post-training KC activity would require retraining, which is excluded. This single diagnostic therefore probes the reproducible **initial frozen states** of the same preselected students 651–653; archived post-training rates will be discussed separately and cannot be treated as the same weights.
- **One fixed probe.** For each student and 12 fixed stream keys, reset the neural state, present each of the three existing previous-move VPN inputs (`laneVpn[state].slice(2)`) at 150 Hz from 0–240 ms, reset spike counts at 50 ms, and call the existing `RpsBioRL.decide` at 240 ms. Replay the same stream key for all three states, with identical weights and no opponent, feedback, teacher, or learning. Save every active KC identity/count, three action-MBON group rates, paper-minus-scissors rate, chosen action and three-way zero ties. Verify weights unchanged.
- **Predeclared separation gate.** Using only the first six stream keys per state as calibration, form per-state mean binary active-KC vectors and classify the six remaining keys per state by nearest squared Euclidean centroid (fixed tie order 0,1,2). State 2 counts as **distinct and recruited** only if, in **each** student, at least 5/6 held-out state-2 inputs classify as 2, no more than 2/12 held-out state-0/1 inputs classify as 2, and mean state-2 active KCs is at least one. Otherwise the existing input representation does not pass this operational separation gate; this does not prove no information exists under every readout.
- **Predeclared competition gate and decision.** If the KC gate passes, call the paper pathway competitively suppressed only if, in **each** student, paper-minus-scissors is negative on at least 10/12 state-2 trials and the unchanged action readout chooses paper on no more than 2/12 state-2 trials. Report strict and tie-adjusted paper-max frequency for all states. A KC failure points first to input representation; KC pass plus competition pass points to action competition. Mixed results are inconclusive. No parameter adjustment, retraining, teacher run, or second diagnostic follows; an input-only correction may be *proposed* only after a KC failure, and a readout/learning-rule change is not authorized.

### RPS frozen-input diagnostic result

- **Experiment / controls.** Ran the plan above once on initial, frozen students 651–653, with 12 matched random-stream keys for each of previous-move inputs 0/1/2. Each trial reset neural state, drove the *unchanged* VPN input at 150 Hz, reset counts at 50 ms, and used the original 240-ms three-way action-MBON readout. No opponent, reward, dopamine update, teacher activity, or weight change occurred. The result contains all 108 trial-level active-KC identities and spike counts, MBON rates, actions, margins, and ties. No trained-weight snapshot exists in the baseline artifacts, so this is an initial-state mechanism test, **not** a frozen replay of the trained networks.
- **KC representation gate.** Each student's held-out 3×3 confusion matrix was the identity with **6/6** per state; state 2 had **6/6** recall and **0/12** false state-2 classifications from states 0/1 in every student. Mean active KCs on state 2 were **6.83, 6.83, and 8.50**, above the one-KC gate. Frequent state-2 identities included **67, 309, 519, 1777** (all 12/12 for 651 and 653; 309 was 11/12 for 652), while state 0 prominently recruited 354/1808/2041 and state 1 recruited 283/823/1374. Identity 67 was shared with state 0; the complete per-trial sets are in the raw file. Thus the existing state-2 input is recruited and operationally distinguishable without altering its encoding.
- **Rates and competition.** Mean action-MBON rates are in **rock / paper / scissors** order; margin is paper minus scissors. `P chosen` is the unchanged readout, and `P max` is the strict-max count (tie-adjusted paper-max opportunities are noted below). Each row has 12 trials.

| Student | Previous move | Mean active KCs | Mean MBON rates R / P / S | Mean P−S | P chosen | Strict P max | Three-way zero ties |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 651 | 0 | 8.50 | 0.649 / 0.577 / 0.702 | −0.125 | 1 | 0 | 0 |
| 651 | 1 | 7.50 | 0.155 / 0.018 / 0.042 | −0.024 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| 651 | 2 | 6.83 | 0.696 / 0.613 / 1.458 | −0.845 | **0** | **0** | 0 |
| 652 | 0 | 7.75 | 0.524 / 0.446 / 0.530 | −0.083 | 3 | 0 | 0 |
| 652 | 1 | 7.17 | 0.125 / 0.024 / 0.036 | −0.012 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
