- **Locked decision and limits.** On state 2, paper-minus-scissors was negative on **12/12** trials and paper won the unchanged action choice on **0/12** in each student, passing the predeclared competition-suppression gate. Paper was never a strict maximum on any state; its tie-adjusted maximum opportunities totaled 0.5/0.5/0 (651), 2/0/0 (652), and 1/0.833/0 (653) across states 0/1/2. There were **no state-2 zero-rate ties**. The input-representation failure branch is unsupported for these initial states. The strongest observed limitation is downstream action competition: state 2 reaches a distinct KC population, yet scissors dominates paper. Archived *trained* state-2 MBON means also favored scissors (paper/scissors 0.578/1.321, 0.490/1.282, 0.636/1.315) and never selected paper, but trained KCs cannot be reconstructed from the saved files. The probe does not isolate which downstream synapses cause the bias or prove every trained KC pattern is distinct.
- **Next experiment / stop.** No input-only re-encoding is justified by this diagnostic. Under the current rule prohibiting action-readout and learning-rule changes without approval, there is no authorized minimal correction or teacher-student RPS experiment. Preserve this negative mechanism result and stop after one diagnostic. Reproduce once in a location without the raw output using `node tools/check_rps_frozen_input.mjs`; raw results: `data/rps_frozen_input.json`. No Git operation or further simulation was performed.

### Approved balanced-action RPS training plan (locked before execution, )

- **Hypothesis.** State-2 input is KC-distinguishable, but the unchanged MBON maximum suppresses paper and starves it of rewarded KC→MBON updates. Balanced *training-only* action experience may give the original dopamine rule enough rewarded paper opportunities to produce autonomous three-action choices later. This tests learning from action experience, not teacher communication.
- **Single intervention.** Reuse the existing `explore` pattern from `tools/two_cue_transfer.mjs`: after the ordinary `RpsBioRL.decide` computes MBON rates, optionally replace the *training* action with rock/paper/scissors in turn for each publicly observed previous-move state, independently of the next opponent move and reward. Initial rounds without a previous move remain autonomous. Recompute the selected action's ordinary value prediction and KC eligibility before the existing opponent reward and dopamine update. No input, neural, connectome, plasticity-rule, or frozen-readout change. Frozen tests set exploration off.
- **Matched arms and budget.** Preselected students **651, 652, 653** only; deterministic `previous_opponent`, offset 1, noise 0; **12 episodes × 24 training rounds** each. Compare (1) original autonomous actions with learning, (2) balanced training actions with learning, and (3) the same balanced actions with learning disabled. The action cycle continues across episode boundaries so choices per observed previous-move state differ by at most one across the whole training budget. Every arm starts from identical weights, opponent training sequences `41000`–`41011`, episode-level neural resets, and matched trial-keyed network/tie random streams. All arms get the same unforced, learning-disabled 72-round frozen evaluations before/after on held-out opponent seeds `51000`–`51002`. The original  baseline remains archived separately; this is a newly matched comparison.
- **Measurements / validity.** Preserve per-episode and per-trial actions, true opponent moves, rewards, native MBON rates/actions, action frequencies, positive-reward trials, positive-reward weight-changing steps by selected pathway, and frozen held-out wins by previous state and action. Verify that all arms see identical opponent moves, matched initial weights and stream keys, balanced action counts differ by at most one *within each observed state across the full budget*, frozen evaluations leave weights unchanged and never force actions, the no-learning control leaves weights unchanged, and its frozen before/after results match. Any validity failure stops without changing seeds or parameters.
- **Predeclared success gate.** **Every** student must have at least one positive-reward paper weight-changing step in balanced training; score at least **58/72** unforced frozen held-out wins; gain at least **10/72** wins over its matched autonomous-training arm; win at least **70%** in **each** observed previous-move state; and choose **each** rock/paper/scissors at least **12 times** among the 69 observed held-out rounds. The balanced no-learning control must have unchanged weights and fail the ≥10/72 improvement-over-autonomous gate. A miss in any student fails the reliable three-action-learning claim; no extra seeds, sweep, or teacher experiment follows. A pass would establish learning under externally balanced training actions, not autonomous learning under the original training policy.

### Balanced-action RPS implementation audit and correction

The first execution reset its rock/paper/scissors counters at each episode boundary. Because each previous-move state occurred 7–8 times per episode, aggregate training action counts per state became **36/34/24** or **36/28/24**, violating the approved requirement to cycle actions equally *within each observed state across the fixed training budget*. Its other matched-arm checks passed, but it is an **invalid implementation of the intervention**, excluded from the locked success inference. Preserve its full raw output at `data/rps_balanced_training.json`; do not treat its 48/47/48 balanced held-out wins as the approved experiment. Before a replacement, the same three seeds, twelve episodes, opponent sequences, random streams, baseline arms, readout, and success gates were retained. The only correction is to carry the existing per-state action counters across episode boundaries and explicitly require within-one action balance for every observed state. The corrected replacement will be written separately; no parameter or gate was tuned to the first output.

### Corrected balanced-action RPS result: learning opportunities, no reliable three-action learning

- **Experiment and validity.** Ran one corrected replacement of the approved fixed-budget design on seeds 651–653. Corrected balanced training selected rock/paper/scissors **32/31/31** times for previous moves 0 and 2 and **30/29/29** times for previous move 1, for each seed and both balanced arms. The cycle was independent of the next move and reward. All arms had identical previous/next-opponent sequences and the same initial weights and trial-keyed neural/tie stream keys. Balanced and balanced-no-learning training actions matched on every observed-state round. Frozen evaluation had exploration off, called the original MBON maximum readout, left weights unchanged, and had 69 observed rounds out of the same 72 held-out rounds. The balanced-no-learning arm changed **zero** weights and reproduced its own frozen initial actions and rates exactly. All validity checks passed. This replacement, `data/rps_balanced_training_corrected.json`, is the sole valid result for the locked gates; the earlier file remains invalid.
- **Frozen held-out results.** Wins are out of 72; state scores are previous-move **0/1/2** out of **23/24/22**; action counts are **rock/paper/scissors** on the 69 observed rounds. `Paper updates` counts positive-reward paper steps that actually changed at least one KC→MBON multiplier during training. Frozen wins equal implied next-move prediction accuracy for this mandatory counter-action task.

| Student | Arm | Initial wins | Final wins | State 0 / 1 / 2 wins | Frozen R / P / S choices | Paper updates | Gain over matched autonomous |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 651 | Autonomous | 10 | 34 | 23 / 10 / 0 | 10 / 0 / 59 | 0 | — |
| 651 | Balanced + learning | 10 | **52** | 17 / 24 / 10 | 24 / 16 / 29 | **31** | **+18** |
| 651 | Balanced, no learning | 10 | 10 | 6 / 3 / 0 | 18 / 4 / 47 | 0 | −24 |
| 652 | Autonomous | 11 | 47 | 23 / 24 / 0 | 24 / 0 / 45 | 0 | — |
| 652 | Balanced + learning | 11 | **44** | 16 / 24 / 4 | 24 / 11 / 34 | **31** | **−3** |
| 652 | Balanced, no learning | 11 | 11 | 6 / 5 / 0 | 19 / 3 / 47 | 0 | −36 |
| 653 | Autonomous | 8 | 47 | 23 / 24 / 0 | 24 / 0 / 45 | 0 | — |
| 653 | Balanced + learning | 8 | **50** | 11 / 24 / 15 | 24 / 27 / 18 | **31** | **+3** |
| 653 | Balanced, no learning | 8 | 8 | 6 / 2 / 0 | 15 / 4 / 50 | 0 | −39 |

- **Locked gates.** Rewarded paper weight-change opportunities passed (**31** per student), and the no-learning control passed its unchanged-weight and failed-improvement requirement. All three balanced students still missed **58/72** final wins. Only 651 met the **+10/72** matched-autonomous margin. No student reached **70% in all three observed states**: state 2 was **10/22, 4/22, 15/22**, and state 0 was also below 70% for 652 and 653. Student 652 selected paper **11/69**, missing the **12/69 per-action** gate; 651 and 653 passed that action-frequency gate. Thus the all-three-seed success criterion **failed**. The balanced training win curves are descriptive because actions were externally cycled; they are saved with all per-trial data.
- **Interpretation / stop.** The intervention solved *paper update starvation*: all 31 paper choices on state-2 training trials were rewarded and changed weights in every student. On unforced frozen evaluation, paper emerged in all students but remained inaccurate or displaced needed actions on other states, so balanced experience did **not** yield reliable three-action RPS behavior. The matched autonomous 651 score (34/72) differs from its older archived 47/72 because this comparison used trial-keyed streams for all three arms; comparisons here are within the new matched run, not across the earlier random-stream protocol. This tests training action experience, not teacher activity or autonomous acquisition under the original policy. No teacher-student experiment, parameter retuning, extra seed, or follow-on diagnostic is justified by this failed locked gate. Reproduce once in a location without the corrected output with `node tools/check_rps_balanced_training.mjs`; the code refuses to overwrite saved results. The corrected artifact preserves per-trial rates/actions/rewards, all frozen evaluations, training curves, gates, and sparse changed weights for future read-only inspection. No Git operations were performed.

### Final RPS paper-pathway specificity analysis

- **Question / source audit.** Did the valid balanced-training run strengthen the paper response mainly on previous-move **2**, where paper wins, or similarly across states? `data/rps_balanced_training_corrected.json` saves every changed synaptic multiplier as an index/value pair for each trained arm; all omitted multipliers retain their initial value **1**. It also saves frozen MBON rates, but **not trained KC counts**. These complete sparse weight states allow a reward-free replay of trained KC and MBON activity without retraining. The earlier initial KC probe supplies an exact replay check. The invalid first balanced-action output was excluded.
- **One fixed analysis / controls.** Reconstructed initial, matched autonomous-trained, and balanced-trained networks for the same students 651–653. For each weight state, replayed the three unchanged previous-move VPN inputs under the same **12** paired stream keys and per-trial resets as `check_rps_frozen_input.mjs`: 150 Hz through 240 ms, count reset at 50 ms, ordinary action-MBON readout at 240 ms, no opponent, reward, dopamine learning, or teacher. Checked that sparse changes apply only to KC→MBON plastic edges, all weights remain frozen, and every initial action, rate, and active-KC set reproduces the archived initial probe exactly. Saved active KC identities/counts, rock/paper/scissors rates, paper-minus-scissors and paper-minus-strongest-other margins, choices, and zero ties. The paper-input proxy is the summed `w × multiplier × KC trace` over KC→paper-MBON edges divided by paper-MBON count; it is **not** a direct measured synaptic current and can change with KC activity as well as multipliers. Before replay, the operational state-2-specific criterion was positive balanced-minus-initial changes in both paper rate and strongest-competitor margin, each larger on state 2 than states 0 and 1 in **every** student.
- **Paired frozen results.** Each triplet is previous-move **0 / 1 / 2**. Rates and margins are spikes per action MBON; input-proxy values are in model-weighted trace units.

| Student | Δ paper rate, balanced − initial | Δ paper − best-other margin | Δ paper-input proxy | Balanced paper choices on state 0 / 1 / 2 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 651 | +0.113 / +0.125 / **+0.655** | +0.012 / −0.940 / **+0.744** | +6.29 / −9.32 / **+54.70** | 3 / 0 / 3 of 12 |
| 652 | +0.286 / +0.131 / **+0.708** | +0.018 / −1.006 / **+0.768** | +6.95 / −8.55 / **+60.91** | 3 / 0 / 6 of 12 |
| 653 | +0.333 / +0.185 / **+0.649** | +0.155 / −0.982 / **+0.952** | +16.34 / −7.59 / **+67.35** | 8 / 0 / 6 of 12 |

- **Result and interpretation.** The predeclared operational state-2 preference held in **all three** students. On state 2, balanced paper MBON rate rose from **0.613→1.268**, **0.661→1.369**, and **0.720→1.369**; paper-minus-strongest-other margin improved but remained slightly negative at **−0.101, −0.054, −0.030**. The matched autonomous-trained state-2 paper rates were only **0.506, 0.536, 0.595**, and paper was chosen **0/12** in each; balanced training produced **3/12, 6/12, 6/12** paper choices. State-2 KC activity was retained (mean active KCs initial→balanced **6.83→7.00**, **6.83→7.17**, **8.50→7.42**), with paired active-set Jaccard overlap approximately **0.72, 0.75, 0.78**. This supports **preferential functional strengthening** for the paper-requiring state, not a uniform rise across all states. It was **not perfectly selective**: paper rates also increased on state 0, and balanced paper won the action competition there on 3/12, 3/12, and 8/12 probes, where scissors is the correct move. The stored 72-round frozen held-out records show the same qualitative pattern under episode dynamics: state-2 paper rates increased more than state-0/1 rates, but state-0 paper choices and missed wins remained. The input proxy mixes synaptic and network-state changes, and a 12-replay descriptive comparison is not independent confirmation or proof of a unique synaptic mechanism.
- **Conclusion / stop.** Balanced training did give the paper pathway a stronger response preferentially on the relevant state, but incomplete separation from scissors on state 2 and spillover into state 0 explain why the locked three-action learning gate remained failed. No further RPS learning experiment or teacher-student RPS run is justified under the current evidence. Do not rerun or tune the failed training. Reproduce this **one frozen analysis only** with `node tools/check_rps_paper_specificity.mjs` in a location without its output; raw per-trial KC identities, rates, margins, controls, and saved-state checks: `data/rps_paper_specificity.json`. Existing two-cue results and all earlier RPS artifacts are unchanged; no Git operations were performed.

### Prospective opt-in RPS MBON normalization test (locked before calibration/training, )

- **Hypothesis.** Fixed action-group baseline differences in the raw MBON maximum bias RPS choices toward scissors; subtracting an untrained, reward-free mean rate for each action may expose state-dependent changes produced by the existing dopamine plasticity. The earlier balanced-training gate remains a valid negative result. This is **one** authorized action-readout change, not a modification of input, network, learning rule, or teacher communication.
- **One label-free normalization.** Use the already saved `data/rps_frozen_input.json`: three initial-weight students 651–653, 12 reward-free probes for each of three previous-move inputs (108 trials). Compute one pooled three-number baseline `b[a] = mean(raw MBON group rate[a])` across **all 108 probes**, ignoring state, opponent, reward, correctness, and held-out records. At an opt-in RPS decision, choose `argmax_a (rawRate[a] − b[a])` using the same tie stream; retain ordinary raw selected-action rate for the existing value prediction and the selected action's ordinary KC eligibility. Baseline numbers are frozen before training. Default `RpsBioRL` remains raw `argmax`.
- **Calibration/validity stop before training.** Require exactly 12 initial reward-free recordings per state and seed, finite three-action rates, no reward/outcome fields, and a finite baseline. On these calibration rates alone, the corrected scores must give **each action at least one winner for every seed**; this checks that the fixed readout does not leave an action entirely inaccessible before learning. No correctness labels are used in this check. If any requirement fails, stop without student training or a second normalization setting. Save the baseline and validity result separately.
- **Locked matched comparison.** If calibration passes, reuse the **valid** corrected raw-readout balanced-learning and balanced-no-learning arms in `data/rps_balanced_training_corrected.json`; do not rerun them. For normalized-readout balanced-learning and balanced-no-learning arms, use the same preselected students **651–653**, initial multipliers, real wiring, deterministic `previous_opponent` with offset 1/noise 0, training opponent seeds `41000`–`41011`, **12 × 24** rounds, per-state continuous balanced-action cycle, episode neural resets, and exactly the same trial-keyed network/tie stream formula. Frozen evaluation has exploration and learning off, uses the arm's fixed readout on the identical held-out seeds `51000`–`51002` (72 rounds, 69 with a previous move), and verifies unchanged weights. The only experimental variable is the readout baseline subtraction; the no-learning arm is a plasticity control.
- **Measurements and validity.** Record pre/post frozen wins and implied next-move prediction accuracy (numerically equal to wins in mandatory RPS), scores for previous-move states 0/1/2, rock/paper/scissors choice counts on the 69 observed rounds, per-episode training wins, rewarded trials and weight-changing steps by action, and trial-level action-MBON rates. Require source raw arms to have passed their saved validity checks; identical initial weights, opponent sequences, trial stream keys and balanced action schedules across arms; no teacher; frozen weights unchanged; and normalized no-learning weights and frozen pre/post outcomes identical. Any technical validity failure stops inference without tuning.
- **Locked success / stop.** **Every** normalized-learning student must score at least **58/72** unforced frozen wins, at least **70%** in **each** observed previous-move state, choose **each** action at least **12/69** observed rounds, and beat its paired raw-readout balanced-learning score by at least **10/72** wins (the prior practical matched-arm margin). Each normalized no-learning student must have unchanged weights, identical pre/post frozen behavior, and **fewer than 58/72** wins. If any criterion fails, the fixed normalization fails to establish reliable dopamine-driven three-action RPS learning; stop without another setting or teacher-student test. If it passes, this establishes learnability with this engineered but label-free readout and balanced training, not autonomous learning with the original readout or neural teaching.

### Fixed RPS rate calibration and pretraining validity

Applied the single locked pooled-mean formula to the existing 108 initial, reward-free frozen-input recordings; no neural simulation or outcome labels were used. The fixed **rock/paper/scissors** baselines are **0.472883597883598 / 0.3895502645502646 / 0.7195767195767195** spikes per MBON. The source had exactly 12 trials per state and seed, finite rates, and no opponent/reward/correctness fields. On these same calibration rates, each corrected action was a possible maximum: winner opportunities were **12/11/13**, **7/15/14**, and **11/12/13** for seeds 651–653. The predeclared accessibility gate passed for every seed. The numbers are now frozen in `data/rps_rate_baseline.json` before any normalized student training. Reproduce the read-only calibration with `node tools/calibrate_rps_rate_baseline.mjs` in a location without that output; it refuses overwrite. These are calibration-opportunity counts, **not** held-out accuracy or evidence of RPS learning.

### Locked normalized-readout RPS result: paper/scissors bias reverses, learning gate fails

- **Experiment / controls.** Ran the one approved opt-in correction, `score[a] = raw MBON rate[a] − b[a]`, with the already frozen three-number baseline above. The default raw-rate RPS readout and the original dopamine rule remain intact; the opt-in readout uses the selected action's **raw** rate for ordinary value prediction and its ordinary KC eligibility. Students 651–653 received the same 12×24 deterministic opponent training rounds and balanced-action schedule as the saved valid raw-readout arm. That arm and its no-learning control were reused, not rerun. Each normalized learning/no-learning arm began at identical initial multipliers, opponent sequences, and trial-keyed neural/tie stream keys. Frozen evaluations used the fixed readout with exploration and learning off on the identical 72 held-out rounds. All checks passed: matched observed-state action schedules and opponent moves, balanced training choices, frozen baseline unchanged, frozen weights unchanged, and normalized no-learning weights and before/after action/rate records identical. No teacher source or message was used.
- **Results.** Wins and implied next-move prediction accuracy are numerically equal because the mandatory RPS counter-action uniquely implies a predicted opponent move; they are **not independent** measures. State scores are previous-move **0/1/2** out of **23/24/22**. Action counts are **rock/paper/scissors** across the 69 observed-state held-out rounds. Rewarded updates are positive-reward training steps that actually changed at least one KC→MBON multiplier, in the same action order.

| Student | Normalized initial / learned wins | Matched raw learned wins | Normalized no-learning wins | Normalized state 0 / 1 / 2 wins | Normalized R / P / S choices | Rewarded weight-changing steps R / P / S |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 651 | 10 / **52** | 52 | 10 | **4 / 24 / 22** | 24 / 41 / 4 | 30 / 31 / 31 |
| 652 | 9 / **53** | 44 | 9 | **6 / 24 / 21** | 24 / 38 / 7 | 30 / 31 / 31 |
| 653 | 11 / **56** | 50 | 11 | **8 / 24 / 22** | 24 / 37 / 8 | 30 / 31 / 31 |

- **Locked gates.** All normalized-learning students missed **58/72**, **70% in every observed state**, and **12/69 choices for every action**; scissors was chosen only **4, 7, 8** times. Improvements over matched raw learning were **0, +9, +6/72**, each below the locked **+10/72** margin. The normalized no-learning controls had **zero** weight changes, exactly reproduced their own pretraining frozen behavior, and scored only **10, 9, 11/72**, passing the required negative-control gate. The raw no-learning controls in the saved matched artifact scored 10, 11, 8/72. Thus the fixed normalization **fails** the all-three-student success criterion despite substantial dopamine-driven synaptic changes.
- **Failure localization / limits.** Normalization nearly solved the previous-move-2 paper deficit (**22/22, 21/22, 22/22**) but moved the failure to previous-move 0, where scissors is required (**4/23, 6/23, 8/23**). The saved state-0 frozen **raw** MBON means still had scissors above paper in every normalized student: paper/scissors **0.742/0.798**, **0.724/0.870**, **0.612/0.748**. Subtracting the fixed baseline (scissors **0.720**, paper **0.390**) reverses those action scores, making paper win on **19/23, 17/23, 15/23** state-0 trials. Thus the state-0 failure is an **overcorrection by this fixed readout** in the presence of action-relevant neural rate differences, not evidence that the previous-move input lacks information. The experiment does not establish that every possible label-free readout or training history would fail; calibration reused these students' initial, reward-free probes and is not an independent cohort. It also does not show autonomous learning without externally balanced training actions.
- **Conclusion / stop.** This fixed label-free normalization did **not** make RPS reliably learnable under the locked dopamine-training protocol. No normalization value, training parameter, input, or learning rule was retuned after evaluation; no further readout search or teacher-student RPS run follows. Reproduce the calibration with `node tools/calibrate_rps_rate_baseline.mjs` and, only if it passes, the locked training with `node tools/check_rps_normalized_readout.mjs` in a location without their output files. Preserve raw `data/rps_rate_baseline.json` and `data/rps_normalized_readout.json`, plus the unchanged valid raw control `data/rps_balanced_training_corrected.json`. All two-cue files remain untouched. No Git operations were performed.
### Path B RPS reward-readout information gate (locked before held-out simulation, )

- **Hypothesis / features.** The three action-group MBON spike rates at the existing 240 ms RPS decision point jointly identify the three observed previous-opponent-move states even when raw argmax picks the wrong action. This is the smallest decision-time population already saved in the reward-free frozen-input probe. Archived descending-neuron spikes exist for a different 120 ms, noisy, switched teacher protocol (498 DNs); they do not provide a matched frozen decision-time input for this test. We will not search another population if this one fails.
- **Prospective information test.** Use only the 108 initial-weight, reward-free `data/rps_frozen_input.json` trials from seeds 651–653 for calibration. Compute one pooled centroid per state from the three `log1p(action-group MBON rate)` features, and classify by nearest squared Euclidean centroid with fixed tie order 0,1,2. No opponent move, reward, correct action, or outcome enters the decoder. Record three new independent held-out initial-weight seeds **661–663**, 12 frozen probes per state and seed, using the original 150 Hz VPN input, reset at every probe, counts reset at 50 ms, decision at 240 ms, with deterministic probe streams. No learning or reward. The classifier is only an information measurement and will not be installed as the action readout.
- **Locked pass/fail.** Require at least **87/108 (80.6%)** total held-out state classifications, at least **26/36 (72.2%)** correct for each state pooled over held-out seeds, and at least **27/36 (75%)** correct for each held-out seed. Compare with 36/108 balanced chance and a deterministic 1,000-permutation within-seed, class-balanced shuffled-label baseline; require the real score to exceed the shuffled 95th percentile. Weights must remain frozen, all three states must have 12 probes per seed, and all rates must be finite. Any failure stops Path B before implementation or training. A pass establishes input information only, not reward learnability or teacher benefit.

### Path B information result and reward-readout experiment (locked before training, )

- **Information result.** The predeclared three-rate nearest-centroid assay scored **98/108 (90.7%)** on frozen independent seeds 661–663, versus balanced chance **36/108** and shuffled-label 95th percentile **44/108** (1,000 fixed permutations; mean in raw file). Correct classifications by state were **30/36, 34/36, 34/36**; by held-out seed **30/36, 36/36, 32/36**. All fixed gates passed. This analytical decoder will not be installed as a fly policy. Raw rates, centroids, confusion, and reproduction command `node tools/check_rps_readout_information.mjs` are in `data/rps_readout_information.json`.
- **Hybrid readout hypothesis / architecture.** A three-action linear-softmax REINFORCE readout of the same three action-group MBON spike rates can learn a reward-specific decision boundary while the original dopamine KC→MBON plasticity continues unchanged. The readout receives only `log1p` MBON rates, standardized by one fixed pooled mean and standard deviation computed from the 108 reward-free initial calibration probes **without state labels**. All readout weights and biases start at zero. At training decisions it samples the softmax using the existing seeded agent stream; frozen evaluation takes deterministic `argmax` with tie order 0,1,2. After each ordinary RPS reward and dopamine update, update readout weights and bias with the existing `DeepRL` REINFORCE rule: `lr=0.03`, exponential reward baseline rate `0.05`, and gradient `(reward - baseline)*(chosen-indicator - softmax probability)*feature`. The ordinary selected-action value prediction, eligibility snapshot, dopamine drives, and KC→MBON update remain active. The opt-in readout adds reward-trained parameters; default `RpsBioRL` is unchanged. Disabling readout learning leaves dopamine KC→MBON learning active, isolating the additional mechanism.
- **Locked matched experiment.** Reuse saved **valid** ordinary and balanced raw-MBON training arms in `data/rps_balanced_training_corrected.json`; do not rerun them. Run only hybrid REINFORCE and hybrid readout-learning-disabled arms for preselected students **651–653**, each from identical initial neural weights and zero readout weights. Use the same 12 episodes × 24 rounds, deterministic observable `previous_opponent` offset 1/noise 0, training opponent seeds 41000–41011, trial-keyed neural and action random streams, neural reset at episode start, and 72 held-out rounds from seeds 51000–51002. The hybrid arms use their own training softmax choices (no forced balanced actions); original and balanced MBON arms retain their saved training policies. Frozen evaluations have no sampling or learning and use each arm's own policy. Check identical opponent schedules, trial keys, initial neural weights, unchanged frozen weights/readout, and zero readout changes in the disabled arm. The disabled arm may still exhibit dopamine synaptic changes.
- **Predeclared measurements / gates.** Save episode win curves, pre/post frozen wins and per-state wins (23/24/22 trials), held-out action counts across 69 observed rounds, positive-reward KC→MBON weight-changing steps by action, total/sparse synaptic changes, and readout weight/bias changes. **Every** hybrid-learning student must win at least **58/72**, win at least **70% in each observed state**, select each action at least **12/69** observed rounds, and beat its saved matched ordinary-training MBON arm by at least **10/72** wins. The readout-learning-disabled control must have unchanged readout parameters, score below 58/72 in each seed, and fail the full three-action learning criterion. Any validity failure prevents a learning claim. Any success-gate failure stops Path B without a new architecture, learning rate, budget, or teacher experiment. A pass would establish this one hybrid model's independent RPS learning, not dopamine-only learning or neural teaching.

### Locked Path B RPS hybrid readout result: information present, reliable learning absent

- **Experiment / validity.** The one fixed, opt-in three-feature REINFORCE readout and its readout-learning-disabled arm completed for exactly seeds 651–653. Both kept the ordinary dopamine KC→MBON updates. The valid saved ordinary and balanced MBON arms were reused without rerunning. All arms used the same initial neural weights, opponent schedules, episode neural resets, and trial-keyed neural/action streams. The hybrid used softmax training exploration; frozen tests used deterministic readout argmax, no exploration, and no learning. Checks passed for matched opponent observations/outcomes, 288 training and 72 held-out rounds, unchanged neural and readout parameters during frozen evaluation, and unchanged zero readout weights/biases in the disabled arm. The original raw MBON policy remains the default.
- **Frozen held-out results.** State scores are previous-opponent states 0/1/2 out of 23/24/22 observed trials. Action counts are rock/paper/scissors across 69 observed trials. One initial round per held-out sequence has no previous state and is included in 72-round wins.

| 652 | 47/72 | 44/72 | 24 → **50/72** | **3/23** / 24/24 / 22/22 | 24 / 42 / **3** | 24/72 | +3/72 |
| 653 | 47/72 | 50/72 | 24 → **54/72** | **7/23** / 24/24 / 22/22 | 24 / 38 / **7** | 24/72 | +7/72 |

Hybrid positive-reward KC→MBON weight-changing steps for rock/paper/scissors were **60/87/31, 65/85/32, 53/82/35**; total changed KC→MBON multipliers were **706, 774, 735**. - **Locked gate / interpretation.** All three hybrid students failed **58/72**, the **70% every-state** requirement (state 0), and the **12/69 every-action** requirement (scissors). Only seed 651 met the +10/72 advantage over ordinary MBON training. The readout-disabled negative-control gate passed. Initial untrained MBON activity contained ample three-state information on independent seeds, but this fixed reward-trained readout converged to paper on most state-0 trials, where scissors was required. The data do not separate readout credit-assignment limits from possible drift or overlap in MBON features after dopamine learning; no follow-up diagnostic or retuning is authorized in this Path B test. The hybrid is a **separate neural-learning model**, not the unchanged dopamine-only Fly Hero policy. A teacher-student RPS experiment is not justified by this failed independent-learning gate.

Full trial records, curves, sparse KC→MBON changes, readout weights, gates, and controls: `data/rps_readout_information.json` and `data/rps_reward_readout.json`. Earlier RPS and two-cue artifacts remain unchanged. Stop without another readout, training budget, or teacher communication study.

### Frozen hybrid RPS action-score diagnostic plan (specified before replay, )

- **Question / hypothesis.** The trained hybrid readout contains state-dependent score differences but its paper-over-scissors margin stays positive for both previous-move states 0 and 2; this would explain why it chooses paper when state 0 requires scissors. A pure global paper preference instead predicts paper is the top score across all three states with little state-dependent change.
- **One saved-state replay.** Reconstruct only students 651–653 from `data/rps_reward_readout.json` sparse final KC→MBON multipliers, final readout weights/biases, and fixed feature normalization. Replay exactly the earlier `data/rps_frozen_input.json` reward-free probe: 12 matched stream keys per previous-move input 0/1/2, neural reset each probe, 150 Hz existing VPN input through 240 ms, spike counts reset at 50 ms. Record raw MBON group rates, the three standardized log-rate features received by the readout, three *preselection* linear scores, deterministic selected action, analysis-only correct action, and correct-minus-strongest-other score margin. Use identical initial-network probes and the saved zero-weight untrained readout as a control; additionally apply the trained readout to initial-network features analytically to assess whether changed neural features matter. No game, reward, dopamine, learning, teacher, or parameter change.
- **Interpretation measures / validity.** Report mean score vectors, mean paper-minus-scissors scores, winner counts, and correct margins by input state and seed. Compare the paper-minus-scissors difference between states 2 and 0 on matched repeats. Classify as a global paper preference only if paper is the modal winner in **every** state for each seed and its paper-minus-scissors mean changes by less than 0.25 score units between states 0 and 2. Otherwise, if another action wins a state but paper still beats scissors on most state-0 inputs, describe state dependence with insufficient state-0/2 separation. This is a descriptive mechanism test, not a new learning success gate. Require exact reproduction of the archived untrained MBON rates, valid sparse plastic indices and finite parameters, and unchanged frozen neural/readout weights. Stop if reconstruction or validity fails. The locked hybrid training failure remains unchanged.

### Frozen hybrid RPS action-score diagnostic result

- **Reconstruction / control.** Saved sparse KC→MBON multipliers and all final hybrid readout weights/biases were sufficient for students 651–653. One reward-free replay used all 12 prior reset streams for each of three previous-move inputs, identically for initial and trained networks. Every initial MBON rate exactly matched `data/rps_frozen_input.json`. Initial zero-weight readouts yielded scores **0/0/0** and deterministic rock for every state, so the trained preferences were learned; all neural multipliers and readout weights remained frozen during this replay. The correct winning action (state 0→scissors, 1→rock, 2→paper) was computed **only for analysis**.
- **Pooled trained results.** Each row has 36 probes (12 per student). Features are the actual standardized `log1p` MBON rates fed to the readout; scores are its preselection rock/paper/scissors logits. The final column is the correct-action score minus its strongest competitor.

| Previous state; correct action | Mean features R/P/S | Mean scores R/P/S | Chosen R/P/S | Correct margin | Mean paper − scissors score |
|---|---|---|---:|---:|---:|
| 0; scissors | 0.066 / 1.306 / 0.805 | −1.486 / **1.546** / −0.060 | 0 / 31 / 5 | **−1.606** | +1.606 |
| 1; rock | 2.743 / −0.684 / −0.097 | **2.787** / −0.970 / −1.817 | 36 / 0 / 0 | +3.642 | +0.847 |
| 2; paper | 0.939 / 2.808 / 1.399 | −2.554 / **3.454** / −0.900 | 0 / 36 / 0 | +4.354 | +4.354 |

- **Across students.** For state 0, scissors beat paper in only **1/12, 1/12, 3/12** replays; mean paper-over-scissors score was **+1.459, +1.730, +1.628**. For state 2, paper won **12/12** for all students with mean paper-over-scissors score **+4.059, +4.280, +4.723**. The state-2 minus state-0 paper/scissors score gaps were **+2.600, +2.550, +3.095**—well beyond the predeclared 0.25 near-constant band. Rock won all **12/12** state-1 replays per student. Paper was therefore **not** the top action everywhere, and its advantage varied strongly by state; the predeclared pure global-paper pattern did not occur. However, the learned state-0/2 separation did not cross the paper/scissors decision boundary on most state-0 trials. Notably, the learned bias terms alone favored scissors over paper (`b_paper − b_scissors` = **−0.593, −0.681, −0.359**), so the wrong state-0 paper scores arose from the feature-weight interaction, not a positive constant paper intercept.
- **Initial-feature cross-check / limitations.** Applying the same **trained** readout analytically to the initial-network neural features gave mean paper-minus-scissors scores on state 0 of **+0.506, −0.391, −0.164**, versus **+1.459, +1.730, +1.628** on the trained network; state 2 stayed paper-favoring. This is consistent with KC→MBON plasticity shifting state-0 neural features toward the paper-favoring side of the learned boundary. The cross-check also made state 1 mostly scissors on initial features, so simply freezing the initial network is not supported as a ready remedy. This replay uses the same student seeds and prior probe streams, and is a mechanism analysis, not an independent learning confirmation. It does not isolate readout credit assignment from network/readout co-adaptation, and the failed locked 50/50/54-of-72 hybrid result remains unchanged.
- **Conclusion / next experiment.** The readout learned genuine state-dependent preferences, but **insufficient paper/scissors separation for state 0** is the immediate decision failure. No single readout adjustment or parameter value follows from this diagnostic; do not change or retrain the model automatically. Reproduce this one frozen diagnostic with `node tools/check_rps_hybrid_scores.mjs` in a copy without its output file. Full per-trial rates, features, logits, choices, margins, initial controls, and saved-state checks: `data/rps_hybrid_scores.json`. No teacher experiment or other RPS direction was started.

### Proposed reward-baseline RPS update: duplicate of existing hybrid rule

- **Hypothesis / inspection.** The proposed next intervention was to subtract a causal running expected reward from game reward before the hybrid policy-gradient update. Inspected `js/rps.js` `RpsRewardReadout.afterDopamine` and the shared constants and precedent in `js/agents.js`, alongside the locked hybrid result and frozen score diagnostic above.
- **Existing equation.** With reward `r_t`, baseline `B_t`, softmax probability `p_t(a)`, selected action `A_t`, and neural feature `f_t`, the current hybrid already computes `adv_t = r_t - B_t`, then `B_{t+1} = B_t + 0.05(r_t - B_t)`, and updates each bias/weight by `0.03 adv_t [1(a=A_t)-p_t(a)]` times `1` or `f_t`, respectively. The baseline starts at zero and uses only rewards observed through the current trial. The policy-gradient advantage uses the **pre-update** baseline. The original dopamine KC→MBON update runs separately and is unchanged.
- **Result / controls / stop.** A reward-baseline policy-gradient arm with the requested equation would duplicate the existing hybrid arm, so there is no distinct experimental variable or valid new comparison. No fresh seeds were selected, no training or evaluation was run, no files of experimental results were generated, and no model code or earlier result was changed. The existing 50/50/54-of-72 failed gate and state-0 paper/scissors limitation stand. No teacher-student RPS test is justified. Reproduce this read-only audit by inspecting `js/rps.js` lines defining `RpsRewardReadout.afterDopamine` and `js/agents.js` `DEEP_DEFAULTS`/`DeepRL.afterDopamine`; a simulation command is inapplicable.

To support interference, B must additionally beat paired A by at least **10/72** wins in **every** seed; C must fail the full learning criteria with unchanged weights. - **Neural feature check.** Applying the **previously fixed**, initial-calibration nearest-centroid state decoder to held-out `log1p` MBON rates (no refitting) scored **42/39/28 of 69** in A, **60/62/61 of 69** in B, and **55/66/65 of 69** in C for seeds 671/672/673. B's features therefore remained distinguishable by this fixed reference on the held-out sequences. A's lower scores show weaker alignment with the initial reference, consistent with changed KC→MBON weights, but do **not** prove intrinsic state information was lost or that feature movement caused the behavior difference; the fixed decoder was not trained on A's changed representation. Per-state confusion matrices and mean readout features are saved.
- **Locked decision / limitation.** B improved A by **+5,+4,−1/72**, below the required **+10/72 in every seed**. Only B seed 671 exceeded 58/72, yet its state-0 accuracy **15/23** was below 70%; B seeds 672/673 also missed 58/72, state-0 accuracy, and the 12/69 scissors-choice gate (7 and 11 choices). The B mean paper-minus-scissors score on state 0 changed from positive in A to slightly negative in all seeds, but noisy decisions and, in seeds 672/673, rock selections still prevented state-0 reliability. C retained zero weights/readout and failed the learning gates at 24/72 in all seeds; its negative control supports reward-dependent readout contribution to B's performance. Freezing synapses provided **partial, inconsistent** behavioral improvement and better state-0 average score separation, but the predeclared criterion for interference was **not met**. Both A and B failed reliable three-action learning; freezing KC→MBON plasticity did not resolve RPS under this protocol. This result does not exonerate or establish interference under every regime, and it does not overturn the earlier failed 651–653 hybrid result. Independent RPS learning is still insufficient to justify Fly A→Fly B teaching.
- **Reproduction / stop.** Run `node tools/check_rps_frozen_synapse_hybrid.mjs` in a copy without the result file; the runner refuses overwrite. Raw design, curves, per-trial features/scores/actions/rewards, fixed-centroid predictions, synaptic/readout parameters, validity checks, and gates: `data/rps_frozen_synapse_hybrid.json`. No seeds, budgets, gates, or parameters were adjusted; no further diagnostic, architecture, or teacher experiment was started.

### Prospective nonlinear frozen-synapse RPS controller (locked before implementation and training, )

- **Hypothesis / scope.** The fixed three MBON action-group rates contain state information (external decoder 98/108 held-out), but the linear reward-trained readout did not learn a reliable three-action policy. One nonlinear reward-trained controller may separate the state-0 scissors and state-2 paper decisions while KC→MBON features remain fixed. This is an optional artificial reinforcement-learning controller, not dopamine-only Fly Hero; it does not involve teacher messages.
- **One architecture and initialization.** Input is the existing three `f_j = (log1p(MBON_rate_j) − featureMean_j)/featureStd_j` features, with the exact fixed `featureMean`/`featureStd` from `data/rps_reward_readout.json`. Hidden layer: eight `tanh` units. Output: three logits and softmax. For student seed `s`, initialize the 8×3 hidden weights from the repository's `rng(s*65537+17)`, uniform in `[-sqrt(6/11), +sqrt(6/11)]`; initialize hidden biases, 3×8 output weights, and three output biases to zero. This makes initial action probabilities exactly 1/3, matching the zero-initialized linear arm. Fix the policy-gradient learning rate at the existing `0.03`, reward-baseline rate at `0.05`, and no other hyperparameter search. Use sampled softmax actions during training and deterministic logit argmax during evaluation. The selected-action gradient includes every output action; backpropagate through tanh using pre-update output weights. The controller receives no state labels, hidden strategy, future move, trial index, or correct action.
- **Fixed sample / matched arms.** Three fresh preselected students **681, 682, 683**, not used in earlier RPS investigations. A: existing linear `RpsRewardReadout` with `freezeSynapses=true` and readout learning on. B: new nonlinear readout, frozen synapses, reward learning on. C: same nonlinear readout and identical seed-specific initialization as B, frozen synapses, reward learning disabled. Use the existing real wiring and all-one initial KC→MBON multipliers; identical existing MBON feature transform, opponent `previous_opponent` offset 1/noise 0, 12×24 training rounds, opponent seeds 41000–41011, episode neural-state resets, and trial-keyed network/action streams `key=s*10000000+episodeIndex*1000+round`, network `rng(key+1)`, action `rng(key+501)`. No forced actions or teacher signals. Actions and rewards may subsequently differ by arm. Dopamine activity remains simulated; KC→MBON writes stay frozen in all arms.
- **Frozen evaluation / records.** Evaluate each arm on identical three 24-round held-out sequences, seeds 51000–51002, stream episode keys 100–102, with learning off and deterministic argmax. Record 72-round wins; wins and trial counts for each observed previous move (0/1/2); action frequencies over the 69 observed-state rounds; mean preselection paper-minus-scissors logits on states 0 and 2; 12-episode reward curves; full initial/final controller parameters and change norms; KC→MBON integrity; and trial-level neural rates, standardized features, logits, actions, opponents, rewards, and observations. Preserve the old results without overwriting them.
- **Validity and gates.** Every arm/student must complete exactly 288 training and 72 frozen evaluation rounds, share the same opponent/previous-state schedule and initial neural weights, use the declared stream formula, have no forced action or message, show frozen evaluation action=score argmax, and keep all neural/controller weights and reward baseline unchanged in evaluation. All three arms must have zero KC→MBON multiplier changes; A and B must change controller weights; C must keep controller weights, biases, and baseline exactly at their initial values. B and C must have identical initial controllers. If any validity check fails, do not claim a learning result or rerun with altered settings. **Success requires every B student** to win at least **58/72**, win at least **70% separately in each observed previous-move state**, choose **each action at least 12/69** observed rounds, and beat its paired A in total wins by at least **1/72**. C must fail the complete learning criterion and retain unchanged neural/controller weights. Any failed gate makes this fixed experiment a failure, even if some scores improve. Run exactly these three seeds once; no tuning, replacement, follow-up diagnostic, or teacher experiment.
- **Planned command / artifact.** `node tools/check_rps_nonlinear_readout.mjs`; output `data/rps_nonlinear_readout.json` with overwrite refusal. Run relevant syntax and existing regression checks once after implementation. The next decision is whether this one controller earns a reliable independent-learning baseline; no Fly A→Fly B test is part of this run.

### Locked nonlinear frozen-synapse RPS result: two students pass, full gate fails

- **Implementation / validity.** Added one opt-in `RpsNonlinearReadout` with the declared 3→8 tanh→3 architecture. It inherits the existing MBON feature calculation, softmax action sampling, causal reward baseline, and dopamine/freeze path; only logits and the backpropagated policy-gradient parameter update differ. The single run used only seeds 681–683 and A/B/C as locked above. All recorded validity checks passed: 288 training and 72 held-out trials per arm/student; matched training and evaluation opponent schedules, initial all-one neural weights, initial uniform output policies, matched B/C hidden initialization and trial-key streams; no forced actions/messages; zero KC→MBON changes in all arms; dopamine activity in all arms; unchanged neural and controller parameters during frozen evaluation; and exact frozen logit/feature/action agreement. A and B changed controller weights; C retained every controller parameter and its baseline exactly.
- **Held-out results.** State scores are wins on previous-move 0/1/2 out of **23/24/22** observed rounds. Action counts are rock/paper/scissors over **69** observed rounds. Margins are mean preselection paper-minus-scissors logits on states 0 and 2. The three first rounds without an observed previous move count in total wins.

| 682 | **58/72; 12/23, 24/24, 22/22** | **56/72; 10/23, 24/24, 22/22** | **−2/72** | 31/28/10 | −0.743 / +3.145 | 24/72 |
| 683 | 59/72; 14/23, 23/24, 22/22 | **62/72; 18/23, 22/24, 22/22** | +3/72 | 23/26/20 | −1.135 / +2.913 | 24/72 |

- **Training curves / parameter changes.** A/B/C episode-win curves (12 episodes of 24) for 681: `9,10,9,11,12,11,17,16,16,15,16,17` / `9,9,7,11,11,12,14,16,15,16,13,17` / `8,7,6,3,6,6,9,7,7,13,7,7`; for 682: `13,10,17,14,12,13,14,16,18,15,17,15` / `12,11,13,12,11,15,14,16,20,17,18,15` / `11,9,8,9,5,9,10,6,10,9,11,8`; for 683: `5,14,9,13,12,12,15,18,17,18,19,17` / `5,16,9,13,12,11,15,18,18,18,16` / `5,9,6,8,8,8,3,10,7,7,14,8`. C's weight and bias changes were exactly zero; all KC→MBON multipliers stayed unchanged in every arm. - **Locked decision / limitation.** **The full success gate fails.** B seeds 681 and 683 each pass the 58/72, every-state ≥70%, every-action ≥12/69, and paired-improvement conditions. Seed 682 misses total wins (56/72), state 0 (10/23), scissors frequency (10/69), and the paired improvement (−2/72). In all three B students the average state-0 paper-minus-scissors logit is negative, but seed 682 still chooses rock or paper on **13/23** state-0 trials. The no-learning C arm scores 24/72 and selects rock on all 69 observed trials with unchanged weights, supporting a reward-learning contribution to B's performance under this design. This is partial, seed-dependent improvement, not reliable independent three-action learning. The readout consumes only actual simulated MBON rates, but its added controller is artificial and the neural KC→MBON synapses are frozen; no dopamine-only or Fly A→Fly B teaching claim follows.
- **Reproduce / next experiment.** `node tools/check_rps_nonlinear_readout.mjs` in a separate copy without `data/rps_nonlinear_readout.json`; the script refuses overwrite. Do not rerun with replacement seeds, retune, add a diagnostic, or begin teacher-student RPS training under this failed gate. The earlier cue and RPS archives remain separate and unchanged.

All **216/216** observations, MBON rates, standardized features, three logits, actions, and rewards exactly matched the archived evaluation. Neural weights, controller weights, and reward baselines stayed unchanged. The original locked success failure remains intact. Essential per-state feature/logit means and population standard deviations, margins, probabilities, choices, fixed-centroid predictions, and cross-policy calculations are in `data/rps_nonlinear_seed_comparison.json`.

- **State-0 features and scores.** Standardized rock/paper/scissors MBON feature means for 681/682/683 were **(+0.168,+0.207,−0.295)** / **(−0.058,+0.060,−0.371)** / **(+0.026,+0.153,−0.332)**. Their population SDs were **(0.709,0.481,0.356)** / **(0.494,0.477,0.285)** / **(0.598,0.600,0.503)**. Mean rock/paper/scissors logits were **(−0.522,−0.126,+0.649)** / **(−0.292,−0.225,+0.518)** / **(−0.516,−0.310,+0.825)**; logit SDs were **(0.662,1.076,0.456)** / **(1.053,1.078,0.265)** / **(0.905,1.298,0.487)**. Thus 682 did not lack state-0 feature variation or have a positive *mean* paper-over-scissors logit. Its scissors score failed to beat the strongest competitor on 13/23 individual trials, including **seven rock** and **six paper** choices. Across those 13 errors, mean selected-action softmax probability was **0.588**, with only **3/13 ≥0.7**; errors were generally moderate rather than a confident global paper preference.
- **Boundary comparison / interpretation.** The previously fixed centroid recognized state 0 on **18/23, 18/23, 17/23** inputs for 681/682/683; all three had **60/69, 60/69, 57/69** total observed-state recognition. Applying the already trained 681 and 683 policies *analytically* to the exact 682 features, without changing any fly, chose scissors on **21/23** and **20/23** state-0 inputs, versus 682's own **10/23**. Applying 682's policy to 681/683 features chose scissors on only **7/23** and **12/23**. This supports a seed-682 **learned decision-boundary limitation**, not missing state-0 neural information or a requirement for a uniquely different boundary. It does not identify why reward training converged to that boundary; different successful policies are not evidence of an optimal architecture. The archived 682 training log includes **33** rewarded state-0 scissors choices versus **39** and **36** for 681/683, so its pathway was exposed to positive rewards; the modest count difference alone cannot establish insufficient exploration.
- **Conclusion / next experiment.** No specific learning-rule, architecture, or hyperparameter change follows from this frozen comparison. The smallest independent next test, if authorized later, is a prospectively locked replication of the **unchanged** nonlinear training protocol on new preselected students, reporting the same state-0 margin and choice distribution alongside the original gates. That would test whether this learned-boundary failure recurs across independent seeds; it would not by itself establish its causal training mechanism. No retraining or demo/teacher work was done here. Reproduce only this frozen comparison with `node tools/analyze_rps_nonlinear_seeds.mjs` in a copy without the derived output file; the script refuses overwrite.

Use `previous_opponent` offset 1/noise 0; exactly **12×24** training rounds with opponent seeds **41000–41011**, episode neural resets, and trial streams `key=studentSeed*10000000+episodeIndex*1000+round`, network `rng(key+1)`, action `rng(key+501)`. ### Prospective contextual-bandit RPS comparison (locked before implementation/training, )

- **Hypothesis / inspection.** The existing nonlinear REINFORCE controller passed only 3/7 independently tested students, with recurrent state-0 scissors failures despite informative MBON inputs. A separate expected-reward estimate for each action, updated only from that action's observed game reward, might avoid cross-action policy-gradient credit effects and learn the state-0 boundary more reliably. Inspection found no existing RPS contextual-bandit controller. This is one authorized additional hybrid readout, not the original dopamine-only policy or neural teaching.
- **Fixed controller.** Reuse the exact archived three standardized action-MBON spike-rate features `f_j=(log1p(rate_j)-featureMean_j)/featureStd_j` from `data/rps_reward_readout.json`. For each action `a`, initialize three weights and one bias to **zero** and predict `Q_a(f)=w_a·f+b_a`. During training, use **epsilon=0.20**: one seeded uniform draw chooses a uniformly random action when it falls below epsilon; otherwise choose deterministic `argmax Q` with rock/paper/scissors tie order. This gives fixed exploration without state labels. At the normal dopamine-update time, with the actual selected action `a` and observed RPS reward `r∈{−1,0,+1}`, update only that head: `e=r−Q_a(f)`, `w_a←w_a+0.03 e f`, `b_a←b_a+0.03 e`. Learning rate **0.03** reuses the existing reward-readout step scale; epsilon **0.20** is fixed before evaluation. No reward baseline, correct-action label, future opponent move, hidden strategy, external classifier, or unchosen-action update enters this controller. At frozen evaluation use `argmax Q`, no exploration or learning. The existing connectome, spiking dynamics, dopamine rule, original MBON and hybrid readouts, opponent, and action-readout defaults stay unchanged. Freeze KC→MBON multiplier writes during all three study arms while still simulating dopamine activity.
- **Preselected four students / matched arms.** Use fresh consecutive student seeds **711, 712, 713, 714**, found unused in earlier RPS scripts and saved RPS student seed fields before training. No initial-performance selection or seed replacement. A: unchanged 8-unit nonlinear REINFORCE controller with frozen KC→MBON weights. B: new contextual-bandit controller with reward learning. C: the identical seed-specific contextual-bandit initialization and training action rule with reward learning disabled. All arms use the same real seed-specific wiring, all-one initial synaptic multipliers, three MBON features and fixed transform, `previous_opponent` offset 1/noise 0, **12×24** training rounds with opponent seeds **41000–41011**, episode neural-state resets, and trial-keyed streams `key=studentSeed*10000000+episodeIndex*1000+round`; network `rng(key+1)`, controller `rng(key+501)`. The action policies differ by design, so actions, reward histories, and subsequent neural trajectories can differ; external sequences and initial neural states are matched. No forced balanced action or teacher message.
- **Frozen evaluation / measurements.** Evaluate each arm on the same three held-out 24-round sequences, opponent seeds **51000–51002**, episode keys **100–102**, with deterministic action selection and learning off. Record total wins, state-0/1/2 wins out of the original **23/24/22** observed rounds, rock/paper/scissors counts across 69 observed rounds, state-0 scissors-minus-strongest-other Q margin and choices, 12-episode reward curves, initial/final controller parameters and change norms, dopamine activity, and KC→MBON integrity. Preserve per-trial neural rates, features, action values, actions, and rewards for audit. C must retain exact initial controller parameters and neural multipliers. A/B must update their controllers. Frozen evaluation must leave all parameters unchanged, and chosen action must equal the recorded Q/logit maximum. Require the same opponent/previous-state schedules across arms, no forced choices, 288 training and 72 evaluation rounds, and valid rewards. Any validity failure stops the conclusion without retuning.
- **Locked gates / stop.** For **every** B student: at least **58/72** held-out wins, at least **70%** wins in **each** observed previous-move state, and at least **12/69** choices of **each** action; B must win **strictly more** held-out rounds than its matched A. Every C must fail the full reliability criterion and retain unchanged controller and KC→MBON weights. All four B students must pass every gate for a positive result. Report every failure; if any gate fails, stop without changing epsilon, learning rate, architecture, seeds, or training budget. Passing would establish only this added reward-trained controller's reliability on the fixed observable RPS opponent, not dopamine-only learning or Fly A→Fly B teaching. Run one locked comparison; planned command `node tools/check_rps_contextual_bandit.mjs`, artifact `data/rps_contextual_bandit.json` with overwrite refusal.

- **Learning and controls.** Every B student met the original **58/72**, per-state **70%**, and per-action **12/69** gates. State-0 scissors was selected **23/23, 23/23, 23/23, 21/23** times, unlike the recurrent state-0 failures in the earlier REINFORCE cohort. B controller weight-plus-bias change norms were **0.984, 0.946, 0.993, 0.994**. B's 12-episode reward curves (wins per 24-round episode) were **711: 13,14,14,16,16,22,17,18,18,16,21,19; 712: 16,13,17,16,18,18,19,16,18,18,20,18; 713: 14,17,20,18,19,18,17,17,16,19,20,18; 714: 14,16,14,11,15,19,19,18,20,16,23,17**.
