At **240 ms** the trained teacher computes `1{max(Q)-min(Q)>1.098235868125541}` from its actual simulated MBON features. In the crossed forced-bit conditions, student KCs **506,545** for 0 or **113,1421** for 1 receive **300 Hz at 240-280 ms**; the no-message arm receives none. Student VPN cue then runs **280-520 ms** at 150 Hz, with student counts reset at **330 ms**. At **520 ms** record student three MBON action-group rates, standardized readout features, frozen contextual-bandit scores and action; only then may the opponent advance and reward be computed. The original relative update time is **620 ms**, called with learning disabled; finish at **680 ms**. No neural-state reset occurs at 280 ms. Bit is never passed to Fly B's controller directly. The default 400 ms RPS game is untouched.
- **Measurements and locked gates.** Record target KC spikes during stimulation, active KC identities/spikes at decision, all three MBON rates, teacher scores/source bit, student scores/action, matched MBON-rate changes, and before/after neural/controller parameter integrity. The fixed reward-free `log1p` three-MBON cue centroids from `data/rps_readout_information.json` classify cue states without refit. For each cue, fit only a nearest-Euclidean-centroid bit decoder to standardized decision-time three-MBON features from repeats 0-5 under bit 0 and 1, then require **at least 10/12** correct over repeats 6-11 (six of each bit). **Physical reach** requires each driven KC pair's mean 240-280 ms count to exceed matched no-message by **at least 1 spike**, separately for every cue/bit. **Cue preservation** requires the fixed decoder to recognize every cue on **at least 10/12** held-out repeats 6-17 under **each** bit, and to lose **no more than 2/12** versus matched no-message. **Validity** requires fixed targets/intensities/timing/streams/cues, separate nets, no predecision opponent advance, no 280 ms reset, and exact neural/controller parameter integrity. All gates must pass independently; any failure ends this approach without another receiver design or Fly B training. Planned command: `node tools/check_rps_teacher_first_receiver.mjs`; planned new write-once artifact: `data/rps_teacher_first_receiver.json`.


### Teacher-first 680 ms RPS receiving result: locked gate fails

- **Execution / controls.** Ran the one prospectively locked runner once: trained archived teacher **711**, frozen trained student **712**, three isolated observed-cue states, 18 fixed streams per cue, and matched no-message/forced-bit-0/forced-bit-1 conditions (**162** student probes). Teacher and student used separate real-wiring networks. A separate cue-free first-round probe emitted no teacher bit and stimulated no student KC. The opponent advanced only after the student's 520 ms decision. The student's cue began at 280 ms after the 240-280 ms message window, its counts reset at 330 ms, and the 620 ms update call had learning disabled. No neural or controller parameters changed; no Fly B training occurred. The previous teacher-source action-leak failure remains unchanged under its original rules.
- **Physical reach and decision-time bit information.** Mean extra spikes in the targeted KC pair during 240-280 ms were **14.89/14.83** for bit 0/1 on cue 0, **14.39/14.83** on cue 1, and **14.94/15.22** on cue 2, clearing the +1 physical gate. The decision-time three-MBON bit decoder scored **7/12, 11/12, 8/12** on cues 0/1/2, respectively, against the locked **10/12 on every cue** gate. Per bit, the correct counts were **5/6 and 2/6**, **6/6 and 5/6**, and **4/6 and 4/6**. Stimulation-period KC spiking therefore did not guarantee a reliably separable MBON message at 520 ms. Forced bits were crossed with every cue; the decoder could not pass by identifying the cue alone.
- **Cue representation / frozen action readout.** The prior fixed cue classifier recognized cue 0/1/2 on held-out repeats as **10/12, 12/12, 12/12** with no message; **11/12, 12/12, 10/12** with bit 0; and **10/12, 5/12, 12/12** with bit 1. Bit 1 on cue 1 misses the >=10/12 floor and loses **7/12** versus no message, beyond the <=2/12 drop gate. Its mean rock/paper/scissors MBON rates shifted from **[0.113, 0.012, 0.000]** without a message to **[0.292, 0.226, 0.488]** with bit 1. The unchanged frozen student controller changed its selected action on **4/12, 2/12, 0/12** held-out cue-0/1/2 probes under bit 0, and **4/12, 8/12, 0/12** under bit 1, compared with matched no-message probes. These immediate action changes do not establish helpful teaching or learned improvement.
- **Gates / comparison / stop.** Validity, physical reach, and zero-weight-update gates passed. Decision-time bit discrimination, absolute cue recognition, and the no-large-cue-drop gate failed; the complete receiving protocol **fails**. Relative to the earlier overlapping receiver, this schedule did not eliminate cue-1 distortion (old bit-1 **4/12**, new **5/12**), and bit discrimination remains below threshold for cues 0 and 2 (old **12/12,12/12,8/12**; new **7/12,11/12,8/12**). The previous check used an untrained student seed 721, while this one used a frozen trained student seed 712, so the numerical differences cannot isolate timing as their sole cause. Isolated reset probes also do not establish behavior across continuing game rounds. No adjustment or second receiver test follows. A teacher-student RPS learning-speed experiment is **not technically justified** by this failed gate.
- **Raw result / reproduction.** `data/rps_teacher_first_receiver.json` stores the locked design, first-round check, all 162 probe rows with teacher source bits/scores, target and decision-time KC activity, MBON rates, readout scores/actions, rewards, matched summaries, held-out decoder predictions, and separate gates. `node --check tools/check_rps_teacher_first_receiver.mjs` passed. To reproduce, run `node tools/check_rps_teacher_first_receiver.mjs` in a copy without the artifact; the runner refuses to overwrite it. No prior archive was changed.

### Prospective separate DNge175 RPS receiving gate (locked before implementation and simulation, )

- **Hypothesis / one changed pathway.** The fixed one-bit contextual-bandit teacher message may be measurable in a separate simulated DN pair while leaving the original three action-MBON RPS features usable. The preceding KC receiver remains a valid failure (decision-time bit scores 7/12, 11/12, 8/12; cue-1 bit-1 recognition 5/12). The source's original action-leak failure also remains unchanged; action-related advice is permitted only in this revised hybrid protocol. This is one receiving-only test, not a student-training or learning-speed experiment.
- **Graph-locked target.** Use subgraph indices **3141** (DNge175, bit 0) and **3165** (DNge175, bit 1), each at **300 Hz during 240-280 ms**. Their direct inputs are respectively 1 mid/24 DN and 3 mid/20 DN edges; direct outputs are 6 DN/145 VNC and 6 DN/156 VNC edges. Neither directly connects to a VPN, KC, or MBON, and neither connects to the other. The shortest directed path to an action MBON is three edges, so this is a separate measured population, not a proven independent circuit. Retain the existing trained teacher bit `1{max(Q)-min(Q)>1.098235868125541}` and source seed 711; frozen trained student seed 712. No neuron, threshold, target, or stimulation adjustment will follow results.
- **Deterministic probe count and streams.** For each cue `s=0,1,2` and repeat `r=0..17`, use `key=2026120400+1000*s+10*r`, teacher `rng(key+1)`, student `rng(key+2)`, and a matched student cue-period restart `rng(key+3)` at 280 ms without resetting neural state. Each `(s,r)` has no-message, forced-bit-0, and forced-bit-1 student probes. Thus **3 cues x 18 repeats x 3 conditions = 162 student probes**, plus 54 teacher source responses generated once per `(s,r)` and a separate cue-free first-round validity check excluded from all denominators. The crossed forced bits are independent of cue and of the teacher's emitted bit. Repeats **0-5** are calibration only (54 student probes, of which 36 are bit-labelled); repeats **6-17** are held out (108 student probes). A single global nearest-Euclidean-centroid bit decoder uses only the raw two DN window-spike counts, pooling the **18 calibration samples per bit across all three cues**. It is then fixed. Its bit test is repeats **6-11** only: **6 trials per bit x 2 bits = 12 per cue**, 36 total, with the >=10/12 gate applied separately to each cue. The previously fixed reward-free three-MBON `log1p` cue centroids from `data/rps_readout_information.json` receive no refit and score repeats **6-17**: 12 trials per cue/condition. Calibration and held-out repeat indices do not overlap. This interpretation is frozen before any held-out probe is simulated.
- **Timing / measured feature.** On each 680 ms trial, teacher sees the already observed previous-move cue at 150 Hz during 0-240 ms and resets its counts at 50 ms; student runs blank. At 240 ms teacher emits its real score-spread bit, while the diagnostic crosses no/forced-bit conditions. Drive only the selected student DN at 300 Hz during 240-280 ms. An opt-in two-number counter increments from actual `net.spikes` for indices 3141 and 3165 during those **40 ms**; its raw numerical counts are fixed at 280 ms and available at the 520 ms decision. It never stores or supplies the source bit, selected target ID, or decoded action to the student controller. Student's unchanged VPN cue runs at 150 Hz during 280-520 ms, ordinary counts reset at 330 ms, and the original three action-MBON rates and frozen action readout are measured at 520 ms. At 620 ms call the no-learning update; finish at 680 ms. No reward or dopamine learning is applied. The 330 ms reset would erase the DN spikes from ordinary `net.count`, so this separately measured spike window is an engineered interface, **not persistent neural memory**. No membrane, conductance, trace, or delay state is reset at 280 ms.
- **Independent locked gates and stopping.** (1) For each cue and bit, the stimulated DN's mean 240-280 ms count on held-out repeats 6-17 exceeds its matched no-message mean by **>=1 spike**. (2) The fixed global two-DN decoder scores **>=10/12** on repeats 6-11 separately for each cue. (3) The prior fixed three-MBON cue decoder scores **>=10/12** on repeats 6-17 under each bit separately for each cue. (4) Each cue/bit loses **<=2/12** correct recognitions versus its matched no-message condition. (5) Every teacher/student neural multiplier, controller weight, bias, and baseline remains exactly unchanged. Validity also requires exact timing/streams, separate teacher/student networks, 162 probes, no first-round message, and no neural-state reset at 280 ms. Any gate failure stops without retuning, another receiver test, or Fly B training. Planned write-once artifact `data/rps_dnge175_receiver.json`; reproduction command `node tools/check_rps_dnge175_receiver.mjs`.

### Separate DNge175 RPS receiving result: all frozen gates pass

- **One run / validity.** Executed the prospectively locked `node tools/check_rps_dnge175_receiver.mjs` once, using trained teacher 711, frozen trained student 712, three cues, 18 preselected streams per cue, and matched no-message/forced-bit-0/forced-bit-1 conditions (**162 student probes**, 54 separately simulated teacher responses). Repeats 0-5 alone fit one pooled two-DN nearest-centroid decoder; repeats 6-11 supplied exactly six held-out probes per bit per cue; repeats 6-17 supplied exactly 12 held-out cue probes per cue/condition. The separate cue-free first round had no teacher bit, stimulus, or sensory cue. All validity checks passed: independent teacher/student networks, exact 680 ms timing, no reset at 280 ms, unchanged source threshold and 300 Hz target drive, matched trial streams, exact frozen action-score argmax, and unchanged KC-to-MBON multipliers, controller weights, biases, and baselines. No reward, Fly B training, or learning-speed evaluation occurred. Neither `js/sim.js` nor the default RPS game/controller was changed.
- **Measured DN transmission.** The 240-280 ms spike-window calibration centroids on raw `[count3141,count3165]` were **[7.389,0]** for bit 0 and **[0,7.389]** for bit 1. On held-out repeats 6-17, mean DN counts were **[0,0]** under no message for all three cues; **[7.583,0], [7.667,0], [7.333,0]** under bit 0 for cues 0/1/2; and **[0,7.583], [0,7.667], [0,7.333]** under bit 1. Every intended DN therefore exceeded its matched no-message mean by >1 spike for every cue. The single globally calibrated decoder classified **12/12** held-out bit probes on **each** cue (bit-specific **6/6 and 6/6** for each cue), clearing the >=10/12 gate. Forced bits were crossed with every cue. The actual teacher emitted bit-0/bit-1 counts of **13/5, 0/18, 7/11** over the 18 probes for cues 0/1/2; these source bits were logged but did not select the forced receiving condition or enter the student controller.
- **RPS sensory representation.** The previously fixed three-MBON cue decoder recognized cue 0/1/2 on held-out repeats as **11/12, 12/12, 11/12** without a message; **12/12, 12/12, 11/12** with bit 0; and **12/12, 12/12, 11/12** with bit 1. Every bit/cue met >=10/12; no cue lost any recognition versus its matched no-message condition. The original three-MBON rates and unchanged contextual-bandit readout were recorded. Frozen actions changed on **0/12** held-out probes for every cue and bit compared with matched no-message probes. This supports low interference in this one reset-probe setting, not improved decisions or learning.
- **Engineered count-window limit.** The two numerical features were incremented only when the simulator's actual neurons 3141/3165 spiked during 240-280 ms, then made available at 520 ms. The student's ordinary `net.count` reset at 330 ms erased those earlier spikes: decision-time ordinary DN counts were **zero on all 108 held-out student probes**, while the separate stored spike-window counts carried the bit. The receiver is therefore an **engineered neural spike-count interface**, not persistent neural memory or a decoded teacher answer installed inside Fly B. The frozen three-feature controller did not consume the new counts, so this study demonstrates an available neural measurement, not learned use of it. DN-to-midbrain-to-MBON indirect paths remain despite absent direct DN-to-MBON connections; continuing-round behavior is untested.
- **Gates / conclusion / next experiment.** Physical reach, >=10/12 bit discrimination per cue, >=10/12 cue recognition under each bit, <=2/12 cue-recognition drop, and exact no-weight-update validity **all passed**. This technically justifies *designing* a separate exploratory teacher-student RPS learning-speed experiment with an opt-in hybrid controller consuming the two measured DN counts, fresh independent pairs, and correct/shuffled/untrained/disconnected communication controls. It does **not** establish teaching, faster learning, or an improvement in the original dopamine-only policy. The old KC receiver failure, original source action-leak failure, and contextual-bandit strict paired-gate failure remain unchanged. No student training or further receiver candidate was run.
- **Raw result / reproduction.** `data/rps_dnge175_receiver.json` preserves the locked design, all probe rows and teacher source values, raw DN spike-window counts, ordinary decision-time DN counts, MBON rates, frozen actions, decoder centroids/predictions, per-cue controls, and separate gate outcomes. `node --check tools/check_rps_dnge175_receiver.mjs` passed. To reproduce, run `node tools/check_rps_dnge175_receiver.mjs` in a copy without the artifact; the runner refuses to overwrite it.

### Prospective exploratory DNge175 hybrid RPS teaching-speed experiment (locked before student training, )

- **Hypothesis / scope.** The passed DNge175 receiver makes actual teacher-derived one-bit activity available as two engineered student neural spike-count features without disrupting the three existing MBON features. Test whether that activity accelerates reward-based learning of the observable deterministic RPS opponent. This is one exploratory **hybrid contextual-bandit** teaching experiment, not dopamine-only Fly Hero or proof of natural neural memory. The earlier source action-leak failure, KC receiver failures, and contextual-bandit paired-gate failure remain unchanged. No teacher weights, actions, correct moves, hidden strategy, or bit label enter Fly B's controller.
- **Fixed opt-in controller.** Keep the existing three MBON features `(log1p(rate)-featureMean)/featureStd` and its three zero-initialized action-specific linear Q heads, epsilon **0.20**, and selected-action reward-prediction update `Q_head weights += 0.03*(own reward - selected Q)*feature`; KC-to-MBON multipliers stay frozen at one while dopamine activity is simulated. Add only two features `count3141/7.388888888888889` and `count3165/7.388888888888889`, using actual 240-280 ms DN spikes. The common positive divisor is the mean stimulated count from **calibration repeats 0-5 only** in `data/rps_dnge175_receiver.json`; no mean subtraction or variance division. It uses no game labels or held-out outcomes. A two-number neural-spike counter is retained through the 330 ms ordinary count reset and supplied at the 520 ms decision. The original three-feature controller and game remain default and unchanged. All four arms share this exact five-feature architecture and normalization.
- **Independent fixed sample and source.** Predetermined fresh student seeds **741,742,743** (not found as student seeds in prior RPS scripts/docs), paired in order with archived independently trained contextual-bandit teachers **712,713,714**. All three saved teachers have final controller parameters, all-one neural multipliers, and archived 72-round held-out results (**65,63,65** wins). Use only these pairs; no seed or teacher replacement. Each teacher is reconstructed from its saved final controller and real connectome network, frozen and reward-free, to record actual MBON-based score-spread bits with the unchanged threshold **1.098235868125541** on the locked 680 ms schedule. The corresponding untrained controller has all-zero Q heads, hence emits bit 0 for every observed-cue trial when applied to the same real neural rates. First rounds have no previous cue or teacher message. The deterministic `previous_opponent`, offset 1, noise 0 task has no hidden next-move information beyond Fly B's observed previous move; only learning speed or retained policy improvement could count as teaching evidence.
- **Implementation/receiving validity before training.** Run one frozen smoke check on the three predetermined student seeds: for each seed/cue and 12 preselected reset streams `key=2026130400+100000*(studentSeed-741)+1000*cue+10*repeat`, compare no message, forced bit 0, forced bit 1 (**324 probes**). Each probe uses the exact 680 ms schedule, no reward, no training, and unchanged neural/controller weights. Verify two DN features equal counts from `net.spikes`, the five-feature vector has finite values, first three match the old MBON transform, source bit/target ID/opponent future move never enter the controller, and first-round no-message counts are actual measurements. The prior fixed cue decoder must score **>=10/12 per cue and bit and lose <=2/12 versus no-message**, separately for each student; each intended DN must gain >=1 mean spike versus no message. Verify a matched no-message measurement, no neural reset at 280 ms, and no parameter change. Any failure stops before training; no rescaling or alternate receiver. Reproduction and result artifact are below.
- **Training and matched controls.** If validity passes, train exactly **12 episodes x 24 rounds** per student in four arms: **correct** trained-teacher bits, **shuffled** trained-teacher bits, **untrained** corresponding zero-Q-teacher bits, and **disconnected** no stimulation. Each episode uses opponent seed **41000+episodeIndex**. Teacher bits are pre-recorded once per teacher/episode from real simulated MBON activity and reused across arms. The shuffled arm uses a fixed Fisher-Yates shuffle of observed-cue rounds **1-23 within each episode**, retaining the exact episode bit distribution and leaving round 0 message-free; shuffle RNG seed `2026140400+1000*teacherSeed+episodeIndex`. All arms use the same student seed-specific all-one neural multipliers, zero five-feature Q weights/biases, 150 Hz VPN cue, DN 300 Hz/40 ms, 680 ms timeline, reward rule, and episode neural reset. For student seed `s`, episode key `e`, and round `r`, student network stream starts `rng(s*10000000+1000*e+r+2)`, action stream `rng(key+501)`, and cue-period network stream restarts `rng(key+3)` at 280 ms without resetting neural state. Student actions/rewards may diverge across arms, which can alter later within-episode neural state despite matched initial streams. No Fly A controller or KC-to-MBON weight is trained here; Fly B's selected action and own game reward alone update its Q head.
- **Frozen learning-speed checkpoints.** Immediately after training episodes **2,4,6** (1-indexed), copy each arm's controller and evaluate it with **communication disconnected**, exploration/learning off, on the same 24-round opponent sequence seed **52000**, with evaluation episode key **200** and the same keyed student random-stream formula. The primary early-speed metric for each student/arm is the **mean wins per 24** over those three frozen checkpoints. Correct training must exceed **each** matched shuffled, untrained, and disconnected arm by a **positive** mean for every student; report pairwise differences and cohort means regardless of pass/fail. This isolates acquired early policy quality from immediate message assistance.
- **Final evaluation / distinct gates.** After episode 12, freeze every arm and evaluate separately (A) **disconnected** and (B) under the **same fixed correct held-out trained-teacher bits** for all arms, each on three 24-round opponent sequences **51000-51002** and student stream episode keys **100-102**. Held-out teacher bits are pre-recorded with real trained Fly A activity under the same first-round rule. Report total wins, previous-move-state wins (expected 23/24/22), R/P/S choices on the 69 observed rounds, per-episode training wins, all checkpoint scores, actual DN counts, controller parameter changes, and neural-weight integrity. **Reliability** requires every correct-trained student under disconnected final evaluation to win **>=58/72**, win **>=70%** in each observed state, and select **each action >=12/69**. **Retention** requires every correct-trained student to beat **each** matched control by **>=4/72** under disconnected final evaluation. **Learning acceleration** requires the positive early-checkpoint mean difference against each control for every student as above. Report the three gates independently; complete exploratory teaching success requires all three plus validity. Evaluation with correct messages is descriptive immediate assistance and uses an identical schedule across arms; it cannot substitute for disconnected retention. Any failure remains a negative result; no retuning, second architecture, or follow-up experiment.
- **Planned artifact / reproduction.** Add one opt-in five-feature path to `RpsContextualBandit` and one diagnostic/training runner `tools/check_rps_dnge175_teaching.mjs`, preserving the default controller. Write only new `data/rps_dnge175_teaching.json` with overwrite refusal, including any validity-only stop and all raw per-trial controls/results. Planned command: `node tools/check_rps_dnge175_teaching.mjs`. No Git operations.

### DNge175 hybrid teaching-speed study stopped at frozen validity gate

- **Hypothesis / execution.** The prospectively fixed two-DN spike-count features might provide a measurable teacher signal while preserving each fresh student's three-MBON cue representation. Ran the one locked frozen smoke stage with students **741-743**, all three cues, 12 predetermined reset streams per cue, and matched no-message/forced-bit-0/forced-bit-1 conditions: **324 reward-free probes** plus one cue-free first-round probe per student. The five-feature contextual-bandit path was opt-in. No source teacher was simulated for training, no student was trained, and no learning-speed or held-out teaching result was obtained.
- **Physical signal and controls.** Every stimulated target gained at least **7.08 mean spikes** above matched no-message trials; no-message DN counts were **[0,0]**. The two controller inputs were the actual DN window-spike counts divided by the frozen calibration scale, and the first three inputs exactly matched the existing MBON transform. Probe counts, first-round handling, active cue, finite features, unchanged neural multipliers, and unchanged controller parameters all passed. No cue/bit lost more than **2/12** recognitions against its matched no-message control.
- **Fixed cue-recognition gate failed.** Under no message / bit 0 / bit 1, the prior fixed three-MBON cue decoder scored: student **741** cue 0 **9/12 / 9/12 / 9/12**, cue 1 **12/12 / 12/12 / 12/12**, cue 2 **12/12 / 12/12 / 12/12**; student **742** cue 0 **12/12 / 12/12 / 12/12**, cue 1 **11/12 / 11/12 / 11/12**, cue 2 **11/12 / 10/12 / 10/12**; student **743** cue 0 **10/12 / 9/12 / 9/12**, cue 1 **10/12 / 10/12 / 10/12**, cue 2 **12/12 / 12/12 / 12/12**. The locked requirement was **>=10/12 for every cue under each bit for every student**. Students 741 and 743 failed on cue 0. The identical 9/12 with and without stimulation for 741 shows this failure cannot be attributed solely to DN interference; the fixed decoder/representation on these streams was already below the floor. Student 743 lost 1/12 under stimulation, within the separate drop gate, but still missed the absolute floor.
- **Decision and limits.** `smokePassed=false`; the runner wrote `status=stopped_before_training` and exited before teacher recording, student training, checkpoint evaluation, or final evaluation. The earlier frozen receiver success on seed 712 remains valid, as do the older KC and source failures. This new cross-student result prevents a valid learning-speed claim under the locked protocol. No seed replacement, decoder refit, stimulation change, or follow-up experiment was performed. The opt-in five-feature code is reversible; the default three-feature controller and RPS game are unchanged. The actual data are in `data/rps_dnge175_teaching.json` (all probe rows, per-student controls, gates, and locked design). Reproduce in a copy without that artifact using `node tools/check_rps_dnge175_teaching.mjs`; overwrite is refused. `node --check js/rps.js` and `node --check tools/check_rps_dnge175_teaching.mjs` passed.

### Saved-probe paired DNge175 cue-recognition analysis

- **Hypothesis / scope.** The failed cue-0 floor might reflect low no-message recognition, additional DN-induced errors, or both. This is a read-only analysis of the **324 existing** cue probes in `data/rps_dnge175_teaching.json`; every student/cue/repeat has no-message, bit-0, and bit-1 rows with the same key. No simulation, refitting, retraining, threshold change, or teacher-student experiment occurred. The original `smokePassed=false` and stop-before-training result remain unchanged.
- **Paired recognition.** Each entry is correct/12 for none / bit 0 / bit 1; parentheses give newly incorrect matched repeats under either bit versus no message. Student **741**: cue 0 **9/9/9** (none; baseline wrong repeats 1,7,8), cue 1 **12/12/12**, cue 2 **12/12/12**. Student **742**: cue 0 **12/12/12**, cue 1 **11/11/11**, cue 2 **11/10/10** (new error repeat 8). Student **743**: cue 0 **10/9/9** (new error repeat 6), cue 1 **10/10/10**, cue 2 **12/12/12**. There were **no rescued errors**. Across the 108 matched `(student,cue,repeat)` groups, no-message recognition was **99/108**, and each forced bit scored **97/108**. Bit 0 and bit 1 caused the same two new errors, not four independent events. Baseline cue 0 for student 741 was already **9/12**, below the original absolute 10/12 floor; student 743 additionally crossed below the floor after a one-probe message-related loss. Student 742's one-probe cue-2 loss stayed above the floor. Every loss remained within the separate <=2/12 drop gate.
- **Measured neural changes.** Mean *paired* MBON-rate differences `[rock,paper,scissors]` (bit minus none), rounded to 0.001:

  | Student | Cue | Bit 0 | Bit 1 |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | 741 | 0 | [-0.012, +0.012, +0.018] | [-0.012, +0.012, +0.018] |
  | 741 | 1 | [0, 0, 0] | [0, 0, 0] |
  | 741 | 2 | [+0.018, -0.018, -0.101] | [+0.012, -0.018, -0.071] |
  | 742 | 0 | [-0.042, -0.006, 0] | [-0.042, -0.006, 0] |
  | 742 | 1 | [0, 0, 0] | [0, 0, 0] |
  | 742 | 2 | [-0.083, -0.065, -0.173] | [-0.083, -0.065, -0.173] |
  | 743 | 0 | [+0.054, +0.131, +0.054] | [+0.054, +0.131, +0.054] |
  | 743 | 1 | [0, 0, 0] | [0, 0, 0] |
  | 743 | 2 | [+0.006, -0.018, +0.012] | [-0.006, -0.060, -0.048] |

- **DN separation / decisions / integrity.** Every no-message row measured **[0,0]** DN window spikes. On **216/216** forced-bit rows, the intended DN count exceeded the other DN count; the intended count was at least **4** and the off-target count was always **0**. This is direct count-pattern separation on saved probes, not a newly trained decoder score or teaching evidence. The archived smoke used a frozen, zero-initialized five-feature contextual-bandit controller; all action scores were `[0,0,0]`, so it chose rock on every probe and had **0/108** paired action changes for each bit. This cannot test whether a *trained* three-feature readout would change decisions. The archived per-student integrity checks report unchanged controller parameters and all-one KC-to-MBON multipliers.
- **Conclusion / next experiment.** Both baseline sensory variability and small additional DN-associated perturbations are observed. The two new paired errors establish message-induced recognition changes on these controlled streams; they do not show a broad or bit-specific disruption. The earlier absolute 10/12 gate **still fails** for students 741 and 743, so teaching training remains unjustified under that protocol. One possible future, independently locked frozen replication would use a few new predetermined students with the same decoder and matched no/bit-0/bit-1 probes to estimate separately how often no-message recognition is below 10/12 and how often messages add errors. Do not select students by baseline or begin training; that replication was **not run**.
- **Reproduction.** `node tools/analyze_rps_dnge175_saved_probes.mjs` reads the existing raw JSON only, checks 324 complete matched probes, and prints per-student/cue paired outcomes, rate deltas, DN separation, action changes, and weight-integrity flags. No new raw result was written. `node --check tools/analyze_rps_dnge175_saved_probes.mjs` passed.

### Prospective two-student DNge175 hybrid teaching pilot (locked before new probes or training, )

- **Purpose and relationship to prior failures.** Test once whether the unchanged learned contextual-bandit teacher bit speeds Fly B's RPS learning through measured DN spikes. The previous three-student teaching-readiness gate **failed** and remains failed; its saved paired probes showed baseline variability and two message-associated flips. This is a new small exploratory pilot, not a revised result or confirmation. The original source action-leak gate also remains failed; action-related advice is permitted in this hybrid test. No claim about dopamine-only learning or biological spike persistence follows from this engineered window-count interface.
- **Predetermined sample and immutable interface.** Use one archived independently trained teacher **712** (saved final contextual-bandit parameters, all-one neural multipliers, archived 65/72 held-out wins) and fresh student seeds **751, 752**; no prior RPS use of those seeds was found before selection. Never replace a seed. Preserve the teacher bit `1{max(Q)-min(Q)>1.098235868125541}`, bit-0/bit-1 DNge175 targets **3141/3165**, **300 Hz from 240-280 ms**, 150 Hz student VPN cue **280-520 ms**, ordinary count reset **330 ms**, decision **520 ms**, no-learning/update point **620 ms**, and trial end **680 ms**. Teacher cue occupies **0-240 ms**. Student controller retains its three fixed standardized MBON features and appends only the two **actual** DN window-spike counts divided by the previously fixed calibration-only **7.388888888888889**. Three action-specific zero-initialized Q heads, alpha **0.03**, epsilon **0.20**, selected-action own-reward update, frozen KC-to-MBON weights, neural dynamics, and dopamine rule are unchanged. No raw bit, target ID, teacher action/scores, opponent rule, or correct answer enters the controller.
- **Conditional frozen receiving gate.** The earlier seed-712 receiver and 741-743 saved probes cannot verify new students, so run only the necessary frozen matched check first: each selected student, cue **0/1/2**, repeat **0-11**, conditions **no message/forced bit 0/forced bit 1** (**216 cue probes** plus one cue-free first-round probe per student). Key `2026150400+100000*(studentSeed-751)+1000*cue+10*repeat`; each matched triple has identical neural seed, initial all-one synapses, zero controller, and state reset. No reward/training. Apply the previously fixed `log1p` three-MBON cue decoder without refit. For **each student/cue**, require both driven DNs to gain >=1 mean spike versus no message, each bit's count to exceed the other DN count on >=10/12 probes, cue recognition >=10/12 under **each bit**, and each bit's cue recognition to lose <=2/12 versus matched no message. Report no-message accuracy and paired new/rescued errors regardless of result. Require 5 finite features with correct MBON transform and measured DN counts, no first-round message, intact weights and source/timing checks. Any failure stops **before teacher recording for training or student training**. These are prospective gates for this pilot; the previous failed gate is not recalculated or lowered.
- **Training and controls, only if receiving passes.** Exactly **12 episodes x 24 rounds** per student in four arms: correct trained-teacher bits, Fisher-Yates trial-shuffled trained bits within each episode's observed rounds 1-23 (same bit counts, first round null), corresponding untrained zero-Q-teacher bits, and disconnected/no stimulation. Use the same five-feature controller, zero initial controller weights, all-one neural weights, opponent `previous_opponent`, offset 1, noise 0, episode opponent seeds **41000-41011**, and exact reset/reward/schedule in every arm. Teacher activity is simulated once per training episode from saved trained teacher 712 and reused; the untrained zero-Q controller uses the same actual teacher MBON rates and emits zero. Shuffle RNG is `2026140400+1000*712+episodeIndex`. Student stream key is `studentSeed*10000000+1000*episodeKey+round`; network RNG starts at `key+2`, action RNG `key+501`, and cue-period network RNG restarts at `key+3` at 280 ms without resetting neural state. Teacher starts at `rng(712*10000000+1000*episodeKey+round+1)`. Different actions/rewards can cause later within-episode state divergence even with matched streams. Every arm updates only the selected Q head from its own action and reward; synaptic multipliers stay one.
- **Frozen checkpoints, held-out evaluation, and success.** After episodes **2,4,6**, evaluate each arm frozen and disconnected on the same 24-round opponent seed **52000**, episode key **200**. Primary early-speed metric is mean wins/24 across these three checkpoints; the correct arm must exceed **each** of the three controls for **both** students. After episode 12, separately evaluate every arm frozen (no exploration or learning) (i) disconnected and (ii) with the **same correct trained-teacher held-out bits** on three 24-round opponent sequences **51000-51002**, episode keys **100-102**. Report training curves, checkpoints, final wins, cue-specific wins, action frequencies, DN counts, controller changes, and KC-to-MBON integrity. Reliability: under **disconnected** final evaluation, each correct-trained student must achieve >=58/72 wins, >=70% in each observed cue, and >=12 selections of each action across 69 observed-cue rounds. Retention: correct-trained final disconnected wins must exceed **each** matched control by >=4/72 for **both** students. Complete pilot success requires receiving validity, early speed, reliability, and retention; report each separately. Correct-message evaluation describes immediate assistance, never substituted for disconnected learning comparisons. One or two positive students would remain preliminary and require independent confirmation. Any failed gate is preserved; no retuning or second run.
- **Reproduction and artifact.** Run `node tools/check_rps_dnge175_teaching.mjs pilot` once after static checks. The opt-in `pilot` mode writes only `data/rps_dnge175_teaching_pilot.json` with overwrite refusal, including a validity-only stop if applicable. The original command and existing `data/rps_dnge175_teaching.json` remain intact. No Git operations.

### Two-student DNge175 hybrid teaching pilot: valid, no learning-speed or retention advantage

- **Execution / validity.** Ran the separately locked `node tools/check_rps_dnge175_teaching.mjs pilot` **once** on teacher 712 and predetermined students 751/752. The prospectively fixed **216 cue probes plus two cue-free first-round probes** passed all implementation/receiving checks before any training. No-message / bit-0 / bit-1 cue recognitions were: student **751**, cue 0 **11/11/11**, cue 1 **11/11/11**, cue 2 **10/11/11**; student **752**, cue 0 **9/11/11**, cue 1 **11/11/11**, cue 2 **12/12/12**. Thus 752 had a low **no-message** cue-0 baseline, which was reported rather than used for seed selection; both message conditions met the separately locked 10/12 floor. No cue lost recognition in net count, but paired probes show that **751 cue 2 gained one new error (repeat 8) and rescued two (6,7)** under each bit; **752 cue 0 rescued two (7,10)** under each bit; other student/cue pairs had no recognition flips. Intended DN targets gained **6.83-7.92 mean spikes** versus no message and each intended count exceeded the other DN on >=10/12 probes per cue/bit. Five measured features, first-round no message, and exact no-weight-change checks passed. The prior 741/743 failed readiness result remains untouched.
- **Matched training and controls.** Each student completed the fixed **12 x 24** rounds in correct, shuffled, untrained-teacher, and disconnected arms. The source produced **162 bit-0 and 114 bit-1** values over 276 observed training rounds; shuffling changed **136/276** bit positions while preserving counts, so the shuffled arm was distinct. Untrained teacher emitted bit 0, and disconnected trials used actual no-stimulation DN measurements. For each student, all arms had the same zero five-feature Q initialization, all-one synaptic multipliers, opponent schedule, keyed streams, and four-arm reward rule; action-history differences could affect continuing neural state. The saved validity checks confirm matched cue/opponent sequences, source/message schedules, action-specific own-reward updates, frozen evaluations, unchanged teacher/neural weights, and nonzero student-controller changes. No raw bit or correct action entered the student controller.
- **Training curves and early frozen checkpoints.** Training wins in episodes **1-6 / 7-12** (each half has 144 rounds), followed by disconnected frozen checkpoint wins after episodes **2,4,6** and their mean per 24:

  | Student | Arm | Training wins halves | Checkpoints | Early mean |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | 751 | Correct | 90 / 108 | 17,18,20 | 18.33 |
  | 751 | Shuffled | 90 / 110 | 15,18,20 | 17.67 |
  | 751 | Untrained | 93 / 100 | 17,20,20 | 19.00 |
  | 751 | Disconnected | 92 / 113 | 16,18,19 | 17.67 |
  | 752 | Correct | 90 / 107 | 18,20,20 | 19.33 |
  | 752 | Shuffled | 98 / 107 | 18,20,20 | 19.33 |
  | 752 | Untrained | 88 / 108 | 16,18,20 | 18.00 |
  | 752 | Disconnected | 99 / 105 | 18,20,20 | 19.33 |

  Correct-minus-shuffled/untrained/disconnected early mean differences were **+0.67/-0.67/+0.67** for 751 and **0/+1.33/0** for 752. Correct training therefore failed the locked strictly-positive early advantage against every control in each student. The raw artifact preserves all 12 episode wins per arm and every frozen checkpoint trial.
- **Final frozen, matched evaluation.** Each evaluation used the same three 24-round held-out opponent sequences. The table gives disconnected wins, by-cue wins for previous moves **0/1/2** (denominators **23/24/22**), rock/paper/scissors selections across 69 observed rounds, and wins when *every* arm instead received the same correct teacher messages:

  | Student | Training arm | Disconnected wins | By cue | Actions R/P/S | Correct-message wins |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | 751 | Correct | 64/72 | 20/23,22/24,22/22 | 22/25/22 | 61/72 |
  | 751 | Shuffled | 65/72 | 19/23,24/24,22/22 | 24/26/19 | 64/72 |
  | 751 | Untrained | 65/72 | 19/23,24/24,22/22 | 24/26/19 | 66/72 |
  | 751 | Disconnected | 67/72 | 21/23,24/24,22/22 | 24/24/21 | 64/72 |
  | 752 | Correct | 63/72 | 18/23,23/24,22/22 | 23/27/19 | 59/72 |
  | 752 | Shuffled | 65/72 | 21/23,21/24,22/22 | 21/24/24 | 61/72 |
  | 752 | Untrained | 61/72 | 17/23,21/24,22/22 | 22/27/20 | 64/72 |
  | 752 | Disconnected | 62/72 | 20/23,20/24,22/22 | 20/25/24 | 62/72 |

  Under correct-message evaluation, the correct-trained arm's cue-specific wins were **23/23,17/24,21/22** for 751 and **20/23,17/24,22/22** for 752; its observed-round action counts were **18/21/30** and **17/25/27**. Thus adding messages at evaluation reduced that arm's total wins by **3** and **4**, driven mainly by cue-1 performance; this is a matched immediate condition comparison, not learned retention. All disconnected evaluation DN counts were zero; correct-message evaluation used measured nonzero DN spikes. Raw per-trial DN counts, rewards, weights, actions, and cues are saved.
- **Gates / conclusion.** Both correct-trained students passed the *individual* three-action reliability gate under disconnected evaluation: >=58/72 total, >=70% on each cue, >=12 choices of each action. **Learning speed failed** as above. **Retention failed:** correct-minus-shuffled/untrained/disconnected disconnected wins were **-1/-1/-3** for 751 and **-2/+2/+1** for 752, all below the required **+4 versus every control for both students**. Controller parameter-change norms were positive in all eight runs (~0.88-0.98); KC-to-MBON multipliers stayed at one. Full pilot success was **false**. The strongest observed limitation is lack of a reproducible early or retained advantage from *correct* teacher messages despite reliable DN reception and three-action learning; message-on evaluation also hurt cue 1 in the correct-trained arm. This does not establish a general harmful mechanism or justify independent teaching confirmation. Do not tune or extend this pilot. The original source action-leak failure, older receiver failures, and previous 741/743 gate failure remain valid.
- **Raw result / reproduction.** `data/rps_dnge175_teaching_pilot.json` is the write-once raw artifact with the locked design, all 218 frozen receiving rows, teacher recordings, 2 x 4 x 288 training rows, checkpoint/final rows, arm-specific controller changes, integrity checks, and gates. Reproduce in a copy **without** that artifact with `node tools/check_rps_dnge175_teaching.mjs pilot`; overwrite is refused. `node --check js/rps.js` and `node --check tools/check_rps_dnge175_teaching.mjs` passed before execution. No existing research artifact was modified and no Git operation was performed.

### Read-only review of pilot headroom and teacher-bit utility

- **Question / source / method.** Could fast independent learning or a redundant source bit explain why the *valid negative* DNge175 pilot found no correct-teacher speed or retention advantage? Read only `data/rps_dnge175_teaching_pilot.json` and the existing `js/rps.js` opponent/action definitions. No new simulation, retraining, code change, decoder fit, or Git operation. The earlier pilot's gates and failure remain unchanged. The teacher 712 recording is deterministic and identical in both student entries; source counts below use it **once**, not as two independent teacher samples.
- **Before training and frozen learning headroom.** No pretraining frozen checkpoint was saved. However, every arm's saved initial Q weights and biases are exactly zero, and frozen argmax selects action 0 (rock) for every input. The saved opponent sequences are independent of student action under `previous_opponent`, offset 1, noise 0. Therefore an initial frozen rock-only policy is **analytically inferred**, not measured: **8/24** wins on the checkpoint sequence and **24/72** on the final sequences. The episode-1 training wins are not a frozen baseline because exploration and reward updates occur within that episode. Frozen disconnected checkpoint wins after episodes 2/4/6 were:

  | Student | Correct | Shuffled | Untrained | Disconnected |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | 751 | 17,18,20 | 15,18,20 | 17,20,20 | 16,18,19 |
  | 752 | 18,20,20 | 18,20,20 | 16,18,20 | 18,20,20 |

  The numerical **58/72-equivalent** threshold on a 24-round check is >=20/24 (since 58/72 x 24 = 19.33). This is only a descriptive rate comparison, **not** the locked 72-round reliability gate or proof of generalization. Student 751 first reached 20/24 at episode 6 in correct and shuffled arms, at episode 4 in untrained, and not by episode 6 in disconnected. Student 752 reached it at episode 4 in correct, shuffled, and disconnected, and episode 6 in untrained. At episode 2 there was still 6-9 wins/24 of arithmetic headroom; by episode 4-6 most arms were at 20/24. The teacher was present from the first training round, so it is incorrect to say learning saturated *before* it could act. There are no frozen checkpoints at episode 0 or 1; a shorter-lived effect there cannot be evaluated. At **none** of the three saved checkpoints did correct training beat *all three* controls in either student. Thus the locked mean did not conceal a checkpoint-level all-control success.
- **Actual trained-teacher signal.** Across **276 observed training rounds** from teacher 712, bit 0/1 counts were **162/114**; adjacent observed-round bits changed **135/264** times within episodes. Conditional on previous-opponent cue, bit-0/bit-1 counts were **82/12** for cue 0 (94 trials), **37/51** for cue 1 (88), and **43/51** for cue 2 (94). Both bits occurred within the same cue in **7/12 cue-0 episodes** and **12/12 episodes for each of cues 1 and 2**. Held-out teacher messages had bit-0/bit-1 counts **22/1** on cue 0, **11/13** on cue 1, and **10/12** on cue 2. The bit is therefore **not a deterministic function of cue** in the saved recordings, though cue 0 strongly predicts bit 0. The trained controller emitted bit 1 where the untrained zero-Q controller always emitted bit 0; this is learning-dependent *controller* output, not learned neural synapses.
- **Message controls and task information.** Trial shuffling preserved **162/114** overall counts but changed **136/276** bit positions and changed cue-conditioned bit-0/bit-1 counts to **57/37**, **51/37**, **54/40** for cues 0/1/2. The untrained-teacher arm sent bit 0 on all 276 observed rounds; disconnected sent no message. Thus controls differ in experimentally relevant stimulus distribution as intended. The opponent's next move is exactly `(previousOpponent+1) mod 3`, so the correct winning action is fixed by Fly B's already available previous-move cue. Mathematically, the teacher bit contains **zero additional correct-action information conditional on a perfectly observed cue**, despite its within-cue variability. This does not make its neural feature numerically redundant when Fly B's cue encoding is noisy; the saved 752 no-message cue-0 check was only 9/12. The source bit may convey learned confidence or neural noise, but these data do not establish useful conditional decision information. As one cautionary observation, on cue-0 training rounds teacher 712's own argmax won **5/12** when its score-spread bit was 1 versus **77/82** when it was 0; a large spread was not uniformly reliable advice. The bit transmits neither action nor score directly, and this association does not prove why Fly B failed.
- **Conclusion / one alternative design, not implemented.** The task becomes easy for these independent controllers by the later saved checkpoints, leaving a short observable speed window, and its correct action is already determined by the student's cue. Yet the first saved checkpoint still had headroom, and the pilot does not isolate task ease from limited usefulness of this particular bit or possible readout effects. A *single* alternative RPS design worth **feasibility testing**, not assuming successful, is a **blocked deterministic offset reversal**: the opponent uses `(previous+1) mod 3` and later `(previous+2) mod 3`, with the switch fixed before data collection, no mapping label provided, and reward/opponent outcomes observable after each trial. The same cue then requires different actions across blocks; the fly must infer the current mapping from experience. Two independent fly brains retain the DNge175 receiver, unchanged five-feature contextual-bandit equation, and frozen KC-to-MBON weights. A trained teacher may adapt its existing controller to the switch and send only the unchanged score-spread bit. **The smallest next experiment is teacher-source feasibility only**: on prospectively fixed calibration/held-out reversal recordings, check whether that fixed bit distinguishes the two mapping blocks *conditional on each cue* and reflects teacher adaptation. If it fails, stop before student training. Only if it passes should a later, separately locked student comparison use fresh predetermined seeds, correct/shuffled/untrained/disconnected message training, matched frozen checkpoints before/after the switch, the same held-out messages for all arms, disconnected retention evaluation, and predeclared numerical source/speed/retention gates. This proposal does not claim the current bit can encode mapping; its unsigned score-spread may fail the source gate. No part was run or coded.
- **Read-only reproduction.** `node -e "const x=require('./data/rps_dnge175_teaching_pilot.json'); for(const s of x.students) for(const r of s.runs) console.log(s.seed,r.arm,r.checkpointRows.map(c=>c.wins),r.evaluations.disconnected.wins)"` prints the saved frozen trajectory and final wins. For source counts: `node -e "const x=require('./data/rps_dnge175_teaching_pilot.json'); const z=x.students[0].teacherTraining.flat().filter(r=>r.cue!==null); for(const c of [0,1,2]) console.log(c,[0,1].map(b=>z.filter(r=>r.cue===c&&r.bit===b).length))"`. Both commands read only the preserved pilot artifact.

### Prospective blocked RPS reversal teacher/source feasibility (locked before neural simulation, )

- **Hypothesis / scope.** In a task where the same previous-opponent cue requires different actions before and after a deterministic offset switch, an independently reward-trained contextual-bandit Fly A might adapt to the reversal and its **unchanged** one-bit score-spread signal might carry mapping information conditional on cue. This tests *teacher feasibility and source information only*; no Fly B receiving/training, student selection, or teaching claim. The valid but negative DNge175 teaching pilot, its failed speed/retention gates, and all prior source/receiver findings remain intact.
- **Task and causal state coverage.** Use existing `Opponent({strategy:'switch',offset:1,secondOffset:2,switchAfter:144,noise:0,seed:62000})` continuously for **12 x 24 = 288** training rounds. Mapping A holds for global rounds **0-143**, B for **144-287**. Opponent state is carried across episodes, while neural state is reset at each episode boundary as in `RpsEpisode`. The first round alone has no previous cue; the switch is **not announced** to the controller. Pure opponent-code verification before any neural simulation showed observed-cue counts **[47,48,48]** in A (plus one null) and **[48,48,48]** in B. Both offsets cycle through all three cue states, so no artificial schedule is added. Rewards and observed opponent moves follow each normal round; no mapping label or future move enters the fly.
- **Preselected teachers, fixed model, and streams.** Calibration teacher seed **761**; independent held-out teacher seeds **762,763**. They were not found as prior RPS teacher seeds before selection; none may be replaced. Each uses the existing real-wiring connectome, standard three standardized action-MBON rates from the archived calibration, zero-initialized three-head `RpsContextualBandit`, epsilon **0.20**, alpha **0.03**, original selected-action own-reward update, frozen KC-to-MBON multipliers, and original 400 ms teacher-only RPS timing. Teacher receives neither mapping identity nor an action target. Training neural RNG on each round uses `rng(seed*10000000+episodeIndex*1000+round+1)` and action RNG `rng(key+501)`, matching the existing contextual-bandit runner. No controller or learning parameter changes. At each 240 ms decision record real MBON rates, three Q scores, action, actual opponent move/reward, and the fixed bit `1{max(Q)-min(Q)>1.098235868125541}` *before* the reward update. The untrained zero-Q controller applied analytically to the same MBON features emits bit 0; its weights are not installed in the trained fly.
- **Teacher-adaptation gate, before source analysis.** After episode **6**, freeze the controller and evaluate it without exploration/learning for 24 rounds on a fresh Mapping-A opponent and separately a Mapping-B opponent, both seed **63000** and identical keyed neural streams; the B evaluation is **before any B training reward**. After episode **12**, freeze and evaluate on the same 24-round Mapping-B protocol. Evaluations use separate same-seed networks, preserve training state/weights, and record all cues, actions, wins, Q scores, bits, and weight integrity. For **each of the three predetermined teachers**, require A-after-6 >=**18/24** wins, B-after-12 >=**18/24** wins, and B-after-12 minus B-before-switch >=**8/24** wins. This establishes useful performance in A and rewarded adaptation in B. If any teacher fails, stop **before** fitting/scoring a mapping-bit decoder. Report all failures and do not alter block length, seeds, model, or gate.
- **Conditional fixed-bit information gate.** Only if adaptation passes, use calibration teacher **761**'s frozen A-after-6 and B-after-12 evaluation bits to fit a *per-cue analytical* binary-bit-to-mapping lookup by empirical conditional bit frequency with balanced mapping priors; ties predict A. No decoder enters either fly. Score it unchanged on independent held-out teacher **762/763** A-after-6 and B-after-12 recordings; Mapping A/B labels are analysis-only truth. For **each cue**, require pooled held-out **balanced** mapping accuracy >=**75%** and >=**10 percentage points** above a single preseeded cue-stratified shuffle baseline (shuffle fixed seed **2026110401**, permuting held-out bits across A/B within cue while preserving cue counts). Also require each held-out teacher's overall three-cue balanced accuracy >=**70%** and at least one bit-1 response in each teacher's A or B recordings. Compare to the untrained constant-zero source, whose balanced mapping accuracy is **50%** for each cue, and record all trained/untrained/shuffled bit distributions. These thresholds are prospective and apply only after adaptation. Within-cue variation alone is insufficient. A failed source gate stops: no DN receiving test or Fly B learning experiment.
- **Outputs and limits.** One write-once runner/artifact, `tools/check_rps_blocked_teacher_source.mjs` -> `data/rps_blocked_teacher_source.json`, with raw training and frozen evaluation rows, per-seed gate results, calibration lookup if reached, held-out confusion/accuracy, shuffled control, and reproduction command `node tools/check_rps_blocked_teacher_source.mjs` in a copy without the artifact. A pass would only justify *designing* a later controlled teacher-student reversal study, not teaching. No Git operations or model changes.

### Blocked RPS reversal stopped at teacher-adaptation gate

- **One run and validity.** Ran `node tools/check_rps_blocked_teacher_source.mjs` once on the three predeclared teachers. The existing offset-switch opponent visited all cues as planned (**47/48/48** in A plus one initial null; **48/48/48** in B), and all 12 x 24 reward-training rounds and three frozen 24-round evaluations per teacher completed. Exact reward/opponent rule, zero initialization, selected-action own-reward Q updates, 400 ms neural timing, unchanged all-one KC-to-MBON multipliers, and frozen evaluation parameter integrity passed for all seeds. No mapping label entered the controller. Training win curves per episode 1-12 are preserved in the raw artifact.
- **Frozen teacher adaptation (wins/24).** The cue-specific columns list wins/trials for cues 0/1/2; each frozen A or B evaluation has a cue-free first round, so denominators total 23 observed cues plus that round.

  | Teacher | A after 6 | A by cue | B before B rewards | B after 12 | Final B by cue | Gate |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | 761 calibration | 13/24 | 6/8, 0/8, 7/7 | 0/24 | 20/24 | 4/8, 7/7, 8/8 | A fails |
  | 762 held out | 14/24 | 7/8, 0/8, 7/7 | 0/24 | 16/24 | 0/8, 7/7, 8/8 | A and B fail |
  | 763 held out | 22/24 | 7/8, 8/8, 7/7 | 0/24 | 15/24 | 7/8, 0/7, 8/8 | B fails |

  The locked criteria were **A after 6 >=18/24**, **B after 12 >=18/24**, and **B improvement >=8/24** versus the immediate pre-B frozen controller, for **every** teacher. All three improved on B by at least 8, but no teacher passed all criteria: 761 and 762 missed A; 762 and 763 missed final B. The complete training win curves were **761: 6,15,14,12,13,12 | 1,4,3,13,14,16**; **762: 10,7,14,16,13,13 | 2,11,13,15,14,14**; **763: 12,15,18,18,17,21 | 1,4,10,9,11,14** (A | B episodes). These are exploratory training wins with action exploration, distinct from the frozen gate scores.
- **Stop and interpretation.** `adaptationPassed=false`, `status=stopped_after_teacher_adaptation`, `sourceStatus=not_evaluated`. As prospectively required, **no calibration bit-to-mapping lookup was fitted, no held-out source-information or shuffled/untrained bit comparison was scored, and no Fly B receiving/training experiment was run**. The raw teacher rows include scores and fixed-threshold bits collected during the adaptation measurement, but they were not used to rescue the failed gate. The obstacle observed here is incomplete, cue-specific teacher adaptation under the fixed six-episode blocks: different held-out teachers lost an entire cue under A or final B. The score-spread bit's mapping information remains **unknown** in this task. Do not infer that the bit failed, retune the training budget, or select the sole teacher that passed final B. The earlier valid negative teacher-student RPS pilot remains unchanged.
- **Raw data / reproduction.** `data/rps_blocked_teacher_source.json` stores locked design, all 864 training rows, 216 frozen evaluation rows, per-cue accuracies, curves, weight-integrity checks, adaptation gates, and the explicit source-not-evaluated stop. Reproduce in a copy without that artifact with `node tools/check_rps_blocked_teacher_source.mjs`; overwrite is refused. The runner syntax check passed. No Git operation, controller change, or other experiment followed.

### Read-only review of blocked reversal learning curves

- **Question / source.** Why did the three teachers fail the locked adaptation gate? Read only `data/rps_blocked_teacher_source.json`; no simulation, retraining, code change, or Git operation. The actual locked budget was **12 episodes total: six A and six B**, with reversal after round 144. The original failure and thresholds remain unchanged.
- **Learning curves.** Training wins per 24-round episode, with exploration on, were 761 **6,15,14,12,13,12 | 1,4,3,13,14,16**; 762 **10,7,14,16,13,13 | 2,11,13,15,14,14**; 763 **12,15,18,18,17,21 | 1,4,10,9,11,14** (A | B). Thus A learning was inconsistent; B training rose late for 761/763 but plateaued at 14 for 762. These training wins cannot be substituted for frozen accuracy. Frozen checks exist only after A episode 6 (A 13/24,14/24,22/24; immediate B 0/24 each) and after B episode 6 (20/24,16/24,15/24). There is no intermediate frozen B checkpoint.
- **Cue-specific action opportunities.** For A cue 1, where rock was correct, 761 and 762 chose rock only **1/48** and **4/48** training times; each scored **0/8** on that cue in frozen A evaluation. For B cue 0, where rock was correct, 762 chose rock **0/48** times, received zero rewarded correct-action updates on that cue, and scored **0/8** frozen. For B cue 1, where paper was correct, 763 chose rock/paper/scissors **40/1/7** times: only **one** paper reward opportunity, then **0/7** frozen cue-1 wins. Its A cue-1 choices had been **40/4/4**, with rock correct; the A-learned rock preference persisted after reversal. On B cue 1 its per-episode paper choices were **0,0,0,0,0,1**; the correct-action score margin stayed negative, with no sustained late recovery. All three actions occurred globally, but the required action was nearly absent for particular cues.
- **Parameter and causal limits.** Initial and final contextual-bandit parameters differ (L2 changes about **0.71, 0.79, 0.49** for 761/762/763); KC-to-MBON weights remained frozen. No parameter snapshot after A episode 6 exists, so A and B parameter changes cannot be separated. The saved rows support **state-specific action and reward-update starvation**, and show policy persistence for 763; they do not prove that A learning caused its B failure. Longer training might raise aggregate B wins for 761/763, but 762's plateau and zero correct B cue-0 selections, plus 763's persistently negative B cue-1 margin, do not support a reliable all-teacher rescue by extending the budget. Oscillatory update instability and insufficient time are unproven as the primary cause.
- **Conclusion / next step.** **Stop the current reversal-learning approach** under this unchanged controller and fixed protocol. There is no evidence-based reason to run a longer-budget pass attempt or a further stability diagnostic now. The one-bit source-information test remains unperformed because the original teacher-adaptation gate failed. Read-only reproduction: `node -e "const d=require('./data/rps_blocked_teacher_source.json'); for(const t of d.teachers) console.log(t.seed,t.curve.map(e=>e.wins),['A:1','B:0','B:1'].map(s=>{const [m,c]=s.split(':'); const r=t.trainingRows.filter(x=>x.mapping===m&&x.cue===+c); return s+':'+[0,1,2].map(a=>r.filter(x=>x.action===a).length).join('/')}))"`.

### One pairwise teacher-message candidate stops at calibration

- **Hypothesis / source.** A signed contrast between the trained teacher's paper and scissors action-value estimates might convey a relative preference that the old unsigned maximum-minus-minimum confidence bit discarded. Use only the existing contextual-bandit teacher's response to real three-MBON activity under the original deterministic previous-move RPS opponent. No controller, learning, neural pathway, or opponent changed. Archived calibration teacher **711** and independent reserved held-out teachers **712–714** are the same pre-existing, distinct source split. The saved calibration rows in `data/rps_hybrid_message_source.json` contain the actual teacher MBON rates and three Q scores. The untrained matched controller has zero Q scores. No new neural simulation was needed.
- **Single candidate.** `bit = 1{Q_paper > Q_scissors}` with a fixed **zero** comparison threshold and strict tie-to-zero rule. This is at most one bit and could use the existing DNge175 bit-0/bit-1 targets if source feasibility passed. It sends neither weights nor Q magnitudes, mapping identity, or an explicit three-way correct action. It is a separate candidate from the previously failed spread bit; the earlier source action-leak failure and valid negative teaching pilot remain unchanged. No other contrast or threshold was tried.
- **Teacher values before compression.** Teacher 711's mean trained Q vectors (rock/paper/scissors) were **[-0.028,-0.084,+0.631]** on cue 0, **[+0.762,-0.375,+0.465]** on cue 1, and **[-0.450,+0.877,-0.085]** on cue 2. Its argmax actions were scissors **23/23** on cue 0, rock **20/24** and scissors **4/24** on cue 1, and paper **22/22** on cue 2. Thus the full learned values vary within and across cues; the chosen pairwise ordering, however, is constant within each cue. The archived untrained controller's three Q values are exactly zero, with unchanged parameters.
- **Calibration feasibility / result.** A prerequisite for testing useful information beyond Fly B's observed cue is that the candidate not be a deterministic cue recoding in calibration. On teacher 711's **69** observed-cue recordings, cue-0 bit counts were **23/0**, cue-1 **24/0**, and cue-2 **0/22** (bit 0/1). Mean paper-minus-scissors contrasts were **-0.715, -0.840, +0.962**; ranges were respectively **[-1.185,-0.166]**, **[-1.584,-0.474]**, and **[+0.429,+1.341]**. Thus the bit agrees **69/69** with `1{previous move = 2}`. The untrained zero-Q source would emit bit 0 throughout, so the trained source uses both values, but its calibration behavior supplies no information beyond the perfectly observed cue and partly signals the cue's corresponding correct action. This candidate fails the nonredundancy prerequisite despite its training dependence and unsaturated pooled 47/22 bit count.
- **Stop / controls / limits.** Because a meaningful candidate was not justified by calibration, **no held-out teacher rows were scored for this new rule**, no shuffled-message comparison or quantitative held-out source gate was applied, and no DN receiving or student-training experiment was run. This is a calibration stop, **not** a held-out failure or a conclusion that every possible one-bit teacher signal is redundant. In the deterministic task, even a variable teacher bit cannot add next-move information conditional on a perfectly observed cue; its potential to help a noisy neural representation would require separate evidence. No new message candidate or threshold search follows in this session. Raw derived measurements: `data/rps_pairwise_teacher_source_calibration.json`; original Q/rate rows: `data/rps_hybrid_message_source.json`. Read-only reproduction: `node -e "const a=require('./data/rps_hybrid_message_source.json').calibration.rows; for(const c of [0,1,2]){const x=a.filter(r=>r.state===c),v=x.map(r=>r.q[1]-r.q[2]); console.log(c,x.length,v.filter(y=>y<=0).length,v.filter(y=>y>0).length,Math.min(...v),Math.max(...v))}"`.

### Prospective independent hidden-strategy RPS teacher gate (locked before training, )

- **Hypothesis and scope.** Independent reward training, with no A-to-B reversal, may produce reliable teachers for each of two deterministic opponent strategies. Mapping A is `(previous opponent move + 1) mod 3`; B is `+2 mod 3`. The same observed previous move requires different winning actions, and mapping identity is never an input. This is **teacher learning only**: no teacher message, DN receiving, or student training. The failed reversal adaptation, failed prior one-bit source candidates, and valid negative teaching pilot remain unchanged.
- **Preselected teachers and model.** Fresh, non-replaceable teacher seeds **781,782,783** for A and **784,785,786** for B; pre-run search of RPS artifact design seeds found none of these. Use the original real-wiring connectome and three standardized action-MBON rates, zero-initialized three-head `RpsContextualBandit`, fixed KC-to-MBON weights, alpha **0.03**, epsilon **0.20**, the ordinary selected-action reward-prediction update, original RPS rewards and **400 ms** rounds. No supervised targets, mapping input, controller change, or model selection. Each teacher trains on only its assigned mapping for exactly **12 episodes x 24 rounds**. Training opponent episode seeds are the existing **41000–41011**; each episode starts a fresh opponent and neural state. The neural RNG key is `seed*10000000 + episodeIndex*1000 + round + 1`; action RNG uses the same base key `+501`, matching the existing contextual-bandit runner.
- **Schedule verification and matched evaluation.** Pure `Opponent` code inspection, without neural simulation, found that the 12 training sequences visit cues 0/1/2 **94/88/94** times for A and **88/94/94** for B, plus 12 cue-free first rounds. The frozen held-out sequence seeds are **52000,52003,52004**, chosen before training because their initial moves are respectively **0,1,2**; under either mapping, the three 24-round sequences contain **23/23/23** observed cues plus three cue-free first rounds. All teachers use those same sequence seeds, per-round neural/action stream formula, and frozen `argmax` with exploration and learning disabled. Analytically, an untrained zero-Q controller always chooses rock and wins **24/72** on this balanced schedule; this is a baseline, not a simulated control.
- **Locked validity and success.** Verify every trained episode has the prescribed mapping, coverage, 24 valid reward outcomes, exact selected-head Q update, zero-initialized equal controller weights, unchanged all-one KC-to-MBON multipliers, and no frozen-evaluation controller change. Frozen evaluation must contain **72** rounds and **23** observations per cue. Each of **all six** teachers must achieve **>=58/72** held-out wins, **>=70% per cue** (at least **17/23** each), and **>=12** rock, paper, and scissors choices among **69** observed rounds. Report every seed, training curve, cue wins, action counts, controller change, and any failed criterion; no seed replacement, early outcome-based stop, extra episodes, or tuning. The overall teacher-feasibility gate passes only if every seed passes every validity and reliability rule. A pass would justify a separately designed one-bit source study, not establish teaching.
- **Planned write-once result / reproduction.** `node tools/check_rps_independent_teachers.mjs` writes `data/rps_independent_teachers.json` once and refuses overwrite. Training and frozen decision rows will retain essential rates, scores, actions, rewards, and cues; only one prospective execution. This Stage 1 protocol is recorded before training results are inspected.

Syntax and archived calibration checks passed. Every teacher completed 12 x 24 training rounds and three 24-round frozen held-out sequences; exact mapping/reward and selected-action update checks, prescribed cue coverage, zero controller initialization, all-one KC-to-MBON multipliers, and frozen controller integrity passed. The balanced held-out schedule gave 23 trials per observed cue plus three cue-free first rounds. Initial zero-Q argmax rock has an analytical 24/72-win baseline under either mapping; it was not separately simulated.
- **Frozen held-out result.** Cue columns are wins/23 for previous opponent moves 0/1/2; actions are rock/paper/scissors choices across the 69 observed rounds. Success required >=58/72, >=17/23 on **each** cue, and >=12 of **each** action for **every** teacher.

### Read-only Strategy B teacher-784 cue-1 boundary comparison

- **Question / sufficiency.** Use only `data/rps_independent_teachers.json` to compare failed teacher 784 with passing B teachers 785/786. The artifact contains each final controller's three Q-head weights/biases, the all-one frozen KC-to-MBON weight check, common feature normalization, exact evaluation streams, and every frozen trial's MBON rates, standardized features, Q scores, and action. Recomputed `f_j=(log1p(rate_j)-mean_j)/std_j`, `Q_a=b_a+W_a·f`, and argmax reproduced **all 72 frozen actions and scores for each B teacher** within 1e-10. Thus no neural replay or new learning run was needed; continuing neural state is represented by the saved trial features but not separately snapshotted.
- **Cue-1 measurements.** Under Mapping B, paper is correct. Standard deviations below are across the 23 saved frozen cue-1 trials; features are standardized rock/paper/scissors MBON rates. Margins are paper score minus the larger rock/scissors score. All eight of 784's wrong decisions selected rock.

  | Teacher | Mean MBON features ± SD, R/P/S | Mean Q scores ± SD, R/P/S | Paper margin mean ± SD | Paper choices |
  | --- | --- | --- | ---: | ---: |
  | 784 | −1.267±0.387 / −1.009±0.318 / −0.441±0.246 | +0.529±0.100 / +0.727±0.168 / −0.330±0.172 | +0.197±0.202 | 15/23 |
  | 785 | −1.238±0.363 / −0.949±0.347 / −0.367±0.265 | +0.416±0.135 / +0.808±0.177 / −0.387±0.180 | +0.393±0.269 | 21/23 |
  | 786 | −1.402±0.310 / −1.108±0.241 / −0.426±0.281 | +0.560±0.161 / +0.929±0.133 / −0.408±0.139 | +0.369±0.233 | 20/23 |

- **Error pattern and cross-controller control.** Teacher 784's eight wrong paper margins were **−0.136 to −0.003**, mean **−0.060**: wrong choices were marginal score reversals, not large rock advantages. Its wrong-trial mean first MBON feature was **−0.912**, versus **−1.457** on its correct trials; second-feature means were **−0.840** versus **−1.099**. The error-associated features overlap the correct range and indicate a nearby boundary under variable neural input. Their overall feature SDs are broadly comparable to passing teachers, with no clear exceptional cue-1 variability in 784. Applying each *unchanged* trained controller analytically to **784's same 23 saved feature vectors** yields paper on **15/23** (784), **22/23** (785), and **20/23** (786); 785/786 recover **7/8** and **5/8** of 784's wrong choices. This is an input-matched diagnostic, not a replacement controller or new teacher evaluation. Passing controllers' imperfect scores also preclude claiming the representation is noiseless.
- **Learning evidence / interpretation.** Teacher 784 chose rewarding paper on **72/94** cue-1 training rounds and did so **6/7** times in its final training episode; the runner verified its selected-action reward updates. Its final frozen cue-1 margin was nevertheless smaller and crossed zero more often than the passing controllers' margins. The data support a **weaker learned paper-versus-rock decision boundary** on usable, variable MBON features; they do not identify why that boundary resulted from this reward sequence, nor show that more training or a parameter change would fix it. No specific algorithmic correction is justified. If pursued separately, the smallest informative future experiment is a prospectively locked replication of the **unchanged** Strategy-B training protocol on predetermined independent teachers, recording the same frozen cue-1 margins to test whether this boundary failure recurs; it was not run here. The original **six-teacher gate remains failed**, and no source, receiver, or student experiment follows.
- **Read-only reproduction.** `node -e "const d=require('./data/rps_independent_teachers.json');const ts=d.teachers.filter(t=>t.mapping==='B');const x=ts[0].frozen.rows.filter(r=>r.cue===1);const q=(p,f)=>p.W.map((w,a)=>w.reduce((s,v,j)=>s+v*f[j],p.b[a]));for(const t of ts){const own=t.frozen.rows.filter(r=>r.cue===1),m=own.map(r=>r.scores[1]-Math.max(r.scores[0],r.scores[2])),cross=x.filter(r=>{const s=q(t.final,r.features);return s.indexOf(Math.max(...s))===1}).length;console.log(t.seed,own.filter(r=>r.action===1).length,m.reduce((s,v)=>s+v,0)/m.length,cross)}"`. Full per-trial inputs, scores, actions, and training rows remain in the original raw artifact; no result or code file was modified.

### Unchanged Strategy B teacher replication: all three pass

- **Execution and validity.** Ran the separately locked `node tools/check_rps_strategy_b_replication.mjs` once for exactly teachers **791,792,793**. A static comparison confirmed the new runner differs from the original independent-teacher runner only in the B-only seed group and write-once output/command identifiers. No teacher message, receiver, or student experiment ran. The earlier six-teacher result, including 784's failure, remains failed.
- **Frozen held-out results.** Every teacher cleared >=58/72 wins, >=17/23 on each cue, and >=12 observed-round choices of each action. Cue columns give wins for previous moves 0/1/2; action counts are rock/paper/scissors across 69 observed rounds. Paper-minus-rock margins are means over 23 cue-1 frozen trials; all cue-1 errors selected rock, none scissors.

  | Teacher | Wins/72 | Cue 0/1/2 | Actions R/P/S | Cue-1 paper−rock mean | Wrong rock on cue 1 | Gate |
  | --- | ---: | --- | --- | ---: | ---: | --- |

  | 792 | 65 | 21/23, 20/23, 23/23 | 24/20/25 | +0.329 | 3/23 | Pass |
  | 793 | 68 | 23/23, 21/23, 23/23 | 25/21/23 | +0.459 | 2/23 | Pass |

Frozen cue-1 paper-minus-rock means for prior B teachers **784/785/786** were **+0.197/+0.393/+0.369**, with **8/2/3** wrong-rock choices; the new teachers were **+0.299/+0.329/+0.459**, with **2/3/2**. Thus 784's broad near-boundary cue-1 failure did **not** recur in the three predetermined replication seeds. Errors still occurred: their wrong-choice rock-over-paper deficits averaged **0.370/0.117/0.085** for 791/792/793, with maxima **0.390/0.150/0.160**. Across the original and new B sets, **five of six** predetermined teachers passed; this is neither universal reliability nor a retroactive repair of the original failed all-six experiment. It is reasonable to **design** a separate exploratory one-bit source-information study with prospectively chosen calibration and held-out teachers, explicit handling of failed teacher 784, within-cue mapping information gates, and untrained/shuffled controls. It is **not** justified to claim teaching, select only favourable teachers without disclosure, or execute source/student experiments in this session.
