### Fixed teacher split for one-bit hidden-strategy source test (before source-rule selection, )

- **Purpose / records.** Inspect only saved final controllers, real-MBON frozen features, and Q scores from `data/rps_independent_teachers.json` and `data/rps_strategy_b_replication.json`; no retraining or neural replay is needed if the archived score/action path verifies. This is a source-information test only, distinct from the earlier valid negative teaching pilot and the still-failed six-teacher feasibility gate.
- **Fixed split.** Calibration teachers are **A: 781; B: 784,785,791**. Reserved independent source-test teachers are **A: 782,783; B: 786,792,793**. Teacher 784 remains in calibration despite missing its individual cue-1 reliability criterion. All nine teachers remain accounted for. Each has a saved final Q controller, unchanged KC-to-MBON weight checks, and 72 frozen trial rows with MBON rates, standardized features, predecision Q scores, and actual action; 23 rows per cue.
- **Prior-data boundary.** Some reserved teachers' held-out *policy* scores and cue-1 margins were inspected during the preceding reliability/784 analyses; they are not untouched with respect to general teacher performance. No one-bit fixed-strategy source rule was previously selected or scored using these reserved teachers. The new rule will be chosen solely from calibration rows and the already fixed opponent/action geometry. If that separation cannot be maintained, stop rather than claim independent source validation. The source analysis must not select teachers by their passing status or revise 784's failure.
- **Single source rule, chosen from task geometry and calibration only.** For an observed previous-move cue `c`, emit `bit = 1{Q_c > max(Q_a : a != c)}` from Fly A's **trained** three-head contextual-bandit response to its recorded real-MBON rates; ties emit 0. This reports whether Fly A's preferred action is the one matching its currently observed previous move. The comparison is fixed and cyclic across all cues; it receives no hidden strategy label, correct action, teacher weight transfer, or student outcome. It is action-related *policy advice*, not a direct three-action answer. The two fixed strategies differ in whether that relative preference should be learned, but the bit generator is not supplied either strategy. No other candidate, neuron choice, or threshold was tried. Applying the same rule to the zero-initialized untrained Q controller gives bit 0 for every cue.
- **Calibration observations.** On the 23 archived frozen trials per cue, calibration teacher **781 (A)** had bit-1 counts **2,0,0** for cues 0/1/2; **784 (B)** had **22,15,23**; **785 (B)** had **22,21,23**; and **791 (B)** had **20,21,23**. All Q scores recomputed exactly from saved final controller parameters and standardized MBON features; all archived actions matched argmax. Teacher 784 remains included and its weaker cue-1 response remains visible. These observations justify testing the one fixed rule, not treating calibration as independent proof.
- **Locked analytical decoder and source controls (before opening reserved source rows).** Fit a per-cue bit-to-strategy lookup on **781/784/785/791 only**: for each cue and bit, compare empirical `P(bit | A,cue)` against the pooled B calibration `P(bit | B,cue)` with equal mapping priors; predict the larger likelihood, ties to A. No decoder enters Fly A or B. Score frozen rows from **782/783 (A)** and **786/792/793 (B)** only after this lock, separately by cue and teacher. Cue-only mapping prediction has **50% balanced accuracy** because both mappings contribute exactly 23 observations per cue/teacher. The untrained controller's all-zero Q scores produce bit 0; score this source with the same lookup. One fixed shuffled control uses `rng(2026110402)` to Fisher-Yates-permute the held-out trained bits **within each cue across all reserved teachers**, preserving each cue's bit count while breaking teacher/mapping association; apply the unchanged calibration lookup.
- **Locked quantitative gates and stopping rule.** Require (1) valid saved score/feature/action reconstruction and unchanged neural/controller weights in frozen evaluation; (2) trained B bit 1 on **>=18/23** rows for **each cue of each held-out B teacher**, versus **0/23** from each matched zero-Q untrained source; (3) held-out **balanced mapping accuracy >=80% on each cue** with the calibration decoder, and at least **10 percentage points above both** cue-only 50% and the fixed shuffled-bit baseline on **each cue**; (4) **>=18/23** correct decoded bits for **each cue of each reserved teacher**; (5) both bit values comprise **>=20%** of pooled held-out rows **within every cue**, so the pooled source is not saturated or a fixed cue recoding. Teacher 784's calibration result and all nine teachers' distributions must be reported. Any failed gate stops this single source candidate; no rule, lookup, shuffle seed, or threshold adjustment. A pass would justify separately designing one frozen DNge175 receiving experiment, **not** teaching. Planned read-only source runner: `node tools/check_rps_hidden_strategy_source.mjs`, with new write-once artifact `data/rps_hidden_strategy_source.json`. The original failed six-teacher gate remains failed regardless.

### Saved-recording hidden-strategy bit source passes its locked gates

- **Execution / validity.** Ran `node tools/check_rps_hidden_strategy_source.mjs` once, with no neural simulation, retraining, receiver stimulation, or student run. All nine archived final controllers and 69 observed frozen trial rows per teacher were included. Three standardized MBON features recomputed from real recorded rates, all three Q scores recomputed from the unchanged trained controller, and argmax actions/rewards matched the archived rows within 1e-10. Frozen controller and all-one KC-to-MBON checks remained valid. The bit generator used only `Q` and the observed cue; hidden mapping labels entered only the external evaluator. The calibration-only maximum-likelihood lookup was **bit 0 → A, bit 1 → B on all three cues**.
- **Bit distribution.** Each cell is bit-1 count out of **23** frozen trials at cue 0/1/2; bit-0 count is `23 − bit1`. Teachers marked calibration fitted only the analytical lookup; the five held-out teachers did not affect it.

  | Teacher | Mapping | Source split | Bit 1 on cues 0 / 1 / 2 | Source decode correct total |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | ---: |
  | 781 | A | Calibration | 2 / 0 / 0 | 67/69 |
  | 784 | B | Calibration; failed prior cue-1 policy gate | 22 / **15** / 23 | 60/69 |
  | 785 | B | Calibration | 22 / 21 / 23 | 66/69 |
  | 791 | B | Calibration | 20 / 21 / 23 | 64/69 |
  | 782 | A | Held out | 2 / 0 / 0 | 67/69 |
  | 783 | A | Held out | 0 / 0 / 0 | 69/69 |
  | 786 | B | Held out | 22 / 20 / 23 | 65/69 |
  | 792 | B | Held out | 21 / 20 / 23 | 64/69 |
  | 793 | B | Held out | 23 / 21 / 23 | 67/69 |

- **Within-cue held-out controls and gates.** Held-out A/B denominators are **46/69 per cue**; balanced accuracy averages class-specific accuracy, preventing the three B teachers from dominating the two A teachers. For cue 0, trained correct counts were **44/46 A and 66/69 B**, balanced accuracy **95.7%**; cue 1 **46/46 and 61/69**, **94.2%**; cue 2 **46/46 and 69/69**, **100%**. Cue-only and matched untrained zero-Q bits both scored **50%** balanced on every cue. The one preseeded cue-stratified shuffled-bit control scored **52.2%, 43.5%, 52.9%**. Both trained bit values appeared within every cue, with pooled held-out bit-0/bit-1 counts **47/68, 54/61, 46/69**. Every held-out teacher/cue decoded at least **20/23** correctly, above the locked 18/23 floor. Each held-out B teacher produced bit 1 on at least **20/23** per cue, versus **0/23** from its zero-initialized Q controller. All locked source gates passed without threshold or lookup revision.
- **Meaning and limitations.** This is a **cue-relative learned-policy bit**: it reports whether Fly A's highest Q action is the observed previous move. It contains within-cue information about the two fixed strategies because differently trained controllers prefer different actions for the same cue. It is not a literal mapping label supplied to the message function, and no correct action, Q score, or weight is sent to Fly B. However, for this deliberately restricted two-strategy task it is close to an inferred strategy indicator when the teacher policy is correct. Teacher **784** remains the calibration exception: its B cue-1 bit was 1 on only **15/23** trials, reflecting the previously failed cue-1 policy gate; the source result must not be interpreted as uniformly reliable across all nine teachers. Reserved teachers were independent of this new bit-rule calibration but **not wholly unexamined for prior policy reliability**: passing B teachers had earlier reliability measurements. This limits generalization claims. The original six-teacher failure, earlier confidence-bit action-leak failure, and valid negative teaching pilot remain unchanged.
- **Decision / reproduction.** A separately locked **frozen DNge175 receiving test** of this bit is technically justified for later design; it has **not** been run, and source information alone is not learning acceleration or brain-to-brain teaching. The write-once raw artifact `data/rps_hidden_strategy_source.json` preserves all nine source-row sets, calibration lookup, teacher/cue distributions, every held-out trial, the fixed shuffle assignment, controls, and gates. Reproduce in a copy without the artifact using `node tools/check_rps_hidden_strategy_source.mjs` (overwrite refused). `node --check tools/check_rps_hidden_strategy_source.mjs` and saved-result gate checks passed. No Git operation occurred.

### Prospective frozen hidden-strategy DNge175 receiving test (locked before simulation, )

- **Hypothesis and scope.** The newly validated cue-relative policy bit can cross the already calibrated DNge175 pathway as actual spikes while the original three-MBON representation remains recognizable. This is a reward-free receiving test, not student teaching. The original six-teacher reliability gate and earlier valid negative teaching pilot remain failed.
- **Fixed source and receivers.** Use archived trained A teacher **782** and B teacher **786** (lowest-seed reserved source teachers in each mapping), with their exact saved final three-feature contextual-bandit parameters and original all-one KC-to-MBON weights. Source rule is strictly `Q[cue] > max(Q[other two])`, ties zero; the hidden mapping is for analysis only. Use fresh predetermined receiver seeds **811, 812**, all-one KC-to-MBON weights, and zero-initialized frozen five-feature contextual-bandit controllers. No seed replacement. Existing MBON standardization and DN divisor **7.388888888888889** remain fixed.
- **Matched probes and streams.** Each teacher processes cue **0/1/2** at 150 Hz during **0–240 ms**. For each receiver, cue and repeat **0–17**, run no-message, forced-bit-0, forced-bit-1 student probes under the same stream `key=2026160400+100000*(receiverSeed-811)+1000*cue+10*repeat`. Thus there are **108 source responses** and **324 student probes** (2 receivers × 3 cues × 18 repeats × 3 arms). Each student probe resets neural state at its start, retains state across 280 ms, drives only DN **3141/3165** at **300 Hz 240–280 ms**, drives the normal VPN cue at **150 Hz 280–520 ms**, resets ordinary counts at **330 ms**, measures a frozen decision at **520 ms**, performs no learning at **620 ms**, and ends at **680 ms**. Count actual DN spikes from `net.spikes` in a separate 240–280 ms buffer; feed only the two measured counts, divided by the fixed scale, beside the three MBON features. No bit, target identity, Q score, or strategy label enters Fly B's controller.
- **Calibration, evaluation, controls.** Fit one per-receiver nearest Euclidean centroid decoder on the two raw DN counts from forced-bit repeats **0–5**, pooled over cues: **18 probes per bit per receiver**. Freeze it. Bit discrimination uses forced-bit repeats **6–11**: **12 probes per cue per receiver**, six of each bit. MBON cue preservation uses the previously fixed three-MBON log1p centroid decoder, with no refit, on repeats **6–17**: **12 probes per cue, receiver, and arm**. For source-to-receiver mapping analysis, compute the actual teacher bit on all source probes, select the matching forced-bit measured-count row on held-out repeats **6–17**, decode it through the fixed DN decoder, then apply the prior calibration-only bit-to-mapping lookup (bit 0→A, bit 1→B). Report source-bit and received-bit mapping accuracy separately, balanced across A/B within each cue and receiver; chance and no-message cue-only baselines are **50%**. No-message and both forced bits are crossed with all cues independent of strategy.
- **Locked gates.** Validity requires exact probe counts/splits, unchanged source and receiver controller parameters and all neural weights, exact source rule and five-feature transform, measured spikes and schedule, and no reward or learning. For **every receiver and cue**, each stimulated target must gain **≥1 mean spike** relative to matched no-message probes; frozen bit decoding must be **≥10/12**; original MBON cue recognition under **each forced bit** must be **≥10/12** and lose **≤2/12** versus matched no-message. For each receiver and cue, source-to-receiver mapping accuracy must be **≥80% balanced** on the 12 A and 12 B held-out source responses, **≥10 percentage points above 50%**, and received bits must match actual source bits on **≥10/12 for each teacher**. Any failed gate fails the whole receiving experiment. Do not change source, decoder, stimulation, students, or criteria afterward. Planned write-once command: `node tools/check_rps_hidden_strategy_receiver.mjs`; output `data/rps_hidden_strategy_receiver.json`. A pass would justify designing one separate student-training pilot, not establish teaching.
- **Source stream clarification (before execution).** Teacher `rng(2026160400+1000*cue+10*repeat+teacherSeed*100000+1)` is independent of receiver seed. Student initial RNG is `rng(key+2)` and its cue-period RNG is `rng(key+3)` with the `key` above. The same source response is paired analytically with either receiver's matching forced-bit trial; there are 108 distinct source responses, not 216.

### Frozen hidden-strategy DNge175 receiving result: physical reception passes, end-to-end strategy gate fails

- **Execution and controls.** Ran the single write-once command `node tools/check_rps_hidden_strategy_receiver.mjs` after the above lock. Saved **108** trained-teacher source responses and **324** matched student probes. Calibration comprised 18 forced probes per bit per receiver at repeats 0–5; held-out bit tests used six per bit/cue/receiver at repeats 6–11; held-out cue and source-to-receiver tests used repeats 6–17. The previous fixed cue decoder and source lookup were unchanged. No reward, student training, teacher retraining, model edit, or second candidate was run. All source/receiver controller parameters and KC-to-MBON multipliers remained unchanged; five student features matched actual standardized MBON rates and measured DN counts divided by the fixed scale.
- **Physical channel and cue preservation.** Mean intended-target extra DN spikes versus matched no-message trials were, for receivers **811/812** respectively, cue 0 **8.25/7.92**, cue 1 **7.17/7.67**, cue 2 **7.50/7.50**; both bit targets had the same mean within each cue/receiver. Frozen DN decoding was **12/12 for every cue of both receivers**. Cue recognition from the original three MBON features, written **none / bit 0 / bit 1** out of 12, was: 811 cue 0 **8/10/10**, cue 1 **11/11/11**, cue 2 **11/12/12**; 812 cue 0 **11/11/11**, cue 1 **11/11/11**, cue 2 **12/12/12**. Thus all forced conditions met the absolute 10/12 floor and none lost recognition versus no message. No message-associated new errors occurred on matched held-out probes; 811 gained two correct cue-0 and one cue-2 recognitions under each bit. Mean paired MBON-rate deltas were small and are retained in the raw result. The cue-0 no-message baseline for 811 itself was 8/12, showing baseline variability without invalidating the predeclared forced-message cue gate.
- **Source versus transmission.** The fixed source bit decoded to A for A782 on **12/12** held-out probes at every cue. For B786 it decoded to B on **11/12, 5/12, 12/12** at cues 0/1/2. The measured DN counts reproduced the actual source bit on **12/12** for both teachers, every cue and receiver. Balanced mapping accuracy, both at Fly A's emitted bit and after Fly B's DN transmission, was **95.8%, 70.8%, 100%** at cues 0/1/2 for each receiver; cue-only/no-message strategy accuracy is **50%**. The cue-1 source bit in this isolated teacher-first probe regime was 1 on only **5/12** B786 held-out probes, whereas the earlier continuous-round archived source recording was 1 on **20/23**. Its mean cue-1 correct-Q margin changed from **+0.369** in those archived trials to **−0.070** here. The saved data show no bit loss through DN reception; they do not isolate whether trial-state continuity, random streams, or another difference caused the source change.
- **Decision and limits.** Gates for validity, physical reach, bit discrimination, cue preservation, and no weight changes **passed**. The predeclared **≥80% balanced strategy-information gate failed at cue 1 (70.8%)** for both receivers, so the overall receiving experiment **failed**. The zero-initialized frozen five-feature student scores stayed zero and all actions stayed rock; no action changed under messages. This establishes an available engineered neural spike-count input, not a learned use of it or persistent neural memory. The separate student-training pilot is **not justified under this locked end-to-end gate**. Preserve the earlier passed source-information result on its own archived protocol, the failed original six-teacher gate, and the valid negative teaching pilot. No stimulation, source rule, decoder, or gate was retuned. Raw write-once result: `data/rps_hidden_strategy_receiver.json` (all rows, centroids, paired measurements, and gates). Reproduction in a copy without that file: `node tools/check_rps_hidden_strategy_receiver.mjs`; overwrite is refused. `node --check tools/check_rps_hidden_strategy_receiver.mjs` passed.

### Prospective B786 cue-1 source-context diagnostic (locked before new replay, )

- **Hypothesis.** The weaker B786 bit in isolated teacher-first probes may arise because each probe starts from reset neural state, whereas the original 24-round frozen source recordings carry neural state forward across rounds. The old and new protocols both use a 150 Hz cue during 0–240 ms, reset ordinary counts at 50 ms, decide at 240 ms, and use the same trained Q parameters and MBON feature standardization. The original episode stimulates dopamine after each reward even with weight learning off, then continues neural dynamics; the isolated source probe has no preceding rounds. The original and receiving studies also use different RNG keys, so their saved 20/23 and 5/12 counts are **unmatched**.
- **Single controlled replay.** Use only trained Strategy-B teacher **786** and its saved final controller, with frozen all-one KC-to-MBON multipliers. Take the **23** archived frozen cue-1 rows in `data/rps_independent_teachers.json`. For each, identify its evaluation episode index `i` from sequence seeds **52000, 52003, 52004** and round `r`; use the exact original current-round neural stream `rng(786*10000000+(100+i)*1000+r+1)`. Replay the same cue 1 for 0–240 ms with the same 50 ms count reset, but reset neural state at the start of **each** replayed trial. Keep cue duration, RNG, feature normalization, controller, and strict source bit rule fixed. Compare this isolated replay with the *archived* original continuous-state row with the same episode/round/cue/key; original rows are saved measurements, not newly simulated. This changes one factor: preceding neural state/history. Also recalculate the saved receiving-study 5/12 from its own rows without rerunning it. Never describe those 12 receiving probes as paired with the 23 archive probes.
- **Measurements and interpretation rule.** Save per trial the three raw MBON rates, standardized features, Q scores, `Q_1−max(Q_0,Q_2)`, bit, current-round key, reset schedule, and initial-state condition for archived continuous, matched reset replay, and saved teacher-first probes. Verify archived features/Q/bit exactly against saved final parameters; verify zero weight changes. Summarize bit-positive counts, margin mean/min/median/max and sign flips for the **23 matched archive/replay pairs**; report the 12 receiving held-out rows separately. A material state-history effect is supported if reset replay flips at least **6/23** archive-positive margins to nonpositive and lowers the mean margin by at least **0.20** under identical current-round streams. Otherwise the single-factor cause remains unresolved; no alternate streams, windows, resets, or parameters will be tried. Planned write-once artifact `data/rps_teacher786_cue1_context.json`; reproduction command `node tools/analyze_rps_teacher786_cue1_context.mjs`. The failed receiving gate and original source pass remain unchanged.

### B786 cue-1 matched source-context result: prior-round neural state changes the bit

- **Execution and validity.** Ran the one locked frozen runner once on teacher **786** only. The 23 original continuous-state cue-1 archive rows and 23 new reset-before-cue rows share the **same cue, 150 Hz 0–240 ms drive, 50 ms count reset, exact current-round RNG keys, final trained controller, feature standardization, and strict bit equation**. Original features, three Q scores, argmax actions, and archived source bits recomputed within 1e-10. Teacher controller and all KC-to-MBON multipliers remained unchanged. The separate **12 saved teacher-first** receiving rows reproduced **5/12** bit 1 with recomputed Q values; they use different streams from the 23 matched archive pairs and are **not paired** with them.
- **Measured comparison.** Continuous archive: bit 1 **20/23**; correct-action margin `Q_1−max(Q_0,Q_2)` mean **+0.369**, median **+0.391**, range **−0.150 to +0.734**. Matched reset replay: bit 1 **1/23**; mean **−0.141**, median **−0.150**, range **−0.378 to +0.133**. **19/23** margins flipped from positive to nonpositive, none flipped in reverse, and the mean paired margin fell **0.510**. The saved unmatched teacher-first held-out rows had bit 1 **5/12**, mean margin **−0.070**, median **−0.013**, range **−0.481 to +0.133**. Both predeclared material-effect cutoffs (**≥6 flips, ≥0.20 margin drop**) were exceeded.
- **Neural features and score mechanism.** Mean MBON rates (three action groups) were **0.093/0.059/0.354** in continuous archive, **0.134/0.016/0.006** in matched reset replay, and **0.107/0.012/0.012** in saved teacher-first probes. Mean standardized third MBON feature fell from **−0.426** to **−1.169** in the matched reset replay. In all archived, reset, and teacher-first cue-1 rows, **rock Q was paper's strongest competitor**. Applying the *unchanged* trained Q weights to each matched feature change attributes **−0.491** of the **−0.510** mean paper-minus-rock margin shift to the third MBON feature (first and second features contribute **−0.078** and **+0.059**). Mean Q0/Q1/Q2 moved from **0.560/0.929/−0.408** to **0.994/0.852/−0.698**. The Q implementation and normalization matched exactly; the changed neural input, particularly its third feature, explains the score sign change under this controller.
- **Conclusion / next experiment.** The one-factor matched replay supports a **causal contribution of preceding neural state/history** to B786's cue-1 source failure under isolated receiving probes. The archived 24-round frozen evaluation resets neural state only at episode start and continues dynamics, including reward-driven dopamine stimulation with learning disabled, between trials. The teacher-first source probe resets neural state before every cue. That procedural change is sufficient to produce an even stronger bit loss on the same archived current-round streams; the **1/23** versus **5/12** difference between the two isolated sets remains unassigned because their streams and samples differ. The result does **not** isolate which element of prior history (prior cues, spiking state, reward stimulation, or their interaction) matters. If a separate study is desired, the smallest justified next test is one prospectively locked **frozen continuous-round teacher-first source check** on teacher 786 with the existing bit and schedule, measuring cue-1 margin/bit frequency before any student training; it was **not run**. Do not treat this diagnostic as a repair of the original failed **80%** receiving gate or as teaching evidence. Raw write-once per-trial result: `data/rps_teacher786_cue1_context.json`. Reproduce in a copy without it using `node tools/analyze_rps_teacher786_cue1_context.mjs` (overwrite refused); syntax and saved-result integrity checks passed. No Git operations.

### Prospective continuous-round teacher-first hidden-strategy source test (locked before simulation, )

- **Hypothesis and boundary.** Continuous Fly A neural state across full teacher-first rounds may preserve the trained cue-relative preference that failed under reset-before-each-cue probes. This tests the **unchanged source only**. The archived source pass, failed DN receiving gate, original failed six-teacher gate, and negative teaching pilot remain unchanged. These teachers have been characterized before; this is a protocol test, not a fresh independent-teacher replication.
- **Fixed teachers and split.** Reconstruct all nine trained contextual-bandit controllers and original all-one KC-to-MBON multipliers from `data/rps_independent_teachers.json` and `data/rps_strategy_b_replication.json`. Calibration roles remain **A781; B784,785,791**, including the known weak B784. Held-out roles remain **A782,783; B786,792,793**. No teacher is omitted, retrained, or replaced. Use the original fixed bit `1{Q_c > max(Q_other two)}`, strict tie to zero, with Q calculated by the saved controller from its own simulated three-MBON features. Hidden mapping identity is never an input to that calculation.
- **Schedule, trials, and streams.** Each teacher runs three frozen **24-round** sequences with original evaluation seeds **52000, 52003, 52004** and fixed Strategy A offset **1** or B offset **2**. The seeded initial opponent move is taken from the existing opponent utility; subsequent moves follow the fixed strategy, independent of Fly A's action. Round 0 has no previous-move cue or bit and supplies a cue-free **680 ms** history. Rounds 1–23 have the observed previous move as a 150 Hz VPN cue at **0–240 ms**, reset ordinary spike counts at **50 ms**, clear the cue and compute the bit at **240 ms**, then run the original spiking dynamics without external cue or dopamine through **680 ms**. Reset neural state **once at sequence start**, never between its rounds; ordinary counts may reset after each 240 ms measurement, with the same 50 ms decision-window reset every round. No reward is delivered and no controller/dopamine/KC-to-MBON update occurs. At each round start, reseed only neural random draws with `rng(teacherSeed*10000000+(100+sequenceIndex)*1000+round+1)`, matching the original source evaluation's **current-round** stream. Continue neural time/state through all 680 ms, including the 240–280 ms transmission-reserved window and remaining idle interval. Do **not** concatenate 240 ms cue windows. Each teacher contributes **69 observed rows, exactly 23 per cue**; all nine contribute **621** observed rows. No extra warm-up or history beyond the first cue-free round.
- **Decoder and controls fixed before new data.** Apply the existing calibration-only source lookup from `data/rps_hidden_strategy_source.json` unchanged: **bit 0→A, bit 1→B** for every cue. Report bit counts and decoded accuracy by teacher/cue, and pooled balanced A/B accuracy per cue using 46 held-out A and 69 held-out B observations. Cue-only and an untrained all-zero Q source each give **50% balanced** mapping accuracy and bit 0 for every cue. One fixed shuffled control permutes held-out trained bits **within each cue** across the five held-out teachers using Fisher–Yates `rng(2026110402)`, preserving cue-level bit counts but breaking teacher/mapping association; use the same unchanged lookup. Report cue-conditioned bit-1 fractions in rounds **1–8, 9–16, 17–23** (eight/eight/seven rows per cue/teacher) as descriptive temporal stability, with no retrospective threshold.
- **Locked gates and stop.** Require complete exact trial/cue/strategy coverage, finite real-MBON features and Q, unchanged saved controller and neural weights, original strict bit rule, and no reward/learning. Require every held-out B teacher to emit bit 1 on **≥18/23 per cue** versus zero from its untrained Q control; every held-out teacher to decode correctly on **≥18/23 per cue**; held-out **balanced mapping accuracy ≥80% on each cue**, at least **10 percentage points above both** cue-only 50% and the fixed shuffled-bit control; and both bit values to comprise **≥20%** of pooled held-out observations within **every cue**. All three cues must contain both strategies (46 A and 69 B rows per cue). Any failed gate stops before another receiving or student experiment. Planned write-once output `data/rps_hidden_strategy_continuous_source.json`; command `node tools/check_rps_hidden_strategy_continuous_source.mjs`. No setting, source rule, seed, or threshold will be adjusted afterward.

### Continuous-round teacher-first source result: all locked information gates pass

- **Execution and integrity.** Ran the above write-once frozen source runner once. All **nine** fixed teachers contributed three 24-round sequences (**69 observed source rows each; 621 total; exactly 23 per cue/teacher**) with a cue-free 680 ms first round, continuous neural state within each sequence, and idle dynamics through the full 680 ms between cue onsets. No teacher reward, dopamine drive, training, Fly B, or DN stimulation was simulated. The source bit used only cue and Q from actual MBON rates. All saved controller parameters and KC-to-MBON multipliers remained unchanged. The original calibration-only **0→A, 1→B** lookup was applied without refit. The earlier failed receiving gate was not rerun or revised.
- **Bit distributions.** Each cell is bit 1 out of 23 observed trials at cue 0/1/2; bit 0 is the complement. Every previously fixed teacher remains included.

  | Teacher | Mapping / split | Bit 1 at cues 0 / 1 / 2 |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | 781 | A calibration | 0 / 0 / 0 |
  | 784 | B calibration; previously weak cue 1 | 23 / **18** / 23 |
  | 785 | B calibration | 22 / 22 / 23 |
  | 791 | B calibration | 22 / 22 / 23 |
  | 782 | A held out | 1 / 0 / 0 |
  | 783 | A held out | 0 / 0 / 0 |
  | 786 | B held out | 20 / **19** / 23 |
  | 792 | B held out | 21 / 21 / 23 |
  | 793 | B held out | 21 / 21 / 23 |

- **Held-out discrimination and controls.** Cues **0/1/2**: class-correct counts were A **45/46, 46/46, 46/46** and B **62/69, 61/69, 69/69**. Balanced mapping accuracy was **93.84%, 94.20%, 100%**, compared with cue-only and zero-Q untrained source **50%** each, and the single fixed cue-stratified shuffle **50.36%, 43.48%, 52.90%**. Both bits appeared within every cue; pooled bit-0/bit-1 held-out counts were **52/63, 54/61, 46/69**, above the 20% per-bit floor. Every held-out teacher/cue decoded at least **19/23**, clearing 18/23. Each held-out B teacher emitted bit 1 at least **19/23** per cue, versus zero for the untrained zero-Q source. Both strategies had exactly 46 A and 69 B observations per cue. **All locked gates passed.**
- **History and comparison limits.** B786 cue-1 bit 1 recovered to **19/23** under the continuous 680 ms schedule, with mean preference margin **+0.253**. Its cue-1 early/middle/late bit-1 counts were **6/8, 8/8, 5/7**, so the source did not collapse later in the sequence. Its first cue-1 encounter in each of three sequences was bit 1 on **1/3**, versus **18/20** later cue-1 encounters; this is descriptive, because later rounds have different streams and prior activity. B784 remained weaker on cue 1 (**7/8, 7/8, 4/7** by phase), rather than being hidden by pooling. The reset-based receiving study's **5/12** B786 cue-1 source result and this **19/23** are **unmatched** streams and schedules, so their difference is not a paired causal estimate. The earlier matched reset diagnostic already established a material causal contribution of state carryover. This new run shows that meaningful cue-1 source information persists across 680 ms rounds even **without** prior reward or dopamine events; it cannot identify which part of prior cue-driven neural history matters.
- **Decision and next experiment.** The bit remains strategy-informative under this teacher-first continuous schedule using only causally available previous opponent moves and Fly A's frozen trained Q response; the hidden strategy label is used only by the evaluator/environment, never the message function. A **separate prospectively locked frozen DNge175 receiving experiment** with continuous teacher state is technically justified; it was **not run**. Source feasibility is neither Fly B reception under this new schedule nor neural teaching. Preserve the original passed archived source test, the failed reset-based receiving gate, the failed six-teacher policy gate, and the negative teaching pilot unchanged. Raw write-once artifact: `data/rps_hidden_strategy_continuous_source.json` (all 621 observed rows, cue-free rounds, temporal bins, controls, per-teacher and per-cue gates). Reproduce in a copy without it using `node tools/check_rps_hidden_strategy_continuous_source.mjs` (overwrite refused). `node --check tools/check_rps_hidden_strategy_continuous_source.mjs` and saved-result integrity checks passed; no Git operation.

### Continuous-source DNge175 frozen receiving result: all locked gates pass

- **Execution and integrity.** The first command attempt stopped at static preflight **before any neural probe or output** because the DN scale centroid was read from the later receiver result rather than the original DN calibration artifact. Corrected that artifact reference without changing the locked design, gates, parameters, or data; then ran the single frozen receiver simulation once. The runner verified all **621 saved continuous-state teacher source rows**, original trained Q parameters, strict bit rule, and 680 ms/one-reset-per-sequence source schedule, including B784. It simulated **522** matched full-680 ms receiver cue probes plus two cue-free first-round controls on fixed receiver seeds 811/812. First **18** repeats per receiver/cue/condition matched the earlier saved receiver's DN counts, MBON rates, features, scores, cue guesses, and actions **exactly**; repeats 18–28 followed the locked stream extension. All controllers and KC-to-MBON multipliers remained unchanged; no reward, dopamine update, or student/teacher training ran. The previous failed reset-based receiving gate remains failed.
- **Physical reception and cue preservation.** Both DN targets received actual stimulation: their respective mean target increases over matched no-message probes, for receivers **811/812** and cues **0/1/2**, were **8.25/7.92**, **7.17/7.67**, **7.50/7.50** spikes; bit 0 and bit 1 had the same mean in each receiver/cue. Off-target DN and no-message window counts were zero in all probes. Forced-bit decoding from **measured two-DN counts** was **12/12 per cue and receiver**. Across the **1,242** end-to-end teacher/cue/receiver pairings, the decoded bit matched the actual saved teacher bit **1,242/1,242**; every teacher/cue/receiver was **23/23**, above the locked 21/23 floor. Ordinary fixed three-MBON cue recognition, **none / bit 0 / bit 1** out of 12, was 811: cue 0 **8/10/10**, cue 1 **11/11/11**, cue 2 **11/12/12**; 812: cue 0 **11/11/11**, cue 1 **11/11/11**, cue 2 **12/12/12**. Every forced condition met 10/12, none lost a matched recognition, and zero new message-associated cue errors occurred. The lower 811 no-message cue-0 baseline (**8/12**) remains visible. Paired MBON-rate deltas and all zero-rate/action ties are saved.
- **Per-teacher received strategy accuracy.** Each cell is correct **out of 23** at cue 0/1/2 under the fixed bit-to-mapping lookup, **identical for receivers 811 and 812** because no source bit was lost. The first four teachers are calibration-role reports only; the last five define the held-out mapping gate.

  | Teacher | Strategy / role | Received mapping correct at cues 0 / 1 / 2 |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | 781 | A calibration | 23 / 23 / 23 |
  | 784 | B calibration; known weak cue 1 | 23 / **18** / 23 |

  | 786 | B held out | 20 / **19** / 23 |
  | 792 | B held out | 21 / 21 / 23 |
  | 793 | B held out | 21 / 21 / 23 |

- **End-to-end information and controls.** With **46 A and 69 B** held-out source rows per cue, class-correct counts after actual DN decoding were cue 0 A **45/46**, B **62/69**; cue 1 A **46/46**, B **61/69**; cue 2 A **46/46**, B **69/69**. Balanced strategy discrimination was **93.84%, 94.20%, 100%** for **each receiver**, exactly matching the continuous Fly A source because DN bit recovery was perfect. Cue-only and untrained-zero-Q strategies give **50% balanced**. Teacher B786's cue-1 bit was **19/23** here versus **5/12** in the prior reset-per-probe source; those teacher trials have different streams/histories and are **unpaired**. The receiver's first 18 forced/no-message trials are exactly matched to the older result, so its physical/cue behavior did not change. The earlier failed study's **70.8%** end-to-end cue-1 gate is preserved as a valid result for its reset-source schedule.
- **Decision and limits.** **All predeclared receiving gates passed**: source/receiver validity, physical reach, 12/12 forced-bit decoding, ≥21/23 actual-bit recovery, cue preservation without loss, ≥80% balanced strategy information on each cue/receiver, and unchanged weights. This demonstrates an **engineered neural spike-count communication interface** carrying the trained teacher bit under this frozen continuous-source protocol. The separate 240–280 ms measurement buffer retains real DN spike counts after the ordinary count reset; it is not persistent neural memory. The zero-initialized frozen Fly B controllers scored zero and selected rock on **all 522** cue probes, so no learned or immediate action benefit was shown. Offline pairing of saved continuous teacher source rows with physically stimulated forced receiver branches is valid under the locked reset-per-probe receiver, but does **not** test a continuous Fly B learning trajectory. One **separately prospectively locked student-training pilot** is technically justified, not run here. Preserve the original source pass, reset-based receiver failure, six-teacher policy failure, and negative teaching pilot unchanged. Raw write-once result: `data/rps_hidden_strategy_continuous_receiver.json` (all source/receiver/transmission rows, centroids, controls, paired MBON effects, and gates). Reproduce in a copy without it: `node tools/check_rps_hidden_strategy_continuous_receiver.mjs` (overwrite refused). `node --check tools/check_rps_hidden_strategy_continuous_receiver.mjs` and saved-artifact gate checks passed. No Git operation.
### Prospective continuous-state hidden-strategy teaching pilot (locked before training, )

- **Hypothesis.** A pretrained Fly A's fixed cue-relative Q bit, transmitted as actual DNge175 spikes, accelerates Fly B's own reward learning on either deterministic hidden RPS strategy, with a benefit retained when messages are disconnected. The previous valid negative teaching pilot and failed receiving/teacher gates remain unchanged.
- **Fixed sample and source.** Archived frozen A teacher **783** and B teacher **793** were selected from the previously characterized held-out source set: their continuous-state source bits were respectively 0/23 and 21–23/23 bit-1 per cue, and B793 had 68/72 archived policy wins. This selection uses prior teacher results and limits generalization. Fresh consecutive student seeds **821, 822** face A (`offset=1`); **823, 824** face B (`offset=2`). A search of existing RPS records found no prior use of these student seeds. No replacement. Teacher final Q parameters and all-one KC→MBON multipliers are reconstructed from the existing archives; teachers do not learn in this pilot. The bit is exactly `1{Q_c > max(Q_other two)}`, tie 0, from real MBON activity and the observed cue, never a strategy label.
- **Task and communication.** Each arm runs **12×24** rounds, opponent seeds **41000–41011**, first round cue/message absent. Teacher neural state resets once per episode and carries across 680 ms rounds; cue 150 Hz at 0–240 ms, count reset 50 ms, source bit at 240 ms, then idle to 680 ms. Student DN 3141/3165 receives 300 Hz at 240–280 ms; ordinary VPN cue is 150 Hz at 280–520 ms, ordinary counts reset 330 ms, decision/reward 520 ms, unchanged controller update 620 ms. Only two *measured DN spike counts* divided by fixed 7.388888888888889 join the three original standardized MBON rates. Five-feature contextual-bandit alpha 0.03, epsilon 0.20, zero initial Q, and frozen KC→MBON multipliers are unchanged. Teacher/student cue and opponent sequences must match trial by trial. Neural/action streams follow the existing teaching runner: teacher `rng(teacherSeed×10^7+episodeKey×1000+round+1)`; student round key `studentSeed×10^7+episodeKey×1000+round`, network `rng(key+2)`, cue-period network `rng(key+3)`, action `rng(key+501)`.
- **Matched arms.** Correct uses the trained teacher bit. Shuffled uses one fixed `rng(2026110500+cue)` Fisher–Yates permutation of all observed training bits pooled across A and B *within each cue*, then returns bits to their original trial slots: this preserves pooled cue-specific counts while breaking strategy association. Untrained uses the corresponding zero-Q teacher's tie-to-zero bit through the same physical DN pathway. Disconnected has no DN pulse. All four arms use identical student initial states, neural/action random streams, opponent sequence, reward rule, architecture, and budget within seed. The shuffle must have at most **60%** balanced A/B bit-label accuracy per cue or the pilot stops as an invalid control; source bits must correctly indicate their assigned strategy on at least **80%** of each cue's training observations for both teachers, or stop before student training. Frozen teacher parameters and student synapses must remain unchanged; stimulated DN count must exceed the other target on at least **95%** of sent-message rounds per student/arm/cue. These technical checks are not teaching evidence.
- **Frozen checkpoints.** Before training and after episodes **2, 4, 6, 8, 12**, evaluate each arm twice on the same balanced **72-round** opponent seeds **52000, 52003, 52004**: first disconnected (primary), then with the common correct trained-teacher message schedule (secondary). Evaluation episode keys are 100–102 in both modes, with learning and exploration off; controller and neural weights must not change. All arms must have identical disconnected checkpoint-0 action/win sequences per seed. Track wins, each cue's wins, observed-cue rock/paper/scissors counts, DN counts, source bits, controller changes, and full training curves.
- **Locked teaching gates and ties.** For **each of all four students**, (1) early disconnected speed is mean wins/72 at checkpoints **2,4,6,8** at least **4/72 higher than each** shuffled, untrained, and disconnected control; (2) the correct arm reaches the reliability threshold at least **one checkpoint earlier than each control**, with never reaching counted as infinity and ties failing; (3) at final checkpoint 12, correct-arm disconnected wins are at least **4/72 above each control** and the correct arm meets reliability: **≥58/72** wins, **≥70%** wins on every observed cue, and **≥12** choices of every action across 69 observed rounds. Reliability threshold for (2) is that same three-part criterion. All technical validity checks must pass. The pilot passes only if all four students pass every gate; no average or favourable seed can mask a failure. Message-connected evaluations are diagnostic and cannot satisfy a disconnected gate. One positive pilot would need independent confirmation; no tuning, replacement, or confirmation follows a failure.
- **Planned single write-once run.** `node tools/check_rps_hidden_strategy_teaching_pilot.mjs` → `data/rps_hidden_strategy_teaching_pilot.json`. This is one exploratory pilot, not a retroactive revision of previous results.

### Continuous-state hidden-strategy teaching pilot: valid negative result

- **Execution and validity.** The single preset run completed all **4 students × 4 arms × 12 × 24 training rounds**, plus frozen 72-round disconnected and correct-message evaluations before training and after episodes **2/4/6/8/12**. The write-once raw artifact is `data/rps_hidden_strategy_teaching_pilot.json`. Its technical preflight and all per-student validity checks passed: teacher controller/neural weights stayed frozen; teacher/student opponent cues and moves aligned every round; student initial Q heads and disconnected checkpoint-0 action/win sequences matched within each seed; student KC→MBON weights stayed all one; controller weights changed under each learning arm; frozen evaluations were greedy and weight-invariant. New frozen A783/B793 evaluation source rows reproduced the matching archived continuous-source bits and Q scores on **69/69** observed rounds each (zero mismatches). Each 72-round held-out evaluation has 23 observations per cue, plus three cue-free first rounds. All four arms executed correctly.
- **Source, shuffle, and physical receipt.** Training correct-source bits indicated A on **91/94, 88/88, 94/94** cue-0/1/2 trials and B on **71/88, 88/94, 94/94**, clearing the locked 80% source floor (B cue 0 only narrowly). The one pooled cue-stratified shuffle's balanced strategy accuracies were **46.9%, 55.0%, 54.3%** on cues 0/1/2, within the locked ≤60% control bound. Zero-Q untrained teachers emitted bit 0 analytically from tied Q scores; those messages were still delivered through the real DN pulse and measured count buffer. For every student and cue in every stimulated training arm, the intended DN spike count exceeded the other target on **all** observed rounds, exceeding the 95% gate. For example, correct-arm mean counts at B823 cues 0/1/2 were **(1.52,5.98), (0.46,7.02), (0,7.50)** for DNs 3141/3165; A821 means were **(7.41,0.24), (7.56,0), (7.84,0)**. Disconnected counts stayed zero. These are physical reception and source checks, not evidence of beneficial learning.
- **Frozen checkpoint wins.** Each six-number sequence is wins out of 72 at **0/2/4/6/8/12 episodes**. `D` has communication disconnected and is the primary retained-learning measure; `C` uses the *same correct pretrained teacher* at evaluation for every training arm and is secondary. Final cue wins are out of 23 each, and R/P/S choices are counts across 69 observed rounds. Per-episode training wins, trial-level actions/rewards/features/DN counts, full controller parameters, and all frozen rows are retained in the raw artifact; training wins are not substituted for frozen scores.

- **Action-specific outcome and locked gates.** Correct-arm final disconnected observed-round wins/choices by R/P/S were **821: 19/19,23/27,20/23**; **822: 19/21,23/28,16/20**; **823: 22/26,19/19,23/24**; **824: 23/24,22/22,23/23**. Correct training increased disconnected wins from a matched **24/72** pretraining baseline to **63,59,65,69/72**, showing each student learned a policy from its own rewards. But early disconnected mean differences (correct minus shuffled/untrained/disconnected, units wins/72) were **821: −1.75/−1/+0.75**, **822: +0.25/+0.5/+0.25**, **823: −11.75/0/−7.5**, **824: −10.5/−2.25/−5**; none met +4 versus every control. First reliability-threshold checkpoints for correct/shuffled/untrained/disconnected were **821: 8/8/8/8**, **822: 6/6/6/6**, **823: 8/6/12/6**, **824: 12/6/12/8**: no correct arm reached it sooner than all controls. Final disconnected correct-minus-shuffled/untrained/disconnected differences were **821: −3/0/−3**, **822: 0/0/−5**, **823: 0/−3/0**, **824: +3/+1/+2**, all short of the locked +4 versus every control. A822 additionally failed final cue-0 reliability at **16/23** (<70%); the other three correct arms met individual three-action reliability. Consequently **every teaching gate failed at the four-student level**, though technical validity passed.
- **Interpretation, failure, and reproduction.** Correct-message connected final scores were **59,64,65,67/72** versus their own disconnected **63,59,65,69/72**: only A822 showed an isolated +5/72 immediate connected difference; no consistent immediate benefit and no retained teaching advantage. Strategy A's trained message was bit 0 on **273/276** training observations, nearly identical to the zero-Q untrained control's 276/276 zeros; this limits that within-strategy contrast. Strategy B supplied more discriminating bits, yet correct-message early learning was materially slower than shuffled and disconnected controls for both students. The pilot therefore supports **no measurable teaching benefit**, without establishing a specific causal reason for the failure. It does not revise the previous valid negative pilot or any earlier failed gate. The runner completed and wrote the raw JSON once, then a console-only `ReferenceError` occurred while printing its summary (`e` was out of scope). That reporting line was corrected *after* the saved result; no simulation was rerun and the artifact was audited read-only. `node --check tools/check_rps_hidden_strategy_teaching_pilot.mjs` validates the corrected runner. To reproduce, use `node tools/check_rps_hidden_strategy_teaching_pilot.mjs` in a copy **without** the write-once output file; the runner refuses overwrite. No independent confirmation or interface retuning was started.

### Saved-state diagnostic: did students use the received DN features?

- **Hypothesis and available evidence.** A message can be physically received yet provide little added decision value if Fly B's learned DN terms are weak relative to its three MBON terms, or if they shift scores without crossing an action boundary. Analyze only `data/rps_hidden_strategy_teaching_pilot.json`; preserve its valid negative teaching result and all locked gates. It contains zero initial and final trained controller parameters for all **16** arms, every three-MBON feature, two *measured* DN spike counts, frozen score/action, trial ID, and reward. **Intermediate checkpoint controller parameters and forced-bit-0/bit-1 DN counts for each held-out trial are not saved.** No simulator, student/teacher training, reward update, or parameter reconstruction from outcomes was run. The final saved Q scores and argmax actions were independently recomputed from final weights/features for **2,304/2,304** final frozen rows across all arms and both modes.
- **Counterfactual method.** For each correct-trained student, take all **69** observed final held-out *correct-message* rows. Keep that row's three recorded standardized MBON features and final controller fixed; compare its *actual* recorded DN counts with the matched *disconnected* row's recorded DN counts (empirically **[0,0]** on these rows). Recomputed actual scores/actions equal the archive, to 1e-10. Strategy/opponent outcomes are used only to score offline action correctness. This is an **analytical controller counterfactual**, not physical neural stimulation: a true disconnected replay can also change MBON rates and later neural state. On the same input rows, score each shuffled, untrained, and disconnected controller's final parameters to compare policy dependence fairly. Initial DN weights were zero in all arms; final nonzero values show learning over training, but the missing intermediate states preclude a DN-weight learning curve.

  | Student | Final MBON / DN coefficient L2 | Non-DN / DN mean action-score spread¹ | Held-out actual DN vs matched no-message action flips | Helped / harmed among flips | Controls' DN L2: shuffled / untrained / disconnected | Controls' flips on same inputs |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | A821 | 0.892 / 0.259 | 1.110 / 0.222 | 5/69 | 3 / 2 | 0.224 / 0.310 / 0 | 1 / 8 / 0 |
  | A822 | 0.834 / 0.301 | 0.972 / 0.148 | 1/69 | 1 / 0 | 0.280 / 0.314 / 0 | 0 / 1 / 0 |
  | B823 | 0.792 / 0.315 | 1.104 / 0.175 | 1/69 | 0 / 1 | 0.324 / 0.302 / 0 | 2 / 1 / 0 |
  | B824 | 0.807 / 0.290 | 1.047 / 0.060 | 1/69 | 1 / 0 | 0.315 / 0.329 / 0 | 1 / 0 / 0 |

  ¹ The non-DN spread includes MBON contributions **and learned action biases**; the DN spread is the range of the two DN feature contributions across three action heads. On the connected held-out rows, standardized MBON feature RMS values were approximately **0.83–1.06**; the active DN count divided by the fixed 7.3889 scale was about **1.0**, while the unused DN feature was near zero. Thus comparing these score spreads accounts for the actual feature scaling. The full 3×2 DN matrices and action scores are in the derived file.
- **Matched actual forced-bit subset.** The archived *training* arms supply both real bit-0 and bit-1 DN count vectors on **123/276** observed trial keys per A student and **260/276** per B student. Select only rows with both bits physically present, matching student, episode, round, cue, key, and opponent across arms. For each, hold the correct arm's recorded MBON vector and its **final** controller fixed; use its actual correct-teacher counts, the matched disconnected counts, bit-0 counts from correct or untrained arm, and bit-1 counts from correct or shuffled arm. Mean measured counts were about **(7.5,0)** for bit 0 and **(0,7.5)** for bit 1; no-message vectors were measured **(0,0)**. Bit-0 versus bit-1 changed actions on **5/123, 2/123, 18/260, 4/260** rows for 821–824. Correct winning-action counts under bit 0 versus bit 1 were **108/106**, **117/119**, **226/243**, **248/250**. B823's bit-1 advantage over bit 0 arose from **17** newly correct choices and no newly incorrect choices, but its no-message condition also had **243/260** wins: a learned *bit-0 penalty* is more evident than an added bit-1 benefit over silence. These are **in-sample analytical final-policy evaluations**; the forced-bit counts were generated in different training arms with potentially different prior neural histories, and final weights did not exist at those training times. They cannot substitute for matched held-out physical forced-bit trials.
- **Controls, early checkpoints, and conclusion.** All correct-arm DN coefficient matrices changed from zero, and the resulting score shifts were measurable. Shuffled and untrained training produced DN coefficient norms of similar size; disconnected training left DN coefficients exactly zero. Correct-message learning therefore did **not** yield a uniquely strong or reliably useful DN dependence. Final held-out fixed-MBON message-versus-silence effects were only **+1,+1,−1,+1** wins on the 69 observed rounds for 821–824. The original full connected-versus-disconnected final differences (**−4,+5,0,−2/72**) are different because MBON features and neural history can change. At early checkpoints 2/4/6/8, physically connected and disconnected frozen runs selected different actions on **821: 9/14/10/11**, **822: 18/10/10/10**, **823: 9/15/6/7**, **824: 12/8/8/6** of 69 observed rows, but MBON features differed on at least **61/69** matched rows at every such checkpoint; without intermediate W these differences cannot be assigned to the DN coefficients or said to have disappeared. The supported finding is **DN-dependent value learning with rare and mixed final held-out action effects**, plus no retained advantage in the original locked pilot. A plausible reason retention fails is that disconnected evaluation removes the learned DN term while cue-based MBON learning already solves each fixed strategy; the causal contribution to training is unresolved. Do not revise the negative pilot.
- **One narrow possible follow-up; not run.** A prospectively locked, reward-free **frozen matched forced-bit/no-message receiver probe** on the same four final student controllers, with common reset-per-probe cue streams and no training, could supply the missing same-student held-out physical 0/1/no-message count branches and test whether the final policies respond differently to controlled bit changes. It would be a receiving-sensitivity measurement under a *new reset protocol*, not a repair or repetition of the failed teaching pilot and not proof of retained teaching. No new student-learning run is justified by this diagnostic. Derived write-once measurements: `data/rps_hidden_strategy_dn_use.json`. Reproduce in a copy without that derived file using `node tools/analyze_rps_hidden_strategy_dn_use.mjs`; the script reads the pilot result, performs no simulation, and refuses overwrite. `node --check tools/analyze_rps_hidden_strategy_dn_use.mjs` passed. No Git operation.
### Prospective frozen matched forced-bit RPS receiver test (locked before new probes, )

- **Question and distinction.** The saved final correct-teacher student controllers 821–824 have nonzero DN-feature weights but rare final held-out message-dependent actions. Test whether controlled physical bit-0 versus bit-1 delivery changes frozen decisions helpfully on matched ordinary RPS inputs. This is a receiving-sensitivity diagnostic under a new reset-per-probe student protocol, **not** student learning or a revision of the valid negative teaching pilot. Do not train, reward, update, tune, or replace any controller, source, target, or student.
- **Archived teacher and student states.** Use only correct-arm final five-feature contextual-bandit W/b/baseline for A students **821,822** and B students **823,824** from `data/rps_hidden_strategy_teaching_pilot.json`; initial and final KC→MBON multipliers are all one. Reconstruct each student with `Network(subgraph,wiring(real,studentSeed))` and the unchanged MBON feature mean/std and DN divisor **7.388888888888889**. Teachers remain frozen **A783** and **B793**; reuse their already simulated continuous-state 680 ms teacher-first source rows from that same write-once pilot, generated after a single neural reset per 24-round episode and cue processing at 0–240 ms. Recompute `bit=1{Q_c>max(Q_other)}` from saved real-MBON Q scores, strict ties to zero, verify source controller integrity and ≥18/23 expected bits per cue. No mapping label or correct action enters Fly B.
- **Balanced matched probe set and streams.** Use the unchanged held-out opponent seeds **52000,52003,52004**, episode keys **100,101,102**, and their 69 observed teacher rows per strategy: **23 observations of each cue** per student (three cue-free first rounds excluded). For every student/sequence/round with observed cue, run exactly **three** reward-free frozen student probes: no message, forced bit 0, forced bit 1 (**4×69×3=828 probes**). Reset the student's neural state *before each probe* so all three branches start identically; preserve the existing 680 ms schedule. Student key is `studentSeed×10000000+episodeKey×1000+round`; set network `rng(key+2)` at round start, `rng(key+3)` at 280 ms, and controller `rng(key+501)` (exploration disabled). At 240–280 ms stimulate only DN **3141** for bit 0 or **3165** for bit 1 at **300 Hz**; no-message has no DN pulse. Drive the unchanged VPN cue **150 Hz at 280–520 ms**, reset ordinary counts at **330 ms**, read frozen action at **520 ms**, call no-learning update at **620 ms**, and end at **680 ms**. Count actual simulated DN 3141/3165 spikes during 240–280 ms in the existing separate buffer and pass *only these measured counts* plus three standardized MBON rates to the controller. No reward or dopamine event occurs. This reset-per-probe Fly B state differs from the original continuous-episode teaching evaluation; do not equate their action scores.
- **Analyses and locked criteria.** For each matched triplet, save actual MBON rates/features, measured DN counts, three Q scores, chosen action, top-two margin, fixed previous-move cue, teacher bit, and offline winning action. First report the **full physically simulated** decisions under all three conditions. Separately hold the three MBON features at that triplet's no-message values and rescore the same frozen controller using each condition's *actual measured* DN count vector; this is an analytical controller counterfactual, not a neural replay. Use the previously fixed three-MBON cue decoder without refitting. Technical validity requires exact triplets/streams and 23 probes per cue/condition/student; no controller/neural weight change; finite correctly transformed features; the intended target gaining ≥1 mean spike against matched no-message and exceeding the other DN count on **≥22/23** probes for every cue and bit; source bits correct for the assigned strategy on **≥18/23** per cue; and cue recognition under no message and each bit **≥19/23** per cue with **≤2/23** loss versus no message. Failures remain reported; no student is replaced.
- **Sensitivity and usefulness gates, before outcomes.** A student has meaningful frozen decision sensitivity only if **bit 0 versus bit 1 switches ≥7/69 actions** *and* the actual teacher-selected bit versus no message switches **≥4/69 actions** in the fixed-MBON analysis. Useful message selection additionally requires the teacher-selected branch to win at least **4/69 more** than both no-message and opposite-bit branches, with more helpful than harmful action changes against each. All **four** students must meet technical validity, sensitivity, and usefulness for an all-student pass; ties at an advantage boundary count only if the specified ≥4 numerical threshold is reached. Report each condition, cue, and student regardless. Full simulated decisions and fixed-MBON counterfactuals must be kept separate. A pass would establish frozen receiving sensitivity only, not a retained teaching effect or authorization for student retraining.
- **Planned one write-once command.** `node tools/check_rps_hidden_strategy_frozen_forced_bits.mjs` → `data/rps_hidden_strategy_frozen_forced_bits.json`. No other stimulation setting or run will follow an outcome.

### Frozen matched forced-bit receiver result: locked gates failed

- **Hypothesis and execution.** The saved correct-teacher-trained five-feature controllers might change decisions differently and helpfully when receiving the two actual DN spike patterns. The single locked run completed **828** reward-free, frozen student probes: four students × 69 observed held-out rounds × matched no-message/forced-0/forced-1 branches. Teachers A783/B793 were **not resimulated**; their continuous-state source bits and Q scores were verified against both archives. Student state was reset before each matched probe, so this is a new frozen receiving comparison, not a rerun of the continuous-episode teaching pilot. All controllers and KC→MBON multipliers remained unchanged. The original valid negative teaching result and every earlier failed gate remain unchanged.
- **Source, physical receipt, and cue representation.** Expected source bits occurred on **23/23 per cue** for A students and **21/23, 21/23, 23/23** for B students. Each stimulated DN gained **7.13–8.26 mean spikes** over no-message, and its count exceeded the other DN on **23/23** forced probes for every student/cue/bit. The fixed MBON decoder's cue-0 recognitions (no message / bit 0 / bit 1, each out of 23) were **821: 19/20/20**, **822: 20/18/18**, **823: 18/20/20**, **824: 18/15/15**. Cues 1 and 2 were **22–23/23** under every condition for every student. Thus cue preservation failed for **822** (18 below absolute 19 floor), **823** (no-message baseline 18), and **824** (baseline 18 plus three additional errors under either pulse, exceeding the allowed two-error drop). Student 821 alone met all technical gates. Physical delivery does not imply cue preservation or useful decisions.
- **Frozen decisions, isolating DN features.** The table holds each trial's **no-message three-MBON vector fixed** and substitutes only actual measured DN counts from the forced branches into the same frozen controller. `0/none` and `1/none` are action changes; `0↔1` is the between-bit count. Teacher helpful/harmful changes compare its actual bit with no message. DN spread is mean range of the three action-score contributions from teacher-selected DN counts, in score units. All counts are out of **69** observed rounds per student; offline winning actions were used only for scoring.

  | Student | 0/none | 1/none | 0↔1 | Teacher vs none: helpful/harmful | Wins none / teacher / opposite | DN score spread | Technical / sensitivity / usefulness |
  | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | --- |
  | A821 | 4 | 0 | 4 | 4/0 | 39 / 43 / 39 | 0.231 | pass / fail / pass |
  | A822 | 4 | 3 | 7 | 4/0 | 51 / 55 / 48 | 0.148 | fail / pass / pass |
  | B823 | 8 | 5 | 3 | 2/3 | 47 / 46 / 43 | 0.179 | fail / fail / fail |
  | B824 | 7 | 2 | 5 | 0/2 | 53 / 51 / 48 | 0.057 | fail / fail / fail |

- **Full physical neural replay, kept separate.** With each branch's own measured MBON rates and DN counts, wins under no message / bit 0 / bit 1 / actual teacher bit were **821: 39/45/42/45**, **822: 51/53/46/53**, **823: 47/45/48/48**, **824: 53/43/47/47**, each out of 69. Teacher versus silence changed **6, 6, 7, 6** physical actions respectively, helping/harming **6/0, 4/2, 4/3, 0/6**. These physical differences can include DN-associated changes in the ordinary MBON vector and must not be interpreted as isolated DN-count effects. Teacher versus opposite bit changed **3, 7, 3, 6** physical actions. The fixed-MBON analysis is the direct measure of controller sensitivity to the measured DN features.
- **Conclusion, controls, and limits.** The predeclared **all-four technical, sensitivity, and usefulness gates all failed**. Forced bits affected some actions, but no consistent between-bit sensitivity or useful teacher choice occurred across students. A821 and A822 had helpful isolated teacher-bit choices; B823 and B824 did not beat silence. The small DN score spread, especially B824's **0.057**, and mixed helpful/harmful switches support **modest score contribution plus inconsistent learned DN–action associations**; these frozen data do not identify why training produced them. A's teacher bit was nearly always 0 and B's nearly always 1, so this comparison cannot isolate more nuanced cue-dependent message content. The student reset-per-probe state differs from the earlier continuous evaluation, and an immediate frozen action benefit would not establish accelerated or retained learning. **No further student training is justified by this result.** The smallest possible future test, if a distinct hypothesis later motivates it, would first be a prospectively locked frozen receiver-compatibility and decision-sensitivity replication on independent, unselected students; none is launched here. The current hypothesis has failed the teaching pilot and this frozen sensitivity test, so no automatic continuation follows.
- **Raw result and reproduction.** Write-once `data/rps_hidden_strategy_frozen_forced_bits.json` retains all 276 matched triplets, 828 physical probe rows, analytical counterfactuals, cue outcomes, three scores and margins, measured DN counts, comparisons, and gates. In a copy **without** the result file, run `node tools/check_rps_hidden_strategy_frozen_forced_bits.mjs`; the runner refuses to overwrite the archive. `node --check tools/check_rps_hidden_strategy_frozen_forced_bits.mjs` passed, and a read-only saved-artifact check verified 828 probe rows, frozen weights, and the failed gates. No additional simulation, parameter change, or Git operation followed.

### Prospective offline five-feature information test (locked before fitting, )

- **Hypothesis and scope.** A simple supervised analytical readout may recover hidden-strategy and correct-action information from the five already measured neural features more accurately than from the three ordinary MBON features. This tests available information, **not** what the reward-trained Fly B controller learned. No neural simulation, teacher/student training, or parameter change will occur. The valid negative teaching pilot and failed 828-probe gate are preserved.
- **Archive audit and dataset.** `data/rps_hidden_strategy_teaching_pilot.json`, `data/rps_hidden_strategy_dn_use.json`, and `data/rps_hidden_strategy_frozen_forced_bits.json` contain three MBON features, two measured DN counts, cue, opponent/correct action, assigned strategy, student/teacher IDs, streams, and message conditions. They cannot support the primary independent strategy comparison because each student 821–824 saw only its assigned A or B strategy. Use **only** `data/rps_hidden_strategy_continuous_receiver.json` for model fitting and scoring: it contains archived continuous-state source rows for both mappings and frozen actual physical no/bit-0/bit-1 receiver rows for **both** seeds 811 and 812. Their source and receiver streams were recorded separately; pair each teacher's 23 observed rows **within each cue**, ordered by `(sequenceIndex,round)`, to frozen receiver repeats **6–28** in order. Choose the real forced branch indicated by the archived teacher Q bit, then use its three already standardized MBON features and two measured DN spike counts divided by the fixed **7.388888888888889**. This is an offline source/receiver pairing of real recordings, not a simultaneous teacher–student round. The hidden strategy and `(opponent+1) mod 3` winning action are used as **offline supervised labels/scoring only**, never as input features. No numerical bit enters the readout.
- **Fixed separation and leakage control.** Source teachers **781(A), 784(B), 785(B), 791(B)** are calibration; **782(A), 783(A), 786(B), 792(B), 793(B)** are held out. Fit once on receiver **811** with calibration teachers and score on receiver **812** with held-out teachers, then symmetrically fit on receiver 812 calibration and score on receiver 811 held-out. Each fold has **276** calibration and **345** held-out teacher events (69 per teacher); all three cues have 23 events/teacher. All rows of a teacher stay in one side of the partition, and the receiver seed also changes across fit/test. Repeated use of a forced receiver row by different teachers is grouped by `(receiverSeed,cue,repeat)` and never appears in both fit and test within a fold. Folds share the same teacher partition and are not independent replications. No hyperparameter, feature, or model choice follows held-out scores.
- **Locked models and controls.** Fit one deterministic **ridge linear classifier** separately for binary strategy and three-action winning move, with intercept, one-hot targets, class-balanced **strategy** sample weights (applied to both outcomes), and fixed L2 coefficient **1** on feature coefficients but not intercept. Solve weighted normal equations exactly; choose the largest of two/three scores (lowest index ties). Compare (A) three saved MBON features with (B) those same three plus the two fixed-scale *measured DN counts*, on exactly the same labels and split. No additional feature interactions or nonlinear model. Controls: cue-only predicts the calibration-majority mapping B and its cue-conditional winning action; an untrained zero-Q teacher selects the real forced-bit-0 receiver rows for both mappings; one predetermined `mulberry32(2026110505 + 100*fold + partition)` Fisher–Yates permutation of source bits **within each cue and partition**, then selects real forced receiver rows, preserving bit counts while breaking source–mapping pairing. Fit each control model on its own calibration rows and score its held-out rows. No teacher bit, cue ID, teacher ID, receiver ID, mapping label, or answer is a model feature.
- **Metrics and prospectively fixed criteria.** Report raw and mapping-balanced strategy accuracy, raw and mapping-balanced action accuracy, plus both outcomes by cue, held-out teacher, and receiver fold. Mapping-balanced accuracy averages A and B accuracy; cue-only is **50%** balanced even though the held-out teacher count is 2 A/3 B. **Strategy-information pass:** in both receiver folds the five-feature model reaches **≥80% mapping-balanced strategy accuracy**, beats MBON-only by **≥20 percentage points**, beats both shuffled and untrained five-feature controls by **≥20 points**, and reaches **≥75% balanced accuracy on each cue**. **Direct linear action-support pass:** in both folds the five-feature model reaches **≥60% mapping-balanced action accuracy**, improves over MBON-only and each five-feature control by **≥10 points**, and reaches **≥50% balanced accuracy on each cue**. Report either gate separately; do not reclassify a failed direct action gate as a success based on source-bit decoding. A linear score with no cue×message interaction may have limited capacity for the cyclic two-mapping action rule, so a direct-action failure would not prove that the five neural features lack information. The supervised decoder is an analytical probe, **never** installed inside Fly B.
- **Planned write-once analysis.** `node tools/analyze_rps_hidden_strategy_offline_information.mjs` → `data/rps_hidden_strategy_offline_information.json`; no simulation or new learning experiment follows.

### Offline five-feature information result: strategy passes, direct action fails

- **Execution and controls.** The single read-only script used exactly the locked archive pairing: **276 calibration events** from four teachers and one receiver, then **345 held-out events** from five other teachers and the other receiver, in each of the two receiver-fold directions. The same source events, labels, and partitions were used for MBON-only and MBON+measured-DN models. A fixed ridge linear classifier was solved once for each outcome and feature/control condition. No Fly A or Fly B simulation, controller update, reward, or change to older artifacts occurred. The previously failed teaching pilot and forced-bit gates remain failed. Raw and mapping-balanced scores, per-cue/per-teacher scores, coefficients, actual physical count features, source/message bits, and held-out predictions are in the new write-once result.
- **Held-out accuracy.** Each entry is **mapping-balanced accuracy**; the parenthesized number is raw accuracy over 345 held-out teacher events. The five-feature model receives only standardized MBON rates and measured DN counts, not the bit or label. Cue-only uses the calibration-majority B mapping and therefore scores 50% balanced but 60% raw on the 2-A/3-B held-out teacher set.

  | Calibration → held-out receiver | Input/control | Hidden strategy | Correct RPS action |
  | --- | --- | ---: | ---: |
  | 811 → 812 | Cue-only | 50.0% (60.0%) | 50.0% (60.0%) |
  | 811 → 812 | MBON only | 50.0% (48.7%) | 50.7% (49.6%) |
  | 811 → 812 | MBON + measured DN | **96.0% (95.4%)** | **49.3% (48.4%)** |
  | 811 → 812 | Shuffled teacher bits, same five features | 47.1% (48.4%) | 49.3% (48.4%) |
  | 811 → 812 | Untrained bit 0, same five features | 50.0% (53.9%) | 50.0% (49.3%) |
  | 812 → 811 | Cue-only | 50.0% (60.0%) | 50.0% (60.0%) |
  | 812 → 811 | MBON only | 50.7% (51.0%) | 50.0% (49.3%) |
  | 812 → 811 | MBON + measured DN | **96.0% (95.4%)** | **47.2% (47.2%)** |
  | 812 → 811 | Shuffled teacher bits, same five features | 54.0% (53.9%) | 49.5% (49.6%) |
  | 812 → 811 | Untrained bit 0, same five features | 50.0% (50.4%) | 49.3% (49.6%) |

- **Cue and group detail.** Five-feature strategy balanced accuracies on cues 0/1/2 were **93.8% / 94.2% / 100%** in both receiver folds; MBON-only was **50.0% / 50.0% / 50.0%** for 811→812 and **50.0% / 50.0% / 52.2%** for 812→811. Five-feature direct-action balanced accuracies were **50.0% / 41.3% / 56.5%** and **48.9% / 40.6% / 52.2%** respectively. Held-out strategy accuracy by teacher 782/783/786/792/793 was **98.6% / 100% / 89.9% / 94.2% / 94.2%** in each receiver fold; all were above chance, although teacher 786 was weaker. Direct-action accuracy by teacher ranged **44.9–53.6%** for held-out receiver 812 and **46.4–49.3%** for held-out receiver 811. There is no consistent action benefit across cues or teachers.
- **Locked decision and interpretation.** The **strategy-information gate passed** in both folds: 96.0% mapping-balanced strategy accuracy beat MBON-only by **45.3–46.0 points** and shuffled/untrained controls by at least **42.0 points**; every cue exceeded the 75% floor. The **direct linear action-support gate failed** in both folds: 49.3% and 47.2% balanced accuracy missed the 60% floor and did not beat MBON-only or controls. Real DN spike counts therefore contain recoverable mapping information beyond the ordinary MBON features, but this locked additive linear action readout does **not** turn it into correct three-action decisions. The earlier frozen student analysis likewise found nonzero DN weights with rare, mixed useful action changes; this offline result is consistent with a problem integrating message and cue for action selection, but cannot identify the reward-learning mechanism that caused the negative pilot.
- **Representational and sampling limits.** With MBON inputs held fixed, a linear action score adds a bit-dependent offset to each action. Perfect cyclic switching from A's correct actions **(2,0,1)** by cue to B's **(0,1,2)** would require offset differences `Δ0>Δ2`, `Δ1>Δ0`, and `Δ2>Δ1` simultaneously, which is impossible. Actual DN pulses can also slightly change MBON rates, so this is a limitation of the fixed-MBON additive case rather than a proof of impossibility for every physical row. Cue decoding is imperfect for some archived receiver probes, and the source/receiver trials are *offline paired*, not jointly simulated. Reusing the same receiver branches across teacher events reduces the effective physical sample size; the two fold directions also share teacher partitions. Prior source and receiver data were already characterized, so this is **not** untouched independent confirmation. Supervised correct-action and mapping labels were used only to fit/score this external analytical probe; they were never supplied to Fly B. High mapping decoding does not show that the reward-trained controller learned the message or retained a teaching advantage.
- **Next experiment, not run.** The smallest distinct test would be one prospectively locked, reward-free **offline** action-information analysis with a predeclared cue×DN interaction and independent teacher/receiver recordings, asking whether an interpretable readout can combine the two sources into the cyclic correct-action mapping. Do not install that decoder in Fly B or start another student-training study based on this result alone. Reproduce this analysis in a copy **without** the result file using `node tools/analyze_rps_hidden_strategy_offline_information.mjs`; the runner refuses overwrite. `node --check tools/analyze_rps_hidden_strategy_offline_information.mjs` and read-only artifact partition/prediction checks passed. No Git operation.

### Prospective offline MBON×DN action interaction test (locked before fitting, )
