- **Hypothesis.** The earlier additive three-action linear readout failed at **49.3% and 47.2% mapping-balanced** held-out accuracy despite **96.0%** strategy decoding because the cyclic RPS action rule requires cue-dependent use of the message. One fixed multiplicative MBON×DN feature expansion may allow an otherwise unchanged linear classifier to represent that interaction. This is a supervised **offline capacity test**, not a new student or teacher learning experiment; the original direct-action gate, failed teaching pilot, and failed forced-bit receiver gate remain failed.
- **Sufficient saved measurements and cue limitation.** Reuse only `data/rps_hidden_strategy_continuous_receiver.json` for fitting and scoring, with `data/rps_hidden_strategy_offline_information.json` as the exact prior baseline to reproduce before testing the new model; inspect `data/rps_hidden_strategy_frozen_forced_bits.json` for historical status only. The archive has all three standardized MBON features, both actual measured DN counts, source bits/Q scores, cue, mapping, and teacher/receiver identifiers. The fixed prior MBON cue decoder on receiver 811 repeats 6–28 recognized cue 0 on **17/23 without a message** and **19/23 under either forced bit**; receiver 812 had **21/23** in each condition. This pre-existing cue variability is a limitation; **no true cue label** is supplied to the classifier or substituted for the neural sensory features.
- **Exact partition and pairing.** Retain calibration teachers **781(A), 784(B), 785(B), 791(B)** and held-out teachers **782(A), 783(A), 786(B), 792(B), 793(B)**. Fold 1 fits receiver **811**, tests **812**; fold 2 fits **812**, tests **811**. In each fold, calibration has **276** events and held-out has **345**. For each teacher/cue, sort its 23 saved source rows by `(sequenceIndex,round)` and pair ordinally with the actual physical receiver repeats **6–28** for that cue; choose the measured forced-bit branch indicated by the teacher's archived cue-relative Q bit. The teacher and receiver recordings are offline paired, not simultaneous. No teacher or receiver appears on both sides of a fold. The folds share teacher partitions and are not independent confirmations. Hidden mapping, correct action, source bit, and cue IDs are **labels/analysis fields only**, never inference features.
- **One fixed feature change.** Baseline is the original **five features** `(m0,m1,m2,d0,d1)`, where `m` are the archive's fixed standardized MBON rates and `d` are actual DN counts divided by **7.388888888888889**. The sole new model appends exactly **six products** `(m0*d0,m1*d0,m2*d0,m0*d1,m1*d1,m2*d1)`. No true cue, bit, mapping, or correct action enters the feature vector. Fit a three-class one-hot **weighted ridge linear classifier** with the previous intercept, mapping-balanced sample weights, coefficient L2 **1**, exact normal-equation solver, and lowest-index argmax tie. No other feature, optimizer, threshold, or regularization trial. Reconstruct the prior three-MBON and five-feature linear baselines from the same calibration rows and verify that their held-out predictions and scores reproduce the saved result before computing new interaction scores.
- **Matched controls and locked success.** Apply the *same* interaction expansion, fit rule, teacher/receiver partition, and labels to (i) the previously fixed cue-stratified shuffled-source bits, permuted separately within calibration and held-out partitions with `mulberry32(2026110505 + 100*fold + partition)`, and (ii) untrained zero-Q bit-0 physical branches. Reuse the prior cue-only and MBON-only scores as references, with exact reconstruction check. Report raw and mapping-balanced held-out action accuracy; accuracies on each cue, each held-out teacher and receiver; confusion matrices; and coefficients including all six interaction terms. With each fitted model frozen, score **both** archived forced-bit branches on each held-out source event and report bit-0/bit-1 action switches plus teacher-selected versus opposite-bit correctness by cue and strategy; this is an offline counterfactual, not a new neural simulation. **Pass only if, in both receiver folds, the interaction model has ≥60% raw and mapping-balanced action accuracy, ≥10 percentage-point mapping-balanced improvement over each of the original five-feature linear, MBON-only, shuffled-interaction, and untrained-interaction models, and ≥50% mapping-balanced accuracy on every cue.** These are new prospective gates, not a revision of the earlier failed 60% direct-action gate. Report whether every cue improves even if the locked gate does not require it. Check weight/feature and split integrity before interpretation.
- **Planned write-once command and stopping rule.** `node tools/analyze_rps_hidden_strategy_interaction.mjs` → `data/rps_hidden_strategy_interaction.json`. If prior baseline reconstruction or archive integrity fails, stop without fitting an interaction readout. One run, no neural simulation, no student training, no model search. A pass establishes representational feasibility only and would justify designing a separate reward-learning feasibility study, not performing one now; a fail ends this line of investigation for this session.

### Offline MBON×DN interaction result: representational gate passed

- **Execution and validity.** The single locked read-only run used the same **276 calibration** and **345 held-out** source/receiver pairings in each 811→812 and 812→811 fold. In each fold, the earlier MBON-only and additive five-feature model coefficients, held-out predictions, and action metrics reproduced the archived prior diagnostic **exactly before** the interaction model was fitted. Teachers and receiver seed were disjoint across each fold's calibration/evaluation split; no held-out label, true cue, strategy, or teacher bit entered the feature vector. The saved three MBON features and two actual measured DN count features were transformed as predeclared, with no neural simulation or reward training. The original additive direct-action **60% gate remains failed**; the previous negative teaching pilot and failed frozen receiver gates are unchanged.
- **Held-out action results.** Each accuracy below is **mapping-balanced**; raw accuracy is in parentheses. Both receivers' held-out sets comprise 345 teacher events (2 A teachers and 3 B teachers). The cue-only calibration-majority B baseline is 50.0% balanced and 60.0% raw in each fold.

  | Calibration → held-out receiver | Three MBON | Original MBON+DN linear | Locked MBON×DN interaction | Shuffled-message interaction | Untrained-bit-0 interaction |
  | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
  | 811 → 812 | 50.7% (49.6%) | 49.3% (48.4%) | **93.1% (92.5%; 319/345)** | 45.3% (46.4%) | 50.0% (48.4%) |
  | 812 → 811 | 50.0% (49.3%) | 47.2% (47.2%) | **93.1% (92.5%; 319/345)** | 47.7% (48.1%) | 49.3% (49.0%) |

- **Cue and teacher detail.** Interaction mapping-balanced accuracy on cues **0/1/2** was **89.5% / 89.9% / 100%** with held-out receiver 812 and **85.1% / 94.2% / 100%** with held-out receiver 811. The original additive model scored **50.0% / 41.3% / 56.5%** and **48.9% / 40.6% / 52.2%**, respectively: **every cue improved**. Held-out teacher action accuracies for 782/783/786/792/793 were **95.7% / 97.1% / 87.0% / 91.3% / 91.3%** in both folds; B786 remained weakest but no teacher was excluded. Errors did **not** concentrate on cue 1 under the interaction model; cue 0 was lower, especially for receiver 811, whose fixed decoder earlier had a weak cue-0 baseline. Confusion matrices have true action rows and predicted action columns (rock/paper/scissors): held-out **812** `[[103,5,7],[8,104,3],[3,0,112]]`; held-out **811** `[[102,3,10],[8,107,0],[1,4,110]]`. The prior additive matrices and all per-teacher/cue rows remain in the write-once artifact.
- **Message-specific use.** The six fixed interaction coefficients had nonzero combined norms per action of **0.769/0.491/1.073** (811→812) and **0.919/0.606/1.242** (812→811), with every coefficient saved. Scoring the *same* held-out source events under both already recorded physical DN bit branches with the fitted model frozen changed the action on **345/345** and **340/345** events. Actual teacher-selected messages gave **319/345** correct actions in each fold; opposite-bit branches gave only **22/345** and **18/345**. By cue, teacher-selected wins were **102/115, 102/115, 115/115** and **97/115, 107/115, 115/115**. This supports learned *offline* cue-dependent use of measured DN information; the true source bit was used to choose a previously simulated physical branch, not supplied as a model input.
- **Locked decision and limits.** **Both prospective receiver-fold gates passed**: raw and balanced interaction accuracy exceeded 60%; balanced gains over the original five-feature linear, MBON-only, shuffled, and untrained controls were at least **42.4 percentage points** in every required comparison; every cue exceeded 50%. This strongly supports a **representational limitation of the original additive linear action readout for joint A/B decoding under these archived pairings**, because a single fixed six-product expansion made supervised correct-action decoding possible. The earlier teaching-pilot students each faced **only one fixed strategy**, so this joint-mapping capacity gap is **not established as the cause** of that pilot's failure. The result also does **not** prove that the existing reward-trained contextual-bandit student could discover those coefficients, that cue variability is absent, or that teacher-assisted training accelerates learning. Physical receiver branches were reused across teacher events and the two folds share the teacher partition; source and receiver recordings were offline paired rather than jointly simulated. Earlier source/receiver recordings were already characterized, so this is not an untouched independent confirmation. Preserve all earlier negative gates.
- **Next experiment, not run.** A separate prospectively locked **reward-only learning feasibility** study could test one opt-in contextual-bandit readout with exactly these fixed interaction features, matched correct/shuffled/untrained/disconnected message arms, independent teachers/students, and frozen connected/disconnected evaluations. The present supervised coefficients must **not** be installed in Fly B; this would test whether rewards can learn the mapping, a distinct question requiring its own design/authorization. No new student training occurred. Write-once artifact `data/rps_hidden_strategy_interaction.json` saves all models, confusion matrices, forced-bit comparisons, predictions, and gates. Reproduce in a copy **without** that file using `node tools/analyze_rps_hidden_strategy_interaction.mjs` (overwrite refused). `node --check tools/analyze_rps_hidden_strategy_interaction.mjs` and read-only artifact integrity checks passed. No Git operation.

### Mixed-strategy reward-only interaction pilot: stopped at teacher preflight

- **Question / intended experiment.** Could Fly B learn the previously fixed 11-feature `(m0,m1,m2,d0,d1,m0*d0,m1*d0,m2*d0,m0*d1,m1*d1,m2*d1)` contextual-bandit readout from its own RPS rewards on a mixed A/B schedule, compared with the original additive five-feature model and matched correct/shuffled/untrained/disconnected teacher conditions? The offline supervised interaction result passed at **93.1% mapping-balanced action accuracy** in both receiver folds, but did not establish reward learning. The present request required a nonprivileged, frozen teacher source before any student simulation or feature implementation.
- **Preflight and saved evidence.** The existing `RpsContextualBandit` in `js/rps.js` standardizes three real MBON rates, appends two measured DNge175 counts divided by **7.388888888888889** when its opt-in DN interface is used, chooses an action from three scores, and updates only the selected action's reward prediction with fixed alpha **0.03** and epsilon **0.20**; KC→MBON synapses stay frozen. The offline expansion adds six products in the fixed order above. No supervised coefficients may be transferred. Archived A teachers **781–783** were trained only on A; archived B teachers **784–786 and 791–793** only on B. The previously validated continuous source bits and physical receiver pairings combine those separate teacher groups **offline**. On a mixed online schedule, selecting the A or B teacher from the hidden active mapping would explicitly leak the mapping through teacher identity. The only archived single-teacher A→B reversal attempt, seeds **761–763**, failed its locked adaptation gate: frozen A-after-6 / B-after-12 wins were **13/24 and 20/24**, **14/24 and 16/24**, **22/24 and 15/24**; no teacher met the required **≥18/24 on both**, and the fixed cue-relative Q-bit source was **not evaluated** there. Earlier RPS switch artifacts used a different communication source and do not validate the present bit on this mixed task. There is no saved single frozen teacher demonstrated to infer and convey the active A/B mapping from experience.
- **Stop decision / controls.** The requested Stage-2 condition says to **stop if the mixed schedule would require an external controller to select an A or B teacher using the hidden strategy**. It does, given the available validated sources. Therefore no balanced mixed schedule, student seeds, learning budget, checkpoints, or success gates were locked; no opt-in readout was implemented; no teacher/student neural simulation or reward update was run. The proposed student arms cannot be meaningfully compared without a valid nonprivileged source. This is a **design-prerequisite failure, not a reward-learning failure**. The prior negative teaching pilot, failed frozen forced-bit gate, and successful offline supervised interaction result remain unchanged.
- **Next step, not run.** A separate prospective **single-teacher mixed-strategy source-feasibility** study would first need to establish that one frozen teacher, without a hidden-label input or teacher switching, can carry the locked cue-relative Q bit across both strategies under a causally valid observable history. Only a passing teacher source would justify designing the reward-only Fly B comparison. Write-once preflight record: `data/rps_hidden_strategy_interaction_reward_preflight.json`. Read-only reproduction: `node -e "const b=require('./data/rps_blocked_teacher_source.json'),a=require('./data/rps_independent_teachers.json'),r=require('./data/rps_strategy_b_replication.json'); console.log(b.adaptationPassed,b.sourceStatus,b.teachers.map(t=>[t.seed,t.after6A.wins,t.after12B.wins])); console.log(a.teachers.map(t=>[t.seed,t.mapping]),r.teachers.map(t=>[t.seed,t.mapping]));"`. No Git operation.

### Prospective single-teacher mixed-strategy source test (locked before simulation, )

- **Hypothesis and teacher status.** The same frozen, previously A-trained Fly A **783** may emit strategy-informative cue-relative Q bits under a mixed opponent sequence because ordinary opponent-move observations alter its continuous neural state. Its trained Q weights cannot adapt: **no teacher reward, dopamine event, exploration, or controller update** occurs. This test can establish only incidentally useful information from the fixed trained controller plus ongoing neural state, **not reward-based reversal adaptation**. Teacher 783 was chosen before any mixed outcome because its complete saved final state passed the independent A teacher test (**61/72**) and supplied the prior pilot's A source. The failed A→B adaptation gate of teachers 761–763 and the stopped student preflight remain valid. Teacher 783 sees only current observed previous opponent move as VPN cue; comparing consecutive observed cues could causally reveal the preceding transition, but no hidden strategy, switch time, trial number, or correct action is an input to its message function.
- **Locked two-sequence schedule and streams.** Use **one teacher seed 783** and two predetermined independent opponent initial seeds **53000** (calibration) and **53003** (held-out). Each sequence has four **24-round** blocks with neural state reset **only once at sequence start** and carried across all **96 × 680 ms** rounds. Calibration block mappings are **A/B/A/B**; held-out are **B/A/B/A**. Switches are before rounds 24, 48, and 72; opposing block orders counterbalance mapping against block position and elapsed time. The first round of each sequence has no prior-move cue or message; every later cue is the previous *observed* opponent move. The next opponent move is `(cue+1)%3` under A or `(cue+2)%3` under B, with initial moves **0** and **1** from the existing seeded Opponent. This strategy schedule is used by the opponent and offline evaluator only; Fly A receives no mapping ID. Pure opponent-schedule inspection before neural simulation found cue-0/1/2 counts **8/8/7** in the first block and **8/8/8** in each subsequent block in both sequences. Each mapping therefore has **47 or 48** observed trials per sequence and at least **15** observations of every cue. Teacher round key is `783*10000000+(200+sequenceIndex)*1000+round+1`, with `net.rand=rng(key)` at each round start; no exploration RNG is consumed.
- **Unchanged 680 ms source protocol.** Reconstruct the saved A783 final three-MBON contextual-bandit W/b/baseline from `data/rps_independent_teachers.json`, with unchanged standardized MBON features, real connectome wiring, all-one KC→MBON multipliers, and frozen controller. Drive the ordinary VPN cue at **150 Hz during 0–240 ms**, reset ordinary counts at **50 ms**, compute actual MBON rates, three Q scores, greedy action, and the locked bit `1{Q_c>max(Q_other two)}` at **240 ms** (strict ties zero), remove cue drive, and continue neural simulation to **680 ms** with no reward/dopamine. The teacher's bit depends only on its real simulated Q scores and observed cue. Use no DN receiver or Fly B. Archive every cue, mapping label for offline analysis, block/round/key, MBON rates/features, Q scores, action, bit, opponent move, and offline win.
- **Calibration, controls, and locked gates.** Fit **one per-cue two-bit lookup** on calibration sequence 53000 only: for each `(cue,bit)`, choose the majority A/B calibration label, ties to A. Require at least **4 of each bit per cue in calibration** so the lookup is estimable. Freeze it before scoring held-out 53003. Held-out must include both mappings with **≥15 observations per cue/mapping**. Source gate requires **≥80% mapping-balanced A/B decoding on each cue**, **≥10 percentage points above 50% cue-only** and above one fixed held-out within-cue shuffled-bit control, and **≥20 points above zero-Q untrained bit-0 control** on every cue. To require persistence across switches, the unchanged lookup must score **≥75% in each of the four held-out 24-round blocks** (first has 23 observed cues) and **≥75% in the last 12 observed rounds of each post-switch block**. Verify complete schedule, identical saved/final teacher controller, all-one synapses, real MBON/Q recomputation, and no reward/learning. The shuffle uses one `rng(2026110507)` Fisher–Yates permutation separately within each cue across held-out trials; it preserves cue-specific bit counts and breaks strategy pairing. Untrained zero-Q Q scores tie and emit bit 0 throughout; no new untrained neural simulation is required. Report bit distributions by cue/mapping/block, both controls, teacher offline action wins by block and around switches, and any failures. A high bit score alone cannot demonstrate adaptive learning.
- **Stopping / reproduction.** Run **once** via `node tools/check_rps_single_teacher_mixed_source.mjs` to a new write-once `data/rps_single_teacher_mixed_source.json`; the runner refuses overwrite. Fail any gate → stop without teacher replacement, source tuning, receiving test, or student training. A pass would justify **designing** a separate reward-only student experiment, not running one now. No earlier negative result changes.

### Single-teacher mixed-strategy source result: locked gate failed

- **Hypothesis and execution.** The one preselected A-trained teacher 783 might carry active-strategy information in its unchanged cue-relative Q bit through continuous neural state. The locked runner simulated one 96-round A/B/A/B calibration sequence and one independent-seed 96-round B/A/B/A held-out sequence, resetting neural state only between sequences. Each had 95 observed-cue messages. Teacher Q parameters and KC→MBON weights were identical before and after; no reward, dopamine update, teacher switching, DN receiver, or Fly B training occurred.
- **Source and controls.** The bit was **0 on all 190 observed-cue trials**, including every cue under both mappings in calibration and held-out data. For each held-out cue, the A/B distributions were **16/16 versus 16/16** for cues 0 and 1, and **16/16 versus 15/15** for cue 2, where each numerator is bit-0 count over mapping-specific trials; bit 1 occurred zero times. The predeclared per-cue calibration minimum of four examples of *each* bit failed. The calibration majority lookup therefore has no discriminative input; its held-out mapping-balanced accuracy was **50% on each cue**, equal to cue-only, the fixed within-cue shuffled-bit control, and the zero-Q untrained bit-0 control. The held-out coverage gate passed, but the ≥80% per-cue information gate and the ≥75% block/late-switch stability gates failed. Held-out block decoding was **7/23, 16/24, 8/24, 16/24** in B/A/B/A order; late-12 counts were **4/12, 8/12, 4/12, 8/12**. These figures are decoder scores, not teacher RPS wins.
- **Teacher behavior and interpretation.** With frozen A-trained weights, offline teacher action wins were **22/23, 0/24, 23/24, 0/24** in calibration A/B/A/B blocks and **0/23, 23/24, 0/24, 23/24** in held-out B/A/B/A blocks. Thus the teacher retained an A policy and did not adapt to B; adaptation was not being tested because no reward updates were allowed. The actual continuous-state Q bit supplied no within-cue hidden-strategy information. All integrity checks passed, but the **overall locked source gate failed**. This one frozen teacher cannot support the proposed mixed-strategy student teaching test. The separate archived A/B teachers' offline information results, earlier failed reversal test, valid negative teaching pilot, and failed receiving gates remain unchanged. No receiver or student run follows. A different source hypothesis would require a separately justified prospective study; none is launched here.
- **Raw data and reproduction.** Write-once `data/rps_single_teacher_mixed_source.json` stores the protocol, 190 trial rows, Q scores, bit distributions, controls, block results, and all gates. `node --check tools/check_rps_single_teacher_mixed_source.mjs` passed. In a copy without the result file, run `node tools/check_rps_single_teacher_mixed_source.mjs`; the runner refuses to overwrite the archive. A read-only saved-result check confirmed 95 rows per sequence, identical initial/final controller parameters, and failed gates. No Git operation.

### Read-only contextual-bandit reversal feasibility review

- **Hypothesis / scope.** The existing reward update might reverse an A-trained teacher's action preferences after a hidden A→B switch, but the archived failures may reflect too few correct-action samples on particular cues. Reviewed `js/rps.js`, `tools/check_rps_blocked_teacher_source.mjs`, `data/rps_blocked_teacher_source.json`, the independent-teacher findings, and the frozen single-teacher source result. No simulation, training, code change, or revised gate.
- **Algorithm.** During training `explore=true`; `epsilon=0.20` stays fixed. One uniform draw chooses each of the three actions with probability **0.20/3 = 1/15** from exploration, while the greedy action gets the other **0.80** (plus its exploratory share). Thus every action has a nonzero nominal floor, but only **3.2 exploratory choices are expected in 48 visits to one cue**; zero or one is possible in a finite run. With standardized MBON feature vector `f`, score `Q_a=b_a+W_a·f`, and own RPS reward `r∈{-1,0,1}`, only the *selected* head updates: `b_a←b_a+0.03(r−Q_a)` and `W_a←W_a+0.03(r−Q_a)f`. The other heads do not update on that trial. Obsolete values can be revised when their actions are selected, including old A winners that tie under B, but unselected B-winning actions receive no correction. This permits adaptation in principle; it supplies neither guaranteed sampling nor independent per-cue forgetting because features and weights are shared across cue inputs. KC→MBON weights remain frozen in this controller.
- **Archived evidence and limits.** The locked six-A/six-B study failed its all-teacher ≥18/24 A and B gates: frozen A/B scores were **13/20, 14/16, 22/15** for 761–763. Teacher **762** selected correct B rock **0/48** times on cue 0; teacher **763** selected correct B paper **1/48** times on cue 1 and retained A's rock action **40/48** times on that B cue. The archived selected-action reward counts on those failing 48-trial cue slices were **762 B cue 0: 0 wins, 31 ties, 17 losses** and **763 B cue 1: 1 win, 40 ties, 7 losses**. Their mean correct-action score margins stayed negative from the first to last 12 such cue visits (**−0.461→−0.349** and **−0.824→−0.475**, respectively). This directly supports cue-specific correct-action reward starvation and persistence of old preferences. It does not show that zero/one opportunities were caused by a structural exploration defect, neural-feature instability, insufficient time, or a particular parameter; no after-A or intermediate-B frozen parameter snapshot exists. Independent A and B teachers show each mapping can be learned from scratch, but their separate training does not establish reversal reliability. Frozen A-trained teacher 783's all-zero bit under 680 ms continuous state had no reward updates and cannot test reward-driven adaptation.
- **Conclusion / next experiment.** The existing learner is theoretically reversible, and all three archived teachers improved on B from 0/24, but the observed all-teacher failure is best explained at the measured level by **cue-specific scarcity of rewarded correct actions**, with old-policy persistence for 763. A longer unchanged run might help some cues, yet 762's plateau and the absence of reliable correct-action sampling give no specific evidence that a new unchanged-algorithm protocol will pass both policy and source-information gates. Continuous neural state would be a new protocol choice, not an established rescue. The previous read-only review's stop recommendation remains appropriate: **stop the current mixed-strategy route**; no prospective training experiment is proposed or run. The fixed cue-relative bit on a reward-adapting single teacher remains untested; the frozen single-teacher bit failure and every earlier negative gate stand. Read-only reproduction: inspect `js/rps.js` lines 180–201 and the archived `trainingRows`, `curve`, and frozen checks in `data/rps_blocked_teacher_source.json`.

### Read-only RPS teaching-task design review: stop before a new pilot

- **Hypothesis / sources.** Could a teacher's prior reward experience on a stationary hidden RPS mapping help a reward-limited or inexperienced student learn a cue-based policy that persists after messages cease? Reviewed the valid negative continuous-source teaching pilot (`data/rps_hidden_strategy_teaching_pilot.json`), saved DN-use and forced-bit diagnostics, the 93.1% offline MBON×DN interaction result, mixed-strategy preflight, frozen single-teacher source failure, and the existing contextual-bandit/680 ms code. This is design analysis only: no simulation, training, code change, new seed, or revised gate.
- **Why the prior fixed task lacked headroom.** The ordinary previous-move cue determines the next opponent move once one mapping is fixed. In the valid four-student pilot, all arms started at **24/72** frozen disconnected wins, yet disconnected controls reached **63, 64, 65, 67/72** after 12 episodes for students 821–824; correct-message arms reached **63, 59, 65, 69/72**. Several disconnected controls reached the reliability threshold as early as episode 6. The cue and ordinary game rewards were already sufficient for fast independent learning. Lowering student reward frequency or budget would create numerical headroom, but archived data do not show that the current message could fill it; it changes the task rather than fixing the demonstrated message-use gap.
- **Message information versus learning.** Separately trained A/B teachers produced an informative fixed cue-relative Q bit, physically preserved in measured DNge175 spike counts. In a fixed-strategy student session, however, A's trained bit was **0 on 273/276** training observations, nearly matching the untrained bit-0 control, while B's bit was often 1. Correct-arm DN weights became nonzero but were comparable in norm to shuffled/untrained arms. Holding the same final MBON input fixed and replacing real DN counts with no-message counts changed only **5/69, 1/69, 1/69, 1/69** final connected held-out actions for students 821–824, with mixed effects. In a separate forced-bit frozen probe, the teacher-selected DN contribution shifted action-score spread modestly (**0.057–0.231** on average), and the all-student sensitivity/usefulness gates failed. These data support limited, inconsistent use of DN spikes, not a proven causal reason for the negative pilot.
- **Why the 93.1% result does not rescue teaching.** The offline decoder was *supervised* with correct actions on calibration recordings and appended six fixed MBON×DN products to the three MBON plus two measured DN features. It scored **93.1% mapping-balanced action accuracy** in each held-out receiver fold, whereas the existing additive five-feature linear readout scored **49.3% and 47.2%**. This establishes that a chosen interaction representation can decode joint cue/message information under offline source/receiver pairing. It does **not** establish that Fly B can discover the interaction coefficients from sparse own rewards, or that the earlier fixed-strategy student's failure was caused by an additive capacity limit. No supervised decoder was installed in a fly.
- **Best remaining task idea, and causal obstacle.** A single teacher could legitimately learn one stationary opponent by its own rewards before a novice student faces *the same* opponent; no hidden-label teacher selection or reversal would be needed. Its experience could make `bit=1{Q_c>max(Q_other)}` distinguish A/B through actual DN spikes. The student already sees the cue and its own rewards but not the mapping. For retained teaching, that bit would have to change early student actions, produce extra *rewarded* updates to the student's MBON-dependent action values, and improve frozen no-message performance over correct/shuffled/untrained/disconnected controls. The archived fixed-strategy pilot and forced-bit test do not show this chain. Restricting rewards or hiding the student's cue is not justified: the former may simply impair all arms, while the current bit mostly conveys mapping rather than the missing cue. On a balanced mixed A/B task, the same cue demands opposite actions; a student evaluated without messages and without another observable mapping context cannot reliably solve both. Selecting separate A/B teachers by the hidden mapping would leak it, and the one frozen teacher's mixed-sequence bit was constant zero. A reward-adapting single teacher failed the earlier all-teacher reversal gate. Message-connected accuracy alone is therefore not evidence of a retained cue policy.
- **Decision / preflight.** Choose **C: stop new RPS teaching research and consolidate existing results before the research cutoff**. There is no supported task-only change that simultaneously supplies teacher-specific information, measurable independent-learning headroom, and a plausible demonstrated path to disconnected retention with the current controller. A new preflight with fixed seeds and numerical gates is **not specified or run** because its first causal prerequisite—teacher-dependent student action and reward-update opportunities beyond the matched controls—is not supported by archived evidence. A different reward-learning mechanism or observable-context task would be a separate experimental model needing its own rationale and approval, not a revision of the failed pilots. Reproduce the read-only comparisons from the saved pilot, DN-use, forced-bit, offline-interaction, and single-teacher source artifacts named above; all earlier gates and raw files remain unchanged. No Git operation.

### Proposed RPS teacher-guided exploration feasibility study (design only, )

- **New scope / evidence.** The preceding stop decision applied to task-only changes with the existing message-conditioned action-value policy. This review considers an explicitly new *training-only* exploration mechanism; no code or experiment was run. In the valid hidden-strategy pilot, no-message students learned a stationary mapping quickly (disconnected controls ended at **63–67/72**), whereas correct messages had no consistent early or retained advantage. Correct-trained DN coefficients were nonzero but removing their measured counts while holding MBON input fixed switched just **5/69, 1/69, 1/69, 1/69** final actions. The separate forced-bit test failed all-student usefulness. A supervised, offline six-product MBON×DN decoder reached **93.1% mapping-balanced correct-action accuracy** in both receiver folds versus **49.3%/47.2%** for the additive five-feature decoder. This shows joint cue/message *decodability*, not a reward-trained Fly B policy or a reason to expect disconnected retention.
- **Three designs compared.** **A, interaction-aware bandit:** append the six fixed MBON×measured-DN products to the existing three-MBON/two-DN features and expand `RpsContextualBandit.W` to 11 columns in an opt-in mode; keep selected-action own-reward updates. It may learn message-conditioned actions from rewards, but all DN terms vanish at disconnection, and the supervised fitted coefficients cannot be copied in. A cue-only or non-neural explicit-context bandit is the comparison. **B, teacher-guided exploration (selected):** use one measured DN bit to approve/reject Fly B's *provisional* cue-only action during training, then update only the three-MBON cue-only Q heads from Fly B's actual game reward. The bit can change which action receives rewarding experience while leaving the evaluated policy independent of DN input. A count-matched random veto/shuffled bit and an ordinary no-message bandit are essential baselines. **C, message dropout:** vary the fraction of physically delivered messages across training in the current five-feature controller; no new learner is required, but it does not itself make a weak DN signal change useful actions or solve conflicting A/B cue-only targets. A matched random dropout schedule is required. Designs A/C may improve connected performance without improving a disconnected policy; B has the clearest direct credit path. None has been tested here.
- **Chosen information path and scientific change.** One **new** one-bit source asks whether the teacher's highest real MBON-driven Q score agrees with Fly B's publicly proposed action: `approve=1{Q_candidate>max(Q_other two)}`, strict ties reject. This is a teacher *estimate*, not the opponent move, true winning action, or a supervised label; the teacher can be wrong. The student proposal is an explicit **new public behavioral input to the teaching interface**, disclosed as a scope change; it is not a neural B→A message or hidden strategy label. The bit is delivered only by stimulating existing DNge175 **3141 (reject/0)** or **3165 (approve/1)** at **300 Hz for 240–280 ms**, and Fly B acts on **measured 240–280 ms DN spike counts**, never the raw bit. On approval keep the proposed action; on rejection draw uniformly from the other two actions using a separate preseeded stream independent of opponent move/reward. This one bit does not identify which of three actions wins: a rejected proposal leaves two alternatives. Fly B's cue-only three-feature `RpsContextualBandit` retains alpha **0.03**, epsilon **0.20** for the *proposal*, and selected-action own-reward update; KC→MBON weights stay frozen. The final selected action, its own reward, and the saved pre-message MBON features drive the ordinary Q update. Any extra correct rewarded samples can therefore change weights that still operate at disconnected evaluation. This is an engineered, interactive action-advice protocol, not the original unidirectional teacher-first channel or unchanged biological policy.

  With uniformly proposed actions and teacher top-action correctness `p`, approval plus random selection after rejection would make the final action correct with probability `(1+3p)/6`, at most **2/3** even for a perfect teacher, versus **1/3** without useful advice. The actual epsilon-greedy proposal distribution must be measured rather than treated as uniform. This is why the source and extra rewarded-update gates are necessary.
- **Prospective task and seeds, if separately authorized.** Fix one stationary observable Strategy **B**, `next=(previous+2)%3`, for the entire study; it had slower early student learning than A in the archived pilot. Preselect **one new teacher seed 830** and **student seeds 831,832** (no RPS archive seed entries found in a read-only exact-seed search); never replace one. Teacher 830 learns B from its **own** rewards using unchanged three-MBON contextual-bandit training for **12×24 rounds** on opponent seeds **61000–61011**, then its controller is frozen. The experimenter does not select an A/B teacher at runtime and never passes the B label to either fly. During student preflight/training, simulate this same teacher's real cue-driven Q scores with neural state reset once per 24-round episode, then carried through rounds. Both brains use independent real connectomes and preselected keyed streams; students within a seed start from identical zero Q and all-one synaptic multipliers in every arm. Student training is only **one 24-round episode**, opponent seed **41000**, then matched frozen 72-round evaluation on **52000/52003/52004** with no exploration, messages, or learning. Evaluate also before training. One positive preflight would warrant a separately locked larger pilot, not revise any failed result.

  Fix stream keys before outcomes: teacher base key `830*10000000+episodeKey*1000+round`, neural `rng(key+1)` and training action `rng(key+501)`; use episode keys **0–11** for teacher pretraining and **0** for its frozen student-training source. Student base key `studentSeed*10000000+episodeKey*1000+round`, neural `rng(key+2)`, provisional action `rng(key+501)`, and fallback alternative `rng(key+701)`. Student training episode key **0**, preflight probe keys **200–235**, and frozen evaluation episode keys **100–102** for the three held-out opponent seeds. Shuffle each student's correct-arm training bits across its 23 observed slots once with `rng(2026110601+studentSeed)` and Fisher–Yates; cue-free slot stays null. All matched arms use the same exogenous keys; their learned actions and resulting neural histories may diverge.
- **Revised 680 ms training schedule and controls.** Both brains receive the same already observed previous-opponent-move VPN cue at **150 Hz from 0–240 ms** (first round has no cue/advice); ordinary counts reset at **50 ms**. At **240 ms**, Fly B computes one provisional action from its three original standardized MBON features and its existing epsilon-greedy rule. The teacher's frozen Q scores evaluate that public proposal; no opponent future move or reward has been seen. Stimulate the corresponding DN from **240–280 ms** and separately count its actual spikes. At **280 ms**, decode only the two counts (bit 1 iff count3165>count3141; invalid ties fail) and apply the approval/rejection action rule. The student receives its own RPS reward after the final action, and the existing contextual-bandit update runs at **620 ms**; the round ends at **680 ms**. Pre-message MBON features and scores are held for that update. This changes cue/decision timing and adds a public proposal; it must be labeled as a new protocol. Compare **correct trained-teacher approval**, a single preseeded **trial-shuffled approval** schedule preserving the correct arm's bit counts over the 23 observed training trials, **untrained zero-Q teacher** approval (strict ties reject), **no message/no veto**, and a **non-neural transition-count lookup mentor** trained only on the teacher's earlier observed opponent moves. The lookup sends the same one-bit approval through the same physical DNs and uses the same student veto/update rule; it is a simple task-learning benchmark, not a fly teacher. The shuffled arm is a count-matched random-veto baseline. Same opponent moves, initial student weights, keyed proposal and fallback random streams, reward rule, and frozen evaluation for all arms; action/reward histories may cause later neural-state differences.
- **Locked stop gates for this proposed preflight.** **Teacher/source first:** frozen teacher 830 must score **≥58/72 wins**, **≥17/23 wins per observed cue**, and **≥12 choices of every action across 69 observed rounds**; on those same 69 real-Q cue events, test all three hypothetical public proposals offline and require **≥80% balanced approve/reject correctness on each cue** against the true winning action (truth for scoring only). This tests whether the *new* bit is relevant, not merely variable. **Receiver second:** with frozen zero-weight students 831/832, run 12 matched two-round no/forced-0/forced-1 probes per cue under the revised timing; each forced bit's intended DN must beat the other on **≥11/12** first-round probes and gain **≥1 mean spike** versus no message; the fixed three-MBON cue decoder must recognize the pre-message cue and the following round's cue on **≥10/12** per student/cue/branch, with **≤2/12** recognition loss after either first-round pulse versus its matched no-pulse branch. No weight changes. **Headroom:** each student's matched frozen pretraining disconnected score must be **≤36/72**; no seed replacement. **Control validity:** the fixed shuffled schedule must move at least **4/23** bit positions and have **≤60% balanced approve/reject correctness** on its own proposals, scored only after that arm's proposals are recorded; otherwise stop as an invalid shuffle. **One-episode learning mechanism:** correct advice must produce **≥4 more rewarded correct-action updates on the two initially nongreedy B cue/action pairs combined** than no-message training for each student, with at least one update on each pair. At the frozen post-training disconnected evaluation, each correct-trained student must improve **≥8/72** over its own pretraining score and beat **each** shuffled, untrained, and no-message arm by **≥4/72**; the non-neural lookup arm is a benchmark, not a required target to beat. Report all cue/action counts. Require exact controller and KC→MBON integrity, physical DN decoding, and unchanged frozen weights. Any failed gate stops before a larger pilot; no tuning, added seeds, or changed threshold. These are *new prospective criteria*, not replacements for any archived gate.
- **Minimal implementation plan / limits.** Add one write-once opt-in runner modeled on `tools/check_rps_hidden_strategy_teaching_pilot.mjs` and `tools/check_rps_blocked_teacher_source.mjs`; reuse `Network`/`wiring` in `js/sim.js`, `Opponent` and `RpsContextualBandit` in `js/rps.js`, the existing DN spike counter/targets, fixed cue decoder, and the balanced-action runner's final-action bookkeeping pattern. No change to `js/rps.js`, `js/sim.js`, connectome, neural dynamics, dopamine plasticity, or the default game is required. Record the candidate, Q scores, actual DN counts, decoded advice, final action/reward, selected-head update, Q weights, and disconnected evaluations. A failure of teacher advice accuracy, DN reception, extra correct-action reward updates, or disconnected advantage would falsify the proposed mechanism at the corresponding stage. The public-proposal feedback and revised timing limit comparison with prior one-way teaching pilots. **Nothing was implemented or simulated in this review.**

### Execution interpretation locked before teacher training

- The authorized runner will write only `data/rps_teacher_guided_exploration_preflight.json`, refusing overwrite. Stage 1 teacher policy accuracy uses the archived ordinary **400 ms** frozen RPS evaluation on seeds 52000/52003/52004. Advice quality uses the **actual revised 680 ms** frozen teacher-source trace on those same 72 opponent sequences and all three hypothetical candidate actions on each of 69 observed-cue rounds; balanced approve/reject correctness is the mean of approval sensitivity on truly winning proposals and rejection specificity on the other two proposals, separately per cue. This resolves the two possible teacher timing interpretations before source data are inspected. Teacher-source weights remain the final 400 ms reward-trained weights.
- In the new 680 ms student round, both fly brains process the cue before 240 ms. Fly B's proposal and its three MBON features/Q scores are frozen at 240 ms; only then is teacher approval computed. The DN pulse occupies 240–280 ms; the final action/reward occur at 280 ms, with the selected-action Q update at 620 ms. The teacher's public proposal input is exactly one action index, and one approval bit is emitted per observed-cue round. Frozen disconnected evaluation uses this same cue/decision timing, with no pulse, veto, exploration, or update. A 400 ms teacher policy score cannot substitute for source advice accuracy, and a connected action score cannot substitute for disconnected student learning.
- Stage 2's 12 matched two-round probes per cue use preflight keys 200–235: first cue `c`, next causally consistent cue `(c+2)%3`, with neural state continuous within the pair and reset between pairs. No/forced-0/forced-1 first-round branches use identical initial weights and streams; the second round has no pulse. Apply the archived fixed three-MBON cue decoder to first-round pre-pulse and second-round pre-pulse rates, without refitting. The intended DN must beat the other on at least 11/12 first rounds for each bit/student/cue; a count tie in any *actual advice* training round is invalid. Student initial and post-training frozen evaluations each consist of three 24-round sequences with keys 100–102 and the same held-out opponent seeds. Correct-arm teacher bits from the one 24-round training episode are permuted once per student for shuffled training; shuffled advice correctness is judged against *that arm's own* provisional proposals and offline winning action, not the correct arm's proposals. The simple lookup mentor predicts the opponent move from counts of `cue → next opponent move` observed during teacher pretraining (smallest-index ties), then approves only if the candidate would beat its predicted move. Labels enter this non-neural benchmark, never Fly A or Fly B. Any stage failure writes completed raw data and stops without running later stages.
### Teacher-guided exploration preflight: stopped at receiver gate

- **Hypothesis / experiment.** Test whether reward-trained teacher 830 can issue useful approval of a public provisional action through the existing DNge175 spike-count interface while students 831/832 preserve ordinary RPS cue recognition. The opt-in, write-once runner `tools/check_rps_teacher_guided_exploration_preflight.mjs` used the locked Strategy B, seeds, schedules, controls, and thresholds above. It changed no default controller, connectome, spiking dynamics, dopamine rule, or archived result. This is a new interactive teaching protocol, not a repeat of the previous one-way teaching pilot.
- **Teacher gate passed.** Teacher 830 completed its own-reward 12×24 training. Frozen ordinary-policy evaluation scored **64/72** (cue 0 **23/23**, cue 1 **17/23**, cue 2 **23/23**) with action counts **rock 29, paper 17, scissors 23** across the 69 observed rounds. Under the revised 680 ms frozen source schedule, balanced approve/reject accuracy on the 23 observed events per cue was **96.7% / 83.7% / 100%** for cues 0/1/2, using all three hypothetical proposals for scoring. These pass the predeclared 58/72, 17/23, 12/action, and 80%/cue gates. Controller and KC→MBON weights were frozen for evaluation.
- **Receiver receipt passed; cue gate failed.** All **216** matched two-round probes were completed (two students × three first cues × 12 repeats × no pulse/bit 0/bit 1). Each forced target's measured DN spike count beat the other on **12/12** probes for every student/cue/bit; mean target gains were **7.42–7.92 spikes** versus no pulse. ### Locked frozen receiver-history diagnostic protocol (; before new probes)

- **Question and existing evidence.** Student 832's following cue 0 after first cue 1 scored 6/12 without DN and 9/12 under either forced bit in the stopped preflight. The original 10/12 gate remains failed. Reuse all 216 saved paired two-round rows in `data/rps_teacher_guided_exploration_preflight.json`; do not repeat them or train anyone. The existing teacher-guided schedule presents cue 0–240 ms, resets ordinary counts at 50 ms, computes a provisional action at 240 ms, pulses DN 240–280 ms, clears DN and resets counts at 280 ms, and carries neural state through the 680 ms round and into the next round. Frozen probes have no game reward or update; the simulated 620 ms update point is learning-disabled. The old teacher-first receiver instead presents DN 240–280 ms, then VPN cue 280–520 ms, resets counts at 330 ms, and decides at 520 ms; it also preserves state across rounds. The preflight's first-cue MBON measurement precedes DN, so first-cue recognition errors cannot be caused by that round's DN pulse. The following cue can carry effects of first-round neural activity even though spike counts are reset.
- **New probes, fixed before execution.** Use only original receiver seeds **831/832**, each first cue **0/1/2**, repeats **0–11**, and the same second cue `(first+2)%3`. Keys are `seed*10000000+(200+12*firstCue+repeat)*1000+roundIndex`; neural stream is `rng(key+2)` for the revised schedule, and the earlier teacher-first schedule also switches to `rng(key+3)` at 280 ms as its archived implementation did. All start from the same seed's untrained zero-Q controller and all-one KC→MBON multipliers, with no rewards, exploration, or learning. The **isolated-second** condition simulates only the second cue under revised timing after a full neural-state reset, using the exact saved second-round key: **72 new rounds**. The **earlier-schedule continuous** condition simulates both causally ordered cues with no DN under its original timing, resetting state only before each pair: **72 new pairs / 144 rounds**. These are timing/history comparisons, not substituted results for the failed preflight; no new forced-DN simulations are needed because its matched forced-bit rows were saved. Total new simulation: **216 frozen rounds**. Apply the unchanged archived log1p three-MBON nearest-centroid cue decoder; do not fit anything. Store per-probe rates, standardized MBON features, cue guesses, spike-count resets and cue-drive checks, and frozen-weight integrity. Write once to `data/rps_teacher_guided_receiver_history.json`.
- **Predeclared diagnostic interpretation.** The saved 6/12, 9/12, 9/12 sequence must be reproduced by read-only recount of student 832 first-cue-1 probes. Baseline failure is established when no-message recognition is below 10/12. Treat at least **three paired net corrections out of 12** for the isolated second cue relative to saved continuous no-message as evidence of a neural-history contribution; smaller changes leave that contribution unresolved. Treat at least **three paired net corrections** under the earlier teacher-first continuous schedule relative to saved revised no-message as evidence that the schedule matters, while recognizing that timing and its keyed cue-period stream change together. DN interference is supported only by **more than two net losses out of 12** relative to matched no-message on a cue; forced-bit accuracy below 10/12 alone does not prove interference. A procedural defect requires a concrete violation of the locked cue drive, reset, stream, timing, or frozen-weight rules, not merely poor classification. Report per-cue/seed first and second accuracy, paired gains/losses, MBON means/variation, and measured DN counts from saved rows. No outcome will retroactively pass the original receiver gate or trigger student training.
### Frozen receiver-history diagnostic result

- **Hypothesis / method.** The stopped teacher-guided receiver's cue-0 failure on student 832 may be caused by sensory neural state carried from preceding cue 1 under the revised 680 ms schedule, rather than by a missing cue or DN interference. Following the prospectively locked protocol above, this one reward-free diagnostic reused all 216 archived revised-schedule pairs, then ran only 72 fresh-state second-cue rounds and 72 no-message continuous two-round pairs under the earlier teacher-first timing. Seeds 831/832, cues 0/1/2, 12 repeats, streams, connectomes, untrained Q weights, and fixed MBON decoder were unchanged. No teacher or student training was run.
- **Frozen recognition, out of 12.** Entries `a/b` are first-cue and following-cue recognition. The fresh-state column scores only the isolated following cue. The last column uses the earlier teacher-first 280–520 ms sensory window and its original `key+3` cue-period stream; it is a schedule comparison, not a new receiver pass claim.

  | Student | First cue → next cue | Revised no DN | Revised bit 0 | Revised bit 1 | Fresh-state next cue | Earlier continuous no DN |
  | --- | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
  | 831 | 0 → 2 | 10/12 | 10/12 | 10/12 | 12/12 | 9/12 |
  | 831 | 1 → 0 | 12/10 | 12/10 | 12/10 | 10/12 | 11/10 |
  | 831 | 2 → 1 | 12/11 | 12/10 | 12/11 | 12/12 | 12/11 |
  | 832 | 0 → 2 | 11/12 | 11/12 | 11/12 | 12/12 | 10/11 |
  | 832 | 1 → 0 | **12/6** | **12/9** | **12/9** | **11/12** | **10/10** |
  | 832 | 2 → 1 | 10/10 | 10/10 | 10/10 | 12/12 | 12/10 |

- **Matched cause check.** Student 832 correctly recognized first cue 1 on **12/12** no-message trials, before any DN pulse, but recognized the following cue 0 on only **6/12**. Resetting neural state before that second cue, while keeping its cue, 0–240 ms stimulation, key, and decoder fixed, corrected **5** of the 6 missed repeats and introduced **0** errors, yielding **11/12**. The earlier teacher-first continuous schedule corrected **6** and lost **2** matched repeats, yielding **10/12**; it changes timing and the predeclared `key+3` cue-period stream together, so it does not isolate either one. Revised continuous no-message MBON means (rock/paper/scissors groups) on this second cue were **0.696/0.565/0.768**, with SD **0.256/0.217/0.462**; fresh-state means were **0.589/0.476/0.589**, SD **0.198/0.203/0.264**. The broadened third-group response is consistent with history-dependent neural variability on this cue, but does not by itself identify a cellular mechanism. Student 832's revised no-message cue-0 errors after cue 1 occurred at repeats **0, 2, 4, 7, 10, 11**; only repeat 4 stayed wrong with a fresh state.
- **DN and procedural checks.** The saved forced-bit target dominated on **12/12** probes per bit/student/cue; for the failing student-832 cue-1 pair it averaged **7.67** intended spikes and zero off-target spikes. Either bit rescued **3** matched following-cue errors and caused **0** losses, moving 6/12 to 9/12. No seed/cue/bit had more than two net DN-associated recognition losses, so the predeclared interference criterion was not met. Source inspection confirms the revised schedule drives VPN at 150 Hz during 0–240 ms, measures the provisional decision at 240 ms before DN stimulation, resets ordinary counts at 50 and 280 ms, and retains membrane/conductance/synaptic neural state across rounds. Fresh probes verified cue drive, expected reset/decision windows, frozen controller weights, and all-one KC→MBON multipliers. **No implementation defect was established**; ordinary count reset was never specified to reset the full neural state.
- **Conclusion / next experiment.** The original **6/12 no-message** result is reproduced exactly from saved probes. The same-timing fresh-state comparison passes the predeclared ≥3 paired net-correction criterion (**+5/12**) and most strongly supports a neural-history contribution under the revised continuous schedule. The teacher-first comparison also exceeds its schedule-difference criterion (**+4/12**) but its 831 first-cue-0 score is only **9/12**, so neither comparison retroactively validates a full receiving interface. DN interference is not supported as the cause of the 832 failure. The prior preflight's absolute 10/12 gate **remains failed**; headroom and student training remain unrun. The smallest future step, if pursued, is a separately locked frozen test of whether a task-compatible pre-cue state reset preserves cue recognition across both students; do not insert that reset into the teaching protocol without a new causal and biological justification. No correction or new training was performed here.

### Locked student-only between-round reset feasibility protocol (; before simulation)

- **Scope and causal boundary.** This is a new frozen receiving protocol, not a correction of the failed teacher-guided preflight. In the existing two-round probe, first cue `c` reaches Fly B's VPNs at 150 Hz during 0–240 ms, counts reset at 50 ms, and its cue-only provisional action is selected at 240 ms. Fly A's frozen Q scores can approve/reject that public proposal; a forced-bit receiving probe stimulates Fly B DN 3141 or 3165 at 300 Hz during 240–280 ms. Fly B separately measures actual DN spikes and decodes them at 280 ms, when the first round's final action is selected; the 620 ms update point is disabled in this reward-free study. The first round ends at 680 ms. The following cue `(c+2)%3` is a **new, message-free second round**, presented at its own 0–240 ms, with action at its own 280 ms. Thus the only reset that changes *only* between-round state without erasing a still-needed provisional decision or message is **immediately after the first 680 ms and before the second round's 0 ms VPN input**. The first round's numeric DN-count buffer, decoded advice, provisional action, final action, and teacher response live outside `Network` and are retained as records through the reset. First-round advice is consumed at its 280 ms action; it is not asserted to guide the second round. Moving the reset to 280 ms or postponing the first final action would be a different, unapproved schedule. Fly A's state is not reset between rounds.
- **Exact intervention.** Call Fly B's existing `Network.resetState()` once at that boundary in the reset arm only. It resets `t`, membrane `v`, conductance `g`, refractory `refr`, eligibility `trace`, ordinary `count`, `lastSpike`, three spike-delay `ring` arrays, and `drive`; it does **not** reset connectome weights `m`, MBON noise configuration, the RNG object, controller Q weights/bias/baseline, or the external measured-DN buffer. The existing keyed neural stream is set at each round start in both arms. No learning, reward, dopamine pulse, source change, or Fly A reset occurs. Record Fly A's frozen MBON-Q approval of the same public provisional action on a separate continuous two-round teacher trace; forced message conditions remain independent of that approval for receiver isolation.
- **Fixed comparison and budget.** Reuse the archived **continuous-state** no-message/forced-0/forced-1 results in `data/rps_teacher_guided_exploration_preflight.json` as arm A, with no rerun. Arm B uses a new reset-state two-round simulation for students **831/832**, all first-cue transitions **0→2, 1→0, 2→1**, repeats **0–11**, and all three branches: **216 new student pairs**. Use the archived keys `seed*10000000+(200+12*c+repeat)*1000+roundIndex`; neural `rng(key+2)`, provisional `rng(key+501)`, and rejection alternative `rng(key+701)`. Reset student state only between the two rounds; reset the pair once initially in both arms. Fly A seed **830** uses its saved trained controller, initial state reset once per pair, keyed neural `rng(830*10000000+(200+12*c+repeat)*1000+roundIndex+1)`, and continuous state across the pair. Each first-round teacher approval is computed from its real MBON-driven Q scores and the student's public proposal, but the receiver probe's forced-bit stimulation is unchanged. No teacher or student weights update. Apply the archived fixed log1p three-MBON nearest-centroid cue decoder without refitting. Write new raw results once to `data/rps_teacher_guided_between_round_reset.json`.
- **Locked gates, scored separately by seed/first cue/branch.** (1) Both first- and second-cue recognition must be **≥10/12** for each no-message, bit-0, and bit-1 branch. (2) Each forced-bit branch must have **at least as many** first- and second-cue correct recognitions as its matched no-message branch; record individual paired gains/losses as well. (3) The measured first-round DN counts must decode the forced bit on **12/12** probes for each student/cue/bit. (4) The intended DN must gain **≥1 mean spike** over matched no-message trials for each student/cue/bit, and beat the off-target count on all 12 probes. (5) Every reset-arm first-round provisional action, MBON rates, DN counts, decoded bit, and final action must exactly match its archived continuous counterpart; the external numeric DN buffer and decoded advice must survive the reset unchanged, with the original first-round final action already using them. (6) Teacher `Network.t` must advance continuously through both 680 ms rounds with no between-round reset; its saved controller and both flies' KC→MBON multipliers stay unchanged. Any gate failure fails this **new** feasibility study. Report every condition, the original failed gate unchanged, and the limitation that this does not test retention or teacher-assisted student learning.
### Student-only between-round reset feasibility result

- **Hypothesis / experiment.** A full Fly B `Network.resetState()` after its first 680 ms round, before the next VPN cue, may remove the neural-history interference seen in student 832 while leaving the *already consumed* first-round DN advice available as an external measured-count record. This is the new, opt-in frozen protocol locked above. We reused the failed preflight's 216 continuous-state rows, then simulated exactly 216 new reset-arm student pairs (831/832 × three causal cue transitions × 12 repeats × no/forced-0/forced-1). Separately, trained Fly A 830 was replayed frozen through two 680 ms rounds with a single initial state reset per pair and no between-round reset. Forced-bit reception, not the teacher's natural bit, was the stimulus under comparison. No rewards, learning, training, or model changes occurred.
- **Recognition, first cue / following cue, each out of 12.** All first-round reset-arm values exactly matched the archived continuous arm, as they must because reset occurred afterward. Each cell reports no message, forced bit 0, and forced bit 1 in that order.

  | Student | Transition | Continuous: no / bit 0 / bit 1 | Student reset: no / bit 0 / bit 1 |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | 831 | 0→2 | 10/12 · 10/12 · 10/12 | 10/12 · 10/12 · 10/12 |
  | 831 | 1→0 | 12/10 · 12/10 · 12/10 | 12/10 · 12/10 · 12/10 |
  | 831 | 2→1 | 12/11 · 12/10 · 12/11 | 12/12 · 12/12 · 12/12 |
  | 832 | 0→2 | 11/12 · 11/12 · 11/12 | 11/12 · 11/12 · 11/12 |
  | 832 | 1→0 | **12/6 · 12/9 · 12/9** | **12/11 · 12/11 · 12/11** |
  | 832 | 2→1 | 10/10 · 10/10 · 10/10 | 10/12 · 10/12 · 10/12 |

- **Paired and communication checks.** For student 832's 1→0 transition, reset corrected **5/12** matched no-message outcomes and **2/12** under each forced bit, introducing **zero** losses; every reset-arm first- and following-cue condition was at least **10/12**. Neither forced-bit condition lost correct recognitions relative to its matched no-message branch for any student/transition. Actual first-round DN counts decoded each forced bit on **12/12** probes per student/cue/bit; intended counts dominated on every probe and gained **7.42–7.92 mean spikes** over no message. The first-round provisional actions, MBON rates, DN counts, decoded bits, and final actions matched all **216** archived continuous records exactly. The numeric DN-count/decoded-advice record and Fly A's Q-based approval persisted outside the network across all 216 student resets. Fly A's own simulated neural time advanced continuously from 680 to 1360 ms in each pair, with no between-round reset; its saved controller, Fly B's controller, and both KC→MBON multipliers remained unchanged. Thus every **new locked gate passed**.
- **Interpretation / limit.** The intervention restores the failing student's cue recognition in this frozen two-round test without disrupting **first-round** neural message receipt or action selection. It does not demonstrate a DN memory trace across the reset: measured counts are retained in an explicit numeric buffer, and the following round receives **no new message**. First-round approval was consumed at its 280 ms final action, before the 680 ms reset. The real Fly A trace confirms source continuity, but forced bits—not its natural approvals—were delivered to isolate receiving. Resetting the whole Fly B neural state between rounds is a substantive new protocol for future learning; reward and eligibility consequences were not tested. The original preflight remains a **valid failed result**; neither its gate nor any previous negative result changes. A separately locked teacher-guided exploration preflight with the new reset rule is **technically justified**, conditional on its own headroom, reward-update, and matched-control gates. It was not run here.
- **Raw data / reproduction.** Write-once `data/rps_teacher_guided_between_round_reset.json` contains all reset-arm trial records, archived continuous comparisons, frozen teacher traces, and seven gate outcomes. `node --check tools/check_rps_teacher_guided_between_round_reset.mjs` and saved-artifact count/integrity checks passed. Reproduce in a copy without the result file using `node tools/check_rps_teacher_guided_between_round_reset.mjs`; the runner refuses overwrite here. Inspect the existing result without simulation using `node -e "const d=require('./data/rps_teacher_guided_between_round_reset.json'); console.log(d.summary,d.gates,d.passed)"`.
### Locked reset-schedule teacher-guided exploration preflight (; before student training)

- **Hypothesis and provenance.** This is a **new** one-episode teaching preflight. The original teacher-guided preflight stopped at its failed continuous-state cue gate and remains failed; the separate frozen student-only between-round reset study passed its own receiving gates. The single new training variable is Fly B `Network.resetState()` immediately after each completed **680 ms** round and before the next 0–240 ms VPN cue. At that boundary the first-round approval was already decoded from measured DN spikes and used for the 280 ms final action/reward, and its own-reward contextual-bandit update occurred at 620 ms. The reset clears only simulator neural state (`t`, `v`, `g`, `refr`, `trace`, `count`, `lastSpike`, `ring`, `drive`), **not** Fly B's cue-only Q weights/bias/baseline, synaptic multipliers, stored rewards/advice, or Fly A's neural state. No neuron, stimulation, threshold, feature, source rule, opponent, or learning equation changes. The reset must be disclosed as a new task protocol because it discards Fly B neural carryover between rounds.
- **Teacher and task.** Load **teacher 830's already trained controller** from the write-once original preflight artifact; do not retrain it. Verify frozen weights and the archived teacher readiness numbers (**64/72 wins, cue wins 23/23,17/23,23/23; action counts 29/17/23; balanced approval accuracy 96.7%/83.7%/100%**) against the original ≥58/72, ≥17/23/cue, ≥12/action, and ≥80%/cue gates. Strategy B remains `next=(previous+2)%3` with no strategy label passed to either fly. During each student episode, replay teacher 830 from the saved controller with a state reset **only at episode start**, then carry its neural state through the 24 rounds. It receives the public cue and provisional student action at 240 ms and emits `1{Q_candidate>max(Q_other two)}`; no future move or reward enters its message.

Reuse opponent training seed **41000**, frozen held-out seeds **52000/52003/52004**, teacher/student episode key **0** for training, keys **100–102** for frozen evaluation, and receiver keys **200–235**. Teacher neural stream is `rng(830*10000000+episodeKey*1000+round+1)`. Fly B stream is `rng(studentSeed*10000000+episodeKey*1000+round+2)`, proposal stream `+501`, rejection alternative `+701`; shuffle with `rng(2026110601+studentSeed)` over the 23 observed-cue slots. Student cue is 150 Hz from 0–240 ms, count reset 50 ms, proposal at 240 ms, DN 3141/3165 at 300 Hz 240–280 ms, measured-count decoding/final action/reward at 280 ms, selected-action own-reward Q update at 620 ms, round end 680 ms, then **student-only reset before the next cue**. Preserve the original arms: correct trained-teacher advice, trial-shuffled correct-arm bit schedule, untrained zero-Q teacher (strict ties reject), no-message/disconnected, and non-neural transition-count lookup mentor from the archived teacher training observations. ### Reset-schedule teacher-guided exploration preflight: valid negative result

- **Hypothesis / experiment.** The student-only between-round reset might make Fly B's sensory representation reliable enough for genuine teacher-guided exploration: advice changes early actions, supplies additional correctly rewarded updates on the initially nongreedy cue/action pairs, and improves the student's own disconnected cue-only policy over all required controls. We executed the prospectively locked protocol above once with already trained/frozen teacher **830**, students **831/832**, fixed Strategy B, **one 24-round student episode per arm**, and the same **72-round frozen disconnected evaluation**. Teacher 830 was **not retrained**. The sole protocol change from the original stopped preflight was Fly B `Network.resetState()` after each 680 ms round, after its 620 ms reward update and before the next cue. All earlier failed gates remain unchanged.
- **Prerequisite validity passed.** The archived teacher policy/source gates remained true (frozen **64/72**; approval balance **96.7%/83.7%/100%** per cue). The write-once reset receiver fixture passed all 216 frozen probes: **≥10/12** recognition per cue/branch, no message-associated loss below no message, **12/12** bit decoding and target dominance, and unchanged weights. New reset-schedule initial frozen disconnected scores were **24/72 for each student**, below the ≤36/72 headroom ceiling. A new reward-free, no-learning actual-message smoke on the fixed 24-round source sequence decoded **23/23** observed-cue teacher approvals for **each** student and applied the prescribed accept/reject action rule; teacher/source and student weights stayed frozen. The fixed shuffle moved **14/23** message positions for each seed, preserved bit counts, and had balanced correctness **34.1%** (831) and **44.2%** (832) on its own proposals, below the ≤60% validity maximum. Matched training cue/opponent schedules, initial Q weights, per-round keyed streams, 23 resets per episode, DN decoding, and all-one KC→MBON multipliers were verified from the raw artifact. Frozen evaluations used identical held-out cue/opponent streams, no messages, no exploration, and no weight changes.
- **Training and frozen results.** Target updates are positively rewarded, weight-changing actions on **cue 1→paper plus cue 2→scissors**; `cue wins` and action counts are from the 69 observed-cue held-out rounds. `Changed/helpful/harmful` counts compare final played action with the provisional action during training. The lookup arm is a reference benchmark and is excluded from formal teacher-advantage gates.

  | Student | Training arm | Training wins/24 | Target updates (cue 1, cue 2) | Changed / helpful / harmful | Frozen wins/72 | Frozen cue wins (0,1,2) | Frozen rock/paper/scissors choices |
  | --- | --- | ---: | --- | --- | ---: | --- | --- |
  | 831 | Correct teacher | 16 | 12 (5,7) | 15 / 7 / 2 | **49** | 2,23,23 | 2,31,36 |
  | 831 | Shuffled | 7 | 3 (2,1) | 15 / 4 / 9 | 54 | 7,23,23 | 7,31,31 |
  | 831 | Untrained teacher | 8 | 4 (2,2) | 23 / 7 / 10 | 52 | 5,23,23 | 5,31,33 |
  | 831 | Disconnected | 7 | 2 (0,2) | 0 / 0 / 0 | 54 | 13,17,23 | 19,17,33 |
  | 831 | Lookup benchmark | 18 | 14 (7,7) | 13 / 7 / 0 | 47 | 0,23,23 | 0,32,37 |
  | 832 | Correct teacher | 17 | 11 (4,7) | 10 / 5 / 1 | **56** | 10,22,23 | 11,26,32 |

- **Locked gate decision.** Correct advice exceeded disconnected training by **+10** (831) and **+11** (832) correctly rewarded target updates, with at least one on each target pair; this passes the ≥4 learning-opportunity gate. Correct-trained frozen wins improved over each own **24/72** baseline by **+25/72** and **+32/72**, passing the ≥8/72 gate. But correct teacher's frozen differences versus **shuffled / untrained / disconnected** were **−5 / −3 / −5** wins for 831, and **−4 / +1 / +32** for 832. Neither seed beat every required control by the locked **+4/72**; the all-student preflight therefore **failed at Stage 4, retained-learning advantage**. Correct advice did change actions and produce more immediately rewarded target updates, but that did not translate into a reliable *relative* disconnected policy advantage. Cue 0 was the visible weakness in the correct-trained frozen policies (**2/23** and **10/23**), compared with disconnected controls (**13/23** and **20/23**). These observations do not establish a particular synaptic or Q-boundary mechanism. The new reset protocol's negative result is valid and distinct from the original preflight's Stage-2 failure; no gate was retuned, seed replaced, or experiment rerun.
- **Conclusion / next step.** The reset repaired receiving compatibility, and teacher advice supplied more early rewarding experience, but the full teaching hypothesis did **not** pass its matched-control learning gate. Do not expand to an independent teaching pilot on this result. If a later decision requires one mechanistic next step, use a **read-only cue-0 analysis of the saved Q scores, provisional/final choices, and held-out MBON features** to test why correct-advice training lost cue-0 frozen accuracy; do not automatically change the protocol or launch training. No such follow-up was run here.
