### Read-only cue-0 controller audit: cross-cue Q transfer offsets rewarded cue-0 learning

- **Hypothesis / validity.** Extra rewarded teacher-guided choices could have strengthened the correct action on their own cues but also raised competing action values on cue 0 because all three cues share the same three MBON features and action heads. The archived preflight contains all 24 training updates, initial/final controller parameters, real DN counts, provisional/final actions, rewards, and all 72 frozen input vectors/scores per arm. We ran **no neural simulation or training**. For each student, all five arms' **23 ordered cue-0 held-out MBON vectors, raw rates, opponent moves, and sequence IDs matched exactly**. The recorded selected-head update steps reconstructed every final `W,b` to numerical tolerance; `Q_a=b_a+W_a·f` reconstructed all saved frozen Q scores and greedy actions. The fixed MBON cue decoder recognized **19/23** cue-0 inputs for each student; it was not refit. This is sensory variability, but the identical vectors yield different decisions with different saved controllers.
- **Frozen cue-0 policy and local rewarded updates.** Rock is the correct cue-0 action. `Q` entries are mean rock/paper/scissors values on the 23 matched vectors. Margin is mean `Q_rock - max(Q_paper,Q_scissors)`, an uncalibrated score gap rather than action probability. The `rock updates` column counts **cue-0** positive-reward, weight-changing rock updates, distinct from the preflight's combined cue-1/cue-2 target counts.

  | Student | Training arm | Cue-0 rock updates | Frozen cue-0 actions R/P/S | Cue-0 wins | Mean Q R/P/S | Mean correct margin |
  | --- | --- | ---: | --- | ---: | --- | ---: |
  | 831 | Correct teacher | 3 | 2/8/13 | 2/23 | 0.123/−0.007/0.183 | **−0.180** |
  | 831 | Shuffled | 3 | 7/8/8 | 7/23 | 0.078/−0.009/0.018 | −0.059 |
  | 831 | Untrained teacher | 3 | 5/8/10 | 5/23 | 0.058/−0.007/0.047 | −0.101 |
  | 831 | Disconnected | 4 | 13/0/10 | 13/23 | 0.070/−0.050/0.064 | −0.003 |
  | 831 | Lookup mentor | 3 | 0/9/14 | 0/23 | 0.124/−0.005/0.194 | −0.190 |
  | 832 | Correct teacher | 6 | 10/4/9 | 10/23 | 0.165/0.005/0.122 | **−0.052** |
  | 832 | Shuffled | 4 | 14/0/9 | 14/23 | 0.081/−0.015/0.070 | −0.035 |
  | 832 | Untrained teacher | 5 | 9/7/7 | 9/23 | 0.043/0.009/−0.037 | −0.024 |
  | 832 | Disconnected | 5 | 20/3/0 | 20/23 | 0.067/−0.028/0.000 | +0.061 |
  | 832 | Lookup mentor | 6 | 7/7/9 | 7/23 | 0.168/0.017/0.122 | −0.077 |

- **Training trace and algebraic mechanism.** Correct advice changed **15/24** and **10/24** actions for 831/832. It produced **12** and **11** rewarded changes on the targeted cue-1/paper and cue-2/scissors pairs versus **2** and **0** in disconnected training, yet cue-0 rock itself had only **3** versus **4** rewards for 831, and **6** versus **5** for 832. The correct-trained cue-0 examples increased the mean held-out **rock-minus-scissors** Q difference by **+0.158** (831) and **+0.209** (832). Updates on *cue 2* contributed **−0.232** and **−0.200** to that same difference, offsetting or nearly offsetting the cue-0 benefit. Cue-1 contributions were +0.011/+0.033, and the initial cue-free round +0.002/0.000. Against paper, cue-0 updates contributed +0.193/+0.259, but other-cue contributions lowered these differences too. This decomposition includes rewarded and nonrewarded selected-head updates; it proves cross-cue parameter transfer in the recorded linear Q policy, without isolating which individual event caused every error.
- **Wrong decisions and lookup control.** Correct-trained cue-0 wrong-action Q gaps had median **0.134** (831) and **0.098** (832), mean **0.197/0.141**, and maxima **0.812/0.494**; there are small and substantial errors, and Q gaps are not calibrated confidence. The exact same cue-0 vectors produced **5/3/11/2** different decisions for 831's shuffled/untrained/disconnected/lookup controllers relative to its correct-trained controller; for 832, **4/5/13/3** differed. The lookup mentor earned still more target rewards (**14/13**) but gave the same **3/6 cue-0 rock rewards** as the correct teacher. Its cue-2 update contributions to mean cue-0 rock-minus-scissors were similarly adverse (**−0.237/−0.200**), and its cue-0 correct margins were **−0.190/−0.077**, explaining why extra *target* rewards alone did not ensure retained cue-0 control. This does **not** establish a defective reward rule; the one-episode schedule, shared features, and finite reward opportunities remain intertwined.
- **Conclusion / next experiment.** The strongest supported immediate cause of weak cue-0 frozen policies is the learned **action-value boundary**, particularly cue-2/scissors updates generalizing onto cue-0 features and canceling cue-0/rock gains. Inadequate cue-0 neural information is not established as the sole cause, and teacher-specific misinformation is disfavored by the lookup result. The teacher-assisted preflight still **fails** its original matched-control retained-learning gate. If pursued, the single smallest prospective test is a matched, opt-in **cue-specific contextual-bandit value head** with the same reward update and training schedule, compared against the unchanged shared-feature head under correct-advice and lookup conditions; lock its evaluation gate before training. That would test whether limiting cross-cue Q transfer improves disconnected retention, but it changes the student's readout representation and requires separate authorization. Do not implement it or restart training from this diagnostic.
- **Derived data / reproduction.** `data/rps_teacher_guided_reset_cue0_audit.json` contains all five arms' full cue/action training counts, selected-head update magnitudes, final parameters, cue-0 Q and margin distributions, feature variation, cue-source score contributions, and paired controller differences. Source: the unchanged `data/rps_teacher_guided_reset_preflight.json`. The derived artifact is write-once. `node --check tools/analyze_rps_teacher_guided_reset_cue0.mjs` passed; reproduce in a copy without the derived artifact using `node tools/analyze_rps_teacher_guided_reset_cue0.mjs`. No prior result or gate was revised.
