VPN spike routing was correct on **23/23 training** and **69/69 frozen observed cues** per student/arm. The previously fixed three-MBON cue decoder, applied *analytically* to the same saved frozen rates, identifies only **64/69** observed cues per student (cue 0: 19/23, cue 1: 22/23, cue 2: 23/23). If that old estimate selects the **already trained** cue heads at evaluation, without rerunning or retraining anything, the correct-advice score falls from **70/72 to 65/72** for both students; the five cue errors change five actions. This establishes frozen-policy sensitivity to the superior VPN router. It is **not** a training counterfactual: the archive lacks a cue-head policy trained with MBON-based routing under otherwise matched conditions. Likewise it lacks a shared-head learner with comparable direct VPN cue access. Thus the 49→70 and 56→70 gains cannot be uniquely assigned to head separation or router information.
- **Correct versus shuffled advice.** `Useful bit` means approval of a correct proposal or rejection of an incorrect one; `helpful/harmful changes` compare the played action with the proposal. Target updates are positive-reward, weight-changing cue-1/paper plus cue-2/scissors updates. Margin entries are mean final correct-action Q gap over the strongest competitor on cue 0/1/2; they are *uncalibrated value gaps*. Every arm's full provisional/final 3×3 cue/action counts and rewarded-update matrix are preserved in the derived artifact.

  | Student | Advice | Approve/reject | Useful bits / 23 | Changed: helpful/harmful | Training wins/24 | Target updates | Frozen margin 0/1/2 | Frozen wins/72 |
  | --- | --- | --- | ---: | --- | ---: | ---: | --- | ---: |
  | 831 | Correct | 14/9 | 21 | 9: 4/2 | 19 | 12 | +0.212/+0.578/+0.643 | 70 |
  | 831 | Shuffled | 14/9 | 9 | 9: 2/6 | 9 | 6 | +0.044/+0.507/+0.261 | 68 |
  | 832 | Correct | 12/11 | 21 | 11: 3/2 | 15 | 9 | +0.197/+0.564/+0.426 | 70 |
  | 832 | Shuffled | 12/11 | 8 | 11: 2/7 | 6 | 4 | +0.076/+0.407/+0.149 | 70 |

- **Why shuffled and other controls learned.** Student 831's shuffled schedule accidentally **approved 6 correct proposals and rejected 3 incorrect**; student 832's approved **4** and rejected **4**. It also approved **8 incorrect** and rejected **6/7 correct** proposals, so it is not reliable teacher information, yet its vetoes gave **6/4** target rewards and a positive final correct-action margin on all three cue heads. The correct teacher gave **+10/+9 training wins**, **+6/+5 target updates**, and a larger final Q margin on **every cue** relative to shuffled; this is a distinct measurable reward-learning effect, but final frozen wins differed only **+2/0**. The untrained teacher always rejected (23/23 changed actions), gave **4/2 target updates**, and reached **68/69** frozen wins. The lookup mentor gave **14/11 target updates** and reached **70/70**. These controls show that changed exploration plus a few timely correct rewards can suffice for near-ceiling final performance with the cue-head controller; shuffled bits were useful on individual trials despite their broken overall mapping association.
- **Disconnected limitation and early-learning uncertainty.** With no message, each student's 24 training proposals produced **zero** positive-reward updates for cue 1→paper or cue 2→scissors. Their frozen policies selected rock on **46/69**, paper on **23/69**, and scissors on **0/69** observed rounds, winning only **24/72** overall despite perfect VPN cue routing. This directly supports missing rewarded action opportunities in this *short* disconnected run; it does not establish that the unchanged reward rule could never learn with more experience. Although correct advice built larger terminal margins, only **initial and final** frozen evaluations were saved. The archive cannot determine early disconnected accuracy, time to reliable three-action performance, or whether the 70/72 ceiling concealed an earlier teacher-specific advantage. Training wins cannot substitute for those missing frozen checkpoints.
- **Conclusion / one next experiment.** The tested controller combined three mechanisms: physically measured VPN cue routing, isolated cue-head reward values, and advice-induced exploration. The archive establishes head-update isolation, superior VPN cue recognition, useful shuffled vetoes, and stronger terminal Q margins under correct advice. It does **not** isolate the two controller changes during training or show a passed teacher-specific retention gate. The smallest future test of the remaining *teaching-speed* question is one prospectively locked, unchanged-controller study on fresh predetermined students with correct, shuffled, untrained, and disconnected advice and **one early frozen disconnected checkpoint after 12 of the same 24 training rounds**, in addition to the final checkpoint. Predeclare an early advantage over all controls and stop without tuning. This is a proposal only; no new run was launched. A separate matched MBON-routed cue-head training counterfactual would be required to isolate the VPN router's training contribution, and is **not** answered by this analysis.
- **Derived artifact / reproduction.** Write-once `data/rps_teacher_guided_cue_heads_attribution.json` stores each arm's cue/action proposal and played-action matrices, advice contingency and changed-action counts, rewarded update matrix, reconstructed parameter norms, frozen margins/accuracy, and the clearly labeled evaluation-only MBON-rerouting analysis. Reproduce read-only in a copy lacking that derived artifact with `node tools/analyze_rps_teacher_guided_cue_heads.mjs`; the script refuses overwrite in this workspace.
### Locked early retained-learning cue-head replication (; before training)

- **Hypothesis.** Correct approval/rejection advice from archived trained teacher 830 increases reward-driven, cue-specific Q learning early enough to improve Fly B's *disconnected* RPS policy after 12 rounds. The earlier 831/832 comparison failed its final teacher-advantage gate and remains failed. This is one new experiment on fresh, preselected students **841/842**; a search of research docs, runners, and explicit seed fields in saved artifacts found no previous use of either seed. No replacement is allowed.
- **Fixed protocol.** Reuse `tools/check_rps_teacher_guided_cue_heads.mjs` logic in one opt-in runner with no model changes. Strategy B opponent seed 41000, one 24-round episode, teacher 830's archived continuous-neural-state Q trace, the same MBON feature normalization, VPN-spike cue router, four isolated contextual-bandit heads, epsilon 0.20, alpha 0.03, selected-action own-reward update, 680 ms rounds, student-only between-round neural reset, and actual DNge175 3141/3165 spike-count communication. Train matched correct, trial-shuffled, always-reject untrained, and disconnected arms; retain the non-neural lookup mentor as a separate benchmark. Each arm begins from identical zero Q heads and all-one KC→MBON weights. Neural, proposal, rejection, and shuffle streams retain the prior formulas. The shuffle permutes only the 23 observed-cue bits with `rng(2026110601+studentSeed)`, preserves counts, and must move at least four positions and have at most 60% balanced advice correctness on its own proposals. The first cue-free bit stays null.
- **Checkpoints and noninvasiveness.** Frozen, exploration-free and disconnected evaluations use opponent seeds **52000/52003/52004**, 72 rounds, at training rounds **0, 12, 24** for every arm. At round 12, copy controller parameters and evaluate them in *new* Network/game/controller instances, then continue the original live training objects. Before and after evaluation, verify identical controller weights and baseline, neural arrays/time/counts/drives, game state and reward state, and unchanged random-generator references. If this check fails, stop without interpreting learning. Record total and per-cue wins, action frequencies, correct-action Q margins, and per-action wins. No extra checkpoints.
- **Locked technical gates.** Archived source teacher 830 remains frozen and the prior DN/reset receiving fixture remains passed; actual stimulated DN counts must decode every emitted bit with intended-target dominance. Every training/evaluation observed cue must route from Fly B's own VPN counts (23/23 and 69/69), with cue-free first round and no ties. MBON feature normalization, rewards, head-isolated selected-action Q updates, fixed KC→MBON multipliers, frozen evaluation weights, matched opponent/neural streams across arms, and noninvasive checkpoint state must all verify. Starting frozen wins must be ≤36/72 and identical across arms. A technical failure makes the pilot invalid; no seed or parameter replacement.
- **Primary early gate.** At round 12, **each** correct-advice student must exceed its matched disconnected arm by **≥8/72** and each shuffled and untrained arm by **≥4/72** frozen wins; its mean correct-action Q margin must be **strictly positive for every observed cue**; and it must have **≥2 additional correctly rewarded weight-changing target updates** (cue 1/paper and cue 2/scissors combined) versus disconnected, with **≥1 on each target cue**. Both students and all technical gates must pass. The 24-round outcome is secondary and is reported even if the early gate fails; the prior final comparison and gates are not retroactively changed. Training wins describe immediate assistance only, not retained learning.
- **Measurements and stopping.** Save every round's cue, VPN route, proposal, advice, measured/decoded DN bit, action, reward, updated head, and rewarded updates; save all frozen rows and controller parameters at the three checkpoints, including per-action wins and Q margins. Run once with a new write-once artifact. If initial validity or checkpoint noninvasiveness fails, stop; otherwise complete the fixed 24 rounds and all five arms, then apply the locked gate. No further student training follows this pilot.

### Early retained-learning result: no teacher-specific advantage

- **Execution and validity.** The first write-once runner stopped on seed **842** immediately after its predefined shuffled arm: the fixed permutation moved **8/23** bits and preserved counts, but its balanced advice quality was **61.7%**, exceeding the locked **60%** ceiling. This interrupted artifact remains `data/rps_teacher_guided_cue_heads_early.json`; it was not overwritten. A separate write-once continuation ran only the three *unstarted* 842 arms and the final frozen evaluations for both students, with no repeated training or changed shuffle. The combined data are `data/rps_teacher_guided_cue_heads_early_continuation.json`. Seed 841's shuffle moved **12/23** bits and scored **43.6%**, passing. Both initial disconnected scores were **24/72**, under the ≤36 headroom gate. The independent round-12 frozen evaluation left the live neural arrays/time, controller weights and baseline, reward/game state, and RNG references unchanged for every arm. All cue routing, matched sensory streams, actual DN bit decoding with intended-target dominance, head-isolated reward updates, frozen weights, and KC→MBON integrity checks passed. The **842 shuffle-control validity gate failed**, so this experiment cannot be declared a valid passed teaching test.
- **Frozen disconnected wins/72 at the three locked checkpoints.** Initial score is common to all five matched arms within each student. The lookup mentor is a non-neural reference.

  | Student | Training arm | 0 rounds | 12 rounds | 24 rounds | 12-round cue wins (0,1,2) | 24-round cue wins (0,1,2) |
  | --- | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | --- | --- |
  | 841 | Correct teacher | 24 | **69** | **69** | 22,23,23 | 22,23,23 |
  | 841 | Shuffled | 24 | 69 | 69 | 22,23,23 | 22,23,23 |
  | 841 | Untrained teacher | 24 | 69 | 69 | 22,23,23 | 22,23,23 |
  | 841 | Disconnected | 24 | 23 | 46 | 22,0,0 | 22,0,23 |
  | 841 | Lookup mentor | 24 | 69 | 69 | 22,23,23 | 22,23,23 |
  | 842 | Correct teacher | 24 | **70** | **70** | 23,23,23 | 23,23,23 |
  | 842 | Shuffled (invalid quality) | 24 | 70 | 70 | 23,23,23 | 23,23,23 |
  | 842 | Untrained teacher | 24 | 68 | 68 | 21,23,23 | 21,23,23 |
  | 842 | Disconnected | 24 | 22 | 24 | 21,0,0 | 23,0,0 |
  | 842 | Lookup mentor | 24 | 70 | 70 | 23,23,23 | 23,23,23 |

- **Reward opportunities and action results.** In the first 12 training rounds, correct advice produced **5** and **6** correctly rewarded target updates (cue 1/paper, cue 2/scissors: **4/1** for 841; **2/4** for 842) versus **0/0** under disconnection; this passed the early target-update criterion. By 24 rounds, correct advice yielded **12/12** combined target updates versus disconnected **2/0**. Correct arms won **18/24** and **19/24** training games, versus disconnected **7/24** and **6/24**, showing immediate guidance and reward opportunities, not specific retained teaching. At 12 rounds, correct-trained frozen rock/paper/scissors selections on the **69 observed-cue rounds** were **22/24/23** (841) and **23/23/23** (842); each had **23/23/23** cue wins except 841's cue-0 **22/23**. Correct-action mean Q margins on cues 0/1/2 were **+0.151/+0.504/+0.133** and **+0.129/+0.306/+0.421**. All three margin gates passed. Full per-action wins, actions, Q scores, DN counts, and trial records are in the combined artifact; every delivered bit decoded correctly, with at least **4** intended DN spikes in a delivered window.
- **Locked early decision.** At 12 rounds, correct minus disconnected wins were **+46/72** and **+48/72**, above the ≥8 gate. But correct minus shuffled was **0/72 for both**; correct minus untrained was **0/72** (841) and **+2/72** (842), below the locked ≥4 for each. Seed 841 alone therefore fails the early gate even with a valid shuffled control. Seed 842's shuffled control additionally fails its predeclared quality criterion. The non-neural lookup scored **69/72 and 70/72** at 12 rounds, matching correct advice. At 24 rounds correct again tied shuffled in both students, tied untrained in 841, and exceeded untrained by only 2 in 842. Both students' correct policies improved and retained three-action competence without messages, but no trained-teacher-specific *learning-speed* advantage was established. The prior cue-head final failure and the earlier preflight failures remain unchanged. No confirmation or tuning is justified from this result.
- **Reproduction.** `node --check tools/check_rps_teacher_guided_cue_heads_early.mjs`; `node --check tools/complete_rps_teacher_guided_cue_heads_early.mjs`. In a copy lacking both write-once output files, run `node tools/check_rps_teacher_guided_cue_heads_early.mjs` (expected Stage-2 shuffle stop), then `node tools/complete_rps_teacher_guided_cue_heads_early.mjs` (completes only missing arms and final evaluations). The two artifacts preserve the actual interruption and continuation separately. No prior raw file was changed.

### Read-only early advice attribution: generic vetoes can discover rewarded actions

- **Question and sources.** Why did correct, shuffled, and untrained advice converge to almost the same 12-round disconnected accuracy? Analyze the existing interrupted and continuation artifacts for **841/842**, with the earlier 831/832 cue-head attribution as historical context only. No simulator, training, or reward update was run. The continuation retained all completed training rows exactly from the interrupted artifact; every arm has 24 saved training rows, frozen 12- and 24-round rows, and the saved 12-round controller parameters. The first-round cue-free record is excluded from the 11 observed-cue rounds in the following table. `R/P/S` means rock/paper/scissors. `Rewards 0/1/2` count positive-reward, weight-changing updates on each cue's correct action. A helpful change turns a wrong proposal into the winning action; a harmful change turns a winning proposal into a wrong action; other changes remain wrong.

  | Student | Training message | Provisional R/P/S | Played R/P/S | Approve/reject | Changed: helpful/harmful/other | Rewards 0/1/2 | Frozen 12 wins |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | ---: |
  | 841 | Correct | 5/4/2 | 4/6/1 | 4/7 | 7: 4/0/3 | 3/4/1 | 69/72 |
  | 841 | Shuffled | 5/4/2 | 2/5/4 | 6/5 | 5: 2/2/1 | 1/2/1 | 69/72 |
  | 841 | Untrained | 5/4/2 | 3/6/2 | 0/11 | 11: 4/4/3 | 1/2/1 | 69/72 |
  | 841 | Disconnected | 7/2/2 | 7/2/2 | — | 0 | 2/0/0 | 23/72 |
  | 842 | Correct | 6/2/3 | 4/2/5 | 7/4 | 4: 3/0/1 | 3/2/4 | 70/72 |
  | 842 | Shuffled* | 6/2/3 | 6/2/3 | 7/4 | 4: 3/1/0 | 3/2/3 | 70/72 |
  | 842 | Untrained | 5/2/4 | 3/4/4 | 0/11 | 11: 3/5/3 | 1/1/1 | 68/72 |
  | 842 | Disconnected | 7/4/0 | 7/4/0 | — | 0 | 2/0/0 | 22/72 |

- **First rewarded choices and early Q margins.** The first rewarded cue-0/rock, cue-1/paper, cue-2/scissors training rounds (zero-indexed) were **3/2/10** under both correct and shuffled advice for 841, **6/2/10** under untrained advice, and **3/never/never** under disconnection. For 842 they were **3/5/1** under correct and shuffled, **6/5/1** under untrained, and **3/never/never** under disconnection. The first paper and scissors rewards in every message arm came from *changed* actions; neither disconnected arm selected a winning paper or scissors action in its first 12 rounds. The 12-round frozen mean correct-action Q margins by cue 0/1/2 were: 841 correct **+.151/+.504/+.133**, shuffled **+.040/+.318/+.133**, untrained **+.030/+.273/+.133**, disconnected **+.123/0/0**; 842 correct **+.129/+.306/+.421**, shuffled **+.129/+.306/+.323**, untrained **+.019/+.167/+.150**, disconnected **+.108/0/0**. All three message arms thus acquired positive margins on every cue, while disconnected arms did not on cues 1 and 2.
- **How few rewards sufficed in these saved runs.** For 841, cue-2/scissors received **one** correct-action reward in each of the correct, shuffled, and untrained arms, yet all scored **23/23** frozen cue-2 wins with positive margins by round 12. For 842, the untrained arm had **one** correct reward on *each* cue and scored **21/23, 23/23, 23/23**. In these one-reward cases, the saved correct-action `W,b` at round 12 equal exactly that single recorded selected-action update from zero initialization; no other selected action could alter that action's parameters. This establishes that one correct reward was enough **within the accompanying recorded competing-action updates and fixed neural inputs** to yield a dominant correct action at the checkpoint. It does not establish that one reward alone would suffice with a different competing-action trajectory. Only 0-, 12-, and 24-round frozen checkpoints exist; the exact first training round at which any Q margin became positive is unknown.
- **Mechanism and causal limit.** The untrained source emitted **bit 0 on all 23 observed training rounds** for both students. A decoded rejection always changed the proposal by sampling one of the other two actions using the fixed keyed random stream, independently of reward. Thus it acted as systematic exploration rather than learned advice and reached winning paper/scissors actions that disconnected training missed. Shuffled bits were not useless on every trial: in the first 12 rounds they caused **2/3 helpful** changes for 841/842, even though they also caused **2/1 harmful** changes. Correct trained advice was more selective (**4/3 helpful, zero harmful**) and earned more rewards, so it carried useful *immediate action-filtering information*. Yet all three message arms discovered the same winning actions and their cue-specific heads reached near-ceiling disconnected accuracy by 12 rounds. This supports an **exploration-opportunity plus fast cue-head learning** explanation for their similar accuracy. It does not isolate the causal effect of teacher-specific information because the arms did not match actual action-change frequencies or played-action distributions, and their later provisional choices diverged as their Q heads learned.
- **Control validity and next test.** *Seed 842 shuffled advice remains invalid:* its preserved permutation moved 8/23 bits but its balanced quality was **61.7%**, **1.7 percentage points** above the locked ≤60% ceiling. Its 70/72 score is descriptive; no valid counterfactual score for a different shuffle is available. Seed **841** had a valid 43.6% shuffle, and correct, shuffled, and untrained arms still tied **69/72**, independently ruling out the locked +4/72 teacher-specific early advantage for that student. The original two-student early-teaching result remains **failed**, and the 842 controlled comparison remains incomplete. If revisited, the single smallest prospective test is to add one **cue-stratified, rejection-count-yoked, message-independent veto control** to the same 12-round matched study: fix the number of vetoes per cue from a separate calibration schedule, permute their round positions independently of proposals and rewards, deliver them through the unchanged DN receiver, and keep the same own-reward Q updates, initial states, streams, and frozen disconnected evaluation. Predeclare an action-distribution matching check and compare correct advice against this control plus shuffled, untrained, and disconnected arms. This would test whether trained advice adds value beyond a matched amount of generic exploration; no such experiment was run.
- **Derived artifact / reproduction.** Write-once `data/rps_teacher_guided_cue_heads_early_attribution.json` contains per-cue provisional/played action counts, advice effects, first correct rewards, rewarded cue/action updates, 12- and 24-round Q-margin ranges and frozen outcomes, and explicit limitations. `node --check tools/analyze_rps_teacher_guided_cue_heads_early.mjs` passed. Reproduce read-only in a copy without the derived file using `node tools/analyze_rps_teacher_guided_cue_heads_early.mjs`. The script verifies saved completed-arm identity, physical DN bit decoding, frozen score reconstruction, and unchanged saved checkpoints; it refuses overwrite. No historical artifact or gate changed.

### Locked rejection-count-matched veto experiment (; before training)

- **Hypothesis.** Correct teacher 830 neural advice may improve disconnected student learning beyond generic exploration because it selectively rejects poor provisional actions while preserving promising ones. Prior failed teacher-advantage gates remain failed. Fresh, preselected students are **851 and 852**; searches of research files, runner seed records, explicit saved JSON seed fields, and filenames found no previous use. No replacement is allowed.
- **Fixed task and five arms.** Reuse Strategy B `next=(previous+2)%3`, training opponent seed **41000**, exactly **12 rounds** per arm: correct trained-teacher DN advice, generic cue-stratified veto, untrained-teacher DN advice (always reject), shuffled trained-teacher DN advice, and disconnected. Initial all-zero cue-specific Q heads, all-one KC→MBON multipliers, trained teacher 830's saved *continuous neural-state* 24-round source trace (first 12 used here), original one-bit approval rule, actual DNge175 3141/3165 counts at 300 Hz 240–280 ms, VPN-spike cue routing before the pulse, student-only between-round neural reset, MBON normalization, epsilon **0.20**, alpha **0.03**, and selected-action own-reward Q update remain unchanged. The generic veto is a **non-teacher-generated control bit physically delivered through the same DN spike interface**; it carries no learned teacher Q, action label, reward, or hidden strategy. It uses the student's *own* premessage VPN-derived head, never the environment cue integer, to select a fixed cue-occurrence veto position. Rejection invokes the unchanged keyed sample of the other two actions.
- **Leakage-free generic allocation, fixed now.** Independent archived **831/832** correct-advice calibration had first-12-round cue counts **3/4/4** and rejection counts **0/3/2** and **1/3/4** by cues 0/1/2. The componentwise half-up mean gives generic quotas **1/3/3**. For each student/cue, shuffle cue-occurrence ordinals `1..[3,4,4][cue]` with `rng(2026110701 + 10×studentSeed + cue)` and reject the first quota ordinals. The resulting sorted reject positions are fixed **851: cue 0 [2], cue 1 [1,2,3], cue 2 [1,3,4]**; **852: cue 0 [3], cue 1 [1,2,3], cue 2 [1,2,4]**. These were generated before student training, independently of 851/852 teacher messages, proposals, and rewards. If a cue route is wrong or a cue count differs, fail validity; do not use a true cue to repair it. Quotas match *calibration*, not unknowable future correct-arm counts. Report any realized mismatch.
- **Streams and checkpoints.** Training round key `studentSeed×10000000 + round`; neural `rng(key+2)`, proposal `rng(key+501)`, rejection alternative `rng(key+701)`. Correct-arm bits are Fisher–Yates shuffled over the **11 observed-cue rounds** with `rng(2026110601+studentSeed)` for the shuffled arm, preserving the cue-free null; no shuffle is selected after outcomes. All arms share initial weights, Strategy B cue/reward schedule, and keyed streams. Evaluate frozen, exploration-free and disconnected policies at **0, 6, 12** training rounds on the same 72 held-out games (opponent seeds **52000/52003/52004**, episode keys 100–102). At round 6, evaluate copied controller parameters in independent simulator/game instances and verify that live controller/baseline, neural arrays/time, game/reward state, and RNG references are exactly unchanged before resuming. No extra checkpoints.
- **Technical and control-validity gates.** Archived teacher 830 must retain its passed source record (64/72 frozen wins, 24 real-neural Q rows) and the prior reset-receiver fixture must remain passed. Every delivered correct, shuffled, untrained, or generic bit must decode from actual DN spikes with intended-target dominance. VPN routing must match observed cues on **11/11 training** and **69/69 frozen** rounds for each arm/student, with cue-free first round. Every reward and selected-head Q update must match the unchanged equation; other heads and KC→MBON multipliers remain fixed; all frozen parameters remain unchanged. Initial frozen wins must be **≤36/72**. Generic realized quota must equal **1/3/3** by the student's estimated cues, and its per-cue rejection-count difference versus the correct arm must be **≤1** at rounds 6 and 12 (total difference **≤2** at 12). As an action-distribution check, each of rock/paper/scissors must differ by **≤3 selections** between correct and generic over the 11 observed training rounds; a larger gap invalidates a matched-action-distribution interpretation. Shuffled bits must preserve counts, move **≥2/11** positions, and have **≤60%** balanced advice quality on shuffled-arm proposals. A failed validity gate is reported, never tuned or rerun.
- **Primary success at round 6.** For **both** students, correct-trained frozen disconnected wins must exceed generic, shuffled, and untrained arms each by **≥4/72**, exceed disconnected by **≥8/72**, and produce **≥1 additional** correctly rewarded, weight-changing cue-1/paper or cue-2/scissors update versus disconnected in the first 6 rounds. All technical/control gates must pass. Round-12 accuracy and cue-specific Q margins are secondary and reported regardless of the round-6 result. Immediate training wins and message-induced corrections cannot substitute for disconnected accuracy. If any technical preflight or checkpoint noninvasiveness check fails, stop; otherwise run each fixed arm once, report all control failures, and stop without confirmation or tuning. Save one new write-once artifact with trial rows, DN counts, realized rejections, action distributions, rewarded updates, controller snapshots, and frozen evaluations.

### Matched-veto result: control count drift and no all-control advantage

- **Execution and validity.** Ran only predetermined students **851/852**, five arms each, exactly 12 training rounds per arm and frozen disconnected checkpoints at **0/6/12**. Archived teacher 830 source, receiving fixture, and calibration prerequisites passed. Every delivered bit decoded from the actual DNge175 spike counts with intended-target dominance (minimum **5** spikes for 851, **4** for 852); generic bits were physically delivered through this receiver but came from the fixed non-teacher schedule. VPN routing passed **11/11** training and **69/69** per frozen checkpoint, cue-free first rounds were separate, normalization and matched neural/opponent streams verified, all selected-head reward updates matched the unchanged equation, other heads and KC→MBON weights stayed fixed, and 6-round evaluations did not alter live neural/controller/game/reward or RNG-reference state. Initial frozen wins were **24/72** for both. The shuffles moved **4/11** and **2/11** bits, preserved counts, and scored **41.1%/53.3%** balanced advice quality, passing their locked checks. Generic action-distribution differences stayed within the predeclared ±3-per-action tolerance.
- **Generic control mismatch.** The generic veto used exactly its predeclared **1/3/3** rejections by own estimated cues 0/1/2. Correct trained advice rejected only **0/2/2** for **both** 851 and 852 over 12 rounds. Each per-cue difference was one, but the total was **7 versus 4**, a difference of **3** exceeding the locked **≤2** total mismatch gate. At round 6, correct rejections were **0/1/2**; generic was **0/2/1** (851) and **0/2/2** (852), within the per-cue gate. Because the full 12-round rejection-count match failed, the experiment's **matched-veto control validity failed**. The calibration quotas were not recalculated from these new teacher choices; no arm was rerun. Correct and generic played-action counts across 11 observed training rounds were **3/4/4 versus 6/3/2** (851) and **3/4/4 versus 4/4/3** (852); matching veto counts alone would not imply identical action distributions.
- **Frozen disconnected wins/72.** All rows use the same 72 held-out games, with exploration and messages disabled. The 6-round checkpoint is primary; 12 rounds are secondary.

  | Student | Training arm | 0 rounds | 6 rounds | 12 rounds | 6-round cue wins 0/1/2 |
  | --- | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | --- |
  | 851 | Correct teacher | 24 | **70** | 70 | 23/23/23 |
  | 851 | Generic veto | 24 | 47 | 70 | 23/23/0 |
  | 851 | Untrained teacher | 24 | 70 | 70 | 23/23/23 |
  | 851 | Shuffled teacher | 24 | 47 | 70 | 23/0/23 |
  | 851 | Disconnected | 24 | 24 | 47 | 23/0/0 |
  | 852 | Correct teacher | 24 | **70** | 70 | 23/23/23 |
  | 852 | Generic veto | 24 | 70 | 70 | 23/23/23 |
  | 852 | Untrained teacher | 24 | 70 | 70 | 23/23/23 |
  | 852 | Shuffled teacher | 24 | 47 | 47 | 23/23/0 |
  | 852 | Disconnected | 24 | 24 | 24 | 23/0/0 |

- **Reward opportunity and value margins.** In the first six rounds, correct advice produced **3** correctly rewarded, weight-changing target updates for each student (cue-1/paper **2**, cue-2/scissors **1**) versus **0** disconnected, passing that individual gate. Generic produced **1** (851) and **2** (852); untrained produced **2** for each. Of *rejected* proposals, correct advice led to a rewarded target action on **2/3** rejections per student, generic on **1/3** (851) and **2/4** (852). Correct vetoes made **2 helpful, 0 harmful** action changes per student at six rounds; generic made **1 helpful, 1 harmful** (851) and **2 helpful, 1 harmful** (852). These are small, descriptive counts; quota drift and diverging action choices prevent a clean per-rejection causal estimate. At six rounds, mean correct-action Q margins by cues 0/1/2 were **+.031/+.279/+.138** (851 correct), **+.031/+.140/0** (851 generic), **+.038/+.287/+.096** (852 correct), and **+.038/+.161/+.096** (852 generic). Correct trained advice kept somewhat larger cue-1 margins even where accuracy tied. By 12 rounds, correct margins were **+.098/+.284/+.357** and **+.097/+.409/+.300**; generic margins were **+.065/+.279/+.167** and **+.076/+.268/+.203**. The raw file contains all arms' cue/action rewarded updates, 6/12-round margins, controller snapshots, actions, rewards, and DN counts.
- **Locked decision / limits.** At the primary six-round checkpoint, correct exceeded generic by **+23/72** for 851 but **0/72** for 852; it tied untrained at **70/72 for both**, below the required **+4/72**. It beat shuffled by **+23/72** and disconnected by **+46/72** for both and had additional target updates. Thus **neither student passed every primary comparison**, even before the failed generic total-count validity gate is applied. At 12 rounds, generic caught up to correct for 851 and remained tied for 852; untrained also remained tied. The data show real teacher advice made more selective immediate choices, but **no trained-teacher-specific retained-learning advantage** over generic exploration or all message controls. The fixed calibration quota did not match the new teacher rejection frequency; that is an additional control limitation, not grounds to retune. No independent confirmation is justified from this failed experiment. Prior negative results and criteria remain unchanged. Stop this mechanism for now; no further run or parameter adjustment followed.
- **Raw result / reproduction.** Write-once `data/rps_teacher_guided_matched_veto.json` stores the locked protocol, calibration, every training/frozen trial, actual DN counts, own-VPN cue route and generic veto ordinal, rewards and Q updates, controller snapshots, realized rejection counts, action distributions, and all gates. `node --check tools/check_rps_teacher_guided_matched_veto.mjs` and read-only saved-result validity checks passed. Reproduce in a copy without the output artifact using `node tools/check_rps_teacher_guided_matched_veto.mjs`; the runner refuses overwrite. No original model or historical result file changed.

### Design review: reward-gated learning on a stationary noisy RPS opponent (; no experiment)

- **Evidence and bottleneck.** The current deterministic Strategy B opponent makes the correct action for each of just three observed cues fixed and always rewarded. Each cue-specific `Q_a(f)=b_a+W_a·f` head starts at zero and applies `Δb_a=0.03(r−Q_a)`, `ΔW_a=0.03(r−Q_a)f` only to the played action. One win at feature `f` gives that action `Q_a(f′)=0.03(1+f·f′)` before any later update; wrong actions receive no positive reward and may be driven below zero. Independent cue heads eliminate direct cross-cue parameter interference, and the VPN route supplies a highly reliable cue. In the saved runs, one correct reward in several heads coincided with positive frozen margins and 21–23/23 cue wins. Once any advice arm discovers paper and scissors, frozen accuracy can saturate, masking better teacher selectivity. The trained teacher did make fewer harmful immediate changes, but generic veto/untrained advice also exposed rewarding actions. The latest matched-veto run additionally missed its rejection-count validity gate. None of these failures is revised.
- **One fixed task.** Propose the *existing* `Opponent({strategy:'previous_opponent', offset:2, noise:0.5})`: after the observed previous move `c`, the base next move remains `(c+2)%3`, but on half of rounds the existing opponent code draws a uniform random move. The expected own reward for the best, tie, and losing action is respectively **+0.5, 0, −0.5**; the best action wins with probability **2/3**, and each other action can win by chance with probability **1/6**. Cue information remains observable and an optimal policy exists. This fixed noise value is chosen analytically to create misleading positive rewards; it has **not** been tested or tuned. Do not use the old deterministic 58/72 win gate on this stochastic task; score optimal-action choices by cue using the known opponent distribution **for analysis only**, and report realized wins separately. No hidden mapping identity is given to either fly.
- **Alternatives considered.** **A, hard teacher-guided exploration:** on the noisy task it could improve the mix of sampled rewards, but the prior all-reject arm and failed veto matching show that action-distribution differences remain a serious confound. **B, teacher-biased training action preferences:** a soft approval-dependent preference might avoid forced alternatives, yet still changes played actions and rewards, making generic exploration difficult to match; the teacher-dependent term must vanish or be distilled for disconnected retention. **C, selected proposal (one design):** leave the student's training action unchanged and use the measured DN approval bit only to gate a *positive own-reward* update. At 240 ms Fly A judges Fly B's public proposed action using its own reward-trained cue-specific Q values, sends the existing one-bit approval via real DNge175 spikes, and Fly B retains the two measured spike counts. Fly B plays **the same proposed action regardless of bit**, receives its own RPS reward at 280 ms, and at 620 ms applies the ordinary selected-action cue-head update multiplied by `g=0` only when `reward=+1` and the decoded bit is rejection; otherwise `g=1`. Thus a teacher-rejected lucky win cannot reinforce that action, while negative/zero evidence and approved own rewards update normally. The bit supplies neither a correct action nor a replacement reward. The teacher can be wrong and suppress a useful win. This is a **new opt-in hybrid student learning rule**, not a claim about the unchanged original dopamine-only fly; biological KC→MBON dopamine plasticity, connectome, spiking dynamics, and default policy remain untouched. Frozen evaluation uses the student's own cue-only heads with no message or gate.
- **Why C is the strongest test.** Use the same fixed, label-free balanced action-exposure schedule for every training arm, derived only from Fly B's own VPN cue estimate and a predeclared random stream. The existing balanced-action mechanism can be adapted in the opt-in runner; no correct action or reward chooses an exposure. With matched opponent draws, **played actions, received rewards, cue sequence, and neural input streams can be identical across correct, shuffled, untrained, generic-gate, and disconnected arms**. Any retained difference then comes from which *own-reward updates* the teacher bit allows, not from extra exploration. A generic gate should have the same cue-specific number of suppressed positive-reward updates, assigned by a predetermined shuffle across positive-reward events without using optimal-action labels; the matched counts and broken candidate association must be verified before interpreting a future teaching test. This gate is an experimental control, not a fly signal. Direct MBON×DN reward learning is less suitable for disconnected retention because its DN interaction terms disappear when communication stops; the earlier **93.1% supervised offline decoding** does not establish a reward-trained student or a viable single-teacher mixed-strategy source.
- **First staged feasibility test, before any teacher–student training.** Proposed fixed independent seeds: **861/862** for two teachers and **863/864** for two no-message baseline students, subject to confirming unused status before implementation; no seed selection by outcome. Each teacher and baseline student would receive **12×24** own-reward training rounds on the fixed noisy task with the same fixed label-free balanced exposure rule and existing cue-specific heads; teachers are never given the base action or noise draw. Frozen no-message evaluation is 72 independent rounds with at least **15 observed trials per cue** or the fixed stream is declared invalid without replacement. Task learnability passes only if **both** no-message students choose the expected-optimal action on **≥70% of held-out trials for each cue**, with **≥20 percentage-point overall gain** over their initial frozen policy. Teacher learning passes only if **both** teachers choose the expected-optimal action on **≥75% per cue** in frozen evaluation. Then, without updating either teacher, collect a predeclared **288-round held-out source trace** per teacher; compute the fixed approval bit from each real MBON-driven Q vector for balanced public candidate actions. Source information passes only if **each teacher** attains **≥80% balanced approval classification per cue** for expected-optimal versus other candidate actions and **≥15 percentage points** over within-cue shuffled and untrained-bit controls. For the specific update-gating rationale, require at least **four actually rewarded suboptimal candidate actions per cue** in the fixed held-out trace and teacher rejection of **≥75%** of those misleading wins while approving **≥75%** of rewarded optimal actions. True optimality is used only for scoring, never as fly input or bit construction. Finally, a frozen forced-bit DN/cue-representation check must pass the existing **12/12 bit decoding** and **≥10/12 per-cue recognition** floors for the predetermined receivers before student teaching. Failure at any prerequisite stops this route without changing noise, budget, source, or gates. Only a separate prospectively locked pilot could then test retained learning against matched generic-gate, shuffled, untrained, and disconnected controls. No source assay, baseline, receiving probe, training, simulation, or code change was performed in this review.
- **Conclusion / next experiment.** C is a specific falsifiable alternative because stochastic rewards create positive but misleading updates and the teacher's learned approval can, in principle, filter them while leaving student action experience identical across controls. Its empirical prerequisites—teacher learning and source selectivity, independent student learnability, sufficient misleading wins, and DN/cue compatibility—are **unknown**. The smallest next experiment is the staged no-teaching feasibility test above, starting with the no-message learnability and independent-teacher source gates; do not implement the gated update or launch teaching until those pass.

### Noisy reward-gating prerequisite: protocol-completeness stop (; no simulation)

- **Hypothesis and intended experiment.** The preceding fixed `noise=0.5` design would first test independent student learnability, then independent teacher learning, source selectivity, misleading rewarded actions, and DN receiving compatibility. Seeds 861–864, 12×24 training rounds, 72 frozen rounds, a 288-round source trace, and numerical success gates were recovered unchanged. This is a protocol audit, not a run of those stages.
- **Static environment check.** `js/rps.js` computes the base move from the *actual previous opponent move* and then applies the fixed random-move noise before recording the resulting move as the next cue. At noise 0.5, the base next move has probability 2/3 and each other move 1/6, so all three cues are reachable, the conditional rule is stationary, and a suboptimal action can sometimes win. Finite cue coverage and misleading-win counts require locked streams and were **not measured**. Neither fly would receive the base move or noise draw from the `Opponent` API if only ordinary observations and rewards are routed.
- **Blocking omissions found before data collection.** The design does not fix the training/evaluation/source-trace opponent seeds or keyed random streams; the exact label-free balanced action-exposure algorithm, including the first cue-free round; the neural-state reset rule for noisy training and frozen replay; the 288-round candidate schedule; or the predetermined receiver identities and forced-bit streams. The generic `js/rps.js` balanced-action utility uses `obs.previousOpponent` directly, whereas the design requires routing from the fly's own VPN-derived cue estimate. Earlier deterministic protocols offer several different stream and reset conventions, so silently inheriting one would add an unrecorded design choice. The proposed seed numbers remain untested and unselected by results.
- **Result and controls.** Per the attached instruction to stop if essential settings are missing, **zero students or teachers were trained, zero neural probes were run, and no held-out outcomes were inspected**. All learning, advice-quality, opportunity, and receiving gates are **not evaluated**; no positive or negative teaching conclusion follows. Previous failed experiments and gates remain unchanged. The write-once audit artifact is `data/rps_noisy_reward_gate_prerequisite_protocol_audit.json`. Reproduce this read-only audit by inspecting this section and `js/rps.js` `Opponent.move/play` plus `balancedActions`; there is no simulation reproduction command. A complete prospective protocol is needed before this feasibility experiment can run.

### Locked completion: noisy reward-gating prerequisites (; recorded before simulation)

- **Unchanged hypothesis and gates.** Test the previously proposed stationary `Opponent({strategy:'previous_opponent',offset:2,noise:0.5})` with teacher seeds **861/862**, independent no-message student seeds **863/864**, each at **12 episodes × 24 rounds**. The first round of each opponent episode has no previous-move cue. The 72-round frozen evaluation has three 24-round games and requires **≥15 observed trials per cue** or stops without replacement. Both students must select the expected-optimal action on **≥70% per cue** and gain **≥20 percentage points overall** against their own initial frozen policy, scored over the 69 observed-cue rounds. Both teachers must attain **≥75% expected-optimal action per cue**. Each teacher's fixed approval bit must attain **≥80% balanced optimal-candidate classification per cue** and exceed both within-cue shuffled and untrained controls by **≥15 percentage points**. The source trace must contain **≥4 rewarded suboptimal candidate actions per cue**, reject **≥75%** of those, and approve **≥75%** of rewarded optimal candidates. Forced DN decoding must reach **12/12** and ordinary MBON cue recognition **≥10/12 per cue**. These are the earlier numerical gates, unchanged. Expected optimality and noise outcomes are offline analysis labels only.
- **Recovered implementation.** Reuse `RpsContextualBandit` with its three standardized `log1p` MBON rates, `alpha=.03`, `epsilon=.20`, selected-action own-reward Q update, zero initial Q, and frozen all-one KC→MBON multipliers. Reuse the opt-in four cue-head wrapper and VPN-spike head route from `tools/check_rps_teacher_guided_cue_heads.mjs`, including a cue-free head. Stimulate only `net.laneVpn[cue].slice(2)` at 150 Hz from 0–240 ms, reset spike counts at 50 ms, compute MBON rates and the VPN-derived head at 240 ms, play against the opponent and receive own reward at 280 ms, reset ordinary spike counts then, update Q at 620 ms when training, and end at 680 ms. Student neural state resets with `Network.resetState()` immediately after a completed 680 ms round and before the next cue; Q heads and baseline persist. Teacher neural state stays continuous through each 24-round training episode and through its entire 288-round frozen source trace; a new training episode starts a new neural state, retaining Q. No reset occurs between a cue and any message. Frozen evaluations use no exploration or Q learning. This inherits the prior schedule and validated reset boundary without changing the simulator or original game.
- **Newly fixed opponent and random streams.** For agent seed `s` and training episode `e=0..11`, the causal noisy opponent has seed `41000+1000*s+e`. Initial and final frozen evaluations use the *same* three independent opponent seeds `52000+1000*s+3*i`, `i=0,1,2`, and episode keys `100+i`. The 288-round teacher source uses one opponent seeded `63000+1000*s` and one uninterrupted neural state, episode key `200`. Thus training, evaluation, and source opponent streams are disjoint; no realized sequence is selected by outcome. For every round `r`, let `key=s*10000000+episodeKey*1000+r`; set network noise RNG to `rng(key+2)` and controller action RNG to `rng(key+501)`. Student and teacher wiring use `wiring(sub,'real',s)` and `Network(...,{seed:s})`. These key formulas reuse the prior teacher-guided stream convention with newly fixed, nonoverlapping episode identifiers.
- **Newly fixed label-free training exposure.** At 240 ms the fly first invokes the unchanged cue-head `decide(net)` to obtain real MBON features and a VPN-spike-derived head. For each of the three observed heads, maintain an episode-local occurrence counter initialized to zero. Override the training action with `counter[head]++ % 3` (rock, paper, scissors cycling separately within the **estimated** head), then set the agent's action, value, and eligibility for that actually played action before its own reward and the existing 620 ms update. For the cue-free first round retain the ordinary sampled action. The counter never reads `game.observe().previousOpponent`, the expected-optimal action, next opponent move, or reward. The true previous opponent move is used only to drive the appropriate VPN sensory neurons; if the VPN-derived head is wrong, record it and do not correct the training action. Frozen evaluation never forces actions. This is the prior balanced-action cycle applied to the existing sensory-derived head rather than the generic `js/rps.js` path that directly indexes the opponent observation.
- **Newly fixed source candidates and controls.** In each teacher's 288-round held-out source trace, compute its fixed approval bit `1{Q_candidate>max(Q_other two)}` from its own real MBON-driven Q scores at 240 ms. The candidate is a public proposal, with no proposal on the first cue-free round. For each VPN-derived cue head, independently Fisher–Yates shuffle `[0,1,2]` once with `rng(73000+1000*s+cue)` before the trace, then cycle through that fixed permutation by head-occurrence ordinal. The teacher plays its **own** frozen greedy action at 280 ms against the causal noisy opponent; `result(candidate,opponentMove)` is calculated offline to score a possible student reward, without updating the teacher. Record actual candidate, bit, true cue, teacher Q margins, opponent move, and candidate reward. Balanced accuracy per true cue is `(approval sensitivity for expected-optimal candidates + rejection specificity for others)/2`. The untrained zero-Q control emits bit 0. For the shuffled control, perform exactly **1000** fixed within-true-cue bit shuffles with `rng(74000+1000*s+cue)`; compare the trained balanced accuracy against the **95th percentile** shuffle score. This percentile convention is a conservative new specification of the previously locked 15-point advantage, not a changed threshold. No analytical decoder is fitted.
- **Newly fixed receiver identities and stop order.** Only if the environment, both student gates, both teacher gates, and source/opportunity gates pass, use frozen receiver seeds **863/864** with all-one synapses and zero Q heads. Probe every `(receiver,cue,repeat,branch)` for receivers 863/864, first cues 0/1/2, repeats 0–11, and branches no message/forced bit 0/forced bit 1: **216** predetermined, matched **two-round** probes. Each probe starts with a neural-state reset; the first round presents cue `cue` and the forced bit, ends at 680 ms, then a student-only `Network.resetState()` occurs before the second 680 ms round, which presents the fixed next cue `(cue+2)%3` with no message. These are frozen receiving probes, not sampled opponent-game transitions. First-round key is `key=s*10000000+(300+12*cue+repeat)*1000`, second-round key is `key+1`, common to all branches; network RNG is `rng(key+2)` and action RNG `rng(key+501)` per round. Use the existing 3141/3165 DN targets at 300 Hz during 240–280 ms, the existing separate actual-spike count buffer, and the fixed three-MBON `log1p` nearest-centroid cue decoder from `data/rps_readout_information.json`; do not refit it. Require intended-target mean gain ≥1 spike versus matched no-message, target-dominant **12/12** bit decoding per receiver/first-cue/bit, **≥10/12** recognition of both first and second cues per receiver/cue/branch, and at most 2/12 recognition lost relative to no message; verify weights unchanged. This preserves the earlier DN receiving and between-round reset criteria. The DN buffer is read at 280 ms and retained through the reset for an availability check; the teacher source state is never reset per cue.
- **Causal audit and execution rule.** The source bit uses only teacher Q scores and the public candidate. The next noisy opponent move is sampled only when `Opponent.play` occurs at 280 ms, after action selection; no hidden mapping or noise draw enters either controller. Balanced training actions and source candidates use only VPN-estimated heads and fixed counters/permutations; true cue and expected-optimal action are offline scoring fields. The four stream families are disjoint by documented identifiers and fixed before inspection. Run one opt-in write-once runner, stop at the first failed prerequisite, preserve every earlier negative result, and never implement reward-update gating or train a teacher-assisted student in this session. Reproduction command, result file, and any measured gates will be appended only after execution.
- **Pre-execution code audit.** `tools/check_rps_noisy_reward_gate_prerequisites.mjs` now implements only these stages in this order. Its training action override indexes the `CueBandit.activeHead` obtained from simulated VPN spike counts; the true cue is used to drive VPNs and subsequently score results, and the precomputed opponent trace is compared **after** `Opponent.play` rather than supplied to action selection. The teacher's bit is computed from `agent.scores` and its fixed public candidate only. Neural and controller streams use separate fixed episode identifiers; frozen evaluation opponent seeds differ from training and source seeds. Student `resetState()` occurs only after each completed round, while the teacher source trace has no between-cue reset. `node --check tools/check_rps_noisy_reward_gate_prerequisites.mjs` passed. No simulation or training had run when this paragraph was recorded.

### Noisy reward-gating prerequisites: one locked run stops at receiving

- **Hypothesis / experiment.** The fixed noisy opponent could supply misleading positive rewards that trained teacher approval might selectively reject. Ran the single opt-in staged runner above, with no student reward gate and no teacher-assisted student training. The previous protocol-completeness stop remains intact; this is its separately completed prospective execution. New write-once raw result: `data/rps_noisy_reward_gate_prerequisites.json`.
- **Stage 1 environment.** The fixed opponent streams visited all cues. Across 288 training rounds, cue counts were **88/96/92** (student 863), **92/87/97** (864), **90/90/96** (teacher 861), and **87/87/102** (862); observed noise deviations from the base move were **82, 83, 98, 90**. Each 72-round frozen stream met the predeclared ≥15-per-cue coverage: respectively **23/21/25**, **31/18/20**, **24/20/25**, and **24/22/23**. The underlying offset-2 and noise-0.5 rule did not change; the actual prior opponent move became the next cue. These environment checks passed, while the later source trace measured whether misleading wins were sufficiently numerous.
- **Stage 2 independent students.** Frozen initial expected-optimal selections over the 69 observed-cue rounds were **23/69** for 863 and **31/69** for 864; after their fixed 12×24 own-reward training they were **69/69 and 69/69**, gains of **+66.7 and +55.1 percentage points**. Each cue was 100% expected-optimal in final frozen evaluation, clearing the locked ≥70% floor. Actual stochastic wins changed **25/72→50/72** and **22/72→48/72**, respectively. VPN cue-head routing was **69/69** for each final evaluation; frozen controller and KC→MBON weights were unchanged. This task is learnable without a teacher at the fixed budget, but the final policies reached a ceiling, limiting any later final-accuracy teaching comparison. Early frozen learning speed was not tested here.
- **Stage 3 independent teachers.** After their own separate 12×24 reward-training runs, teacher 861 chose the expected-optimal action on **24/24, 20/20, 25/25** cue-0/1/2 frozen trials and won **48/72** actual rounds. Teacher 862 scored **24/24, 22/22, 23/23**, winning **51/72**. All cue-specific Q margins favored the expected-optimal action; mean margins for 861 were **+0.474/+0.607/+0.377**, for 862 **+0.469/+0.441/+0.537**. Their neural state remained continuous within each training episode, controller/synaptic weights stayed frozen during evaluation, and the ≥75%-per-cue teacher gate passed.
- **Stages 4–5 source and reward opportunities.** Each teacher then ran one 288-round, continuous-neural-state, frozen source trace with its fixed balanced public candidate schedule. Both bit values occurred in every cue. Trained approval classified expected-optimal versus other candidates at **100% balanced accuracy for every teacher/cue**; the fixed within-cue shuffled 95th-percentile scores were **57.1–59.1%**, and zero-Q untrained advice was **50%**. Source gates passed. Teacher 861 encountered **9/15/12** rewarded suboptimal candidates by cue and rejected **all**; teacher 862 encountered **12/11/7** and rejected **all**. They approved all rewarded optimal candidates (**23/18/21** and **29/21/21**). Every cue exceeded the locked four-opportunity, 75%-rejection, and 75%-approval thresholds. These are offline candidate-reward calculations after real opponent moves, not student update suppression or evidence of teaching.
- **Stage 6 receiver — locked failure.** All **216** frozen matched two-round probes retained weights and the measured DN count buffer. Intended DN mean gains versus no message ranged **+6.92 to +8.25 spikes**; bit decoding and target dominance were **12/12 for every receiver/first-cue/forced-bit condition**. The no-large-message-loss gate passed: every first- and second-cue recognition count was identical across no-message, forced-0, and forced-1 branches. But the absolute **≥10/12** sensory-recognition gate failed in two baseline conditions: receiver **863, first cue 1 → second cue 0**, recognized the second cue **9/12** under every branch; receiver **864, first cue 0**, recognized the first cue **8/12** under every branch. All other first/second cue conditions reached at least 10/12. The forced bit arrives after the first 240 ms cue readout, and the student-only 680 ms reset removes neural carryover before the second cue; this frozen check cannot measure trained-policy use of the message. The unchanged recognition counts do **not** support DN-induced interference here. Do not select only favorable receivers or lower the absolute floor.
- **Conclusion / controls / reproduction.** Environment, independent student, independent teacher, source-information, and misleading-win gates passed; the **overall prerequisite failed at receiver cue recognition**. The teacher advice was compared with fixed within-cue shuffles and untrained Q; disconnected no-message receiving branches were matched. No reward gate or teacher-assisted student training was run. The task's 69/69 independent student ceiling is an additional limitation for a future final-accuracy claim. Reproduce in a copy with the write-once result file absent using `node tools/check_rps_noisy_reward_gate_prerequisites.mjs`; the runner refuses overwrite. The archived negative teaching and receiver gates remain unchanged. The next step is to examine whether a separately prospective receiving protocol can meet the absolute baseline floor; no retuning or follow-up simulation was performed here.

### Saved-data diagnostic: early headroom for noisy reward gating (; read-only)

- **Hypothesis / scope.** If misleading positive rewards arrive while a cue's Q preference is still weak, suppressing such updates might have an early learning role. Analyzed only `data/rps_noisy_reward_gate_prerequisites.json`; no neural simulation, training, weight reconstruction, reward gate, or threshold change. Natural fixed analysis windows were training rounds **1–24**, **1–72**, and **1–288**. The archive has each training action, reward, VPN-estimated head, and **pre-action** three Q scores, plus only **initial and final** frozen evaluations. It does **not** contain intermediate frozen checkpoints, full intermediate controller snapshots, or trained teacher neural observations aligned to either student's training trials. Balanced training actions were forced, so training wins and online Q margins are not frozen policy accuracy.
- **Student rewards and online Q.** Student **863** received rewarded expected-optimal actions on **5** of the first 24 rounds, **15** of the first 72, and **70** of all 288; corresponding misleading positive rewards for suboptimal actions were **2, 8, and 27**. Student **864** had **4, 17, 61** rewarded optimal actions and **1, 3, 28** misleading positive rewards. Every misleading positive reward caused a positive selected-action Q step under the saved pre-action score and unchanged `0.03×(reward−Q_played)` rule. On the 276 observed-cue training rounds per student, VPN-head routing was **276/276**. The correct-action pre-action Q margin was positive on **5/23, 29/69, 229/276** observed trials for 863 across those windows and **7/23, 40/69, 215/276** for 864. These margins show within-training preference development only; they cannot locate the first held-out frozen success or show that 69/69 was reached early.
- **Chronology by cue.** First rewarded expected-optimal training rounds (1-indexed) were **863: cue 0/1/2 = 24/12/9**, **864: 37/9/13**. First misleading positive rounds were **863: 42/10/21**, **864: 112/34/7**. Only **one** misleading reward preceded that cue's first rewarded optimal action for each student: 863 cue 1 at round 10 and 864 cue 2 at round 7. Across all training, **6/27** of 863's and **4/28** of 864's misleading wins occurred when the pre-action correct-action Q margin was nonpositive; the others occurred after a positive margin on that specific input. This margin is a local proxy, **not** proof a cue was already reliably learned. Notably, 864's cue 0 waited until round 37 for a rewarded optimal action but had **no** misleading positive reward before then; a gate that only suppresses misleading positive updates could not supply the missing optimal reward.
- **Teacher source, not trial-matched advice.** In each teacher's **independent frozen source trace**, candidates cycled approximately equally among actions per cue. For teacher 861, the first **24/72/288** source rounds contained **3/9/36** misleading rewarded proposals, all rejected, and **6/17/62** rewarded optimal proposals, all approved. Teacher 862 had **0/7/30** misleading rewarded proposals, all rejected, and **5/19/71** rewarded optimal proposals, all approved. The archive does **not** supply these teachers' MBON-driven Q scores on the students' own training trials. Therefore no actual student training reward can be labeled as a teacher-approved or teacher-rejected update; source-trace selectivity does not establish an aligned gating effect or a counterfactual improved student trajectory.
- **Receiver failure mechanism and limits.** The two failing baseline probe sets had intact VPN-derived cue routing (**12/12** for each branch). Receiver **863**, cue 1→0, missed fixed MBON-centroid recognition on second-cue repeats **1, 6, 10** (9/12); receiver **864**, first cue 0, missed repeats **3, 5, 7, 10** (8/12). The *same repeat IDs* failed under no-message, forced-bit-0, and forced-bit-1. The first cue was classified before DN stimulation; the second followed a full student-only `resetState()`. This supports baseline variability of the fixed MBON cue decoder in these probes and gives no evidence of DN interference or carried neural state as the cause. The archive does not compare another timing schedule, so a general timing limitation is unresolved. Perfect MBON-centroid recognition is not logically required for a cue-head learner when its VPN route is correct, but the earlier **absolute 10/12 receiver gate remains failed** and cannot be relaxed here.
- **Conclusion / single next step.** There were early candidate updates a selective teacher might suppress (**11** misleading positives across both students in the first 72 rounds), but only two preceded the first rewarded optimal update for their cues, and no frozen early performance or aligned teacher advice was saved. The students eventually reached **69/69** without a teacher. The archive therefore does **not** establish measurable early learning headroom attributable to reward gating, while the locked receiver prerequisite still fails. Choose **C: stop the current noisy reward-gating route** rather than implement a gate, retest receiver streams, or infer a teaching effect. Derived write-once measurements: `data/rps_noisy_reward_gate_early_headroom.json`. Reproduce read-only with `python tools/analyze_rps_noisy_reward_gate_saved.py` in a copy without that output file; the script opens it exclusively. Prior results and gates remain unchanged.

### Prospective observable-context RPS feasibility (locked before training, )

- **Hypothesis / scope.** A student may learn too quickly on one fixed cue-to-action mapping, but fail to represent conflicting actions when the same opponent-move cue occurs under different rules. Add one *public, arbitrary context token* to the existing previous-opponent-move cue: context 0 uses opponent offset +1; context 1 uses offset +2. The token identifies the situation but does not state its rule. Both flies would receive the same two sensory inputs; the rule association is acquired only through each fly's own reward. This is a task/readout feasibility stage only: no teacher, neural message, or teaching claim is included.
- **Input encoding / isolated implementation.** Leave `js/rps.js`, `js/sim.js`, the connectome, default demo, and dopamine learning untouched. The opt-in runner drives the move cue through the existing `laneVpn[cue].slice(2)` at 150 Hz and context 0/1 through the first two VPNs of lanes 0/1 respectively, simultaneously at 150 Hz from 0–240 ms. The two context VPN pairs are disjoint from all existing move-cue slices. An opt-in student wrapper routes on its own counted context and move VPN spikes to one of six context×move three-action Q heads, plus two context-specific cue-free heads; the existing MBON features, alpha **0.03**, epsilon **0.20**, and selected-action own-reward update remain unchanged. The student neural state resets after each 680 ms round; Q heads persist. Environment context and correct action are used only to drive the physical input and score recorded choices, never as controller inputs.
- **Fixed schedule / seeds.** Use unused student seeds **951, 952, 953**; seeds 901–903 are not eligible because they appear in the earlier two-cue transfer work. Each 19-round episode has one cue-free warm-up, followed by 18 observed rounds. The visible context alternates in fixed three-round blocks, giving three visits to every context×move cell per episode; the opponent's previous move persists across context switches. Train for **12 episodes** on own rewards. Freeze and evaluate at **0, 1, 3, and 12 episodes** on the same three held-out 73-round sequences per seed (one warm-up plus 72 scored trials, 12 per context×move cell). Each evaluation uses a fresh network, greedy actions, no learning, and identical keyed streams across checkpoints. No seed replacement, parameter tuning, or interim selection is allowed.
- **Locked feasibility gates.** First verify exact context/move routing from VPN spike counts (19/19 contexts and 18/18 observed moves per episode, no ties), exactly three training trials per context×move cell per episode, and 12 held-out trials per cell per sequence. Task headroom passes only if **each** student has mean expected-optimal action accuracy **≤75%** across the six cells at episode 1. Independent learnability passes only if **each** student reaches **≥9/12** expected-optimal choices in **every** cell at episode 12. All route, cue-coverage, reward, selected-head-update, frozen-weight, and unchanged KC→MBON integrity checks must pass. Report all seed- and cell-level values even if a gate fails. A failure stops this direction before any teacher training or communication; a pass only justifies a separately locked teacher-source and controlled teaching study.
- **Reproduction / stopping.** The opt-in, write-once runner is `tools/check_rps_context_cued_feasibility.mjs`; it writes `data/rps_context_cued_feasibility.json` and refuses to overwrite it. Run `node tools/check_rps_context_cued_feasibility.mjs` only in a copy without that artifact. This feasibility pilot does not test teacher benefit, and its students cannot serve as independent confirmation for a later teaching claim.

### Read-only system and demonstration audit

- **Question / sources.** With the research cutoff approaching, identify the strongest validated system and the first missing link in teacher-specific retained RPS learning. This audit read the existing implementation and saved results only; it ran no simulation, training, or new test. The main sources are `data/rps_contextual_bandit.json`, `data/rps_teacher_guided_cue_heads.json`, `data/rps_teacher_guided_cue_heads_early.json` plus its continuation and read-only attribution, `data/rps_teacher_guided_matched_veto.json`, and the noisy prerequisite and early-headroom artifacts. The saved-data reproduction commands remain in their respective sections above.
- **Strongest independently learned policy versus strongest tested integration.** The optional three-MBON contextual bandit learned all three actions on held-out games for four predetermined students (711–714: **65, 65, 63, 65/72**), with no-learning controls at **24/72**. Its stricter all-arm paired gate still failed because 712 tied the nonlinear comparator. This is a reward-trained hybrid controller with frozen KC→MBON synapses, not the original dopamine-only RPS policy. The most complete *integrated* teacher–student protocol is the opt-in cue-specific runner: teacher 830's continuous-state, real-MBON-driven Q scores approve/reject a public student proposal; DNge175 3141/3165 carry the bit as measured spikes; Fly B routes its cue from its own VPN spikes, resets only its neural state between 680 ms rounds, samples another action after rejection, and updates only its selected cue head from its own game reward. The cue-head routing, DN receipt, selected-head updates, and disconnected frozen evaluations were checked together for 831/832 and 841/842. The cue heads and VPN readout are engineered additions outside the default RPS game; their 70/72 results are not independent confirmation of teacher-specific learning.
- **Teacher-specific evidence and controls.** Correct advice was more selective in the saved 841/842 first-12-round analysis (**4/3 helpful, 0/0 harmful**, versus shuffled **2/3 helpful, 2/1 harmful** and untrained **4/3 helpful, 4/5 harmful**), yielded more training wins than disconnected (**18/19 versus 7/6** in the early run), and more rewarded paper/scissors updates (**5/6 versus 0/0** at 12 rounds). It also often raised correct-action Q margins. The resulting disconnected policies reached **69/70/72** at 12 rounds, but correct tied valid shuffled and untrained advice at **69/72** for 841; 842's shuffled advice violated its locked quality ceiling. In the fresh 851/852 six-round matched-veto test, correct reached **70/72** for both yet tied untrained for both and generic veto for 852; the generic control additionally failed its locked total rejection-count match (**7 versus 4**). The final cue-head 831/832 experiment likewise failed its locked +4/72 all-message-control advantage (**correct 70/70; shuffled 68/70; untrained 68/69**). These are valid negative or explicitly limited comparisons, not passing teaching results. The earlier reset preflight and original one-bit cue threshold failure remain unchanged.
- **Causal chain.** (1) Learned teacher advice changes Fly B's played action through real DN spikes: **demonstrated** in the opt-in cue-head trials. (2) Those changes create useful own-reward experiences: **demonstrated versus disconnection**, though not uniquely attributable to learned advice because generic/untrained vetoes also generate them. (3) Fly B updates its own isolated cue Q parameters: **demonstrated** by saved selected-action update reconstruction and frozen KC→MBON weights. (4) The resulting policy works after message disconnection: **demonstrated** for the correct-trained arm, but also for several controls. (5) Retained improvement exceeds shuffled, untrained, and generic exploration: **failed to demonstrate** under the locked gates. This fifth link is the first unsupported link in the basic sequence and the decisive gap for a *teacher-specific* retained-learning claim. The noisy reward-update-gate route remains stopped: its receiver's absolute sensory floor failed, its independent students reached a final 69/69 expected-optimal ceiling, and no trial-aligned teacher advice or early frozen checkpoints established a distinct early opportunity.
- **Demo readiness / decision.** `index.html` and `js/rps_app.js` already provide two independent live connectome simulations in human and patterned-opponent RPS with selectable frozen original, linear, or nonlinear saved policies, action/outcome history, and live brain activity. They do not train Fly A in the session or run the cue-head/DNge175 approval protocol. The separate exploratory lab does train Fly B live and has a pre-trained Fly A, but uses the older three-intermediate-neuron quantized visual-neuron `ActivityChannel`, not the DNge175 bit or cue-specific policy; it cannot honestly demonstrate the missing teacher-specific retained effect. A live DN trace and disconnected cue-head checkpoint replay would require new demo code and would still need the failed gate disclosed. **Choose C: stop research experiments and finalize the existing accurate interactive demonstration and report** while presenting the saved causal measurements and negative gates as results. No demo or research-result artifact was changed by this audit.

### Prospective live Challenge learning-speed checkpoints (locked , before code changes or outcomes)

- **Question and scope.** In one illustrative, deterministic two-arm Challenge run, does Fly B trained with real DNge175 advice acquire a stronger *teacher-disconnected frozen* RPS policy earlier than matched Fly B Alone? This is a new single-run measurement, not independent confirmation or a teacher-specific causal test. Prior failed gates and saved data are unchanged.
- **Fixed case.** Preset Strategy A (`next=(previous+1) mod 3`), live Fly A seed **830**, both independent student brains/controller initializations seed **10830**. Fly A trains 12 × 24 rounds with opponent seeds 41000–41011 and round keys `830×10000000 + episode×1000 + round`, then has its existing 72-round frozen evaluation with opponent seeds 51000–51002 and keys using episodes 100–102. Its controller is frozen and its neural state reset once before advising, then continuous across student rounds. Both students train 12 × 24 rounds against matching opponent seeds 41000–41011, keys `10830×10000000 + episode×1000 + round`; only the guided student's real DN spike counts can change its played choice. No controller or learning-rule changes.
- **Checkpoint schedule and isolation.** Evaluate at completed student updates **0, 6, 12, 24, 48, 96, 288**. At each point, copy each student's four cue-head W/b arrays and baseline into newly constructed, seed-10830 student networks/controllers with identical fixed MBON normalization. Copy no teacher state into evaluation. Set exploration off; pass `teacher=null` and `learn=false` to the existing comparison-round runner; use new, paired opponents at seeds 51000–51002 and trial keys `10830×10000000 + (100+episode)×1000 + round`. For custom patterns the same configured sequence would restart at its first element for evaluation and remain continuous across evaluation episodes, but the locked research run uses preset A. Record wins/72 and wins by observed previous move. Compare training parameters, neural state, opponent position, teacher state, and round trajectories with and without checkpoints; any divergence invalidates the measurement. The original final 72-round live evaluation remains and must agree with copied checkpoint 288.
- **Primary metric and criterion.** The primary early learning-speed metric is mean signed frozen win advantage (guided minus alone) at checkpoints **6, 12, 24, 48, 96**. Call this single run a *measured early advantage* only if that mean is **at least +4/72 wins**, guided is ahead by **at least 4 wins at both 6 and 12 rounds**, and is not below alone at 288. Otherwise report similar, slower, or mixed measured curves without calling the pilot a pass. A descriptive proficiency mark is **58/72**, fixed now; report the first sampled checkpoint each student reaches it, if any. These criteria do not establish trained-teacher specificity.
- **Validity and stop rules.** Stop interpretation if initial student weights/neural states differ, any evaluation message or controller/synaptic update occurs, evaluation mutates a training state or random stream, evaluation opponent sequences differ, enabled/disabled training traces or final weights differ, or copied checkpoint 288 disagrees with the original final 72-round evaluation. Do not replace the seed, change the schedule or threshold, or inspect another training run for a favorable outcome. Historical shuffled, untrained, and generic-veto arms are audited separately; their prior generic rejection-frequency match failed, so no teacher-specific attribution is planned from this two-arm run. Pattern lengths 1–5 receive focused functional checks, not five new learning experiments.

Per-round training trace and final controller parameters were **identical** with checkpoints enabled versus disabled, and evaluation left training-state fingerprints unchanged. The copied 288-round frozen scores matched the original final 72-round evaluation in the UI smoke check.
- **Frozen disconnected results.** Each cell gives wins out of 72; cue 0/1/2 denominators are **23/24/22** observed-cue rounds (three cue-free opening rounds remain in the total).
