Syntax checks passed for the changed JavaScript files and runners. Headless Chromium rendered the local Challenge and custom-pattern indicator without page errors; the full post-training chart was checked by DOM smoke, not by a separate browser training run.

### Prospective Strategy-A teacher-specific speed controls (locked  before implementation or results)

- **Hypothesis and fixed cohort.** The cue-specific Fly B trained with a real, learned Fly A approval bit acquires a stronger *disconnected* policy earlier than silence, untrained advice, cue-preserving shuffled trained advice, and equally frequent generic vetoes. Initial pair: the already examined Strategy-A Fly A **830** and student seed **10830**; this directly tests the prior two-arm observation and is not independent confirmation. If and only if every initial validity and learning gate passes, confirm with two predetermined fresh independent pairs: teachers **915/916**, students **10915/10916**. No replacement. Each Fly A trains on its own rewards for 12×24 rounds; each student arm trains 12×24 rounds. Strategy A is `next=(previous+1) mod 3` with no noise. Preset opponent instances reset every 24-round episode at seeds **41000–41011**; each arm faces the same moves. Teacher neural keys are `teacherSeed×10000000 + episode×1000 + round`, with `net.rand=rng(key+1)`; student keys substitute `studentSeed` with neural `rng(key+2)`, provisional-action `rng(key+501)`, and rejection-alternative `rng(key+701)`. The teacher controller is frozen during advice but its neural state remains continuous across the 680 ms student rounds. Each student's own neural state resets at the start of each round, with the existing 240 ms cue/proposal, 240–280 ms DN pulse, 280 ms final action, and 620 ms own-reward update. No fly receives a hidden rule, answer, or another fly's weights.
- **Five matched arms.** (1) Correct: current trained Fly A's MBON-driven Q values approve/reject the current public student proposal; the bit drives DN 3165/3141 at 300 Hz and Fly B uses only measured spike counts. (2) Alone: no pulse. (3) Untrained: a genuinely zero-Q Fly A with its own simulated neural activity judges the current proposal via the unchanged strict `>` rule (therefore rejection is allowed to be frequent); its bit is delivered by measured DN spikes. (4) Shuffled: *before student training*, replay trained Fly A's continuous neural source on the fixed training cue sequence with a balanced, predetermined candidate cycle 0/1/2 within each cue; record actual Q-derived bits, then Fisher–Yates shuffle those bits **within each cue** using `rng(2026110601 + teacherSeed + cue)`. This preserves each cue's source-tape bit frequency, not the eventual correct arm's realized bit frequency. The tape is independent of every student's future proposal, reward, or outcome. Deliver its bits through measured DN spikes and report observed advice quality. (5) Generic: synthetic vetoes are delivered through the same DN targets and measured, explicitly **not** labeled neural teacher advice. Before the correct arm's current trial, reject on cue `c` iff the correct arm's *completed prior* rejection count for `c` exceeds the generic arm's prior rejection count for `c`; otherwise approve. This one-cue-occurrence lag uses only past aggregate exposure, never the generic student's candidate, reward, current/future teacher decision, or future outcome. It conservatively shares teacher-derived past exposure timing but no current candidate-specific advice. The five student controllers and neural initial states are identical, separate objects. Correct and untrained source networks are separate; shuffled source tape is prepared before any student outcome.
- **Calibration and validity gates.** The trained teacher must win **≥58/72** on its own frozen Strategy-A evaluation and **≥70% on every observed cue**; the calibration source tape must contain both bit values on each cue. All arms must begin with identical zero cue heads and frozen KC→MBON multipliers. Correct/untrained/shuffled/generic emitted bits must decode from actual DN 3141/3165 counts with intended target dominance; Alone must have zero DN counts. Every arm's own VPN spike head must match the observed cue; reward, selected-head update, and unchanged nonselected heads/synapses must match the existing runner. Generic and correct rejection totals must differ by **≤1 per cue at every checkpoint and at 288**; this causal lag rule should guarantee the bound on a matched cue schedule. Any failure stops interpretation and no seed is replaced. At checkpoint 0 and after **6, 12, 24, 48, 96, 288** updates, evaluate fresh copies with `teacher=null`, exploration and learning off, against paired 72-round held-out opponent seeds **51000–51002** and student keys using episode indices 100–102. No evaluation may touch training controller/neural/RNG/opponent/teacher state. Checkpoint 0 must reproduce **24/72** for all arms in initial pair; every frozen arm must use identical opponent moves and have zero message/updates. Preserve the original demo and archived result.
- **Primary comparison and stop.** Primary early outcome is mean frozen wins/72 at **6, 12, 24, 48**. The trained arm passes the initial *teacher-specific speed* gate only if it exceeds **each of the four controls by ≥4/72 mean wins**, reaches the fixed **58/72** proficiency threshold by round **48** and at an **earlier sampled checkpoint than every control**, scores ≥58/72 at 288, and all validity gates pass. Report per-cue scores, proposals/final actions, approval/rejection and action-change counts, positively rewarded selected-action updates, and first threshold checkpoint for every arm. A tie or mixed result fails; no post hoc threshold change. If initial gates fail, stop without confirmation. If they all pass, run exactly the two independent pairs 915/10915 and 916/10916 with the same gates and require *both* to pass for an independently supported result. Confirmation is not universal reliability. Write each stage once to a new artifact; no reruns or parameter tuning. Earlier failed teacher-specific and generic-veto gates remain failed.

### Strategy-A teacher-specific speed controls: valid initial failure

- **Execution and validity.** One attempted setup was stopped before any result file or outcome inspection because its pass evaluator mistakenly required every control to reach 58/72; the written gate treats a never-proficient control as later. Only that evaluator was corrected, then the unchanged locked initial pair **830/10830** ran. Teacher 830 won **61/72** frozen (cue 0/1/2: **22/23, 17/24, 22/22**), meeting the source-quality floor. The pre-run, balanced-candidate trained-neural tape had both bits on every cue; within-cue shuffling moved **44/94, 42/88, 48/94** messages. All five students started with identical zero cue heads and matched neural states. Opponent moves and keyed streams matched; every delivered bit decoded from the intended DN spike-count target, VPN cue routing and selected-head own-reward updates passed, KC→MBON and teacher controller weights stayed fixed, and frozen checkpoints were isolated with no messages or updates. The correct/alone curves exactly reproduced the earlier two-arm write-once artifact. The **previous failed generic-veto experiment remains failed**; this is its new, prospectively lag-matched control.
- **Frozen teacher-disconnected wins /72.** The same 72-round held-out streams were used at every checkpoint; observed-cue denominators were 23/24/22. Full per-cue counts, actions, and trial records are in the new artifact.

| Student training rounds | Correct trained advice | Alone | Untrained advice | Shuffled trained tape | Lag-matched generic veto |
| ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| 0 | 24 | 24 | 24 | 24 | 24 |
| 6 | 47 | 24 | 47 | 47 | 47 |
| 12 | 69 | 24 | 68 | 69 | 69 |
| 24 | 69 | 24 | 69 | 68 | 69 |
| 48 | 69 | 24 | 69 | 68 | 69 |
| 96 | 69 | 69 | 69 | 69 | 69 |
| 288 | 69 | 69 | 69 | 69 | 69 |

- **Locked speed decision.** The trained arm first reached **58/72 at round 12**; Alone reached it at **96**, and **untrained, shuffled, and generic all reached it at 12**. Mean frozen wins at rounds 6/12/24/48 were **63.5** trained, **24** Alone, **63.25** untrained, **63** shuffled, **63.5** generic. The trained-minus-control differences were **+39.5, +0.25, +0.5, and 0/72**, respectively. Thus the trained arm cleared the silence comparison but **failed** the predeclared ≥4/72 and earlier-proficiency requirements versus every message control. No confirmation teacher/student pair was run.
- **Exposure and mechanism.** Trained advice rejected **55/276** observed-cue proposals, generic veto also rejected **55/276** (cue 0/1/2: **19/19/17** in each); generic trailed by at most **one rejection per cue at every checkpoint**, satisfying the locked matching tolerance. That generic schedule used only completed prior trained-arm rejection totals, never the generic student's current proposal or reward. It is synthetic DN stimulation with teacher-derived *past exposure timing*, not an independent neural teacher. Untrained advice rejected **276/276** and shuffled advice **190/276**, so their exposure differs substantially. Observed advice balanced accuracy was **97.6% trained**, **50% untrained**, **48.7% shuffled**, and **48.7% generic**. Trained advice yielded **20 helpful and 11 harmful** action changes and **250/288 training wins**; generic had **4 helpful and 47 harmful** changes and **197/288 training wins**. Despite this immediate selectivity and reward advantage, their early and final disconnected frozen curves tied. Training wins are not frozen-policy accuracy. Positive-reward selected-head updates were **241** trained versus **188** generic, **85** shuffled, **20** untrained, and **210** Alone (observed-cue counts; first cue-free rounds are excluded). This single seed shows that a few altered exploration opportunities can produce near-ceiling cue-head policies without teacher-specific advice. It does not prove that trained neural information can never help under another prospectively designed task.
- **Raw artifact / stop.** Write-once `data/rps_teacher_specific_speed_initial.json` contains the locked protocol, source bit distribution, 1,440 training-arm rows, all five per-cue frozen curves, actual DN counts, provisional/final actions, reward updates, exposure differences, and gate outcome. Reproduce only in a copy without that artifact using `node tools/check_rps_teacher_specific_speed.mjs initial`; the runner refuses overwrite. Its `confirm` stage requires an initial pass and was **not run**. The existing Challenge UI and prior archived findings were not changed. The remaining obstacle is demonstrating an *early disconnected* advantage beyond generic matched exploration; this experiment found none at the predeclared checkpoints.
- **Focused regression.** `node --check` passed for the changed opt-in message interface and new runner. `node tools/check_rps_challenge_demo.mjs` passed the archived Replay, custom-pattern checks, live Fly A and two-student Challenge, DN advice, checkpoint charts, original final evaluation, and reset after the optional hook was added. No rhythm, connectome, or learning-rule file was changed.

### Read-only RPS task and early-headroom audit (; no new simulation)

- **Question and deterministic mechanism.** Reviewed `js/rps.js`, `js/rps_challenge_students.js`, the five-arm write-once `data/rps_teacher_specific_speed_initial.json`, and the stopped noisy-opponent prerequisite. Each VPN-routed cue has its own zero-initialized three-action head. Only the played action changes: `Q_a(f)=b_a+W_a·f`, `Δb_a=0.03(r-Q_a)`, `ΔW_a=0.03(r-Q_a)f`. After its first `+1` update from zero, that action has `Q_a(f')=0.03(1+f·f')` before subsequent updates. The deterministic task has one always-winning action per cue, and cue 1 was already correct on all 24/24 frozen trials at initialization. In the saved initial pair, first rewarded optimal updates for cues 0/1/2 arrived at rounds **3/7/11** with trained advice, **6/10/11** with lag-matched generic veto, **3/138/11** with untrained advice, **6/19/11** with shuffled advice, and **51/7/50** without messages. Generic and shuffled each had only one rewarded optimal cue-0 update by round 6 and one cue-2 update by round 12, yet their frozen observed-cue scores were **23/23,24/24,0/22** at round 6 and **23/23,24/24,22/22** at round 12. One win *coincided* with a nearly complete cue policy; negative competitor updates and the initial tie rule also contributed, so the archive does not isolate one win as sufficient in general. The trained arm's **250 versus 197** online wins over generic and **241 versus 188** rewarded observed-cue updates improved immediate play without strengthening an already saturated frozen policy. The locked teacher-specific gate remains failed.
- **Candidate tasks.** A longer repeating sequence still supplies at most three distinct previous-move cues; if each cue has a unique successor, it is the same kind of three-association problem, while ambiguous sequences cannot be perfectly predicted from the current input. A rule requiring extra history lacks that history in the student's present VPN input and would require a new sensory/controller observation. The supported `previous_player` and hidden-switch variants likewise lack a validated cue-only learning and single-teacher source path. The smallest distinct task is the existing stationary `Opponent({strategy:'previous_opponent',offset:2,noise:0.5})`: the observed previous move remains sufficient for the *best expected* action, but an optimal action wins with probability 2/3 and either other action can win with probability 1/6. A lucky suboptimal reward can strengthen the wrong action. This is a task-level hypothesis for the **unchanged teacher-guided exploration update**, separate from the stopped noisy reward-*gating* proposal. Its archived teachers 861/862 learned through their own rewards and their fixed Q-derived approval classified expected-optimal candidates at 100% per cue; independent students 863/864 eventually chose the expected-optimal action on 69/69 observed held-out rounds. Those components do not establish a teaching advantage. The prior locked receiver test failed the absolute cue-recognition floor for 863/864 and remains failed.
- **Additional saved-data headroom finding.** Reconstructed the four cue-head `W,b` arrays analytically from the archived 863/864 training `features`, selected actions, rewards, and the unchanged 0.03 equation. Every saved pre-action Q score reproduced exactly (maximum absolute discrepancy **0**). Scoring these intermediate parameters on the archive's *final held-out neural feature vectors* reproduced each final frozen action **72/72** and each final score exactly. Expected-optimal choices among the 69 observed held-out cues at reconstructed rounds **0/6/12/24/48/96/288** were **23/0/25/67/28/69/69** for 863 and **31/4/21/39/41/69/69** for 864. This is an analytical counterfactual on fixed archived features, **not** a new neural simulation or an archived intermediate frozen checkpoint. It is justified here by frozen KC→MBON weights, per-round neural reset, fixed opponent draws independent of the student's action, and exact final replay; intermediate biological activity was not rerun. The noisy task had early headroom and one student's Q choices later regressed, but no matched generic-veto curve or trial-aligned teacher advice exists. The previous reward-gating stop is not reversed.
- **Single conditional next prerequisite, not authorized for execution here.** If research resumes, reuse the already trained noisy teachers 861/862 and their unchanged bit; preselect fresh receiver/student seeds **10917/10918** with no replacement. First perform the same frozen DNge175 forced-bit/no-message receiving check: intended target spikes, **12/12 bit decoding per cue**, **≥10/12 cue recognition under each branch**, no recognition loss versus matched no-message, and unchanged weights. Stop before any training if either student fails. If both pass, train only independent no-message students on the same fixed noise-0.5 task with the existing cue heads, epsilon, own-reward update, and **12×24** budget; analytically/physically frozen evaluations at **0,6,12,24,48,96,288** must use identical held-out streams. Require **≥15 observed trials per cue**, initial expected-optimal choices **≤36/69**, **≤55/69 at round 12** for both students (measurable early headroom), and by round 288 **≥70% expected-optimal choices per cue** plus **≥20 percentage-point overall gain** for both. The saved teacher source must still clear its prior **≥80% balanced accuracy per cue** and **≥15-point** shuffled/untrained advantage; no retraining or source selection. Any failed gate stops. Passing would justify, not run, one separately locked correct/shuffled/untrained/causally exposure-matched generic/disconnected guided-exploration comparison, with early **frozen expected-optimal action choices** rather than noisy realized wins as primary and all arms using measured DN counts. No teacher-assisted training or controller/task code change occurred in this audit; the possible generic-exploration ceiling remains untested.
- **Required comparison if the prerequisite passes.** Keep each teacher/student assignment and every training/evaluation opponent draw fixed across five separate student arms: correct teacher, shuffled trained bit, untrained teacher, causal lag-matched generic veto, and disconnected. Use the unchanged 12×24 budget and frozen disconnected checkpoints **0/6/12/24/48/96/288**; preserve the previous per-cue generic rejection-count gap **≤1 at each checkpoint** and check actual DN decoding, cue routing, weight integrity, and matched initial state. A prospective teacher-specific pilot would require **both** students to exceed **every** control by **≥4/69 mean expected-optimal choices at checkpoints 6/12/24/48**, reach **≥49/69 by checkpoint 48 at an earlier sampled checkpoint than every control**, and finish with **≥70% expected-optimal choices per cue**; ties fail. Report realized wins separately. These numerical criteria are a design proposal, not a passed gate, and the exact streams and source-to-student alignment would still have to be locked before any teaching run.

### Read-only mechanism audit: why informed advice did not persist

- **Source and update.** In the saved initial Strategy-A five-arm run (`data/rps_teacher_specific_speed_initial.json`), teacher 830's MBON-driven Q scores approved 221/232 proposals that were the cue's winning action and 0/44 other proposals in the correct-advice arm. This is strong *candidate-conditional* information, not an action answer supplied to Fly B. A rejection samples one of the other two actions uniformly. Student 10830 routes its own VPN cue to one of three separate action-value heads; for only the played action it applies `b[a] += .03*(reward-Q[a])` and `W[a,j] += .03*(reward-Q[a])*f[j]`, with standardized MBON feature `f`. KC→MBON synapses stay frozen. Measured DN counts alter the played action; neither teacher Q nor DN counts enter the retained cue-head update directly.
- **Archived first-12-round reconstruction, no simulation.** Correct/generic/shuffled/untrained/alone arms won 8/6/3/3/3 training rounds. First positive rewards for the winning action on observed cues 0/1/2 occurred at rounds **3/7/11**, **6/10/11**, **6/none/11**, **3/none/11**, and **none/7/none**, respectively. Counts of those positive rewards by round 12 were **4/2/1**, **3/1/1**, **1/0/1**, **1/0/1**, and **0/2/0**. Cue 1 was already 24/24 in every frozen baseline, so its absent new reward in shuffled/untrained arms is not a missing learned association. At the round-6 frozen checkpoint, correct/generic/shuffled each scored cue 0 **23/23**, cue 1 **24/24**, cue 2 **0/22**; untrained scored **22/23, 24/24, 0/22**. By round 12 correct/generic/shuffled scored **23/23, 24/24, 22/22**; untrained scored **22/23, 24/24, 22/22**, and alone remained **0/23, 24/24, 0/22**. These checkpoints bracket preference changes; the artifact does not save intermediate frozen Q vectors or MBON features, so exact flip rounds and numerical margins cannot be reconstructed.
- **Interpretation and controls.** The trained message gave 250/288 online wins and 241 correctly rewarded observed-cue updates, versus 197/288 and 188 for generic veto. Both rejected 55/276 observed-cue proposals with the locked per-cue exposure match, yet their disconnected frozen curves were identical. Generic veto is a synthetic control whose exposure schedule follows *completed prior* trained-arm rejection counts; it has no current proposal or reward information but is not wholly teacher-independent. Untrained and shuffled arms had different rejection exposure (276/276 and 190/276), yet also crossed 58/72 by round 12. The evidence supports a deterministic-task/independent-cue-head ceiling: generic exploration supplied enough reward experiences for the same early frozen decisions in this one pair. It does not prove that each single reward causes a flip, that the bit lacks information, or that teacher-specific learning is universally impossible. The prospective teacher-specific gate remains failed and no confirmation pairs ran.
- **Research decision.** Stop further RPS teaching experiments under the present architecture and task. A richer preference message would require a new encoding/receiver and still needs a pathway into retained learning; the existing bit already carries strong candidate information. A stationary noisy task could create a genuine need to average rewards, but both locked noisy receiver attempts failed the absolute cue-recognition gate without messages, and no frozen independent epsilon-greedy learning curve was collected. Routing DN activity into neural plasticity would change the learning mechanism; earlier interaction/readout and noisy reward-gating attempts do not validate it. The smallest prerequisite for reconsideration is a *new evidence-based resolution* of baseline cue recognition, followed by a prospectively locked no-message frozen learning/headroom check before any teacher-student study. No simulation, code, parameters, or archived results changed. Reproduce these aggregates by reading the saved artifact's `pairs[0].training` and `pairs[0].checkpoints`; do not rerun its write-once experimental runner.
### Read-only noisy receiver cue-0 recognition audit (; no simulation)

- **Path and distinction.** The runner converts the opponent's observed previous move into 150 Hz drive on `net.laneVpn[cue].slice(2)` for 0–240 ms, resets ordinary spike counts at 50 ms, and reads the fly at 240 ms. `ChallengeCueHeadBandit.decide` selects a cue-specific Q head from the **VPN lane spike-count maximum** and computes that head's three Q scores from standardized `log1p` action-group MBON rates. The separate receiver *validity* decoder applies `log1p` to the same three MBON rates and chooses the nearest Euclidean centroid fixed from calibration seeds 651–653 (`data/rps_readout_information.json`). It does **not** choose the student head. All 12/12 VPN-head routes were correct in each failing condition (and all other saved conditions); the failure is in this external MBON-centroid recognition check, although variable MBON features could also affect a trained Q policy.
- **Matched saved probes.** From `data/rps_noisy_guided_receiver.json`, student **10917** on the *second* cue 0 after 1→0 had **7/12** correct; errors at repeats **0,2,7,9,11** decoded as **1,2,2,2,2**. Student **10918** on the *first* cue 0 had **9/12** correct; errors at repeats **0,1,9** all decoded as **2**. For 10917, mean three `log1p` MBON features were **[.460,.435,.459]** on correct repeats and **[.552,.522,.677]** on errors. For 10918 they were **[.493,.406,.444]** and **[.727,.690,.862]**. The erroneous high-rate cue-0 vectors lie closer to the fixed cue-2 centroid, especially in the third MBON group; 10917's low-rate repeat 0 lies nearer cue 1. For every matched repeat in these conditions, all three MBON rates and the cue-recognition result were **exactly identical** under no message, forced bit 0, and forced bit 1. Both bits still decoded 12/12 through measured DN spikes. The prior 863/864 receiver also had cue-0 errors without messages (9/12 and 8/12), with high-rate errors often called cue 2 and low-rate errors called cue 1.
- **What this establishes and limits.** The fixed centroid already classified only **29/36** cue-0 calibration vectors and **30/36** independently held-out cue-0 vectors correctly; most errors in each were called cue 2. Thus overlap or variability of pooled MBON features around the fixed decision boundary predates these receivers. In the archived schedule, the first cue is classified *before* DN stimulation, and the second cue follows `Network.resetState()` plus a newly keyed random stream with no second message. Exact paired equality and the reset rule support a baseline, not observed DN-interference or residual-state, explanation for these specific misses. They do not isolate spike stochasticity, seed-specific wiring, calibration shift, KC representation, or information lost by MBON pooling. Saved receiver rows contain only three MBON group means, a VPN-derived head ID, recognition, and DN counts—not actual VPN lane-count margins, KC identities/spikes, individual MBON counts, or neural-state snapshots. Different first/second stream keys prevent attributing 10917's 7/12 specifically to the preceding cue.
- **One next step, not executed.** If this prerequisite is revisited, run one prospectively fixed, reward-free **no-message population probe** on the same 10917/10918 cue-0 cases and matched cue-2 controls, retaining the archived keys and reset schedule. Record VPN lane counts, KC and individual MBON spike counts, and a post-reset state check; first require exact reproduction of the saved three MBON rates and centroid guesses. Then test whether cue-0/cue-2 activity is already overlapping at KC level or only after MBON aggregation. Do not refit the decoder or reclassify the old receiver as passing. No simulation, training, decoder change, or archived-result edit occurred in this audit. Read-only reproduction: inspect `probes` in `data/rps_noisy_guided_receiver.json` and `centroids` in `data/rps_readout_information.json`; apply `Math.log1p` and nearest squared Euclidean distance exactly as in `tools/check_rps_noisy_guided_prerequisite.mjs`.
### Locked no-message RPS population diagnostic (; before simulation)

- **Purpose and scope.** Locate the earliest measured layer at which the fixed cue-0 MBON-centroid errors become evident. This is one reward-free frozen diagnostic, not a receiver correction or a rerun of the failed 10/12 gate. Use only saved receiver seeds **10917/10918**, repeats **0–11**, no-message branches, existing real wiring, untrained frozen cue-head controllers, fixed feature normalization and centroids, 150 Hz VPN input, and the archived two-round 680 ms schedule. Keep the original `key=s*10000000+(300+12*firstCue+repeat)*1000`; second key is `key+1`; network/action streams are `rng(key+2)`/`rng(key+501)`. A student-only `Network.resetState()` precedes each first cue and occurs again between rounds; count reset is at 50 ms, cue/decision at 240 ms, ordinary count reset at 280 ms. No DN drive, teacher, opponent outcome, reward, or learning update is present.
- **Fixed conditions and budget.** First reproduce **24** archived failing-condition pairs: 10917 first cue **1→0** and 10918 first cue **0→2**, each over all 12 repeats. The target observations are respectively second cue 0 and first cue 0. If their 24 target three-MBON rate vectors differ from saved no-message vectors by more than **1e-12** in any component, their fixed centroid decisions differ on any repeat, VPN-derived heads differ, or frozen weights change, stop without population interpretation. If reproduction passes, run only **24** same-seed/same-phase cue-2 comparison pairs already in the saved no-message archive: 10917 **0→2** (second cue 2) and 10918 **2→1** (first cue 2), 12 repeats each. Require their archived first/second MBON rates, head choices, and centroid results to reproduce with the same tolerance. Total maximum: **48 two-round pairs**, no new seeds, streams, or stimulation conditions. Save a write-once artifact even on a reproduction stop; do not overwrite the receiver archive.
- **Prospectively fixed extra measurements.** Without altering `js/sim.js` or stepping, capture at the 50 ms count reset and 240 ms decision: actual three VPN lane spike-count totals and intended-minus-largest-other margin; active KC IDs with spike counts in **0–50** and **50–240 ms** windows; every MBON neuron's spike count in those windows, grouped by the original action groups; the original pooled three rates; all three squared Euclidean distances from `log1p(rates)` to the unchanged centroids, the selected centroid, and nearest-versus-runner-up distance margin. Record compact pre/post reset summaries of simulator time and nonbaseline/nonzero state arrays (`v,g,refr,trace,count,lastSpike,ring,drive`) without saving the connectome. Check controller and KC→MBON weights before/after and record that no DN target was driven. Read-only counters and snapshots must not consume random draws.
- **Analysis fixed before population inspection.** Compare the archived cue-0 correct/error repeats separately for each student; report VPN margins, active-KC counts and pairwise Jaccard overlaps, and individual-MBON count patterns. Compare each target cue-0 set descriptively with its same-seed/same-phase cue-2 controls using KC Jaccard and individual-MBON Euclidean distances; also report the pooled-rate centroid distances. No classifier, centroid refit, threshold, or success gate is fitted to these probes. Distinct VPN counts with overlapping KC would support a KC-level bottleneck; distinct KC but overlapping individual MBON counts would support downstream neural convergence; distinct individual MBON patterns with ambiguous pooled rates would suggest pooling loss. Similarity alone is not causal evidence or proof that a new decoder would generalize. If the minimum controls or saved measurements do not permit localization, report that explicitly rather than selecting a favorable layer.
- **Output and stop.** A single opt-in runner may use existing `Network`, `rng`, `wiring`, `ChallengeCueHeadBandit`, and saved centroids. It must create `data/rps_noisy_cue_population.json` exclusively. Do not simulate any other branches, train students, tune decoder/stimulation, change neural dynamics, or begin teaching. Reproduction command and results will be appended after execution. The historical receiver failure remains failed regardless of this diagnostic.
### Locked no-message RPS population diagnostic: results

- **Reproduction and integrity.** The exact **48** predetermined two-round no-message pairs reproduced all archived first- and second-cue three-MBON rates with **maximum absolute difference 0**, the same fixed-centroid outcomes, and the same VPN-derived head IDs. The target failures remained **7/12** for 10917's second cue 0 and **9/12** for 10918's first cue 0. All 48 pairs had intended VPN-lane dominance in both phases, no DN target drive, unchanged controller/KC→MBON weights, and post-reset summaries at `t=0` with baseline membrane voltage and cleared conductance, refractory, trace, count, last-spike, delay-ring, and drive state. No reward or update occurred. The first execution completed the deterministic probes but the renamed folder's sandbox denied only the final new-file creation (`EPERM`); it produced no artifact. The identical locked runner was executed once more with write permission to save the write-once result. No condition or code changed between executions.
- **Cue-0 population comparison.** The same-seed/same-phase cue-2 controls scored **11/12** under the original fixed centroid for both students. Pairwise similarities/distances below are *descriptive medians over pairs*, not independent accuracies or fitted decoders.

  | Student / target phase | Correct / error cue-0 probes | Minimum intended VPN margin on errors | Mean active KCs correct / error | KC Jaccard: error–correct cue 0 / error–cue 2 | 42-MBON count Euclidean: error–correct cue 0 / error–cue 2 |
  | --- | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
  | 10917, second after 1→0 | 7 / 5 | 374 spikes | 8.57 / 11.20 | 0.600 / 0.118 | 7.35 / 13.67 |
  | 10918, first cue 0 | 9 / 3 | 392 spikes | 8.00 / 16.33 | 0.467 / 0.103 | 9.11 / 12.25 |

- **Localization and calibration.** The intended VPN lane was strongly separated even on every error. Cue-0 errors usually recruited more KCs and produced higher MBON group rates, particularly the third group, but their active-KC identities and individual MBON count patterns remained more similar to cue-0 controls than to cue-2 controls by the fixed descriptive measures. The fixed `log1p` three-pooled-rate centroid nevertheless called **7/8** target errors cue 2 (and one low-rate 10917 repeat cue 1). Mean squared distances for 10918's three errors were **0.371** to cue 0 and **0.090** to cue 2; these are not all near-tie errors. The old centroid already missed cue 0 on **7/36** calibration and **6/36** independent held-out recordings. Thus the first *measured representation under the fixed cue-recognition rule* that loses reliable cue identity is the three pooled MBON rates/centroid boundary, not VPN routing. KC recruitment variability is associated with high MBON rates, but the data do not establish that it caused them. Individual MBON-pattern separability is only descriptive; no independently validated decoder was fitted.
- **Limits and one recommendation.** These probes cannot prove whether KC or individual-MBON information would generalize to unseen students, whether different calibration would help, or whether a trained student Q policy is impaired by the external decoder's mistakes. A whole-population similarity measure is not a causal intervention. Choose **one prospective decoder-validation experiment** if research resumes: predeclare a single simple 42-MBON spike-count feature/nearest-centroid rule, fit it only on new independent calibration recordings, and compare it with the unchanged three-rate decoder on untouched predetermined receivers under the same frozen schedule and original absolute cue-recognition floor. No receiver or teaching experiment was resumed here; do not reinterpret the historical failed gate. Raw write-once artifact: `data/rps_noisy_cue_population.json` (SHA-256 `7D73D9ABF9A324347D17C4A4F1DBBC4D9A69B19572DCB33D68DF7E415ABC568C`). Reproduce only in a copy lacking the artifact with `node tools/check_rps_noisy_cue_population.mjs`; the runner uses exclusive creation. `node --check tools/check_rps_noisy_cue_population.mjs` and read-only saved-result integrity checks passed.
### Prospective 42-MBON cue-decoder validation (locked  before new probes)

- **Hypothesis and independence.** Individual MBON spike-count identities may preserve cue information lost by the three group means. The prior 10917/10918 cue-0 errors motivated this **single** 42-cell candidate, so those inspected students and all eight known failures are excluded from fitting and evaluation. Fixed, previously unused seeds: **10931** for calibration and **10932/10933** for independent held-out evaluation; exact-seed searches of existing docs/tools/artifacts found no prior use. No replacement. The three seeds satisfy the per-session exploratory seed limit. The existing three-rate decoder remains fixed from its historical 651–653 calibration; it is applied to the *same new held-out observations* for a paired comparison. This tests an external measurement decoder, not Fly B's VPN-derived cue-head route or its learning.
- **Frozen recording schedule and budget.** For each seed, first cues **0,1,2**, repeats **0–11**, no-message only: **36 two-round pairs / 72 cue observations per seed**. After the first cue, the second is `(first+2)%3`, so every cue occurs 12 times in each phase. Key `s*10000000+(300+12*firstCue+repeat)*1000`, second key `key+1`; neural/action streams `rng(key+2)`/`rng(key+501)` as in the locked noisy receiver. Use `wiring(sub,'real',s)`, `Network(sub,...,{seed:s})`, and frozen `ChallengeCueHeadBandit` with the saved feature mean/std, exploration off. `resetState()` precedes each first cue and separates rounds; drive only `net.laneVpn[cue].slice(2)` at **150 Hz from 0–240 ms**, reset counts at **50 ms**, stop the drive and measure at **240 ms**, reset ordinary counts at **280 ms**, end at **680 ms**. No DN stimulation, teacher, opponent, reward, dopamine, or controller update. Maximum **108 pairs / 216 observations**, of which only the first seed supplies calibration. The old receiver seeds/streams are never reused.
- **Cells and fixed decoder.** Each feature vector contains the **42 simulator MBON indices 2108–2149 in ascending index order**, with each cell's `net.count` spikes from **50–240 ms** at the 240 ms decision. These are the same fixed cells and window in calibration and evaluation; `net.groupMbon` has 14 per action group. Before any labels are scored, transform each count with **`log1p(count)`** and apply no cell selection, per-cell fitted scaling, clipping, or feature weighting. Historical unlabeled counts ranged from silent cells to several spikes per cell; `log1p` compresses that scale while avoiding unstable variance estimates for often-silent cells. For cue `c`, the 42-dimensional centroid is the componentwise mean of the **24** transformed calibration vectors labeled with that presented sensory cue (12 first, 12 second). Freeze all 126 centroid numbers before the held-out seed is simulated. Predict the minimum **squared Euclidean** distance; ties choose the smallest cue index. Decision margin is second-smallest minus smallest distance. The old comparator applies `log1p` to the same observation's three action-group MBON rates (each the mean of its 14 counts) and uses its existing saved three-dimensional centroids, squared Euclidean distance, and same tie rule. No parameter derives from held-out data.
- **Locked validity, outcome, and stop.** Calibration must contain exactly 24 finite 42-count observations per cue with the fixed cell order; held-out data must contain 12 per seed×cue×phase. All 216 observations must have the intended VPN-derived head, no DN drive, and unchanged controller/KC→MBON weights. If calibration validity fails, stop before held-out simulation and save that outcome; if held-out validity fails, report all probes and do not replace seeds. Report both full confusion matrices, paired predictions, per-seed and per-cue/phase counts and margins. The candidate passes the **absolute gate** only if it reaches **≥10/12 for every one of the 12 held-out seed×cue×phase conditions**. It passes the **predeclared improvement gate** only if it loses **zero** correct recognitions against the old decoder in every such condition, gains **≥4/72 overall per held-out seed**, and gains **≥2/24 on cue 0 per held-out seed**; ties fail improvement. Overall success requires validity, absolute, and improvement gates. These new gates do not revise the failed historical receiver experiment. If any gate fails, stop without changing features, normalization, centroids, seeds, or thresholds. A pass would justify only a separately locked receiver-validation protocol, not teaching or automatic decoder replacement.
- **Output.** One minimal opt-in runner will save calibration centroids, all paired frozen held-out predictions and margins, confusion matrices, per-condition counts, validity checks, and gate outcomes in new write-once `data/rps_individual_mbon_decoder.json`. No demo or archived result changes. Reproduction command and results follow execution.

### Frozen 42-MBON cue-decoder validation: result

- **Execution and validity.** The single locked runner calibrated 42-dimensional nearest centroids on seed **10931** (72 no-message cue observations, 24 per cue), froze them, and then evaluated untouched seeds **10932/10933** (72 observations each). All 216 observations had the intended VPN-routed head, no DN drive, unchanged controller and KC→MBON weights, and the fixed 50–240 ms MBON count window. Each held-out seed had 12 observations per cue and first/second phase. Calibration and evaluation seeds and streams did not overlap. This was an external cue decoder, not a change to the student's VPN cue-head route.
- **Held-out counts on identical observations.** Each cell is individual-MBON correct / fixed pooled-decoder correct out of 12. The individual decoder met the absolute **10/12** floor in all 12 conditions and never lost a correct recognition to the pooled decoder within a condition.

On 10933, individual: `[[22,2,0],[0,24,0],[0,0,24]]`; pooled: `[[20,2,2],[0,24,0],[0,0,24]]`. Trial-level predictions and squared-distance margins for both decoders are saved in the artifact. The new decoder rescued 5 and 2 pooled errors respectively and introduced no new errors on these matched observations.
- **Locked gate and interpretation.** Total accuracies were **70/72 versus 65/72** for 10932 and **70/72 versus 68/72** for 10933. Cue-0 gains were **+4/24** and **+2/24**, meeting that subgate. The required **≥4/72 gain for *each* held-out seed failed** on 10933 (+2/72); validity, absolute floor, no-harm, and cue-0 gain passed, but the overall prospective decoder-validation gate **failed**. The individual population contains useful cue information in these independent observations, yet this fixed rule did not clear the predefined consistent improvement criterion. Do not replace the historical pooled decoder, reinterpret the old 10917/10918 receiver failures, or proceed to a teacher-guided receiver or training experiment. Under the one-candidate stop rule, end this decoder investigation without feature or classifier tuning. An independent receiver protocol would require a new prospective rationale and validation, and is not justified by this failed gate alone.
- **Raw record and reproduction.** Write-once `data/rps_individual_mbon_decoder.json` (SHA-256 `002CEA60EB7FF160496EF640393AF437F5ACBBF770C1CE483FA821BD9C539283`) contains the locked protocol, calibration vectors and centroids, all paired held-out counts/predictions/margins, confusion matrices, and gate results. `node --check tools/check_rps_individual_mbon_decoder.mjs` passed. Reproduce only in a copy lacking the artifact with `node tools/check_rps_individual_mbon_decoder.mjs`; the runner uses exclusive file creation. No training, teacher communication, demo change, or Git operation occurred.

### RPS research closeout synthesis (; documentation only)

- **Supported components and integrated result.** The optional three-MBON contextual bandit learned independently from game rewards: students 711–714 won **65, 65, 63, 65/72** held-out games versus **24/72** for no-learning controls, although its stricter paired all-arm gate failed. A reward-trained Fly A produced approval/rejection from its simulated MBON-driven Q scores; in the locked Strategy A five-arm run its advice was **97.6%** balanced-correct for the public proposal. The DNge175 pathway carried messages as measured spikes, and those messages changed Fly B's played actions. Fly B updated its *own* cue-specific controller from its *own* rewards and retained a policy in frozen teacher-disconnected evaluation. The integrated two-arm Strategy A run reached **58/72** frozen wins after **12** training rounds with trained advice versus **96** without messages. These are hybrid simulated neural systems: KC→MBON weights were frozen in the cited contextual-bandit runs, and the added controller update is distinct from the original dopamine plasticity rule.
- **Decisive specificity control.** In the matched five-arm Strategy A comparison, trained, untrained, shuffled, and lag-matched generic advice all reached **58/72 by round 12**. Trained advice improved *online* wins over generic veto (**250/288 versus 197/288**) and correctly rewarded observed-cue updates (**241 versus 188**), but their **disconnected frozen curves were identical**. The predeclared teacher-specific retained-learning gate failed; no independent confirmation pair ran. The generic exposure control used completed prior trained-arm rejection totals, so it is an exposure match rather than an independent neural teacher. The single two-arm speed observation must not be presented as evidence that the trained teacher uniquely accelerated retained learning.
- **Receiver and decoder stops.** On noisy receiver seeds 10917/10918, forced DN bits decoded **12/12 per cue** and VPN routing selected the intended cue on every saved probe. The *external pooled three-MBON nearest-centroid check*, rather than the student's VPN cue-head route, scored only **7/12** and **9/12** on its locked cue-0 conditions even without a message; no extra paired errors were caused by either bit. The absolute receiver gate failed and no noisy student training followed. A later single fixed 42-individual-MBON decoder scored **70/72** on each independent held-out seed, beating the old decoder by **+5/72** and **+2/72**. It met every **10/12** condition but missed the prospectively required **+4/72 per seed** consistency gate. It was not installed as a replacement; all earlier receiver failures stand.
- **Closure and open questions.** Close the present deterministic teacher-specific speed claim, the stopped noisy receiver prerequisite, and this single-candidate decoder investigation under their locked rules; do not retune them or relabel failures as passes. The archive does not establish a unique retained benefit from *trained* neural information over all controls, independent noisy-task learning after the failed receiver gate, or transfer between biological flies. No additional experiment or learning-system change was made for this synthesis. Source artifacts: `data/rps_teacher_specific_speed_initial.json`, `data/rps_noisy_guided_receiver.json`, and `data/rps_individual_mbon_decoder.json`. Judge-facing wording is in [research-story.md](research-story.md) and the live-versus-archived distinction is in [demo-guide.md](demo-guide.md).

### Prospective action-preference spike teaching: new opt-in protocol (locked  before probes)

- **Hypothesis and scope.** The old proposal/veto bit changed exploration but did not supply a retained *teacher-specific* target. A new, explicitly engineered student can use a teacher's preferred action, decoded from simulated DN spikes, as an auxiliary target for its own cue-based Q parameters while still updating those parameters from its own game reward. This is experimental machine learning, not the original KC→MBON dopamine rule. Preserve every old failed gate and all demo, simulator, and saved-result files. Use one separate runner and write-once result; no parameter search.
- **Fixed source and neural code.** Reconstruct reward-trained teacher **830** using the already documented Strategy A **12×24** `RpsEpisode` procedure, opponent seeds `41000+episode`, and keyed teacher streams; verify its separate frozen `51000–51002` result is **61/72** with ≥70% wins per observed cue, matching the archive. Freeze its controller and KC→MBON weights. For each observed cue, the teacher receives only the ordinary 150 Hz VPN input, carries continuous neural state across rounds, and obtains its preferred action by argmax of its own three MBON-driven Q scores (lowest-index tie). Fixed code: Rock `0 → (DN3141 on, DN3165 off)`, Paper `1 → (off,on)`, Scissors `2 → (on,on)`; each selected DN receives the previously established **300 Hz, 240–280 ms** stimulation. Fly B's receiver measures actual 240–280 ms spikes in both DNs. Decode counts by positive-versus-zero status: `(+,0)→0`, `(0,+)→1`, `(+,+)→2`; `(0,0)` is a missing message, never silently replaced by the source action. This is a two-target, three-pattern engineered interface, not evidence of persistent neural memory. It is fixed before any held-out results.
- **Locked communication prerequisite.** Calibration receiver **11000**, untouched receivers/students **11001/11002** (no replacement); exact-seed search of docs/tools found no earlier use. Each seed receives **12 repeats of every cue 0/1/2 × no-message/forced action 0/1/2**: 144 frozen probes/seed, 432 maximum. Each probe starts with `resetState`, uses `key=seed×10,000,000+(100+48×cue+12×condition+repeat)×1000`, `net.rand=rng(key+2)`, and no reward or update. The student DN pulse occupies 240–280 ms, its *unchanged* 150 Hz VPN sensory cue 280–520 ms, counts reset at 330 ms, decision at 520 ms, and the round ends at 680 ms. Preserve the DN count buffer across the reset. Score the original fixed three-pooled-MBON cue decoder, not a refit, and the student's VPN cue-head route. Calibration must show all three forced codes ≥11/12 decoded per cue, intended DN positive/off-target zero, and cue recognition ≥10/12 under every condition. The same gates must hold on **each** held-out seed, plus VPN routing **12/12**, no forced code may lose >2/12 cue recognitions versus matched no-message, and all neural/controller weights must be unchanged. Stop at the first failed stage. Teacher preferred action must agree with its own frozen Q argmax by construction and choose the actual winning action on ≥70% of observed source trials per cue; no hidden winning action enters Fly B.
- **Student and matched pilot, conditional on prerequisites.** One isolated student class uses the existing three standardized MBON rates, VPN-routed separate cue Q heads, zero initialization, ε=0.20, and the existing selected-action own-reward update `δ=0.03(r−Q_played)`, `Δ(b,W)_played=δ(1,f)` with KC→MBON frozen. At the 620 ms update, after that reward update, an observed-cue decoded teacher action `t` adds one auxiliary softmax cross-entropy step on the *student's* cue head: `p_a=softmax(Q_a(f))`, `Δ(b,W)_a=0.03(1[a=t]−p_a)(1,f)` for all three actions. No raw intended action, teacher Q vector, hidden opponent rule, teacher weights, or correct-action label enters this update. First cue-free rounds receive no message/imitation. An algebraic one-step synthetic check must confirm the decoded target margin increases and weights stay unchanged with imitation disabled; this is only a mechanism check. Before comparison, each 11001/11002 student must learn independently from its own rewards for 12×24 rounds with no messages, attain ≥58/72 frozen wins and ≥70% per observed cue, and begin at ≤50/72; otherwise stop. This no-message run is the pilot's Alone arm.
- **Pilot arms, streams, evaluation, and gates (only if all prerequisites pass).** On each student seed compare `trained`, `alone`, `untrained`, `shuffled`, `generic` with identical initial weights, opponent seeds `41000+episode`, keyed student streams, 288 training rounds, one communication opportunity per observed cue, and identical Q/reward/imitation equations. Trained and untrained source actions use actual frozen neural teacher Q responses (untrained Q is zero/tie Rock). A fixed Fisher–Yates permutation with seed `2026110602` shuffles the trained source action tape *globally across observed rounds*, preserving total frequencies while breaking cue alignment; generic cycles actions `roundIndex mod 3` independent of cue/outcome, matching one message per observed round. Every messaged control must stimulate and decode actual DNs; Alone has no pulse and no imitation. Reuse the same source tape and opponent sequence for paired arms, with no source access to hidden next move. Evaluate separate frozen disconnected copies at **0,6,12,24,48,96,288** updates on the same 72-round `51000–51002` opponent streams, exploration and all learning off. Save per-cue wins/actions, online rewards, decoded DN counts, reward and imitation updates, teacher/source accuracy, and weight integrity. The primary early outcome is mean frozen wins at 6/12/24/48. A teacher-specific pilot passes only if *both* students exceed **each** control by ≥4/72 mean early wins, reach **58/72 by checkpoint 48 at an earlier sampled checkpoint than every control**, and finish ≥58/72 with ≥70% per cue; ties fail. Communication and source gates must remain valid throughout; no parameter adjustment. If the initial two-student pilot passes, only then consider independent confirmation on separately locked seeds; none are selected here. All results, including any failed prerequisite, must be saved write-once.

- **Pre-outcome implementation correction.** The first execution reproduced teacher 830's archived **61/72** frozen result and collected only calibration receiver 11000. All 108 forced-message probes decoded (**12/12 per cue/action**) and every cue/message recognition score reached **≥10/12**. The runner incorrectly compared the missing-message decoder sentinel `-1` with the condition label `null`, yielding an artificial **0/12 no-message decoding** score and `stopped_receiver`. The write-once `data/rps_action_preference_transfer.json` is preserved as an **invalid scoring artifact**, not a failed neural prerequisite; it contains no held-out receiver or student learning. Before any held-out inspection or student training, only that null/sentinel comparison and the output filename were corrected. The replacement uses the identical seeds, streams, neural code, pulse, decoder, source, and locked gates; it writes a separate `data/rps_action_preference_transfer_corrected.json` once. If the corrected receiver fails a real gate, stop without tuning.

### Action-preference spike teaching: stopped at held-out receiver

- **Teacher prerequisite.** Reconstructed teacher **830** from its own 12×24 Strategy A rewards and reproduced the archived frozen **61/72** wins (observed cues 0/1/2: **22/23, 17/24, 22/22**). KC→MBON synaptic multipliers stayed fixed. Its controller Q parameters were reward-trained, but a trial-aligned continuous-state preference tape was **not generated** because the receiver stopped first; this run did not test source-to-receiver alignment or teacher action-message accuracy.
- **Frozen communication probes.** The corrected runner completed calibration seed **11000** and then held-out seed **11001**, stopping before predetermined seed **11002**. On each tested seed, every one of the **108** forced action messages decoded correctly (**12/12 for each cue/action code**). Each selected DN had at least **3 measured spikes** in its 240–280 ms window (maximum 10), with no off-target spikes in the single-target codes. Both DN targets fired for action 2. The student's VPN-derived cue head was correct on **12/12 in every cue/message condition**. No reward or weight update occurred. Calibration 11000's fixed pooled-MBON recognition was cue 0 **11/12 no-message; 10/12 under each code**, cue 1 **12/12 no-message, 12/12 code 0, 11/12 code 1, 12/12 code 2**, and cue 2 **12/12 throughout**; it passed its locked receiver gates.
- **Held-out failure and limit.** Seed **11001** recognized cue 0 with the unchanged external pooled-MBON decoder on only **8/12 without a message**, **10/12 with code 0**, and **8/12 with codes 1 or 2**. Cues 1 and 2 scored **12/12 in every condition**. Thus the absolute **≥10/12 per condition** gate failed *at baseline*, despite perfect observed DN decoding and VPN routing. The locked key included condition index, so message/no-message branches had different random streams; these scores cannot support a paired causal interference claim. The no-message failure alone requires the preregistered stop. No student 11001 or 11002 training, synthetic imitation check, source-tape evaluation, five-arm comparison, or disconnected checkpoints ran. The new student architecture is implemented only in the isolated runner and remains unvalidated.
- **Decision, artifacts, reproduction.** The three-action neural code is technically detectable under the tested frozen probes, but the full communication prerequisite **failed** on 11001's ordinary cue recognition. It neither rescues earlier noisy receiver failures nor establishes teacher-to-student learning. Preserve the invalid first write-once artifact `data/rps_action_preference_transfer.json` and the corrected write-once stop artifact `data/rps_action_preference_transfer_corrected.json` (SHA-256 `B5E77D70A7C28C67A6CC36EF495FE7E68BD6633A1DB18C440E158958FDAD8FE1`). `node --check tools/check_rps_action_preference_transfer.mjs` passed. Reproduce only in a copy without the corrected artifact using `node tools/check_rps_action_preference_transfer.mjs`; exclusive file creation prevents overwriting the existing artifact. No encoding, threshold, stimulation, student-learning equation, original runner, demo, or archived result was changed after held-out inspection. A future study would first need a separately locked, matched-stream baseline cue-recognition validation under this schedule; no student teaching is justified now.

### Prospective receiver revision: actual student inputs under matched streams (locked  before new probes)

- **New question, distinct gate.** The previous action-preference receiver **failed** its locked external pooled-MBON centroid floor, including 11001's **8/12 without messages**. That rule is not used to select the student's cue head: the actual controller routes by counted VPN spikes, correctly on every saved probe, then consumes standardized three-MBON rates *within* that head. The new hypothesis is that DN messages are compatible with these actual inputs even when an external pooled-MBON cue classifier fails. This changes the receiving compatibility criterion for a **new protocol**; it does not rescue or amend the failed one, and the old classifier will still be reported descriptively. No stimulation, source, connectome, student update, or neural dynamics are retuned.
- **Fixed design and streams.** Preserve the prior three-action code `0=(3141 on,3165 off)`, `1=(off,on)`, `2=(on,on)`, 300 Hz 240–280 ms, student VPN cue 150 Hz 280–520 ms, count reset 330 ms, decision 520 ms, end 680 ms, and positive-count decoder. Use fresh predetermined development receiver **11003** and untouched validation receivers **11004/11005**, no replacement; they are also the only prospective student identities if this prerequisite passes. Each receives cues 0/1/2 × 12 repeats × no-message/code 0/code 1/code 2 = **144** reward-free frozen probes per seed. All four conditions for a given seed/cue/repeat use the **same** `key=seed×10,000,000+(500+12×cue+repeat)×1000`, `net.rand=rng(key+2)`, `agent.rand=rng(key+501)`, identical initial weights and neural reset. The branch changes only DN drive. Student and teacher weights do not change; no teacher source tape or student training is run at this stage.
- **Fixed measurements and gates.** Record both DN spike counts, decoded action, VPN lane counts/selected cue head, raw three-MBON rates and their fixed standardized `log1p` features, and the unchanged old pooled-centroid prediction. For each seed/cue, calculate the median Euclidean distance among all 66 *distinct-repeat* no-message standardized feature pairs (natural neural variability). For each forced code, calculate 12 *matched-repeat* message-versus-no-message distances. A receiver seed passes only if every code decodes **12/12 per cue** from measured DNs; the intended target spikes and unstimulated target has zero spikes in every forced probe; no-message DN counts are zero; VPN cue-head routing is **12/12 per cue and condition**; every MBON feature is finite; and each code/cue's median matched distance is **no greater than** its own cue's natural no-message median. Report per-probe differences and old centroid recognition, but the old centroid is not a gate in this new architecture. Ties on the natural-variability comparison pass. Development 11003 must pass before evaluating 11004/11005; any validation failure stops before learning. This criterion tests non-disruption relative to natural trial variability, not improved MBON cue decoding. Passing would justify, not establish, student teaching.
- **Resource and write-once rule.** Maximum three new receiver seeds and **432** probes, one fixed neural code, no sweep. Save new `data/rps_action_preference_receiver_v2.json` exclusively, including all failed conditions. If this stage passes, use only a separately recorded implementation of the already specified reward-plus-imitation student and five-arm comparison, with no change to numerical learning-speed gates. The old failed artifact, its invalid precursor, and historical cue-recognition failures remain intact.

### Matched-stream receiver revision: valid failure

The new opt-in frozen runner used the locked actual-input criterion. Development seed **11003 passed** every three-action DN decoding, VPN routing, and natural-variability comparison. Held-out seed **11004 failed** only the MBON feature-stability gate on **cue 2**: the no-message median distinct-repeat standardized-feature distance was **0.4953**, while matched message-vs-no-message medians were **0.8290**, **0.8705**, and **0.7238** for codes 0/1/2. Every forced code still decoded **12/12 per cue**, and VPN routing was **12/12** for all conditions. ### Prospective post-sensory message architecture (locked before new probes, )

- **Distinct causal hypothesis.** The v2 failure is tied to putting the DN pulse *before* the student's MBON sensory measurement. In a new schedule, Fly A and Fly B receive the same legitimate observed previous-move cue simultaneously at **0–240 ms**, with ordinary counts reset at **50 ms**. At **240 ms**, Fly B stores its VPN-routed cue head, three standardized MBON features, and its own provisional/played action; Fly A independently computes its preferred action from its own MBON-driven frozen Q scores. Only then does Fly B receive the existing three-pattern DN pulse at **300 Hz, 240–280 ms**. Measured DN counts decode a teacher preference for the **620 ms auxiliary imitation update**, after the ordinary game reward at 280 ms. The pulse cannot change the *already measured* MBON decision features; it can still affect later neural state, so each student round uses the existing `resetState()` boundary. The transmitted action is never substituted as Fly B's played action. This is a new timing/learning architecture, not a passing reinterpretation of either earlier failed receiver.
- **Frozen receiver lock.** Development receiver **11006**, untouched validation receivers **11007/11008**, no replacement; max three new seeds and **432** probes. For every seed, cue 0/1/2, repeat 0–11, compare no-message and all three forced action codes under the *same* key `seed×10,000,000+(700+12×cue+repeat)×1000`, `net.rand=rng(key+2)`, `agent.rand=rng(key+501)`, and identical initial state/weights. Measure decision-time MBON rates and standardized features at 240 ms, intended VPN head, and actual DN spikes during 240–280 ms. Each seed passes only if every forced action decodes **12/12 per cue** from measured spikes, intended targets have positive counts and unstimulated targets zero, no-message DN counts zero, VPN cue-head route is **12/12** under every condition, each paired message condition has **exactly identical** pre-pulse MBON rates/features and route to its no-message probe, and all controller/KC→MBON weights remain unchanged. The old pooled centroid is reported descriptively, with no revised claim about its historical 10/12 gate. Stop on development failure or the first held-out failure. Save one write-once artifact `data/rps_action_preference_receiver_v3.json`.
- **Conditional learning test.** Only if the new receiver passes, implement the already locked cue-head own-reward plus `0.03` auxiliary softmax-imitation update using *decoded* DN action; preserve teacher 830's reward-trained Q source, three-action code, five controls, Strategy A, 12×24 budget, 0/6/12/24/48/96/288 frozen disconnected checkpoints, and original **≥4/72 over every control** early-speed and 58/72 proficiency gates from the earlier action-preference protocol. Use 11007/11008 as the prospective students, with no selection. First require independent no-message learning and a synthetic decoded-target mechanism check. Do not perform confirmation unless the two-student initial comparison passes; any confirmation seeds must be locked before their results. All historical failed gates remain failed.

### Post-sensory receiver result and conditional teaching-control lock

- **Receiver passed.** One write-once run completed development **11006** and independent **11007/11008**, all **432** probes. Every forced code decoded **12/12 per cue/seed**, VPN routing was **12/12**, and each paired pre-pulse MBON rate/standardized-feature vector and cue head was **exactly identical** to its no-message match. Both DN targets received measurable spikes when selected; controller/KC→MBON weights were unchanged. The old external pooled-centroid recognition was recorded but was **9/12 on 11007's cue 0** in every branch, preserving its separate limitation; that external check is not the new student's cue-head route. This result validates post-sensory delivery and pre-pulse measurement integrity, not learning or lasting neural memory. Artifact: `data/rps_action_preference_receiver_v3.json`; reproduce only in a copy lacking it with `node tools/check_rps_action_preference_receiver_v3.mjs`.
- **Control implementation fixed before learning outcomes.** The earlier generic description “cycle actions by round index” could align with Strategy A's three-step opponent cycle and act as a hidden periodic oracle. Replace it in this *new post-sensory protocol* with a predetermined pseudorandom sequence from `rng(2026110603)`, one independently sampled 0/1/2 action per observed round, independent of cue, opponent outcome, reward, and teacher. It matches the trained arm's **one neural message per observed round**, not necessarily its action-frequency histogram; report both histograms. The shuffled arm remains one global Fisher–Yates permutation of the trained neural preference tape using `rng(2026110602)`, preserving its exact global action frequencies. Before any advised-student training, require trained source-preference win accuracy **≥70% per observed cue**, shuffled and generic **≤60% per cue**, each action present in both shuffled and generic tapes, and shuffled tape changed on **≥50%** of observed rounds. A failed control-quality or source gate stops; no reshuffle or seed replacement. Untrained source is the actual zero-Q teacher's neural response, which may prefer a single tied action. These checks use true winning actions only for *analysis*, never as inputs to a source or student.

### Post-sensory action-preference teaching: valid initial pilot

- **Prerequisites and integrity.** The isolated write-once runner first reproduced teacher **830's 61/72** frozen result with KC→MBON multipliers fixed. A synthetic update using an actually measured DN code-0 spike pair increased its target logit margin from **0** to **+0.06**; disabling imitation left all controller weights unchanged. Independent no-message students **11007/11008** began at **24/72**, learned from own rewards to **69/72** each at 288, and met every per-cue floor. The continuous-state trained teacher's Q-argmax source chose the winning action on **90/94, 82/88, 93/94** observed cue-0/1/2 rounds; this is an analysis of learned preference, not a correct-action input. The global shuffle moved **174/276** messages; shuffled cue wins as advisory labels were **38/94, 31/88, 34/94**, and pseudorandom generic labels were **27/94, 28/88, 28/94**. Both controls met the locked ≤60% per-cue quality ceiling, and their action histograms were recorded. All five arms used the same student architecture, zero initial cue heads, opponent sequences, keyed random streams, and 12×24 budget. Each messaged arm decoded **276/276** actual DN spike codes and applied **276** auxiliary updates; Alone had none. Controller and KC→MBON weight integrity, matched opponent schedules, and disconnected frozen checkpoints passed.
- **Frozen wins on identical held-out Strategy A sequences.** Each cell is wins/72 after the specified number of completed student training rounds; teacher communication, exploration, and learning were disabled for these evaluations.

  | Student | Arm | 0 | 6 | 12 | 24 | 48 | 96 | 288 |

- **Gate and interpretation.** The trained arm first reached **58/72 at round 6** on both students; the earliest control was shuffled at **12**. Early mean frozen wins at 6/12/24/48 were **69/68.25** trained versus **47/40.5** Alone, **24/24** untrained, **57.25/57.25** shuffled, and **35.5/37** generic. Both students cleared the locked ≥4/72 early mean advantage over *every* control, earlier proficiency, ≥58/72 final, and ≥70% on each cue (final trained cue wins **23/23,24/24,22/22** for both). This is a **passing initial hybrid imitation pilot**, not independent confirmation or biological transfer. It differs from the earlier failed proposal/veto study because decoded teacher action is an explicit auxiliary machine-learning target for Fly B's own Q heads, applied after its own reward; it does not copy teacher weights or directly set played actions. The old failed gates and receiver limitations remain unchanged. Reproduce only in a copy lacking it with `node tools/check_rps_action_preference_teaching_v3.mjs`.

### Prospective independent confirmation of the unchanged post-sensory protocol (locked  before confirmation runs)

- **Predetermined independent pairs and stopping.** Run pair 1 **teacher 11009 / student 11011**, then only if every pair-1 gate passes run pair 2 **teacher 11010 / student 11012**. No replacement, no retuning, and no use of pilot teachers/students for selection. Each pair is one research iteration with two new simulation seeds. A failure in either pair means independent confirmation **fails**; stop without running a later pair after a failure. Both passing would be encouraging two-pair confirmation, not universal reliability. Each pair has a separate write-once `data/rps_action_preference_confirmation_{first,second}.json`.
- **Unchanged source and receiver prerequisites.** Each new teacher learns Strategy A from its own rewards using the original 12×24 RpsContextualBandit procedure and must score **≥58/72** frozen and **≥70% per observed cue** before its Q source is used. Each new student receives the *same* post-sensory forced-code/no-message receiver test as v3: cues 0/1/2 × 12 repeats × four conditions under matched `key=studentSeed×10,000,000+(700+12×cue+repeat)×1000`, with **12/12** DN decoding and VPN routing per cue/condition, positive intended/zero unstimulated DN counts, and exact pre-pulse MBON feature equality to no-message. Report but do not gate on the old external pooled centroid. Stop on failure before student learning. The 3-action code, pulse, timing, and feature normalization stay fixed.
- **Unchanged student, controls, and learning gate.** Repeat the initial pilot's zero-initialized separate cue heads, epsilon **0.20**, selected-action own-reward step **0.03**, post-reward decoded-action softmax-imitation step **0.03**, frozen KC→MBON, Strategy A opponent seeds **41000–41011**, 288 training rounds, fixed `rng(key+2)`/`rng(key+501)` student streams, source teacher stream, global shuffle `rng(2026110602)`, and independent generic code `rng(2026110603)`. First require synthetic decoded-target update and independent no-message student learning from ≤50/72 initial to ≥58/72 final and ≥70% per cue. Before advised training, require source preference ≥70% winning action per cue, shuffled/generic ≤60% per cue, all three actions present, and ≥50% shuffle displacement; stop rather than resampling. All five arms start identically and use one neural message per observed round when applicable. Frozen disconnected checks at **0,6,12,24,48,96,288** use the same **51000–51002** held-out opponent streams, no exploration or updates. Pair passes only if trained advice reaches 58/72 by 48 at an earlier sampled checkpoint than every control, exceeds *each* control by **≥4/72 mean frozen wins over 6/12/24/48**, and finishes ≥58/72 and ≥70% per cue, with all source, receiver, update, communication, and frozen-weight integrity gates satisfied. Ties fail. No change is made to the initial pilot's success criterion.

### Independent confirmation: both predetermined pairs pass

- **Pair 1, teacher 11009 / student 11011.** Teacher learned from its own rewards and scored **60/72** frozen (cue 0/1/2: **20/23,19/24,21/22**). The fresh student receiver passed every three-action DN, VPN-route, and exact pre-pulse MBON-match gate. Its no-message reward-learning arm started **24/72** and finished **69/72**. Teacher Q-preference source chose the winning action on **89/94,81/88,94/94** observed cue trials; shuffled messages moved **175/276** positions, and shuffled/generic source-quality controls met their locked ≤60% per-cue ceiling. The synthetic decoded-target margin check and frozen KC→MBON integrity passed. Every messaged training arm decoded **276/276** actual DN codes and received 276 auxiliary updates; the Alone arm received none. Matched initial controller weights and opponent streams, isolated frozen evaluations, and unchanged evaluation weights were verified. Frozen win curves at **0/6/12/24/48/96/288**: trained **24/69/67/69/69/69/69**; Alone **24/24/24/24/24/46/69**; untrained **24/24/24/24/24/24/24**; shuffled **24/45/66/65/66/66/69**; generic **24/0/42/45/67/69/69**. Trained early mean **68.5/72** versus **24,24,60.5,38.5** for Alone/untrained/shuffled/generic, clearing the ≥4/72 gate over the strongest control by **+8/72**. First 58/72: trained **6**, shuffled **12**, generic **48**, Alone **288**, untrained never. Trained final cue wins **23/23,24/24,22/22**. All pair-1 gates passed.
- **Pair 2, teacher 11010 / student 11012.** This pair ran only after pair 1 passed. Teacher scored **63/72** frozen (cue 0/1/2: **19/23,22/24,22/22**). Its new student receiver, synthetic update, independent no-message learning, source/control quality, DN decoding, matched starts/streams, and frozen-weight gates all passed. Teacher preference won on **86/94,85/88,93/94** observed cues; shuffle moved **173/276** positions. Frozen curves at **0/6/12/24/48/96/288**: trained **24/69/69/69/69/69/69**; Alone **24/24/46/69/69/69/69**; untrained **24/24/24/24/24/24/24**; shuffled **24/22/69/69/69/69/68**; generic **24/0/69/69/69/69/66**. Trained early mean **69/72** versus **52,24,57.25,51.75**, beating the strongest control by **+11.75/72**. First 58/72: trained **6**, shuffled and generic **12**, Alone **24**, untrained never. Trained final **69/72**, with **23/23,24/24,22/22** by cue. All pair-2 gates passed.
- **Conclusion and limits.** The initial two students and **both fresh independent teacher–student confirmation pairs** met the predeclared teacher-specific *early disconnected learning-speed* gates against Alone, untrained neural teacher, globally shuffled trained neural messages, and an exposure-matched pseudorandom generic neural message. All four trained-message students reached 58/72 at the round-6 frozen checkpoint; the earliest control in each pair was round 12. This is a replicated result **for the engineered post-sensory hybrid imitation controller on one deterministic Strategy A task and fixed opponent streams**, not proof of universal reliability, improved final accuracy, pure dopamine-mediated transfer, or biological communication. The student receives a teacher-preference target only after decoding its actual DN spikes; no correct-action label or teacher weights enter its update. The message buffer and auxiliary loss are engineered. Shuffled and generic controls sometimes reached the same final ceiling; the validated claim is **learning speed under disconnection**, not final superiority. The earlier one-bit proposal/veto and noisy pooled-decoder failures remain intact. No further architecture changes or experiments are warranted in this session.
- **Artifacts and reproduction.** Pair-1 write-once `data/rps_action_preference_confirmation_first.json` (SHA-256 `B6954E33ACE7A28A933AC5569CDDCF32A2B65F4E930719F8C4781D4DF3493DEA`); pair-2 write-once `data/rps_action_preference_confirmation_second.json` (SHA-256 `7E7570E7E6028440D8ED468263A40274E29709B86DB2E00429F973DA2E85127B`). Each stores receiver probes, teacher and control-quality summaries, all five student trial rows and seven frozen checkpoints, and exact gate outcomes. Reproduce sequentially only in a copy lacking these artifacts with `node tools/check_rps_action_preference_confirmation.mjs first` then `node tools/check_rps_action_preference_confirmation.mjs second`. The runner refuses a second-stage run unless the first artifact passed. The demo and prior artifacts were not modified.

### Locked Strategy B generalization of post-sensory action-preference teaching

No architecture, message code, learning equation, connectome, neural dynamics, dopamine plasticity, or demo change was made.

The independent audit's Strategy A chronology limitation remains: mutable local files document locking but are not immutable registration.

Each new student passed all **144** receiver probes: all forced action codes, VPN routes, and matched pre-pulse MBON features scored **12/12 per cue/condition**, with intended DN spikes and no off-target counts; controller/KC→MBON weights stayed fixed. Independent no-message students began at **24/72** and finished at **70/72** each with all observed cues correct. The synthetic decoded-target margin test passed. ### Locked causal ablation: DN spikes versus the same direct digital preference

- **Hypothesis and protocol.** The [prospective protocol](connectome-contribution-ablation-protocol.md) (SHA-256 `4DD5BE212C272AD93CCFF2DD4DBFCB988BD47AF510D524A227AD67883B95D9EA`) fixed archived reward-trained Strategy B teachers **11013/11014**, fresh students **11017/11018**, new opponent and evaluation streams, four arms (measured DN spikes, the identical teacher preference delivered digitally at 280 ms, silence, globally scrambled neural preference), 12×24 training, and frozen disconnected checkpoints **0/6/12/24/48/96/288**. The digital arm kept the full ordinary sensory simulation, message timing, and 620 ms update but omitted DN drive. The scramble preserved 276 messages and global action counts. No lesion was included because the update reads a separate DN spike-count buffer rather than postsynaptic DN outputs. The pathway diagram and dependency inventory are in the [report](connectome-contribution-ablation-report.md).
- **Prerequisites and controls.** Both archived teachers retained their earlier **67/72** and **69/72** frozen learning gates. Their new frozen preference tapes chose winning actions **260/276** and **267/276**, with every cue above 70%. Both fresh receivers passed all **144** no-message/forced-code probes: 12/12 decoded/routed per cue and condition, exact paired premessage MBON-feature equality, and unchanged weights. Fixed global scrambles moved **187/276** and **173/276** positions and had winning-action coincidence **91/276** and **100/276**, below 60% for each cue. Within each student, all arms had identical initial weights and cue/opponent sequences; all frozen evaluations lacked messages, exploration, reward learning, and imitation. KC→MBON multipliers remained one.
- **Full frozen wins/72.** Columns are training rounds **0, 6, 12, 24, 48, 96, 288**.

Neural exceeded both silence and scramble by at least the predefined **4/72** for each student.
